# Findings 0251 to 0500

249 findings: 214 confirmed, 14 unconfirmed, 21 refuted. [How the register works](README.md).

<a id="f251"></a>
### #251 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x384c40 — FUN_00384c40 (generator state machine) / FUN_00384800 (spawn) / FUN_003d1960 (enemy model generate)

THE ENEMY SPAWN CHAIN (MODELCTRL is a RED HERRING). be:sd("GENERATER",nil,-2,"START","GENE-xx") is the ONLY enemy-spawn entry point. beGenerater methods = START/STOP/STOPREVERS/RESET/RESTART/PERFECTDROP/ONFREEZE/OFFFREEZE (name table @0x4c6b50). START creates a per-player beGeneraterInfoWork (vtable 0x536050) whose per-frame state machine is FUN_00384c40 and whose spawn call is FUN_00384800; the model itself is built by FUN_003d1960 (vtable slot 0x53e448), which does DATABASE 'enemy' fetch -> MODEL/HPMAX from the row -> reads the row's RARITY (-e12 ref) -> SETRAREICON (crown icon only). Generator InfoWork live fields: +0x14 _enemyRamList (ptr), +0x1c _geneEnemyName ptr ('e_goburi'), +0x20 row, +0x24 HPMAX, +0x28 1.0f. Spawn position = the generator's matrix, which is initialised from the entry _pos/_genePosList. MODELCTRL/GENERATE is a DIFFERENT, per-model-class command path (see the new refutation of #156/#160/#162) and is not how enemies are placed.

**Notes:** PARTIAL CORRECTION (see #254): the spawn position is NOT the pre-baked generator matrix. FUN_00384540 reads position from _genePosList[_genePoint] and facing from _geneRotYRadianList[_genePoint] on EVERY spawn. For _genePosIsGlobal=1 rows _pos is only the radar/range anchor. Chain (GENERATER->beGeneraterData->beGeneraterInfoWork->FUN_00384c40->FUN_00384800->FUN_003d1960) and the MODELCTRL refutation stand.

**Evidence:** Running-game read of the generator InfoWork fields; retail code of FUN_00384c40, FUN_00384800 and FUN_003d1960

<a id="f252"></a>
### #252 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x505078 — stage-pack Lua GENERATER chunks / FUN_00384c40 start modes

ENEMY SPAWN TRIGGERS, TWO LAYERS. (1) LUA/Script: stage chunks call be:sd('GENERATER',nil,-2,'START'/'STOP'/'RESTART','GENE-xx') gated by be:FLGisOn(n)/FLGset, be:PLAYERgetPos (entry point 0..3), be:SYSgetPlayerNinzu, be:SYSctlDeviceFolder(...) and be:DMsetSD(...,\<frame time>) / DMrun. Worked example (stag0102 e621 022-layer-setup-3.lua): GENE-00 (the end-of-bridge e_ogre demo) is STARTed only when FLGisOn(355)==-1 (first visit), together with a demo camera + Digimon-info popup; the crown group GENE-a00..a05 is started from the SYSgetRandom roll; GENE-47/48/49/50 start unconditionally from 004-chunk.lua. (2) ENGINE, per-entry start mode _startModeStr: 'direct' = spawn as soon as the generator runs (no range gate); 'radar' = spawn when the player comes within range, and _startData is the SQUARED radius (live values 625=25^2, 1225=35^2, 2025=45^2, 3025=55^2, 4225=65^2) - this is why walking left triggered the 3 e_goburi; 'timer' = _startData is a countdown. _fillModeStr governs refill: 'wipeout' vs 'add' (and a timer variant). CONFIRMED LIVE: walked the pristine-disc bridge, 3x e_goburi (row 5, HP 649) spawned and generator InfoWorks read row=5 HPMAX=649.

**Evidence:** Stage-pack Lua (stag0102 e621) with a running-game screenshot

<a id="f253"></a>
### #253 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x3d50c0 — FUN_003d50c0 (beNDMWMdlObject family command handler) / FUN_003d4a20 / FUN_003d4ea0

CORRECTION: FUN_003d50c0 IS NOT THE ENEMY SPAWNER -- it is the g_000bcaps CHECKPOINT-CAPSULE command handler, and #156/#160/#162 misidentified it. Evidence from its own code and strings: it dispatches on cmd == 'GENERATE'(0x516648) / 'GENESTAGE'(0x516660) / 'RAISE'(0x5166a0); the big GENESTAGE branch calls GAMERAM::GETCOMMON, reads the player's saved BKCHIPSTAGE(0x5165e8) and BKCHIPPOS(0x5165f8), looks up the checkpoint for player index name_obj+0x8, then spawns model 'g_000bcaps'(0x516680) at that BKCHIPPOS position with sequence SQ_ENTR{n}P (0x516690) and issues GENERATE. Two sibling methods of the same vtable confirm the family: FUN_003d4a20 SAVES the player's current position into BKCHIPPOS, and FUN_003d4ea0 is the proximity switch (PLAYER,NEAR within 900 units -> SQ_SWON/SQ_SWOF, g_000bcaps.lua). The enemy model generate is the separate vtable slot 0x53e448 = FUN_003d1960 (see #251). The GENERATE branch here also calls vtable+0x8c (slot 35) with the literal 'REMOVE', not 'GENESTAGE' as #162 claimed. Net: MODELCTRL GENERATE is a generic per-model-class command path; it is NOT how enemies are placed.

**Notes:** Topic head for FUN_003d50c0 is the checkpoint-capsule handler, not enemy spawn: also read #156, #160, #162 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** FUN_003d50c0's own code and strings

### #254 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x384540 — FUN_00384540 (generator spawn) / beGeneraterData._genePosList / _geneRotYRadianList

POSITION + FACING EDIT PROVEN LIVE. Method: on a fresh area load, injected a UNIQUE float Y=123.456 into EVERY element of every row-5 beGeneraterData._genePosList and 0.0 into every _geneRotYRadianList element (11 entries: GENE-03/06/09/13/19/29/34/36/a00/b00/c00), then walked the bridge to trigger the radar spawns. Result: a RAM-wide search for the float 123.456 returned, besides the 11 data arrays, exactly THREE live model transforms (vtable 0x537F40) containing the translation (185.0, 123.456, 175.0) = GENE-03's authored (x,z) with the injected Y, and an IDENTITY 3x3 rotation (=0 rad, the injected value; GENE-03's authored rotY was 1.5708). The paused capture shows the Goburimon floating in mid-air. This confirms FUN_00384540 reads position from _genePosList[_genePoint] and facing from _geneRotYRadianList[_genePoint] ON EVERY SPAWN (it does NOT bake them at beGenerater::START), then advances _genePoint=(i+1)%count. => Both fields are live-editable knobs; a spawn's position is its own list element, and for _genePosIsGlobal=1 rows _pos is only the radar/range anchor, not the spawn point.

**Evidence:** Running-game edit of the row-5 generator position and rotation lists, with a screenshot

### #255 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x692b30 — beGeneraterInfoWork+0x1c (model name) / beBaseInfoData MODEL string column / FUN_00384c40 init

SPAWNED MODEL CANNOT BE SWAPPED LIVE THE WAY POSITION/FACING CAN (paused, only partly verified -- PINNED). Facts established: (1) a spawn's model name is the ROW's MODEL string (db string column, DB+0x34 -> data[r] -> strObj; text at strObj+8) and FUN_00384c40 LATCHES it into beGeneraterInfoWork+0x1c at generator INIT (first-run, when InfoRam+0x2c==0) -- unlike position/facing, it is not re-read per spawn. (2) Repointing the live InfoWork+0x1c alone changed nothing (no new spawn occurred, so the latched copy was never re-read). (3) Repointing the shared rowaEUR(tm)s MODEL string object (0x01749308+8) from 'e_goburi' to 'n_leomon' AND forcing re-init (write 0 InfoRam(0x535FF0)+0x2c, the InfoWork slot) DID create fresh generator InfoWorks reading model='n_leomon' -- so the string+re-init mechanism works -- but NO Leomon model object was created and none rendered: 'n_leomon' is present in the stage name manifest (0x012bfac0 pool, next to n_digilfm) but its GEOMETRY is evidently not resident in stag0102. (4) Same method with a RESIDENT enemy model ('e_ogre', row 36's string 0x69298c) produced a fresh generator InfoWork with model='e_ogre' and the row-5 enemy population went 3 -> 7, with green club-wielding Ogremon on screen where Goburimon had been -- strongly suggests the model swap DOES work for models that are actually loaded. NOT verified: the decisive MODELCTRL-registry instance-count check was aborted (user moved on). Method notes: forcing re-init by repeatedly zeroing InfoRam+0x2c leaks InfoWork objects; killing via HP=0 alone did not reliably free slots. NEXT: verify with a registry/instance-count check, and test in a stage whose pack actually contains the target model (e.g. a stage with Leomon, or the dev world).

**Notes:** UPDATED (#428): the missing decisive check is DONE and the finding is now fully confirmed. Same-generator A/B (GENE-13, stag0102): row->36 e_ogre (resident) latched and spawned (enemy-list count 1, rendered); row->114 e_mummy latched but spawned nothing (count 0; 0 live row-114 beNDMWMdlPEBaseInfoWork, 4 live row-36), and the stage resource pool has no mummy asset. The model latch and the geometry-residency boundary are both proven; #427 adds the live RAM tool (swap_spawn.py) and the durable ISO rewrite (patch_spawn.py), and explains the re-latch route (clear beGeneraterInfoRam._work at +0x2c). The InfoWork population extends above 0x1800000 (#430).
normalised no evidence recorded

<a id="f256"></a>
### #256 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x384800 — FUN_00384800 (item-drop roll); beGeneraterPlayerData/beGeneraterItemData/beGeneraterItemDataOne

DROP TABLE FORMAT SOLVED. A generator's item drop is a 3-level structure: beGeneraterPlayerData (vtable 0x535DE0) +0x08 _geneCount +0x0c _geneMaxCount +0x10 _itemDataList +0x14 _itemLastDataList; each list is indexed by DIFFICULTY (iVar7+0xc of a GAMERAM 'DIFFICULTY' object) and yields a beGeneraterItemData {+0x08 NAME, +0x0c -> OneList}; the OneList's elements are beGeneraterItemDataOne {+0x08 category string, +0x0c item id, +0x10 _rank, +0x14 _rate}. ROLL (FUN_00384800, the item-drop routine; callers 0x384ed0/0x385140/0x385300 in the beGenerater update): r = RNG%10000; walk the OneList subtracting each _rate; the first entry that takes r below 1 is the drop. So P(entry k) = rate_k/10000 and P(no drop at all) = 1 - sum(rates)/10000 - e.g. the guard-room box table nZ1obje1 has sum 4330, so only 43.3% of breaks drop anything. The drop is then spawned through MODELCTRL GENERATE using the engine category table at 0x4c8f30 (conItem/insItem/impItem/weapon/armor1/armor2/abChip); _itemLastDataList replaces _itemDataList when param_3 != 0 and the generator holds exactly one enemy. beGenerater also has _isPerfectDrop (0x5050b8) and the PERFECTDROP method.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/drop-tables.md

### #257 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00535eb0 — beFileListInfo manifests: NDMW.AFS entries 80-177

STAGE -> AFS PACK MAP FOUND. NDMW.AFS entries 80..177 are beFileListInfo MANIFESTS, one per stage, each holding the stage name plus the AFS entry indices of its packs as '[0.NNN]' strings (a big pack + the entry before it). This gives the authoritative stage->pack table that the earlier work inferred by adjacency. Examples: dng101b1=80->{178,179}, stag0101=139->{613,614}, stag0102=140->{620,621}, stagBS01=155->{729,730}, and stagGF01=161->{771,772}, stagGF02=162->{778,779}, stagGF03=163->{785,786}, stagGF04=164->{792,793}. The three generator-table variants (generatorH/N/V.igb) follow the Lua pack: stagGF01's are 773/774/775, stagGF03's are 787/788/789. The GF pack numbering CORRECTS the lua folder labels: entry 772 is stagGF01 (the Guard Room, which spawns the trap box g_trapboxz05), not 'the gate'; 779 is stagGF02, 786 is stagGF03 (where the e_mecha4 Goblin Fortress boss actually runs), 793 is stagGF04.

**Evidence:** Read from the beFileListInfo manifests, NDMW.AFS entries 80..177 of the disc image

### #258 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x012a9260 — GWCS-05 beGeneraterData row 378; Z1obje1 beGeneraterItemData in AFS 773/774/775

GUARD ROOM (stagGF01) DESTRUCTIBLE-BOX DROP TABLE = Z1obje1 (difficulty-indexed n/h/v). The Guard Room's breakables are generator 'GWCS-05' with stat row 378 = g_woodencasez05 (the GWCS-* family is the fortress's wooden cases), plus Lua-placed MODELCTRL props g_breakinggatez05 x4 and g_trapboxz05 x1 (entry 772 chunk_0009). stagGF01's generator pack (entries 773/774/775) contains exactly two drop-table families: Z1gobr3 (for the GENE-* Goburimon) and Z1obje1 (the object/breakable table), so GWCS-05's drops come from Z1obje1. CONTENT read live from a Goblin Fortress save state and cross-checked against the pack by element count and tuple multiset: nZ1obje1 = 17 entries summing 4330/10000 (so 43.3% drop chance on Normal): weapon ids 0/25/50/75 rate 100 each (Battle Hawk / Long Sword / Dyno Tooth / Kamikiri Maru), armor1 ids 18/9/0 rate 60 (Element / Cipher / Brave Core alpha), insItem 0 ranks 0-4 rate 1000/600/300/100/50, conItem 0 rate 1000, conItem 3 rate 100, conItem 1 rate 300, conItem 7 rate 150, conItem 8 rate 150. hZ1obje1 = 37 entries sum 6050; vZ1obje1 = 30 entries sum 5910. The labels are self-describing: n/h/v = Normal/Hard/Very Hard, Z1 = World 1, obje = 'object' (breakable prop).

### #259 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00536050 — live beGeneraterData _playerDataList -> _itemDataList; AFS 622/623/624 (stag0102) vs 773/774/775 (stagGF01) vs 787/788/789 (stagGF03)

Drop-table names encode difficulty + world + source: nZ1gobr1, hZ1rare3, vZ1blos1, nZ1mec41, nZ1cont1, nZ1obje1... (gobr=Goburimon, ogre=Ogremon, rare=Raremon, blos=Blossomon, mec4=e_mecha4).

<a id="f260"></a>
### #260 · unconfirmed

**Where:** igBinary generator pack list serialisation (NDMW.AFS 608/773/787)

PACK LIST-DATA ENCODING - partially solved, not needed for the drop tables. In a generator pack the object section is followed by a trailing 'list data' pool (hdr[6] = its byte size; hdr[4] = object-data byte size, hdr[5] = object count - all three verified exactly: object sizes tile hdr[4] and the pool is the last hdr[6] bytes). A list object (igVec3fList/igFloatList/beGeneraterItemDataOneList/...) is serialised as {u32 count, u32 capacity, u32 X}; for VALUE lists X locates raw floats in the pool, for OBJECT lists the pool holds the element ids. The ids are NOT object indices and NOT monotone with file order (they are a loader instantiation-order numbering), and X is not a plain u32 pool index (the hard/very-hard id arrays would then overlap, which is impossible since their contents are disjoint). Verified by a consistency search on entry 787 using live ground truth: the nZ1mec41 array sits at pool u32 index 3, hZ1mec41 at index 7 (disjoint, lengths 4 and 34), giving an id->object map with id 9->obj8, 11->9, 12->10, 13->11, 24..32->obj20..28, 39..47->obj34..41. X for those is 10, 18, 21, so X = 2*index+4 fits n and h but not v - the X->offset rule is still open; reading the arrays needs either the loader or live RAM. Conclusion: drop-table CONTENT is fully obtainable statically by count+tuple matching against a live reference, which is what was done here.

**Evidence:** Consistency search on NDMW.AFS entry 787 against running-game values

<a id="f261"></a>
### #261 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x006b5d48 — AREA.AFS entry 9 item tables (live RAM string columns); dw4_itemnames.py

ITEM DISPLAY NAMES RESOLVED for every drop-table category. The loaded AREA.AFS-entry-9 igBinary object graph in RAM holds one string column PER CATEGORY, each row = {NAME, MODEL/slot, DESCRIPTION} (weapons add a STAT row: {name, model, stat, desc} = 4 rows/item). Columns found by the standard string-column pattern (col+0x34 -> container -> +0x10 -> data array of strObj -> +8 = text): conItem col@0x17475c8 data@0x17530f0 (12 items: HP Disk a/b/g, MP Disk a/b/g, Cure Disk, Raise Disk, Gate Disk, Recovery, B. Pack, Key Chain), insItem col@0x174be70 data@0x174dc58 (100 items = 20 elements x 5 grades: Petty/Mega/Giga/Terra/infinity x Fire, Ice, Bolt, Dark, Venom, Impact, Cutter, Bomb, Heal Lt, Aura, Raise, Cure Lt, Cure, Speed, Freeze, Resist, Pwr Up, Pwr Dwn, Sleep, Bind), weapon col@0x174c210 data@0x17597a0 (id = row; model column is the w_* name), abChip col@0x17444c0 (Wisdom/Spirit/Speed chips), plugs col@0x1749658 (BIT/xdata). ID RULES: weapon id = model*5+grade (grades abcde, model order in item_ids.json); armor1 id = model*3+grade (9 cores, order Brave/Aegis/Vulture/Cipher/Astral/Wizard/Element/Spirit/Divine); armor2 id = model*3+grade (boards, order Achilles/Atlas/Meteor/Fire/Ice/Bolt/Dark/Armor/Beast/Rack/EXP/BIT/Mana/Resist); conItem id = row; insItem id = element with rank = grade (flat row = id*5+rank). CAVEAT: the drop routine as decompiled passes only the id + the category ordinal to MODELCTRL GENERATE and does not visibly read +0x10, so insItem's rank->grade plumbing is inferred from the element x 5 table layout + the monotonically decreasing rates of the five same-id rows, not proven from code. Tool: dw4_itemnames.py.

**Notes:** CORRECTED by #262: the weapon / armor1 / armor2 / conItem rows in this finding are verified and unaffected, but the insItem row is WRONG -- I attributed the 20-element x 5-grade table (Petty Fire... infinity Bind) to insItem and it is actually the wazaPanel technique table. The game's DB name list (RAM 0x17dd1e0) shows insItem is a distinct DB from wazaPanel/pcTech/eneatk, and the insItem table is the sub-slot family (BIT, three P Data, eleven plug types x 2 variants). See #262 and docs/systems/drop-tables.md section 5b.

**Evidence:** Running-game read of the AREA.AFS entry 9 string columns

<a id="f262"></a>
### #262 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x17dd1e0 — DATABASE name list @0x17dd1e0; insItem col@0x1749658 vs wazaPanel col@0x174be70

CORRECTION to #261: insItem IS NOT THE TECHNIQUE/SKILL TABLE (after the user pointed out crates cannot drop skills). The loaded igBinary data contains an ALPHABETICAL DATABASE NAME LIST at RAM 0x17dd1e0: abChip, armor1, armor2, conItem, eneatk, enemy, impItem, insItem, pcTech, shinka, weapon, wazaPanel. insItem is therefore a SEPARATE database from wazaPanel (the technique/panel table), pcTech (player techniques) and eneatk (enemy attacks), so the element x 5-grade table (Petty/Mega/Giga/Terra/infinity x Fire/Ice/Bolt/Dark/Venom/Impact/Cutter/Bomb/Heal Lt/Aura/Raise/Cure Lt/Cure/Speed/Freeze/Resist/Pwr Up/Pwr Dwn/Sleep/Bind, 100 rows, string column col@0x174be70 data@0x174dc58, sitting among the waza field rows RANK/SKILL/DELAY) belongs to wazaPanel, NOT to insItem. What the insItem DB actually holds is the SUB-SLOT family: its string column is col@0x1749658 (container 0x174bc78, data 0x174a2b8, 78 rows = 26 items x 3 strings {name, model, slot}) = BIT, three 'P Data' entries, then eleven plug types x 2 variants (Strgth/Def/MaxHP/MaxMP/Wisdom/Spirit/Speed/⊂/⊃/∪/∩ ↑Plug), slots g_000bit / g_000xdata1 / g_000xdata2 -- and the insItem class record at 0x17496cc sits 0x74 bytes from that column. Lesson recorded: #261 matched a table to a category BY LAYOUT (element x 5 grades looked like the drop rows' rank ladder) instead of by an actual link, and that is what produced the wrong 'skills drop from crates' claim. STILL OPEN: (a) which family member insItem id 0/1/2 selects, (b) what the repeated rank 0..4 sub-roll means (a variant/quality ladder of some kind). The weapon/armor1/armor2/conItem names in #261 are NOT affected -- those tables were matched and cross-checked independently.

**Notes:** CONFIRMED LIVE (see #263): forcing insItem id 0 in the Guard Room crate table drops the BIT item, visible on the ground; rank 0 and rank 4 give the same item, so the rank field is a rate ladder only. The remaining unknowns listed in this finding are therefore closed: id 0 = BIT, and the repeated sub-roll is not a grade ladder.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/drop-tables.md

<a id="f263"></a>
### #263 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00384800 — FUN_00384800 drop roll; live GWCS-05 beGeneraterData; stagGF01 crate test

DROP ROLL VERIFIED LIVE IN THE GUARD ROOM and insItem RESOLVED = the BIT item. Method: loaded stagGF01 through the engine's own debug stage-select (dev-world digi-elf-2 state -> 2x 'next page' -> cursor down x2 -> stagGF01 -> 'Guard Room' banner), dumped EE RAM (33554432 B, ~4 min via pine read 0 --size 33554432 --out), located the live beGeneraterData GWCS-05 (0x13efa84, row 378) -> playerData 0x13efbc8 -> _itemDataList -> the three beGeneraterItemData (nZ1obje1/h/vZ1obje1) -> OneLists at 0x13e2390 (17 entries) / 0x13ede80 (37) / 0x13ee010 (30); entry rate field = entry+0x14. Then set EVERY rate to 0 and exactly ONE entry to 10000 (tool gf01_force.py, which matches entries by reading the category string at entry+8) and smashed crates. RESULTS: (1) forcing insItem id 0 rank 0 dropped the BIT item -- visible as a gold item on the ground, confirmed on screen; (2) forcing insItem id 0 rank 4 dropped the SAME item, so the five same-id rows are one item at five probabilities, i.e. the rank field is not used by the drop path (consistent with the decompiled FUN_00384800, which reads only +0x08/+0x0c/+0x14 and never +0x10); (3) with the Hard and Very-Hard tables completely zeroed, drops still happened -> the difficulty in use was NORMAL, so nZ1obje1 is the live table for this save; (4) therefore the insItem rows are NOT inert and the 43.3% Normal drop chance is real. All 84 original rates were written back afterwards and verified (nZ1obje1 sums to 4330 again). Also learned: the menu has NO camera control and DW4 forces analog, so menus need the STICK (d-pad does nothing) -- 'input stick l 0 1.0 --backend pad --ms 100' = exactly one menu step.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/drop-tables.md

<a id="f264"></a>
### #264 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00384800 — FUN_00384800 caller select +0x10/+0x14; live GF01 beGeneraterData set; 01-fortress-gate_e772 Lua

ENEMY DROP TABLES DECODED FOR THE GOBLIN FORTRESS (stagGF01) + two corrections. (1) All 23 enemy generators in GF01 point at the SAME three per-difficulty lists: nZ1gobr3 (11 entries, sum 3502 = 35.02% drop chance), hZ1gobr3 (25, sum 3170 = 31.70%), vZ1gobr3 (21, sum 4050 = 40.50%); every generator resolves to identical table CONTENTS, so which Goburimon dies is irrelevant to the loot. Rows are _geneEnemyId 0 and 2 = ordinary e_goburi (Goburimon) rows, so the stage holds no other enemy type. (2) THE ONE EXCEPTION IS GENE-19: its three lists are all comonNOTG01, which has 0 entries -> those 14 enemies can NEVER drop anything. Lua: 01-fortress-gate_e772/000-dm-run.lua takes control off the player (OFCTRL) and STARTs GENE-23 (the room fight, 12 goburimon) after arming the breaking gates; 017-damage-calc-3.lua (gate cracked) STOPs GENE-23 and STARTs GENE-00 (12) + GENE-19 (14, no drops). The trailing number in START 'GENE-xx', N is a FRAME DELAY not a count (same slot is ONCTRL 59); the total comes from _geneCount. (3) THE 'LAST ENEMY' LIST IS INERT: FUN_00384800's caller selects _itemLastDataList (+0x14) over _itemDataList (+0x10) when the generator is on its final enemy (0x384948 bne v0,v1; 0x384950 lw v0,20(a0) vs 0x384970 lw v0,16(a0)), but ALL 24 spawners in GF01 have the two lists byte-identical, so nothing in the fortress is a guaranteed drop. (4) CORRECTION: _isPerfectDrop is NOT at 0x5050b8 -- that address holds the reflection NAME STRING '_isPerfectDrop' (next string 'beFontInfo'), not the flag. The engine has a PERFECTDROP generator method (index 5 in the name table at 0x4c6b50) but NO Lua script in the game calls it (0 hits across lua/), so no reachable always-drop state. (5) SPEEDRUN SHAPE: enemies are the WORSE source than crates on every difficulty (35.0/31.7/40.5% vs 43.3/60.5/59.1%) and Hard is the enemy table's worst (31.7%) while being the crates' best (60.5%); the enemy table's unique value is Hard's 21% weapon rate (ten weapons at grade EPSILON 1.0-1.5% each + the same ten at grade DELTA 0.8% each), which the crate lists never offer. Normal gives only alpha-grade weapons (10%) and mostly the BIT install item (16.0%); Raise Disk drops from enemies ONLY on Normal (3.0%) and Very Hard (1.0%).

**Evidence:** docs/systems/drop-tables.md

<a id="f265"></a>
### #265 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x012b4ad8 — live stagGF01 enemy table force + pickup; insItem string pool; 0x81F7 glyph

x-data: NAMING + LIVE ENEMY-DROP VERIFICATION. (1) The insItem rows I had been rendering 'P Data' are x-data, and the user confirmed it in game ('yeah it's x data'). The display string in the igBinary pool is b'\x81\xf7 Data' = U+00B6 PILCROW + ' Data' (CP932 0x81F7); the MODEL/slot string immediately after it is 'xdata' (hence slot names g_000xdata1/g_000xdata2), and the pickup icon is an X. So 'P Data' was my mis-rendering of the 0x81F7 glyph; the item is x-data. Note the game also uses OTHER set symbols for other items (0x81B8 = '-element-of-' for 'Data_Destroyer', 0x81C9 = vee for 'BIT_Board_a', 0x81BC-0x81BF = subset/superset/union/intersection for the elemental plugs, 0x81AA = up-arrow for the stat plugs) -- so the symbol byte is significant and must not be flushed to ASCII. (2) ENEMY DROPS VERIFIED LIVE (this closes the gap flagged in #264): in stagGF01 with the nZ1gobr3 OneList forced to a single entry (insItem id 1 @10000, every other weight 0), EVERY Goburimon killed left an x-data pickup on the ground; and the user independently reported 'nothing dropped from those last enemies' at the exact moment the table had been zeroed without any entry set back to 10000 (my force call for insItem id 2 found no match in the Normal list -- id 2 does not exist on Normal -- so the whole table summed to 0). A zero-sum list dropping nothing is exactly what the FUN_00384800 roll predicts, so the pair of observations is a clean positive/negative control. (3) ONE PICKUP = x5: walking over one x-data drop pops the item's icon with '5' beside it (screenshot), matching the user's report that the first lot gave 5. (4) STILL OPEN: whether insItem id 1 and id 2 pay out different amounts -- they never co-occur on a difficulty (id 1 = Normal+Hard, id 2 = Hard+Very Hard), and I could not isolate an id-2-only pickup (a reload to clear the ground and re-kill was attempted but the drops could not be stepped on within the turn budget). (5) Live table addresses in stagGF01 (stable across area reloads, verified twice): nZ1gobr3 OneList DATA 0x13EF460 (11), hZ1gobr3 0x13ED1A8 (25), vZ1gobr3 0x12B47D0 (21); nZ1gobr3 insItem-id-1 entry @0x12B4AD8. NB the enemy-table entries are the ONE-LIST DATA array -- an earlier version of gf01_force.py used the OneList OBJECT address and read garbage.

**Notes:** normalised address corrected from 0x0012b4ad8 (nine digits) to 0x012b4ad8 — the sibling rows cite EE RAM objects at 0x01xxxxxx, and the other reading would sit inside the Sony library code.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/drop-tables.md

**Where:** 0x01173158 — *(0x0058a1e8) matrix translation; stag0101 GWCS/GBRL positions; PLAYERPOINT entry table @0x00dca7d4

PLAYER POSITION FIELD IS A PLACEMENT RECORD, NOT A LIVE TELEPORT TARGET - and the stag0101 case/barrel identities. (1) IDENTITIES, from the live generator set in the goblin_pass_crate_barrell savestate (stage identified from the RAM load log as stag0101, Valley Entrance, at the Goblin Pass gate): the two objects in front of the player are GWCS-01 (row 375, model family g_woodencasez01, 12.5 units away at (425,71,-126)) = the GATE CASE on nZ1gate, and GBRL-01 (row 372, 13.4 units at (415,71,-128)) = the BIT BARREL on nZ1bit. This independently corroborates the user's report that the box gives Gate Disks far more often than the 1.5-2.0% of the ordinary crate/container lists. (2) GWCS-03 IS A THIRD WOODEN CASE, not the barrel: row 375, the RAISE CASE on nZ1reizu, at (115,50,275), 441 units from the save point, in a cluster with GWCS-02 (3 cases, ordinary nZ1obje1) and GWCS-04 (1, ordinary) plus GBRL-02 (4 barrels, ordinary); GBOX-01 (the container box on nZ1cont1) is separate at (-188,61,-20). Generator positions read with the corrected nearby_generators.py: +0x1c holds a POINTER to the position VList whose data is a flat array of 12-byte triples - treating +0x1c itself as the list happens to work for single-point enemy generators only (their _pos anchor IS the spawn point) and reads garbage for multi-point prop generators. (3) THE TRANSFORM AT *(0x0058a1e8) IS NOT WRITABLE AS A TELEPORT. It is the 4th row (translation) of a 4x4 matrix at transform+0x00 (rows at +0x00/+0x10/+0x20 rotation, +0x30 translation = 0x01173158 for 1P). Evidence: (a) it does NOT track walking - after 2s of movement input that visibly moves the character it is unchanged to the bit; (b) writing it does not move the rendered character - writes round-trip and persist (nothing rewrites the field) and were tested at three targets including (-188,62,-20), where the on-screen scenery (a barrel) cannot exist, so the unchanged view is conclusive; (c) it DOES read correctly immediately after a stage load, which is why it has been usable as the player position in earlier sessions. (d) Writing it can WEDGE the character: after a write, movement input produced no pixel change at all until the save state was reloaded (a clean copy was loaded from slot 18 to undo this). The live movement/render position remains unidentified - two two-walk snapshot diffs over 0x800000-0x1300000 and 0x1300000-0x1800000 found no consistently-moving float, but the second attempt is invalid because the state's character is FROZEN: in this state a 2.5-4s movement input changes the same ~0.3% of pixels as idling, so it is a scripted/control-locked moment and diffing cannot reveal movement. (4) FOUND TOOL: the stage's entry-point table (the PLAYERPOINT/SETGENERATEPOS targets) is a flat array of 12-byte xyz triples at 0x00dca7d4 in stag0101 (e.g. (417.01,72.02,-106.22), (417.18,72.00,-119.96), (427.45,72.00,-117.33), (402.37,53.53,-10.06)) - these are the positions the GAME teleports to, i.e. the supported route to move the character. Tool: teleport_player.py (round-trip + verify).

**Evidence:** teleport_player.py

<a id="f270"></a>
### #270 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0111ee50 — player position mirrors; teleport_player.py; stag0101 mirror set

TELEPORTING THE PLAYER WORKS: write the ~8 MIRRORED position triples, not the MDL transform (SOLVED). The live world position is mirrored into EIGHT identical 12-byte xyz triples (stag0101: 0x0111ee50, 0x012fecc0, 0x01383e50, 0x0165135c, 0x01651368, 0x016b9720, 0x016de1c0, 0x017bdf10) plus a 4-corner collision FOOTPRINT at 0x017c25e8/0x017c2648/0x017c26a8/0x017c2708 (+/-3 units; not needed for a teleport). Writing all eight moves the character on screen - verified end to end twice: (445,72,-110) then back to (430,72,-120), each producing ~27% frame change, i.e. the same as a real walk. The mirror set does NOT include *(0x0058a1e8)+48 (the 'MDL transform'): that field is a PLACEMENT RECORD (see #269) - proof: after the teleports it still read (419.69,72,-114.97), the value it had at load, while all eight mirrors read the new position. FINDING THE MIRRORS (the hard part, now solved): they cannot be found by value - pine find reports 'no match' for u32 values that provably exist (0x436f536e read back from 0x0125a438 was not found even with --start/--end covering that address), so scan a RAM window in Python; and the differential method is: read 16 MB at 0x800000, walk the character one step in an OPEN direction, read again, keep triples that moved, then keep those sharing the dominant displacement. THE CRITICAL GOTCHA: walking into a wall yields ZERO candidates - my first three attempts failed for exactly that reason (I walked 'up' twice in a state where only 'down' was open, seeing 0.3% frame change and wrongly concluding the game was control-locked). A control measurement of pixel change separates 'blocked by wall' (0.3%) from 'moved' (27-53%). Addresses are stable across state reloads of the same stage but a different stage allocates differently -> recalibrate per stage. LIMIT: teleporting to a point whose SECTION is not loaded makes the game try to stream it and HANGS the load (observed with a ~300-unit jump: the area-loading tip appeared and stayed), so keep teleports inside the loaded section, walk across a boundary, teleport again ('hopscotch'), and reload the state if it hangs. TOOL: teleport_player.py (--show/--calibrate/X Y Z), cache teleport.json. Verified in the goblin_pass_crate_barrell savestate at the Goblin Pass (stag0101), where the generator positions it was validated against come from nearby_generators.py.

**Evidence:** Running-game teleport test: writing the eight mirrors moved the character on screen, verified twice

<a id="f273"></a>
### #273 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001E6B20 — igMersenneTwisterRandomNumber::sgenrand (vtable slot 23) / Reset (slot 13, 0x1E6B80)

THE GAME RNG IS MT19937 WITH THE ORIGINAL 1997 sgenrand SEEDING (multiplier 69069, NOT 1812433253) AND A SINGLE HARD-CODED START SEED OF **5150 (0x141E)**. sgenrand=0x1E6B20; the reset/init method 0x1E6B80 (vtable slot 13) seeds with the literal 'li a1,5150' at 0x1E6BA4 - the ONLY 5150 constant and the only 69069 (0x10DCD) in the whole ELF. Reset is invoked through the engine's generic object-init path (0x2FF3A0 calls slots 3,13,9,...). PROOF: reconstructing MT19937 from sgenrand(5150) reproduces the live 624-word state EXACTLY in every one of the 8 captured RAM snapshots, at three different points on the sequence (twist#4/5/19).

**Notes:** Tool: scripts/dw4_rng.py (check/seq/sites). Snapshot matches (mti, state0, twist#, draws since boot): crown6 155/6eb16ca3/#4/2027; pre_load+pre_load2 318/2b8d2459/#5/2814; before_spawn 196/a8a088ad/#19/11428; pk_ram2 212/#19/11444; after_spawn 256/#19/11488; nc_ram 280/#19/11512; pink_ram 533/#19/11765. This SUPERSEDES the sgenrand/init_genrand confusion in refuted #167/#168 and extends confirmed #171: #171 correctly found the 69069-based seeding is absent as stock init_genrand, but did not identify the seeding function or the seed. The build is the classic mt19937.c 'sgenrand' variant (mt[i]=seed&0xffff0000; seed=69069*seed+1; mt[i]|=(seed&0xffff0000)>>16; seed=69069*seed+1), confirmed against the asm at 0x1E6B20-0x1E6B78 (mti=624 at the end). Topic head for Game RNG is MT19937 sgenrand(5150): also read #167, #168, #171 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** docs/systems/rng-verification.md

<a id="f274"></a>
### #274 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x006FF180 — igMersenneTwisterRandomNumber singleton; owner = *(0x58A1B8), +0x30

RNG OBJECT LIVE LAYOUT (stable, non-ASLR heap): *(0x58A1B8)=engine manager 0x6FF110; manager+0x30 -> RNG instance 0x6FF180; +0x00 vtable 0x52B8E0; +0x04 refcount/flags (0x0A000001); +0x08 _mti (current index 0..624); +0x0C _mt[624] (2496 bytes; object size 0x9CC, ends at 0x06FFB4C exclusive). genrand reads mt[mti], tempers, increments mti; at mti>=624 it twists in place and resets mti=0. Exactly ONE such object exists in 32MB (only two 0x52B8E0 words in RAM: this object and the class descriptor's vtable field at 0x65A8EC). Implication: mt[mti] is the untempered word consumed next - writing it forces the next output.

**Notes:** Class names igRandomNumber / igMersenneTwisterRandomNumber are in the reflection name pool (0x4E48D0/0x4E4900), with _mti/_mt registered as properties. Class descriptor at *(0x562B88)=0x65A890 (vtable 0x532BB0). Dr. the same layout is used by finding #171 (index@0x6FF188, state@0x6FF18C) - consistent; #171 had no note that +0x04 is a header word, which is why reading mti at +4 (167772161) looks wrong.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/rng.md

<a id="f275"></a>
### #275 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00371F10 — SYSgetRandom (Lua API id 4; stub 0x370888; real impl 0x371F10)

SYSgetRandom IS THE GAME'S ONLY RNG CONSUMER. 0x371F10 = genrand % a0 (divu/mfhi). 'jal 0x371F10' occurs exactly ONCE in the ELF (from the Lua dispatch stub 0x370888), and the manager+0x30 RNG pointer is loaded at exactly ONE site (0x371F28). All 10 reads of the manager global *(0x58A1B8) were inspected; no other uses +0x30. The reset/seed (slot 13->slot 23) is only reachable via the generic object-init path. THEREFORE every state change in play is a Lua be:SYSgetRandom(M) call. ENUMERATED: 112 call sites in 44 chunks across 21 NDMW.AFS entries; moduli {1:28, 2:11, 3:2, 5:1, 6:1, 10:1, 19:14, 99:4, 121:2, 179:12, 239:12, 299:12, 419:12}. AREA.AFS has 0 Lua chunks (0 SYSgetRandom).

**Notes:** REFUTED the claim 'SYSgetRandom is the only RNG consumer' is WRONG. SYSgetRandom IS the only Lua-facing binding and 'jal 0x371F10' does occur once, but native code reaches the SAME MT object via a different chain. Proof: (a) the RNG object at 0x6FF180 has exactly ONE incoming pointer in all of RAM - at manager+0x30 (0x6FF140); (b) 53 of the 60 occurrences of the manager pointer 0x6FF110 in RAM sit at object+0x10 with a vtable at object+0x00, so 'lw v0,16(sX); lw a0,48(v0)' loads the manager then the RNG; (c) at least 11 native functions do exactly that and call vtable slot 24 (genrand) then take a modulo. Examples: FUN_00384800 @0x3849AC (item drop, genrand%10000), 0x376594 (%var), 0x39129C (&0xf), 0x39BDB0 (%6144), 0x429EA0 (%10000), 0x42F288 (%1000), 0x4480C8 (%6000), 0x4480F0 (%var), 0x4481B0 (%620), 0x4489A8 (%10), 0x448CEC (%var). So combat/loot/other systems advance the stream too. See new finding for the corrected inventory. Folded into #281, the current answer for Native and Lua RNG consumer inventory; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/rng-sites.tsv

<a id="f276"></a>
### #276 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x006FF188 — RNG _mti; sequence identity across snapshots

THERE IS NO IN-PLAY RESEED AND NO SECOND RNG SEQUENCE. All 8 captured 32MB RAM snapshots match the SAME MT19937 sequence seeded once by sgenrand(5150) (full 624-word match at twist#4, #5 and #19) - reseeding or a second instance is excluded. Finding #172 already measured an area load as _mti 318->321 (3 draws, state words unchanged), i.e. load does NOT reseed. CONSEQUENCE: loading the game's memory-card save does not reseed either; power-cycling restarts at seed 5150; a fresh boot + a fixed route reproduces the same rolls. A PCSX2 savestate carries the state (full RAM); the 80KB game save was not found to contain it. The reflection system registers _mti/_mt as properties but no path writes them (single-sequence result rules out any restore in these states).

**Notes:** Draws since boot per snapshot (dw4_rng.py check): crown6 2027, pre_load 2814, pink_ram 11765. The counter is exactly the number of SYSgetRandom calls since boot (one _mti bump per call). Implication for manipulation: the whole run's randomness is a function of the draw count; read _mti to predict, spend no-op M==1 draws to shift, or write _mt[_mti] to force the next output.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/rng-verification.md

<a id="f277"></a>
### #277 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0024FEE0 — Lua math library: math.random / math.randomseed

A SECOND, UNRELATED GENERATOR EXISTS: the embedded Lua's math.random (0x24FEE0) and math.randomseed (0x2500A0) use the C library rand/srand (0x128FA0 / 0x128F88), NOT the game's MT19937 (math.random draws rand % 0x7FFFFFFF). It is dead in practice: no game Lua chunk calls math.random/math.randomseed (0 hits in 5291 decompiled chunks), and only one 'jal 0x128FA0' and one 'jal 0x128F88' occur in the ELF (each inside the respective math-lib function). Registered in the Lua math-lib table at 0x4EB9E8 (name 'random' -> 0x24FEE0) / 0x4EB9F0 ('randomseed' -> 0x2500A0).

**Notes:** Don't confuse this with the game RNG. Any tool/manipulation targeting rand would not affect SYSgetRandom outcomes and vice versa.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/rng.md

<a id="f278"></a>
### #278 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x006FF188 — RNG _mti; AFS entry 816 chunk c100 (funcRandom); AFS entry 607 chunk c18

NEW-GAME RNG TIMELINE (measured) + THE HUB DRAINS THE RNG CONTINUOUSLY. The *seed* never changes (always 5150); the *state* advances by one every SYSgetRandom. Measured draw counts on the real new-game path (extracted offline from the curated states via p2s_ram.py + dw4_rng.py check): title screen / character creation (smoke-title, dw4-create-menu) = 0 draws (state is exactly the raw sgenrand(5150) array, mti=624); op-load-in = 16; op-ready = 279; fresh save at the World-1 gate (before the practice-stage load) = 4078 (twist#7, mti=334); fresh save before finishing the practice stage = 5258 (twist#9, mti=266). THE BIG JUMP IS NOT A ROLL: the only resident draw chunk in the World-1-gate state is AFS entry 816 (lobby-ofanimon-seraphimon) chunk c100, whose model WPos handler calls funcRandom = SYSgetRandom(1) (a no-op that still stamps _mti). LIVE: loaded that state and did nothing; _mti = 330 -> 458 -> 590 over 10 s = ~26 draws/second. A full live read-back (vtable 0x52B8E0, mti 323, state0 91738063, twist#9, 5315 draws) is still exactly on the sgenrand(5150) sequence, so no reseed. IN THE PRACTICE STAGE the only resident draw chunk is entry 607/chunk 18 (SYSgetRandom(6), damage-reaction tier), firing on taking damage.

**Notes:** Consequence for prediction/manipulation: the RNG position is a function of FRAMES spent in the hub (real time), not a fixed script, so the crown roll you eventually get depends on how long you lingered. Read _mti live (0x6FF188) rather than trying to replay from boot. Tool: dw4_rng.py check (fixed j=0 -> 0 draws).

**Evidence:** docs/systems/rng-verification.md

At least 11 native sites match, each with a roll-shaped modulus: 0x3849AC %10000 (FUN_00384800 item-drop roll), 0x376594 %var, 0x39129C &0xf, 0x39BDB0 %6144, 0x429EA0 %10000, 0x42F288 %1000, 0x4480C8 %6000, 0x4480F0 %var, 0x4481B0 %620, 0x4489A8 %10, 0x448CEC %var. A separate global *(0x5871D0) is also called via slot 24 at 0x3300F8 - unidentified. These native draws fire on combat/loot/AI, so the RNG advances during normal play and not only via Lua.

**Notes:** The 112 Lua sites and the native sites all hit the SAME object (only one 0x52B8E0 vtable instance in RAM), so the stream is shared. Measured idle drift: ~26 draws/s in the hub (Lua e816/c100) and ~6 draws/s in a field stage (Goblin Pass state) where the only resident Lua draw chunk is flag-gated - consistent with native periodic draws. ### #281 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001E6B20 — native genrand consumers; sgenrand 0x1E6B20 / Reset 0x1E6B80

NATIVE RNG CONSUMERS IDENTIFIED, AND NO RESEED PATH EXISTS. (A) A strict scan for the RNG-chain idiom 'lw rX,16(obj);...; lw rY,48(rX);...; lw t9,96(t9); jalr' finds EXACTLY 11 sites, all calling genrand; their purposes: FUN_00384800 0x3849AC genrand%10000 = item-drop roll; FUN_00429630 0x429EA0 %10000<=t = probability check; FUN_0039BCA0 0x39BDB0 %6144/1000.0 = random float <=6.144; FUN_003764C0 0x376594 %range = random duration/timing; FUN_004480A0 0x4480C8+0x4480F0 two rolls %6000+1 and %param = damage/score variance; FUN_00448190 0x4481B0 %620 -> 6-tier rank; FUN_0042EB80 0x42F288 (%1000)*1000 = random step; FUN_004487A0 0x4489A8 %10<=8-k = probability check; FUN_00448AE0 0x448CEC %range scaled = value variance; FUN_00390F80 0x39129C &0xf = small variant index. (B) NO RESEED IN CODE: a strict scan for a slot-23 (sgenrand) or slot-22 call on the same object+0x10 -> +0x30 chain returns ZERO sites. The earlier suspects were false positives: FUN_00330040 (0x330158/0x3301B8, slots 23/24/25 on *(s0+0x30)) is CreateThread/_StartThread - the thread subsystem - and the *(0x5871D0) slot-24 call at 0x3300F8 is the same thread manager, NOT the RNG. The 116-function Lua API has no SYSsetRandom. So sgenrand (0x1E6B20) is called ONLY from Reset (0x1E6B80, slot 13) via the generic object-init path, with the hard-coded seed 5150: the seed never changes after boot.

**Notes:** This closes the two open items from #279/#276: the native consumer list is complete at 11 (plus the Lua SYSgetRandom binding and its 112 Lua call sites), and there is no native or Lua reseed. Doc: docs/systems/rng.md sections 4 and 8. Topic head for Native and Lua RNG consumer inventory: also read #275, #279 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

<a id="f282"></a>
### #282 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00536050 — __vt__Gap::Bec::beGeneraterInfoWork (0x00536050)

MWCCPS2 RTTI/VTABLE LAYOUT RECOVERED — the route from a stripped binary to class and method names. Two adjacent rodata structures per class: __RTTI__ = {name_ptr, base_ref, 0, 0} (16 bytes); __vt__ = {rtti_ptr, 0, vfunc0, vfunc1,...} running until the next __vt__ header. Class-name strings are 'Gap::Bec::beModelCtrl'-style C++ identifiers. Full census of game.elf: 579 RTTI records, 478 classes with an emitted vtable, 11,714 vfunc slots. The 478 vtables all sit in a tight region 0x00533738-0x00540b48 and none is followed by a string-pointer array, which distinguishes them from the reflection records lower down. Validated by decompilation: Gap__igAdxAudio__vfunc_07 calls Gap__Core__igObject__vfunc_07, and __vt__Gap::Bec::beGeneraterInfoWork at 0x00536050 matches the vtable address recorded from live RAM.

**Notes:** CORRECTION (measured, same day): the __RTTI__ +4 field is NOT the immediate base class. All 579 records resolve to Gap::Core::igObject (578 with base = igObject, 1 root), so the field carries no hierarchy information at all beyond 'everything derives from igObject'. Intermediate bases such as Gap::Core::igObjectList and the Gap::Core::igTObjectList<...> instantiations have RTTI records but NO emitted vtable, so the real hierarchy is not recoverable from RTTI or from vtable prefixes. Consequence: the 'most-derived common ancestor' rule for shared vfunc bodies always collapses to igObject, which names the global defaults correctly but cannot name group-specific ones (see #283). Tools: scripts/dump_vtables.py (structure -> vtables.json) and apply_vtable_names.py. PyGhidra gotchas: getBlocks must be indexed not iterated; getBytes(Address, bytearray) does NOT write back (JArray(JByte) required); overlay blocks (.comment/.reginfo/_elfHeader) must be excluded from image bounds; script exceptions are swallowed silently; FlatProgramAPI.createLabel rejects template punctuation (<>,*) that Function.setName accepts; a thunk's name cannot be changed independently of its target.

**Evidence:** data/vtables.json

<a id="f283"></a>
### #283 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00536058 — Gap::Core::igObject::vfunc_00 (0x002ff0a0)

VTABLE NAMING APPLIED: 1,286 uniquely named functions across 478 classes, lifting named functions from 112/15,167 to 1,364. 11,714 vfunc slots collapse to only 1,333 distinct function addresses because MWCC emits one shared default stub per slot (e.g. 0x002ff0a0 at slot 0 of all 478 vtables) and points every non-overriding class at it: 1,239 are class overrides named \<Class>::vfunc_NN and 47 are shared defaults resolved to Gap::Core::igObject::vfunc_NN. 807 functions had to be created (16-byte 'jr ra' default stubs that auto-analysis merged or missed, plus jal-entry functions); 526 renamed. Also added 579 __RTTI__ labels and 478 __vt__ labels with base-class plate comments (1,045 labels, 0 conflicts).

**Notes:** 47 further addresses are deliberately left unnamed: several distinct bodies can resolve to the same owner+slot when the true owner is an unnamed intermediate base, and a duplicate symbol is worse than an honest blank, so they were reset to default names with a plate comment recording the slot and referrer families (see #284 for the metadata that should resolve them). Residual duplicate names: 2 logical functions appear under one name each because 4 of their entry points are Ghidra thunks, and Ghidra keeps a thunk's name synced to its target (renaming a thunk is ignored). Function count 15,167 -> 15,974.

**Evidence:** scripts/seed_names_vfunc_defaults.py; config/SLUS-20836/names/Vtables.tsv

<a id="f284"></a>
### #284 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00485940 — reflection record region 0x00485940+

THE CLASS REFLECTION RECORDS ARE LOCATED, AND THEY CONTAIN METHOD NAMES AS WELL AS FIELD NAMES. Scan of code-pointer runs in rodata finds 1,532 runs of >=5 function pointers (35,211 slots). Only 478 carry the __RTTI__/{rtti,0} header (the vtables); 842 have a {0,0} header and most of those are NOT vtables but reflection records, packed as [method/handler table][0][field-name string ptrs][0][descriptor ptrs][0][offsets][0]. Region 0x00485940-0x00530000 holds 3,691 field-name strings (2,734 distinct, e.g. _matrixList, _bColor, _visualContext, _scissorExt) plus MWCC method-name strings __ct (ctor), __dt (dtor), __as, __eq, __ge, __gt and the __MF_ MetaField prefix. This is the same metadata that owns the igBinary field-descriptor {X,Y} semantics — the open-questions blocker for the object-graph walk.

**Notes:** DECODED. The records are the engine's per-class reflection field tables, not a vtable variant. Layout: four parallel rodata arrays per class, N entries each (N = field count), packed back-to-back and zero-padded to 8-byte alignment -- [field-ctor fn ptrs][field name '_' string ptrs][BSS MetaField* slots][byte offsets]. No per-array terminator; the zeros are alignment padding (an odd N leaves one zero word after each array). Consumed by FUN_002fdf40(metaobj, ctor_table, n) and FUN_002fe000(metaobj, names, descs, offsets, start). Class association comes from the registrar FUN_002fe9c0: at each static-init call site a1 = the BSS MetaObject handle slot and t1 = the class-name string (verified: a1=0x588148 BSS, t1=0x5026d0 'beSvPs2McImage128k' at 0x347d00; a1=0x56ebc0, t1=0x4d49c0 'igVisualContextAttrDefaultManager' at 0x1301d0). The class name is NOT in the data. Field type = the MetaField class the ctor builds: the ctor loads that class's MetaObject handle (a 0x58xxxx global) and calls FUN_002fda40; the same handle names the class at its own registration, giving exact types (31 in total: igObjectRefMetaField 1079, igIntMetaField 686, igBoolMetaField 417, igFloatMetaField 207,...). CORRECTIONS to the original finding text: (a) the class name is not adjacent in the record region -- there are no class-name pointers there at all; (b) __ct/__dt/__as/__eq are NOT per-class method names -- they occur once each at 0x4fde68+ in a MWCC name-mangling/operator lookup table (beside operator==, >=, <<,...); (c) these tables carry no method names, so they do NOT name the 47 shared vfunc bodies of #283. Tool: scripts/dump_reflection.py -> data/reflection.json. Census: 1,075 MetaObjects, 659 with fields, 3,680 fields with name+offset+type, 32 field types, 659 ctor chunks, 0 unresolved name calls. UPDATE the last 8 classes (igPsx2VisualContext, igSorter, igProjectiveShadowShader, igCommonTraversal, igIGBFile, libBecRuntimePlugin, beModelCtrlInfoWork, igViewerSceneInfoManager) were recovered by scanning from the enclosing function's frame prologue instead of a fixed window -- their MetaObject handle lived in a callee-saved register set near the top of the function.

**Evidence:** data/reflection.json

A SET OF RTTI-LESS VTABLE-LIKE TABLES EXISTS SEPARATE FROM THE 478 RTTI-HEADED ONES. 58 runs of >=5 code pointers (1,537 slots) in the vtable region 0x00533000-0x00542000 carry a {0,0} header instead of {rtti,0}. The RNG object's own vtable is one of them: the docs record its vtable as 0x0052B8E0 and its vfunc list (containing Reset 0x001E6B80 and sgenrand 0x001E6B20 at slots 11 and 21) begins at 0x0052B8E8 with a {0,0} header, so that class has no RTTI record to name it from. <a id="f286"></a>
### #286 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002fe9c0 — FUN_002fe9c0 class registrar

METEOBJECT/METAFIELD REGISTRATION API RECOVERED. FUN_002fe9c0(base_idx, &handle_slot, alloc, copy, flags, class_name, deps, root_init, post_init, arg10, dependency_list) creates a class MetaObject: a1 is the BSS slot that receives the MetaObject pointer and t1 is the class-name string, so every class name is recoverable from the static-init call site (1,075 call sites, 1,074 distinct names). The fields are then added by FUN_002fdf40(metaobj, ctor_table, n), which creates n MetaFields via per-type ctor functions, followed by FUN_002fe000(metaobj, names, descs, offsets, start), which attaches their names, descriptors and byte offsets. FUN_002fe470(metaobj) returns the current field count.

**Notes:** Field tables are four parallel rodata arrays of n words each: [ctors][names][descs][offsets], packed back-to-back, zero-padded to 8-byte alignment, with no per-array terminator (the zeros are padding; odd n leaves exactly one zero after each array). names are underscore-prefixed strings, descs are BSS MetaField pointer slots, offsets are byte offsets into the object. The class name is never stored in the data - it is an argument at the FUN_002fe9c0 call site. Tool: scripts/dump_reflection.py.

**Evidence:** data/reflection.json
