<a id="f289"></a>
### #289 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002e63e0 — FUN_002e63e0 igBinary metadata reader

IGBINARY FIELD DESCRIPTORS ARE 6 BYTES / THREE u16, NOT TWO. Each type-directory record is {NUL-terminated class name}{u16 descriptors...} with the descriptor block starting at the next EVEN offset after the NUL (2-byte aligned, not 4) and each descriptor being {u16 type_id, u16 ordinal, u16 size_bytes}. type_id indexes the blob's 48-entry ig*MetaField name table (0 igObjectRef, 1 igInt, 2 igBool, 6 igEnum, 16 igFloat, 36 igVec3f, 38 igVec4f,...); ordinal is the field's 1-based position in the class's flattened field list; size is the field size in bytes. Confirmed by (a) sizes matching the ELF reflection exactly, (b) type ids matching the ig*MetaField names, and (c) the loader parser FUN_002e63e0 walking the block with a 3-short stride (iStack_40=3), reading descriptor[1] as the offset and descriptor[2] as the count. Worked example igLightAttr decodes to igShort@2/2, igEnum@4/4, igVec3f@6/12, igVec4f@7..9/16, igVec3f@10/12, igFloat@11,12/4, igVec3f@13/12, igFloat@14/4, igVec3f@15,16/12. The earlier 4-byte {X,Y} reading was a mis-parse of the same bytes caused by assuming 4-byte alignment.

**Notes:** Still open, and the actual blocker for the object-graph walk: (1) ordinal is a field POSITION, not a byte offset -- the loader derives the byte offset by walking the flattened field list with alignment and adding a per-parent base offset (it injects inherited slots as the literal strings 'fakeField' and 'Generated'); (2) some fields are absent from the descriptor list (for igLightAttr: _lightId int, _vc objref, _changed/_cacheable bool), so the directory appears to list a subset. Blob tested: NDMW.AFS stage pack magic 0x6300 at 0x1055b000, type dir near 0x1a00-0x2604. Topic head for igBinary field descriptor size: also read #110 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f290"></a>
### #290 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x1055b000 — igBinary type directory, NDMW.AFS stage pack 0x6300 @0x1055b000, dir region 0x2604..0x7105

IGBINARY 'ordinal' RESOLVED: ordinal = (0-based index of the field in the class's declared field list) + K + 1, where K is the class's inherited base-slot count and is CONSTANT per class. A descriptor whose ordinal <= K is an INHERITED-SLOT MARKER and matches no declared field. The descriptor ordinal indexes the class's flattened field-slot list (base slots first, then the class's own declared fields in declaration order); the byte offset is not carried by the descriptor at all -- it is the class's declared field offset, exactly the value already in data/reflection.json. TEST: for each directory class, search K in 1..20 and require every descriptor with ordinal > K to match a distinct declared field by ig*MetaField type in strictly increasing index order with ordinal - 1 - declaredIndex == K. Result over the NDMW 0x6300 pack's 135 records (see #292 for the framing): 93 records have reflection fields, 87 SATISFY the rule and 6 do not; of the 87, 48 are confirmed by >= 2 independently matched descriptors and 39 match on a single one (weak). K distribution: 1->8, 2->21, 3->30, 4->1, 6->12, 7->4, 9->1, 10->8, 11->2. Worked: igAnimationCombiner K=3 -- the (igString,2) and (igObjectRef,3) descriptors are inherited markers (ordinal <= 3), own descriptors ords 4..17 match declared indices 0..13 with a hole at declared index 7 (_hierarchyUpdated), i.e. ordinal 11 is simply absent. igLightAttr K=3; igViewportAttr K=3 with _scissorExt (declared index 4) absent at ordinal 8; igImage K=1. Reproduce: scripts/igb_dir.py --reflection data/reflection.json.

**Notes:** The 6 non-conforming classes (beMatCtrlSearch, igParticleAttr, igParticleArray, ParticleArray, igTextureAttr, igProjectiveShadowShader) are dominated by greedy type-matching ambiguity: where a class repeats a type the subsequence match can bind the wrong declared field, and where a class's reflection field list is not in slot order (igTextureAttr puts _texId late) the index sequence is scrambled. They are not counterexamples, but they are not proof either -- quote 48 (the >=2-descriptor subset) as the solid figure and 87 as 'consistent with'. Inherited markers: 203 total, and NOT mostly igShort (that was an artefact of matching before excluding them) -- igObjectRef 53, igString 50, igInt 44, igBool 24, igShort 23, plus a scatter. Method note: an earlier pass reported 34 classes because it did not exclude descriptors with ordinal <= K before matching, so inherited markers consumed real declared slots. Tool: scripts/igb_dir.py (walks the records validating triples, and cross-checks against reflection).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f291"></a>
### #291 · refuted

**Where:** 0x1055b000 — data/reflection.json beBaseInfo/beBaseInfoData/beNDMWStatusInfo; NDMW+Arena igBinary directories

REFUTED: the hp/mhp/mp/mmp/level/lv/def/power/hit/waza/kazu + stun/sleep/poison/guard/powerdown/attackdown stat layout is NOT in the ELF reflection metadata. The docs (engine/reflection-metadata.md section 8, systems/enemy-data.md) said 'these now have offsets -- read the beNDMWStatusInfo / beBaseInfo* entries in data/reflection.json for the authoritative layout', but in data/reflection.json beNDMWStatusInfo has field_count 0 and beBaseInfoData has exactly one field (_version igIntMetaField @12). beBaseInfo has 2 (_dataList @20, _ramList @24). So the stat fields exist only as NUL-terminated strings in the ELF/data (data/strings.json has 'powerdown'), not as reflection field records, and the enemy-stat blocker is not 'map ordinal to offset' but 'recover the beBaseInfoData/beNDMWStatusInfo layout from elsewhere'. Their igBinary directory records are also nearly empty: NDMW blob beNDMWStatusInfo @blob+0x3fc4 = (igString,2,4)(igBool,4,1)(igObjectRef,5,4)..., beNDMWStatusInfoRam @blob+0x3fee, beBaseInfo @blob+0x1ef8, beBaseInfoData @blob+0x24b2 = (igString,2,4)(igInt,3,4) -- so ordinals below K mark inherited slots and the inherited base slots already hold them.

**Notes:** Refines finding #288 (which already established that beNDMWStatusInfo registers no fields of its own): the new content here is that the DOCS are wrong, not just the class -- engine/reflection-metadata.md section 8 and systems/enemy-data.md both tell the reader to read the beNDMWStatusInfo/beBaseInfo* entries in data/reflection.json for 'the authoritative layout', which cannot work (0 and 1 fields respectively). The stat-name strings exist (data/strings.json) but have no reflection field records. Blocker restated: recover the layout from the stat-name xrefs or by fitting object instances, not from reflection.json. Topic head for Stat field names are not in ELF reflection metadata: also read #37, #107, #288 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-data.md

<a id="f292"></a>
### #292 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x1055b000 — igBinary type directory, NDMW.AFS 0x6300 pack

IGBINARY DESCRIPTOR 'size' IS THE DECLARED ELEMENT SIZE, AND IT IS EXACTLY THE CANONICAL SIZE OF ITS ig*MetaField TYPE. Across all matched descriptors in the NDMW 0x6300 directory, size == canonical(size(type_id)) in 98/98 cases (igInt/igEnum/igMemoryRef/igObjectRef/igUnsignedInt/igString=4, igBool/igChar/igUnsignedChar=1, igShort/igUnsignedShort=2, igLong=8, igVec2f=8, igVec3f=12, igVec4f=16, igMatrix44f=64, igVec4uc=4, igVec3uc=3). The size is NOT the slot stride: igImage's two bools have size 1 but 4-byte strides (offsets 60->64, 80->84), while igColorMaskAttr's four bools have size 1 and 1-byte strides (12,13,14,15). Stride is whatever the class's declared layout says, so the declared offsets in data/reflection.json are the igBinary layout; the descriptor only re-states the element size.

**Notes:** Record framing (final): the NDMW 0x6300 class-record chain is 135 records / 700 triples occupying 0x2604..0x4032, and every triple decodes to a type_id < 48 with the size that type always has. Each record is {NUL-terminated name} padded to the next EVEN offset, then N 6-byte triples, with NO count stored (N is recoverable only from the next record's name). At 0x4032 the region changes: {u32 336, u32 46} then the igMemory type-name pool of 46 names (Bootstrap, Default, Current, NonTracked, System, MetaData, String, Fast, List, Temporary, Vertex, RenderList, Texture, Application, World, Actor,... User0..User15), then a table of 32-byte records {kind, 0x20|0x14, 0, value, flags, 1, 0xffffffff, 0xffffffff}. Those pool names are identifier-shaped, so a walk that does not validate its triples reads them as records: that is how earlier figures of 141 records / 45 bad triples (and before that 179) arose. Size table, observed constant per type id across the chain: igObjectRef/igInt/igString/igEnum/igMemoryRef/igUnsignedInt=4, igUnsignedCharArray=3, igBool/igUnsignedChar=1, igShort=2, igLong/igDouble=8, igVec3f=12, igVec4f=16, igMatrix44f=64, plus large defaults for the array types (igIntArray 128, igFloatArray 768). Tool: scripts/igb_dir.py --verify validates as it walks and reports the boundary.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f293"></a>
### #293 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x1055b000 — NDMW.AFS 0x6300 stage pack header region

hdr[5] IS NOT THE INSTANCE INDEX. In the NDMW.AFS 0x6300 stage pack (0x1055b000) hdr[5]=0x7105 is exactly SIX 32-byte records, 0x7105..0x71c5, each {u32 0x4ff, u32 0x2000, u32 0, u32 offset, u32 0x300, u32 0x100, u32 0xffffff00, u32 0xffffffff} with offset in {0x49600, 0x52200, 0x9b100, 0x9b600, 0xb6800, 0x177c00}. Every field but the offset is constant across the six, so these read as igMemory/arena descriptors (a count of 0x4ff, a 0x2000 size, and a blob offset), not as a per-instance table. docs/engine/igbinary-format.md and docs/systems/enemy-data.md both label hdr[5] 'index table (repeating {0x4ff,0x2000,0,x,...} records)', which invites reading it as the instance list for the object graph; it cannot be, because it is six entries long. The object-instance encoding at hdr[4]=0xf86bc therefore still has no known index.

**Notes:** Method: walk forward from 0x7105 while the {0x4ff,0x2000,*,*,0x300,0x100,0xffffff00,0xffffffff} signature holds; it holds exactly 6 times and fails on the 7th record (0x71c5). The six offsets are not monotone, so they are not a sequential sub-region list either.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-data.md

<a id="f294"></a>
### #294 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x105953d0 — igBinary object data, NDMW.AFS 0x6300 pack at 0x1055b000

The NDMW stage pack also contains an enemy/spawn NAME TABLE inside the blob (not in the AREA-only hash table #107 found): entries are {u32 nameLen, char name[nameLen padded to nameLen], u32 x, u32 y, u32 z}, back to back from about blob+0x3a180. Verified entries: e_goburi (0x3a3cc) -> (4380, 55, 56); e_skulgrey (0x3a300) -> (4890, 44, 28); e_geko (4380, 22, 20); e_mecha5 (4380, 53, 20); e_maridevi/e_mecha3/e_megadra/e_ldknight/e_torta/e_rare/e_infermo/e_cockatri/e_nume/e_ogre/e_scum -> (4380, 44, 24 or 28); e_minotaru/e_mecha4bs -> (4380, 44, 24). Records whose name is a model id (LDMG03/LDMG04) follow their enemy, and y_099eldmg02a (0x3a420) has a 132-byte payload containing FLOATS - 0xc1200000 = -10.0f, 0x41200000 = +10.0f, 0x41000000 = +8.0f - i.e. a position/bounds range. This is a spawn/generator/placement table (tracker D1/D3), NOT the HP stat database: no field in it is in the Goburimon HP band (200-600), and the first value is a near-constant 4380/4890.

**Notes:** Found while hunting the beNDMWStatusInfo instances. The 3 trailing u32s are the reason it is not the stat DB; their meaning is open (4380 = 0x111c and 4890 = 0x131a are not blob offsets - hdr[4]=0xf86bc is much larger). Name/id table for the enemy spawn system rather than stats.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-data.md

<a id="f295"></a>
### #295 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x585be0 — FUN_003022c0 container lookup; FUN_003a9220 = FUN_003022c0(a0, *(0x585be0), key)

*(registry+8) IS 8. The property/container lookup at 0x3022c0 resolves a record's name as record + *(registry+8), with registry = *(0x585be0); the offset is a literal 8 (record name pointer at +8, value at +12, matching the documented record layout). 0x585be0 is only ever READ by this path -- all ~15 references in the ELF are lui at,0x58 + lw a1,23520(at), never a store -- so it is a BSS slot filled by the generic object machinery and cannot be reconstructed statically; it was measured from off-line EE RAM via scripts/p2s_ram.py on the dw4-inworld savestate. The object there is at 0x005da690: +0 vtable 0x00533180, +4 0x04000104 (refcount/flags), +8 = 8, +0xc 0x0067b15c, +0x10 0x00020004, +0x14 4, +0x18 0x005da5c8, +0x1c = 0x00585be0 -- it stores a pointer back to its own handle slot, which is how the instance is reachable from the fixed global.

**Notes:** Method: RAM offset == pine address for EE main RAM, so reading offset 0x585be0 of eeMemory.bin is the live value. Closing tracker T3's first open question, which engine/property-system.md had left as 'very likely 0, 4 or 8'.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/property-system.md

<a id="f296"></a>
### #296 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x372ff0 — sd registration trampolines 0x370c38..0x370ca0; impls 0x372ff0/0x373060/0x3730a0/0x373120

THE FOUR 'sd' IDS (29-32) ARE TWO OPERATIONS x OPTIONAL-ARGUMENT VARIANTS, NOT FOUR OPERATIONS. Trampolines 0x370c38 / 0x370c58 / 0x370c78 / 0x370ca0 pull Lua frame argument slots at a 24-byte stride (+16, +40, +64, +88, +112, +136) and forward them: id29 -> 0x372ff0 (0x38d860(root,a,b,c); if arg5 != 0 also 0x372cf0(arg5); then 0x38e060(root,arg4)); id30 -> 0x373060 (0x38e5f0 = 0x38d860 + 0x38e060); id31 -> 0x3730a0 (0x38e6b0(root,a,b,c); if arg5 != 0 also 0x372cf0(arg5); then 0x38e8f0(root,arg4,arg6)); id32 -> 0x373120 (0x38eca0 = 0x38e6b0 + 0x38e8f0, 5th arg read with ld i.e. 64-bit). Decompiling 0x38e5f0 and 0x38eca0 shows they are literally the concatenations, which is what collapses four ids into two operations x with/without the optional 5th argument (0x372cf0 consumes that argument). All six object-system calls take the root object *(0x58a1c0) as their first argument.

**Notes:** Resolves the second open question in engine/property-system.md. Remaining: which object-system operation each pair performs (create vs lookup) and what 0x372cf0 does with the optional argument.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/property-system.md

<a id="f297"></a>
### #297 · refuted

**Where:** 0x4cedf8 — .data property template, 0x4cedf8

GAMERAM PROPERTY TEMPLATE AT 0x4cedf8: 64 x 12-byte {char *name, i32 default_value, u32 unknown} records, 0x4cedf8..0x4cf0f8, which is what a property container is initialised from. Non-zero defaults: UNIQUE 6, LEVEL 1, SKILL 1, UPCNT 4, EXP 2, YAMITEIKO 6, WEAPONSKILLNO 6, DEVICEFOLDER 6, DISKFOLDER 3, CARDLIST 6, BANKBIT 6, POWERSOCKET 5, POWERSOCKETNAME 6, POWERPOCKET 6, MAINSLOT 6, MAINPOCKET 6, SUBSLOT 3, BASE_FLAG 3, BASE_COUNTER 3, BASE_FLAGFOLDER/BASE_ISUSE/BASE_LEVEL/BASE_EXP/BASE_SKILL/BASE_UPCNT/UPDATA 1, DOWNDATA -1; every other property defaults to 0. The name pointer is at +0 here, unlike the RUNTIME record which has it at +8 (finding #295), so this is an initialiser list and not a container.

**Notes:** Reading it: a pointer to one of the property-name strings at 0x5187e8..0x518a70 makes the stride exactly 12 and the run 64 entries; discovered by scanning RAM for pointers to the 'HP' string after reading the registry. The third word is a small bitmask (LEVEL 0x9, SKILL 0xb, DISKFOLDER 0x32, BANKBIT 0x3, SUBSLOT 0x400, BASE_EXP 0x90, BASE_SKILL 0xb0, BASE_UPCNT 0xb, UPDATA 0x7) whose meaning is NOT established -- do not read it as a size or type id without evidence. Useful for save editing alongside systems/save-format.md. correction (#795): the original 3f7310 passes 4cedf0 to 3f6620, which reads type/count/name at +0/+4/+8. The prior scan started at the name word (+8) and interpreted the following entry as defaults. Its default-value/bitmask claims are refuted; the historical hypothesis is retained.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/property-system.md

<a id="f298"></a>
### #298 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x394400 — FUN_00394400 (MDL stat-block refresher), FUN_003022c0 (container lookup)

THE ENEMY STAT READ PATH IS PROVEN (D4). FUN_00394400 at 0x394400 is the 'focus this model and refresh the cached stat block' function; its provenance, read from 0x394520-0x3945bc, is: container = *(model+0x28); record = FUN_003022c0(container, *(0x585be0), "ONE" @0x50ef60); status = *(record+0x14); then *(0x58a1e4)=*(model+8) (RamNo); *(0x58a1e8)=*(record+0x10)+0x20 (transform); *(0x58a1ec)=*(*(status+8)+0xc) (MHP); *(0x58a1f0)=*(*(status+0xc)+0xc) (HP); *(0x58a1f4)=*(status+0x10) (RecNo); *(0x58a1f8)=the name argument (InfoName). So MHP and HP live in two SEPARATE sub-objects reached through status+8 and status+0xc, each exposing its value at +0xc, and RecNo is a plain int at status+0x10. HP and MHP are therefore not adjacent fields of one struct. The function re-writes HP after the UI update when HP did not change -- a meter clamp, not a second source.

**Notes:** LARGELY DUPLICATES EXISTING WORK, and one label of mine is wrong. #182 (confirmed by live damage: 2816->2431) already gives the current-HP handle as [InfoWork+0x0c] + 0x0c -- identical to this finding's 'HP = *(*(status+0x0c)+0x0c)'. docs/systems/enemy-spawn-system.md already states the enemy InfoWork layout: beNDMWMdlPEBaseInfoWork (vtable 0x53D3E0), +0x10 row, [+0x0c]+0x0c current HP (see #182). So: my 'RecNo' at status+0x10 is the documented ROW index (#182 described it as an elite-scaled stat and refuted it as HP -- both are true: it is the row, and the crown ladder scales that row). My 'MHP = *(*(status+8)+0xc)' label for the +0x08 field object is NOT corroborated anywhere and should not be trusted as a name -- the value it yields for the uncrowned goburi is 649, which is the row-5 HPMAX, so +0x08 is plausibly the max-HP/stat field object, but that needs its own check. What this finding adds that is genuinely new: FUN_00394400 is the sole writer of the MDL block (one store site in the ELF) and the container lookup key is 'ONE' in *(model+0x28).

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-data.md

<a id="f299"></a>
### #299 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0058a1ec — MDL block 0x58a1e0-0x58a220; live entity records in the heap

GOBURIMON MAX HP MEASURED LIVE, AND RECNO IS PER-INSTANCE. Reading the MDL block (0x58a1e4 RamNo, 0x58a1ec MHP, 0x58a1f0 HP, 0x58a1f4 RecNo, 0x58a1f8 InfoName) out of off-line EE RAM across 10 curated savestates: e_goburi is MHP 50 at RecNo 0 (normal_lvl1_before_load, goblin_pass_crate_barrell), MHP 58 at RecNo 1 (normal_lvl1_before_crown_enemy) and MHP 649 at RecNo 5 (before_crown_spawn, before_enemy_spawn, no_crown, pink_crown). The SAME enemy therefore has different RecNo AND different MHP in different stages, so RecNo is the instance's record index into the stage's status table, NOT a fixed per-species id -- and there is no single 'Goburimon HP'. The old doc anchor 'Goburimon HP ~ 200-600' is REFUTED as a validation anchor. The same (name, RecNo, MHP) triples are visible as heap records in a 72-byte-stride table with name at +0xc, RecNo at +0x10, MHP at +0x14, +0x1c = 0x08000281: in before_crown_spawn the 10 loaded entities are e_goburi x7 (5/649), e_ogre x2 (36/662) and g_boxfield (371/100).

**Notes:** NOT NOVEL -- this re-derived already-confirmed work. Corroborates #182 (crown HP ~4.9x, +0x10 = 5 uncrowned / 386 pink) and #205 (the 649-row ISO stat table). The measured values are table rows: 649 = row 5 (ordinary e_goburi), 58 = row 1 (e_goburi), 100 = row 370 (destructible). rng-verification.md already recorded 'row=5 HPMAX=649' and 'row=386 HPMAX=3200 <- RARITY 3 (pink) e_goburi' from live generator reads. The one thing still worth having here is the wide savestate sweep showing which state yields which row -- and that the same enemy type spans several rows (1, 5, 379-393 crown ladder), so a single 'Goburimon HP' does not exist.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-data.md

<a id="f300"></a>
### #300 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x0058a1e0 — FUN_00394400 (0x394400), the sole writer of the MDL MHP

THE MDL STAT BLOCK IS A CACHE AND ITS PRODUCING CHAIN IS NOT REPLAYABLE, so the stat definition table cannot be reached by following it. FUN_00394400 (the only writer of *(0x58a1ec), verified: exactly one store site in the whole ELF at 0x394578) sets the block from status = *( record("ONE") + 0x14), where record is looked up in *(model+0x28) and model = *(0x58a1e0). Read BOTH from a savestate (before_crown_spawn.p2s) and live from a running game with that state loaded (pi-pcsx2 + pi-pine), the chain resolves identically and ends at ZERO: model=0x12c8398, *(model+0x28)=0x157a9e8, container count=1, entry[0]=0x157c2e0 with name@+8='ONE', +0xc=0, +0x10=0x159bd48, +0x14=0x0 -- so *(record+0x14)==0 and the status object is gone. Since FUN_00394400 dereferences that pointer, the record is a heap object whose +0x14 was cleared after the block was written (the object graph mutates). Consequence: the MDL block is a CACHE, not a live view, and chasing it to the stat table is a dead end -- the useful route is the code (which gives the offsets) or the entity records (which carry the values).

**Notes:** Downgraded. The observation is real (the MDL block is a cache whose producing chain resolves to 0 live and from a savestate), but the 'dead end for the stats' framing was wrong: the stats were never behind that chain -- they are the 649-row ISO table (#195/#196/#205), reached through the row index at InfoWork+0x10. Keep only as a note about the MDL block being a cache, not about the stats.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-data.md

<a id="f301"></a>
### #301 · refuted

**Where:** 0x14a13b0 — stage heap: named definition-object registry 0x14a0000-0x14b0000; 72-byte entity records

REFUTED -- WRONG FINDING, MINE. The enemy stat table EXISTS and has been fully decoded as findings #195/#196/#205: it is 649 rows of plain data in the ISO / AREA.AFS, fully patchable with no code patches, laid out as HPMAX i32 COLUMN-major @0x78D64C5 + 12 i16 stats ROW-major @0x78DACB9 + 3 u8 (CHIMEIRITSU/MAHIRITSU/RARITY) ROW-major @0x78DA51D, replicated as 665 byte-identical copies (which is what the tracker's 'x667' was a miscount of). #205 decodes the whole crown ladder from it: 60 of the 649 rows have RARITY>0 = 4 types x 3 tiers x 5 ranks, with anchors row 370 destructible 100HP/RARITY0, row 60 boss 3194/3564, row 1 e_goburi 58, 69 zeroed rows. This finding's scans searched EE RAM only, found no flat table there (correctly -- RAM holds per-instance copies, e.g. row 5 HPMAX=649 at 0x17ff7a0), and then wrongly generalised that to 'the premise is unsupported'. The real error was procedural: I trusted the tracker's stale phrase 'the 88-record beNDMWStatusInfo table' instead of grepping the docs for 'stat table', which would have surfaced #205 immediately -- and docs/README.md line 59 had already flagged the table as known and pointed at enemy-data.md.

**Notes:** Kept as a refuted row so the reasoning is not repeated. Two lessons worth keeping: (1) 649 rows, not 88 -- the '88' was the beNDMWStatusInfo instance count in a doc that predated #205, and 'x88'/'x667' have no other provenance; (2) an entity carrying (name, row, HPMAX) in RAM is a per-instance copy of row data, so the presence of such records is evidence FOR the table, not against it.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-data.md

<a id="f302"></a>
### #302 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x151090 — match_func.py fingerprint target; retail ELF 0x151090 = int f(int){return a;}

THE GAME WAS COMPILED WITH MWCCPS2 3.0 (BUILD 011126, 26 Nov 2001), NOT 2.4 -- and the ELF's '.comment' could never have told us. mwccps2.exe 3.0-011126 has CRC32 C82F0A93; it emits '.comment' = 'MW MIPS C Compiler (2.4.1.01)', byte-identical to the retail ELF's, but SO DOES 2.4-001213 (CRC D37D90D4). The discriminator is the register-move idiom: compiling 'int f1(int a){return a;}' gives 'paddub v0,a0,zero' (MMI) under 2.3.3-000906 and 2.4-001213, but 'move v0,a0' (= daddu, 0x0080102d) under 3.0-011126 -- and the retail ELF contains ZERO 0x70801628 (paddub v0,a0,zero) against 413 0x0080102d (daddu v0,a0,zero). 2.4 was the documented best candidate and is refuted on its first function. 3.0.1b87/b119/b145 are separately ruled out by their '.comment' (3.0.0).

**Notes:** Verified transport: wibo (decompals/wibo 1.2.0 wibo-x86_64), NOT Wine -- under Wine the compiler aborts with 'License check failed: Cannot find license file... Feature: Win32_Plugins_MIPS_PS2'; the decompme archives ship no license.dat and archive.org's 'compilers-no-license' collection is explicitly compilers that 'need to be cracked', yet wibo runs the same binary with no prompt. Byte-identical matches at -O4,p: 0x151090 'int f(int a){return a;}' -> 0800e003 2d108000; 0x10b5c8 'int f(void){return -1;}' -> 0800e003 ffff0224; 0x124d40 'void f(void){return;}' -> 0800e003 00000000. Script: scripts/match_func.py (extracts the retail bytes, compiles the candidate, diffs instruction words; exit 0 = match). Follow-up #1036: retain the original hypothesis and fingerprint evidence, but withdraw the claimed unique original-build attribution. A second compiler matches complete combat bodies; fingerprint agreement alone is insufficient to establish original provenance. See docs/engine/unit-compilers.md.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/toolchain.md

<a id="f303"></a>
### #303 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100080 — scripts/match_func.py; matching-decomp harness

THE MATCHING LOOP WORKS: the original compiler runs locally and reproduces retail bytes. Toolchain = MWCCPS2 3.0-011126 (CRC C82F0A93) driven by wibo, flags -O4,p, target R5900. Tooling in-repo: scripts/match_func.py \<va> \<cand.c> \<func> [--flags] [--mwcc] [--wibo] extracts the retail bytes at the VA (via the ELF's 0x100000 -> file 0x80 mapping, size from data/functions.json), compiles the candidate, and diffs instruction words, with exit 0 on match. Negative control: pointing --mwcc at 2.4-001213 DIFFERs, and its diff line names the exact wrong instruction (game 0080102d vs ours 70801628), which is what makes it a usable fingerprint rather than a coin flip.

**Notes:** Constraint honoured: the compiler is proprietary and NOT committed -- it lives outside the repo at local/mwcps2/3.0-011126/ with wibo at local/wibo. The three matched functions are deliberately trivial 8-byte leaf functions, so this proves the loop and the compiler identity but does NOT settle the optimisation level (-O3 gives the same code for these) or anything about functions with calls, globals and control flow -- that is the next step.

**Evidence:** scripts/match_func.py

<a id="f304"></a>
### #304 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x15e130 — tools/check.py --flags; tools/split.py layout; FUN_0015e130 popcount

OPTIMISATION FLAGS PINNED: '-O3' (equivalently '-O4,s') -- NOT '-O4,p', and the discriminator is '-opt speed', not the level. 0x15e130 is a population count, self-contained (no calls, no globals) so its unlinked object bytes compare directly with retail: 'int f(unsigned v){int n=0;while(v){if(v&1)n++;v>>=1;}return n;}' reproduces it BYTE-FOR-BYTE at -O3 and at -O4,s, and NOT at -O4,p, -O2 or -O1. That is the first function with a loop and control flow to be matched, and it refutes the -O4,p that three 8-byte leaves had appeared to support (they cannot discriminate: every level compiles 'return -1;' the same way). Cross-check on the compiler itself: over a 16-function synthetic corpus (loops, nested conditionals, switch, div/mod, float multiply, strcpy/strlen/bsearch/strcmp loops, rotate, clamp, memzero) -O3 and -O4,s produce BYTE-IDENTICAL code for all 16, while -O4,p differs. '-opt speed' (the 'p' in -O4,p) is therefore the wrong knob; the game was built with level 3, or level 4 without speed.

**Notes:** MWCC 3.0 also defaults to C89: 'for(int i=...)' is a syntax error, so the corpus had to declare loop variables at the top. Worth knowing before writing candidate C at scale. Method note: only functions with no calls and no globals can be compared as unlinked objects, because an object stores RELOCATIONS for those (a gp-relative 'lbu v1,-32112(gp)' becomes offset 0 + R_MIPS_GPREL16), so the raw bytes cannot match until a link places them. That is the real reason M3 (full relink) matters, and why the flag work had to be done on a self-contained function.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/matching-decomp.md

<a id="f305"></a>
### #305 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001001c0 — ELF.reginfo (0x454bd8); __start 0x001001c0 'move gp,a0'

gp = 0x0055c870, READ FROM THE ELF'S.reginfo -- this is what unblocks GP-relative code. __start does 'move gp, a0' at 0x1001c0, so the value is not in the instruction stream; it is in.reginfo (24 bytes: gprmask, cprmask[4], gp_value at +20), which is what the PS2 loader passes in a0. Cross-check that makes it certain: gp - 32112 == 0x00554b00, exactly the first byte of.sbss (the file-backed part of the ELF ends at 0x554b00), so the one-shot guard bytes at 'lbu v1,-32112(gp)' sit at the head of small data. gprmask=0xf7fffffe, cprmask[0]=0, cprmask[1]=0xffffffff.

**Notes:** Without this, any operand of the form 'lbu v1,-32112(gp)' is unresolvable from the ELF alone, and the retail guard-byte initialisers are all that shape. Regression guard: if a future tool hardcodes gp instead of reading.reginfo, a mismatch here will look like a source error.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/matching-decomp.md

<a id="f306"></a>
### #306 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00109ac8 — tools/verify.py, tools/check.py (make check now relocation-aware)

RELOCATION-AWARE VERIFICATION WORKS, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES NON-TRIVIAL FUNCTIONS MATCHABLE. tools/verify.py compiles a candidate with MWCCPS2 3.0/-O3, reads the object's relocations, resolves each symbol (functions from config/SLUS-20836/symbols.txt; data from an address-bearing name like D_0056df30 or DAT_004d22e8), applies them, and diffs per instruction. Three kinds confirmed against retail: R_MIPS_HI16/LO16 (globals -> 0x246780, 'if(p)*p=D_0056df30[2]; return D_0056df30[0];'), R_MIPS_GPREL16 (small data -> 0x130000, the one-shot guard byte at.sbss 0x554b00), and R_MIPS_26 (calls -> 0x109ac8, a tail call passing the constant string '[MPEG ERROR]%s\n' at 0x4d22e8). Before this, only self-contained leaf functions could be compared (raw object bytes), i.e. 4 of 15,974; coverage is now 7 and the method scales to the other 3,078 functions whose callees are already in the layout.

**Notes:** Two gotchas paid for here: (1) MWCC at -O3 emits tail-call 'j', not just 'jal', so a callee scan must accept both; (2) objdump prints jump targets as '0x1756a0', and a naive [0-9a-f]+ regex over that captures only the leading '0' -- which made a first pass report 0 of 5,488 call functions with a resolvable callee. Also: data addressed ABSOLUTELY (lui/lw) is not small data -- declaring an int when retail uses lui/lw means the object is >sdatathreshold (an array, say), which is why D_0056df30 had to be declared as int[4].

**Evidence:** tools/verify.py

<a id="f307"></a>
### #307 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x1301c0 — tools/automatch.py ccall template; FUN_002fe940 registration thunks

LITERAL vs SYMBOL REFERENCE IS VISIBLE IN THE CODEGEN, AND IT COST 591 FUNCTIONS. For 'lui a0,HI; j TARGET; addiu a0,a0,OFF' (591 occurrences, all passing a constant pointer as the single argument of a tail call), writing the C as a literal cast -- FUN_002fe940((char *)0x001301d0) -- produces 'lui v0,0x13; j; ori a0,v0,0x1d0': MWCC materialises a LITERAL through a scratch register and ORs it. Naming the object at that address instead -- an extern char DAT_001301d0[] (or the function's own name, since the target usually IS a function pointer) -- produces 'lui a0,0x13; j; addiu a0,a0,0x1d0', which is retail exactly: a symbol address goes straight into the destination register and uses addiu. Same value, same registers, different instruction. Rule of thumb for this compiler: an absolute address materialised into the real destination with addiu is a symbol reference; the same value through a scratch with ori is a literal.

**Notes:** The 0x1301d0 'constant' is itself code (it disassembles as a prologue), i.e. a function pointer handed to a registration routine, so the natural C is to name the function rather than invent a DAT_ label. Worth checking whenever a materialised address looks like it points into.text.

**Evidence:** tools/automatch.py

<a id="f308"></a>
### #308 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100080 — tools/automatch.py; make check 1783/15974 matching

BULK DECOMPILATION BY TEMPLATE + VERIFICATION WORKS: coverage 7 -> 1783 of 15,974 functions (11.2%), every one proven byte-identical to retail. tools/automatch.py generates candidate C from instruction-shape templates and writes a src/\<name>.c ONLY when tools/verify.py confirms the compiled bytes match, so an auto-generated file is exactly as trustworthy as a hand-written one -- the tool saves typing, it does not lower the bar. Yield by template (all verified): ccall 591/591, empty 184/184, fget 83/83, fzero 175/175, gget 601/609, ident 8/8, jload 21/29, jthunk 39/39, rconst 31/31, setter 46/46. The remaining failures are reported and nothing is written for them. A census showed the top 40 instruction shapes cover 5,042 functions (31.6%), so the headroom for more templates is large; the biggest untried families are the 'addiu sp,-16; sd ra; jal X; ld ra; jr ra' call wrapper (527) and the 'lui at,HI; lw v0,OFF(at); beqz/bnez' lazy-init guard bodies (~1,450 across several shapes), both of which need branchy bodies rather than one-liners.

**Notes:** UPDATE (see #312/#314/#316): coverage is now 4,174/15,974 (26.1%), all verified, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. The 'two biggest untried families' named here are both cracked: the call wrappers = 1,012 banked via the C++-mode wcall/wload templates; the lazy-init guard bodies = 550 banked via the guard template (the other 507 are open, #313). The remaining 12,629 were then run through m2c, which banked 829 more (#316).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f309"></a>
### #309 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100000 — tools/{layout,linkgen,linkcheck}.py; make link

THE GAME RELINKS BYTE-IDENTICALLY. All 4,541,184 bytes of the merged main section (0x100000..0x554b00) reconstruct EXACTLY from 28,260 linked objects: 1,783 functions COMPILED FROM C by MWCCPS2 3.0 at -O3, 14,191 functions as retail bytes, and 12,286 data units as retail bytes. This is the Ship-of-Theseus proof for the whole build: it demonstrates that (a) the compiler reproduces retail code for every decompiled function, (b) the layout tiling is exact, and (c) every cross-reference resolves correctly -- calls into not-yet-decompiled functions, absolute data globals, and gp-relative small data. From here each new function is a mechanical swap: write C, verify, relink; no new infrastructure. Pipeline: tools/layout.py (disjoint tiling), tools/linkgen.py (units -> objects + linker script), mipsel-linux-gnu-ld -T build/link.ld -G0 --no-warn-mismatch, tools/linkcheck.py (compare).

**Notes:** Needed, in order, and each failed in a misleading way: (1) A DISJOINT TILING -- the Ghidra dump has 33 overlapping function pairs; tools/layout.py truncates the earlier unit so every byte is emitted once. (2) EXACT-SIZE OBJECTS -- GNU as rounds EVERY section up to 16 bytes, so an 8-byte unit becomes 16 and shifts the whole layout; retail units are made with 'objcopy -I binary' (exact size, alignment 1). MWCC's.text is already exact-size (8, 8, 12 for small functions), needing only SUBALIGN(1) to stop ld padding before it. (3) THE contents FLAG -- 'objcopy --rename-section.data=.text,alloc,load,readonly,code' WITHOUT ',contents' keeps the section's size but treats it as having no data, so every unit links as ZEROS. That looks exactly like a placement bug and cost the most time. (4) _gp AND -G0 -- __start is 'move gp, a0', so gp is not in the instruction stream; it is in.reginfo (0x0055c870). Without defining it, the C objects' R_MIPS_GPREL16 relocs fail with 'small-data section too large'; -G0 is needed too. (5) NEVER '. = \<va>;' INSIDE AN OUTPUT SECTION -- ld treats it as an offset from the section start and silently prefixes 1,048,576 bytes of zeros. The symptom was a section exactly 0x100000 bytes too big with zeros at the front. (6) COMPARE AT THE FILE OFFSET -- 'objcopy -O binary' pads to the VMA, so the naive extraction is 1 MB too long with a leading hole. tools/linkcheck.py reads the section header instead. Cosmetic residue: MWCC objects carry e_flags 0x20924001 (noreorder, 5900, eabi64) while objcopy -I binary units carry 0x0, so ld warns 'linking 32-bit code with 64-bit code'; --no-warn-mismatch is required and the warning does not affect the code bytes. Remaining nicety: the full 0x100000..0x554b00 SECTION is identical, but the ELF header/entry and MWLD (rather than GNU ld) are not used yet.

**Evidence:** tools/linkcheck.py

<a id="f310"></a>
### #310 · refuted

**Where:** 0x130060 — Gap::* guard wrappers; FUN_00130060

GUARD FAMILY IS NOT TEMPLATABLE AND THE REASON IS A CODEGEN IDIOM WE CANNOT PRODUCE. ~1,062 functions share the shape 'addiu sp,-16; lui at,HI; sd ra; lw v0,OFF(at); beqz v0,CALL; nop; lw v0,36(v0); andi v0,v0,4; sltu v0,zero,v0; bnez v0,END; nop; nop; CALL: jal X; nop; END: lui at,HI; ld ra; lw v0,OFF(at); jr ra; addiu sp,sp,16'. The semantics are clear -- 'if (!g || !(g->f36 & 4)) X; return g;', with a documented example at 0x130060 (g = 0x55ebc0, field +36, mask 4, callee 0x1301c0) -- and everything about it reproduces except one instruction: retail materialises the flag test with 'sltu v0,zero,v0' between the andi and the bnez, and NO source form tried produces that. Tried and all zero (6 forms x 1,062 functions = 6,372 compiles, plus variants): 'if(!g||!(g->f&4))', 'if(g==0||(g->f&4)==0)', 'if(!(g&&(g->f&4)))', 'if(g&&(g->f&4)){}else', nested if/else-if, goto form, early return, '!=0' forms, '==4', '==0u', '&4u', unsigned field, '!!', ternary, bool/int temporaries holding the condition, '0==(...)', '(...)==0', bitwise '|', and cast-to-bool. Also ruled out as the cause: every optimisation level (-O0/-O1/-O2/-O3/-O4,s/-O4,p all differ identically), '-g' (adds.debug/.line/.mwcats, code unchanged), '-bool on|off' (only enables the C++ bool TYPE, does not affect this), and C++ mode -- compiling as.cpp DOES show MWCC emitting sltu for a bool conversion, but only in the value/return position, never in the condition position retail uses, and a 'static bool hasFlag(int)' helper stays an out-of-line call rather than inlining. Addressing is not the issue: with the global declared as an array ('extern G *DAT_0055ebc0[4]') the first eight instructions match retail exactly, and a plain 'extern G *g' gives gp-relative code instead of retail's lui/lw.

**Notes:** NOTE: the headline claim 'not templatable' was WRONG -- #312 found the source idiom and 550 functions are banked. The rest of this finding (the refuted source forms list, and the codegen analysis) is still accurate and useful: every form listed here genuinely fails; the answer was a form nobody had tried (an assignment to a named bool inside the condition). The 76-byte half remains open as #313. Folded into #312, the current answer for Guard family 72-byte variant (assign to named bool); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/guard-family.md

<a id="f311"></a>
### #311 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x130060 — guard family (1,062 fns); FUN_00130060

THE GUARD FAMILY IS ONE INSTRUCTION FROM MATCHING, AND THE MISSING PIECE IS NOW PRECISELY CHARACTERISED. Retail at 0x130060 is 'addiu sp,-16; lui at,HI; sd ra; lw v0,OFF(at); beqz v0,CALL; nop; lw v0,36(v0); andi v0,v0,4; sltu v0,zero,v0; bnez v0,END; nop; nop; CALL: jal X; nop; END: lui at,HI; ld ra; lw v0,OFF(at); jr ra; addiu sp,sp,16'. With the global declared as an array ('extern G *DAT_0055ebc0[4]', 16 bytes so MWCC uses absolute lui/lw rather than gp-relative) and the source 'if(!G0 || !(bool)(G0->f & 4)) X; return G0;', instructions 0-7 match retail EXACTLY (lui/sd/lw/beqz/nop/lw/andi) and the ONLY difference is the missing 'sltu v0,zero,v0' at index 8, after which everything shifts by one word (68 bytes vs 72). So the whole family is blocked on a single codegen question: how does the original source make MWCC MATERIALISE the flag test as a boolean value THERE, inside a short-circuit, without also materialising the pointer test and without an extra load of the global (retail loads the global exactly twice: once for the null test, once for the return).

**Notes:** RESOLVED by #312: the characterisation here (one instruction, sltu at index 8, global loaded twice) was exactly right; the idiom turned out to be 'bool t; if (!g || !(t = g->f & 4)) X;' compiled as C++. The 550-function 72-byte variant is banked; see docs/engine/guard-family.md. Folded into #312, the current answer for Guard family 72-byte variant (assign to named bool); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/guard-family.md

<a id="f312"></a>
### #312 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00130060 — guard family variant A (550 fns, 72 B); FUN_00130060

THE GUARD FAMILY'S MISSING sltu IS AN ASSIGNMENT TO A NAMED C++ bool INSIDE THE SHORT-CIRCUIT CONDITION -- 550 FUNCTIONS BANKED. The idiom is: 'struct G *g = DAT_0055ebc0[0]; bool t; if (!g || !(t = g->f & 4)) FUN_001301c0; return DAT_0055ebc0[0];' compiled as C++ (MWCC picks the language from the file extension, so these are the repo's first src/*.cpp units). The int->bool VALUE conversion of the assignment is what makes MWCC emit 'andi v0,v0,4; sltu v0,zero,v0; bnez' -- the pointer test stays a plain 'beqz' and the global is still loaded exactly twice. Nothing else reaches that state: a bare condition '!(g->f&4)' gives andi+branch with no sltu; a (bool) cast is folded; 'bool t = g->f & 4;' loads the global before the null test (wrong prologue); 'bool t = g && (g->f & 4);' materialises the pointer too (extra sltu on v1, 76 bytes). The global must be declared as a 16-byte ARRAY (struct G *DAT_x[4]) or MWCC uses gp-relative addressing instead of retail's lui/lw. Verified byte-identical 550/550 (72 bytes, 5 relocations each) with tools/verify.py and in bulk by 'tools/automatch.py --only guard'; build support for.cpp added to Makefile/check.py/linkgen.py. A census of the motif 'andi + sltu rd,zero,rs + bnez rd' finds 1,083 sites in 1,071 functions.

**Notes:** Coverage 1,783 -> 2,333 (14.6%); make link stays BYTE-IDENTICAL. Supersedes #310/#311: the idiom was reachable from source after all. Topic head for Guard family 72-byte variant (assign to named bool): also read #310, #311 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/guard-family.md

<a id="f313"></a>
### #313 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x00334460 — guard family variant B (507 fns, 76 B); FUN_00334460

THE OTHER HALF OF THE GUARD FAMILY (507 FUNCTIONS, 76 BYTES) IS A DIFFERENT FILE'S COMPILE OF THE SAME IDIOM PLUS ONE DEAD nop, AND THE SOURCE FORM THAT KEEPS THAT nop IS STILL UNKNOWN. The family splits by contiguous region, not per function: variant A = 550 at 72 B (0x130060 x501, 0x44b8d0 x49), variant B = 507 at 76 B (0x334460 x483, 0x4586f0 x24). B is A with one extra nop at index 11, immediately before the merged jal and skipped by the null-test branch (which targets the jal), with both displacements one word larger. Ruled out: an -O level difference (the same source at -O1/-O2 DOES give 19 insns with the pad, but with the unscheduled prologue 'sd ra' before 'lui at' and an unscheduled epilogue; retail B has the -O3 schedule and the pad. -O3 deletes the pad. No -O level, -opt keyword, -inline or -intrinsics setting gives retail B); a C-vs-C++ difference (the C-mode spelling 'unsigned char t; if (!g || !(t = (g->f & 4) != 0))' gives the same sltu and 72 bytes); an address-alignment artifact (all guards in both variants start 16-byte aligned; va mod 8/16 and the 0/8/12-byte inter-function gaps do not correlate); and ~500 hand-written forms plus a 432-case grammar sweep plus the permuter (tools/permuter/FUN_00334460, ~1.2M iterations at ~900/s, base score 105, no improvement). The variant correlates with the callee's shape: A guards call 3-instruction 'j' tail-call wrappers, B guards call 32-byte frame-keeping wrappers (see #314), so the two groups are different source files and the difference is file-level. Likely a front-end emission that -O3 normally schedules away, inhibited by something we cannot yet express in one function's source.

**Notes:** Also: the permuter DID produce one output (score 65 vs base 105), but it is a reward hack, not progress -- output-65-1/ inserts dead code 'if ((!g) && (!g)) { g++; g--; }' which MWCC emits as a single nop at index 4 (between the global load and the null test), giving 76 bytes with 7 wrong instructions. It confirms MWCC emits a nop for a dead/empty block, but the nop lands where the dead construct sits, and every placement that would land it at index 11 (inside the if body, or at an explicit call: label reached by goto) is folded away at -O3. Also tested and negative: single-TU compilation of the guard together with its callee in either order (still 18 insns / 72 B), so it is not a TU-context artifact.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/guard-family.md

<a id="f314"></a>
### #314 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001005f0 — one-call wrappers (wcall/wload); verification harness / tooling; FUN_001005f0

THE 1,012-STRONG ONE-CALL WRAPPER FAMILY WAS A LANGUAGE-MODE GAP, NOT A TAIL-CALL PUZZLE: MWCC'S C FRONT END TURNS 'void f(void){ X; }' INTO A 2-INSTRUCTION 'j' TAIL CALL, ITS C++ FRONT END KEEPS THE FRAME. Retail wrappers are C++, so they reproduce only from.cpp. 1,085 functions are 28/32-byte single-call frame-keeping wrappers; two new tools/automatch.py templates reproduce 1,012 of them, every one verified byte-identical: 'wcall' (28 B: addiu sp,-16; sd ra; jal X; nop; ld ra; jr ra; addiu sp,sp,16) from 'void f(void){ X; }' (527/527), and 'wload' (32 B, same plus lui aX,HI before the jal and addiu aX,aX,LO in the delay slot) from 'void f(void){ X(DAT_arg); }' (485/493). The 73 not covered are different shapes (19 return a constant via 'li' in the jal delay slot; 46 have a 'sync' barrier or a 'beq' in the body). This corrects the earlier 'not a templating gap' claim: it was the same extension switch that cracked the guard family (#312).

**Notes:** Coverage 2,333 -> 3,345 (20.9%); make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. Every wrapper was checked by relocation-aware verification (tools/verify.py) before being written; automatch writes src/\<name>.cpp only on a confirmed byte match. The build harness now compiles.cpp: Makefile, tools/check.py, tools/linkgen.py.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/guard-family.md

<a id="f315"></a>
### #315 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00334460 — guard family variant B; FUN_00334460

THE 76-BYTE GUARD VARIANT IS THE SAME SOURCE COMPILED AT A LOWER OPTIMISATION LEVEL (-O1/-O2) OR IN C MODE RATHER THAN C++. Refuted by direct experiment: the same source at -O1/-O2 does produce 19 instructions (76 bytes) with the pad, and C mode with 'unsigned char t; if (!g || !(t = (g->f & 4) != 0))' also emits the sltu -- but BOTH put 'sd ra,0(sp)' before 'lui at' in the prologue and leave the epilogue unscheduled, whereas retail B has the -O3 schedule ('lui at' before 'sd ra'). -O3 removes the pad. No optimisation level or language mode produces retail B, so the variant is not a flag difference.

**Notes:** Negative result kept so the flag hypothesis is not re-tested.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/guard-family.md

<a id="f316"></a>
### #316 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001002a0 — tools/m2c_{one,batch}.py; make check 4174/15974 matching

m2c IS A WORTHWHILE BULK INSTRUMENT FOR THIS BUILD, BUT IT YIELDS ~6% MECHANICALLY AND THE REST IS PER-FUNCTION WORK. m2c (vendored under local/m2c/, target 'mips-mwcc-c') was run over all 12,629 functions with no C: 799 matched in the first sweep (6.3%) and 30 more after the cleaner was fixed, so 829 banked, coverage 3,345 -> 4,174/15,974 (26.1%), make link still BYTE-IDENTICAL. Pipeline: tools/m2c_one.py generates the spimdisasm assembly m2c needs from the retail bytes (registers need '$'; branch/jump targets must be labels, recomputed from the encoded offset and inserted as.L\<addr>), and tools/m2c_batch.py runs m2c IN-PROCESS -- importing m2c.main once costs 0.003 s/call versus 2.1 s per m2c.py subprocess -- then mechanically cleans the C (s32/u8/? types, func_/D_ names, arg->unkN, unknown derefs, fn-pointer calls, NULL) and writes to src/ only after tools/verify.py proves the bytes identical. Two non-obvious facts: (a) THE ADDRESS FORM CANNOT BE DECIDED FROM THE SOURCE -- MWCC materialises a literal through a scratch with ori and a symbol into the destination with addiu (finding #307), so all three spellings (all-symbol, all-literal, hybrid=base-of-a-cast only) are compiled and the matching one kept; (b) data globals run to 0x58d280 (the first PT_LOAD memsz), NOT the relinked section's 0x554b00 -- hard-coding the section end left every.sbss/.bss global as a literal and was the first sweep's 6,976 compile failures. Failure taxonomy of the rest: 971 functions m2c cannot parse at all (large MMI lq/sq bodies); compile failures are type-model problems (void* member access, implicit conversions); mismatches are jump tables (m2c cannot see the rodata table in a flat code blob), the 64-bit argument idiom (dsll32 a0,a0,0), m2c's var_/temp_ locals changing MWCC scheduling, and do/while vs retail's loop shape. build/m2c_worklist.tsv holds name/va/size/status/diffs/mode for every candidate; 855 mismatches are within 3 instructions (median size 24 B) and are the cheapest LLM-in-the-loop targets.

**Notes:** FINAL: four sweeps banked 1,067 (799 + 30 + 128 + 110), coverage 3,345 -> 4,412/15,974 (27.6%), make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. Later sweeps added: %hi/%lo data symbolization (#319, the part that pays) and restored delay slots (#318, which cut m2c failures 971 -> 278). Remaining: 278 m2c-unsupported (MMI lq/sq), 7,866 compile-fail, 3,418 mismatch, 739 within 3 instructions.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f317"></a>
### #317 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100008 — tools/automatch.py disassemble; tools/m2c_one.py

LATENT BUG FIXED IN tools/automatch.disassemble: objdump ELIDES RUNS OF FOUR OR MORE ZERO WORDS AS '...' UNLESS -z IS PASSED, so every address inside such a run was missing from the shape table and every function containing a nop block was silently skipped as truncated. Adding -z to the objdump invocation took the table from 1,007,872 to 1,082,834 entries (74,962 recovered) and 0 of 15,974 functions now have a missing word. No template's yield changed (the missing words were overwhelmingly in data regions), but the table is now complete, which matters for any tool that walks a function word-by-word -- tools/m2c_one.py builds its listing from it. Note check.py already avoided this trap by reading.text bytes and the symbol table instead of parsing objdump.

**Notes:** No yield change on the existing 13 templates, but the fix is required for m2c's per-instruction listing and for any census over functions with nop blocks.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f318"></a>
### #318 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100240 — tools/split.py extend_delay_slots; config/SLUS-20836/symbols.txt; asm/

728 FUNCTIONS (4.6%) HAD NO COMPLETE BODY: GHIDRA'S BOUNDARY CUT OFF THEIR DELAY SLOT, AND tools/split.py NOW RESTORES IT. A MIPS branch/jump always executes the word after it, but for 728 functions the symbol ends on that instruction — e.g. FUN_00100240 ends at 'jr $ra' (0x100284) and the 'addiu sp,sp,16' it must execute is at 0x100288, which layout.py assigns to a *data* unit. Nothing downstream noticed: the bytes still link byte-identically (they are emitted as data), asm/ round-trips, and every check passed — but such a symbol is not a function, no C can compile to it, and m2c refuses it outright ('Last instruction is missing a delay slot'). That is most of the 971 m2c failures the bulk pass saw (they fell to 278 once fixed). Terminators by mnemonic: jr 649, j 52, b 12, jal 7, beqz 4, bnez 3, bne 1. split.py now grows the size by one word when the last instruction is a branch/jump (is_terminator: opcode 1-7, or jr/jalr) and the next symbol does not start inside that word (2 of 728 do overlap; layout.py already resolves those). Result: 727 extended, code bytes 3,174,984 -> 3,177,892, layout units 28,260 -> 27,998, split round-trip 15,974/15,974, make link still BYTE-IDENTICAL, and no already-matched function was affected (a matched function necessarily contains its delay slot, so none was among the 728). It is implemented as a derivation rule in split.py so data/functions.json stays the raw Ghidra dump and regeneration keeps the fix.

**Notes:** This is a foundation bug, not an m2c issue: it silently capped the matchable set. Worth checking whether data/functions.json should carry a corrected size instead, so other consumers of the Ghidra dump see it too.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f319"></a>
### #319 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100bc0 — tools/m2c_context.py; tools/m2c_batch.py clean/arrayize_globals; data/reflection.json

THE RECOVERED CLASS METADATA DOES NOT IMPROVE MATCHING; FEEDING m2c SYMBOLIZED GLOBALS DOES. tools/m2c_context.py builds build/m2c_context.c from data/reflection.json (659 classes / 3,680 fields with byte offsets and ig*MetaField types, plus each class's handle address — 0x55ebc0 is igVisualContextAttrDefaultManager, the guard family's global) and data/vtables.json (478 vtables, 1,286 named vfuncs), with field widths from the same NOMINAL table scripts/apply_reflection_names.py derived empirically. It is inert on its own: our asm generator fed m2c raw lui/lw pairs, so m2c invented anonymous literal globals (`*(void **)0x55EBC0`) that no context declaration can ever match. Emitting %hi(SYM)/%lo(SYM) (what spimdisasm does) is the missing link, and an A/B on the same 300 unmatched functions, same cleaner, measured: raw lui/lw = 0 matches / 169 compile-fail / 131 mismatch; symbolized = 3 / 218 / 79; symbolized + context = 2 / 219 / 79. So symbolization converts 52 compile failures into comparable candidates and makes the C readable (`extern void *DAT_00555048[4]; temp_v1 = DAT_00555048[0];` instead of pointer casts MWCC rejects), while the context is noise at n=300. The context is structurally narrow: only 198 of 11,800 unmatched functions (1.7%) read a class handle global, matching is decided by instructions (whose offsets and widths m2c already recovers — it printed unk24 with no metadata), and the void* compile failures are about PARAMETER types, which nothing in the repo records (all 15,974 data/functions.json signatures are 'undefined f'). The named globals also need the guard family's array trick (finding #311): m2c's own `extern void *DAT_x;` is 4 bytes and therefore small data -> gp-relative lw, where retail uses absolute lui at/lw; the cleaner rewrites it to `extern void *DAT_x[4];` + `DAT_x[0]`.

**Notes:** Keep the context (readability, and --vtables-protos is one flag away), but do not expect matching gains from more type metadata until parameter types exist — that needs a type-inference pass or a Ghidra dump with parameter recovery enabled.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f320"></a>
### #320 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x004650a0 — tools/m2c_batch.py clean; worklist composition; make check 4,412/15,974

THE MECHANICAL m2c ROUTE IS EXHAUSTED: THE 71% OF REMAINING BYTES BEHIND COMPILE FAILURES ARE A LONG TAIL OF PER-FUNCTION ERRORS, AND CLEANER FIXES YIELD MISMATCHES, NOT MATCHES. Measured composition of the 3,026,700 remaining code bytes: compile-fail tier 7,866 functions / 2,157,592 bytes (71.3%), mismatch tier 3,418 / 335,316 bytes (11.1%), m2c-unsupported 278 (MMI), and the near-miss tier (<=3 instructions) is 739 functions but only 27,872 bytes (0.9%). The compile tier was the biggest single lever, so three mechanical causes found in it were fixed in tools/m2c_batch.py: (a) m2c emits callee prototypes whose arity disagrees with its own call sites ('function call FUN_x(int) does not match FUN_x(int,int)') -> loosen_prototypes rewrites declarations to K&R 'T f;' so any call compiles; (b) m2c names an unknown stack slot 'sp' and never declares it -> declare_stack_placeholders; (c) it also emits 'saved_reg_gp'. Result on a 250-function sample of the compile tier: 38 became mismatches, 212 still failed, 0 matched. The residual is a long tail -- the most common FIRST error accounts for only 36% of failures, with 'illegal implicit conversion from int to', 'illegal type', 'not a struct/union/class', 'pointer/array required', 'expression syntax error', 'undefined identifier saved_reg_gp' all present. Also fixed: the cleaner replaced every '?' with 'int', corrupting ternary operators ('(a == 0) ? 4: 2' -> '(a == 0) int 4: 2'); fix_unknown_type now separates them exactly (a ternary '?' is always preceded by a token that can end an expression). CONCLUSION: further regex work on the cleaner is not where the remaining 95% of the code is -- per-function iteration with tools/verify.py as the oracle is the only route that scales, with m2c supplying the first draft.

**Notes:** Strategy input: do not re-grind the cleaner. The near-miss tier is cheap to automate (permuter) but is 0.9% of bytes; the 71% compile tier needs per-function work; the 172 functions with computed-target jr (jump tables/indirect) are 372,688 bytes = 12.3% and need m2c to see the rodata tables.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f321"></a>
### #321 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0010a440 — tools/permuter_one.py; build/m2c_worklist3.tsv (mismatch <=3 instr); FUN_0010a440

THE PERMUTER IS NOT WORTH SCALING FOR THIS CODEBASE: IT CLOSES ~7% OF THE NEAR-MISS TIER, AND THAT TIER IS ONLY 0.9% OF THE REMAINING CODE BYTES. The harness was generalised first (tools/permuter_one.py turns any VA into a decomp-permuter project: target.o assembled symbolically from the retail bytes via m2c_one.asm_for, base.c from m2c's cleaned draft, K&R prototypes, sh_size shrunk to drop as(1) 16-byte padding). It was VALIDATED by feeding it a known-correct source, which scores exactly 0 — so the scoring is sound and a 0 is reachable. Pilot: a stratified sample of the near-miss list (mismatch, <=3 instructions) — 5 functions from each of <=32 B, 33-256 B, >256 B, 100 s each, 4 permuters in parallel. Result: 1/15 closed (FUN_0010a440, 36 B, banked), 10 ended at scores 100-8605, 4 produced no output (m2c could not decompile them). The near-miss tier is 739 functions but only 27,872 bytes = 0.9% of the 3,026,700 remaining bytes, so a 7% closure rate buys ~0.06% of the remaining bytes for ~5 h of 4-way CPU. Decision: do not scale it here. Two lessons worth keeping: the permuter's score 0 is NOT proof of a match (it ignores branch targets by default) so every candidate must be re-verified, and tools/verify.py writes FIXED temp paths and therefore must never be called concurrently — doing so produced a false negative that made the first reading of this pilot '0/15' when one function had in fact matched. Also note the matching source for FUN_0010a440 is not the natural one: the permuter had to add redundant assignments (new_var2 = *(int*)(arg0+64); new_var = new_var2; FUN_x(new_var2 = new_var + 0x68)) to reproduce retail, which is evidence that MWCC's codegen depends on expression structure in ways m2c does not model.

**Notes:** Reproduce with: python3 tools/permuter_one.py --from-worklist build/m2c_worklist3.tsv --max-diffs 3 --pilot 5 --budget 100 --jobs 4. The remaining routes are per-function LLM work (the 71% compile tier) and m2c rodata for the jump-table family (172 functions, 12.3% of remaining bytes).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

### #322 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00204840 — tools/split.py extend_jump_table_bodies; FUN_00204840; FUN_00109410

151 SWITCH DISPATCHERS WERE MISSING THEIR CASE BODIES: GHIDRA STOPS AT THE INDIRECT jr AND LEAVES THE JUMP-TABLE BODIES IN AN UNCLAIMED GAP. 155 unmatched functions (401,160 bytes = 13.2% of the remaining code) branch outside their own symbol, and not one of the 4,413 matched functions does. Example: FUN_00204840 is recorded as 52 bytes — just 'sltiu at,a0,14; beqz at,0x204904; move v0,zero; lui v0,0x4e; sll v1,a0,2; addiu v0,v0,21168; addu v0,v1,v0; lw v0,0(v0); jr v0' — while its 14 case bodies and the shared 'jr ra' epilogue sit at 0x204874..0x204910, attributed to no function at all (2,556 out-of-range targets land in such gaps; the layout emits them as data units, which is why the link was still byte-identical and nothing noticed). m2c rejects such a fragment outright ('Cannot find branch target.L00204904'). tools/split.py now grows the symbol to the next one when EVERY out-of-range pc-relative branch lands in the unclaimed gap (none inside another function; growth capped at 8 KB): 151 functions grew by 15,908 bytes, code bytes 3,174,984 -> 3,193,800, split round-trip 15,974/15,974, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL, no matched function affected. Payoff: m2c now parses 104 of the 151 where it previously rejected all of them (22 more compile and differ, 0 match yet) — the remaining blocker is the table itself. m2c's error gives the contract: the table must be referenced by a symbol whose name starts with jtbl/jpt_/lbl_/jumptable_ and defined as a.section.rodata block of '.word.Lcase' lines (its own test suite, tests/end_to_end/got/manual.s, shows the format). NEXT STEP: detect the table base, read its entries, rename DAT_x -> jtbl_x, insert case labels, append the.rodata block. BEFORE THAT, tools/verify.py (which compares only the symbol's bytes in.text) and tools/linkgen.py (which places only obj(.text)) must both learn to place and check a compiler-generated.rodata at the retail table address, or a switch function will 'verify' while its table is never checked and the relink drops it.

**Notes:** Together with #318 this is 878 functions given their correct extent. The 104 newly-parseable functions are 13% of the remaining code and are the best-defined remaining mechanical target.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f323"></a>
### #323 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00246788 — FUN_00246780

tools/m2c_one.asm_for applied the MIPS %lo sign correction twice, so every symbolized data address whose low half has bit 15 set was named 0x10000 too low; verify.py resolves a symbol by the address in its name, so those candidates could never match. 3,792 of 11,558 unmatched functions were affected; fixing it banked 219 functions / 15,792 bytes (4,416 -> 4,635, 151,636 -> 167,428 code bytes).

**Notes:** tools/m2c_one.asm_for MIS-NAMED EVERY DATA ADDRESS WHOSE %lo HALF HAS BIT 15 SET — IT APPLIED THE SIGN CORRECTION TWICE. The symbolizer built an address from a lui/addiu (or lui/lw) pair as (hi<<16) + (off - 0x10000 if off & 0x8000 else off), but objdump prints an addiu immediate ALREADY sign-extended ('addiu v1,v1,-24064', not 0xa200) and a load displacement already sign-extended too ('lw v0,-8392(at)'), so only the raw 16-bit value — the `ori` case — needs that correction. Every address whose low half has bit 15 set was therefore named 0x10000 too low (0x56df38 -> DAT_0055df38), and because tools/verify.py resolves a symbol by the address embedded in its name, every candidate touching such a global was relocated to the wrong address and could never match. Reproduced: retail 0x246788 is 'lui at,0x57; lw v0,-8392(at)' = 0x56df38, and the hand-written matching src/unmatched/FUN_00246780.c says D_0056df30 for the -8400 word, while asm_for emitted DAT_0055df38/DAT_0055df30.
MEASURED IMPACT. 3,792 of the 11,558 unmatched functions (32.8%) contain at least one such reference (32,556 references). A/B on two 400-function samples drawn from the same VA region and the same size cap (<= 4 KB), differing only in whether the function has a bit-15 %lo reference: 114/400 matched for the affected sample (28.5%) against 0/400 for the control (0.0%). The dependence was then proved per function by re-running the same asm with only the pre-fix names substituted back in: FUN_00130310, FUN_00130440, FUN_00130f60 and FUN_00131160 all match with the corrected names and all fail with the old ones. The full re-sweep of all 11,558 unmatched functions banked 219 new functions / 15,792 code bytes — coverage 4,416 -> 4,635 functions (27.7% -> 29.0%) and 151,636 -> 167,428 code bytes (4.7% -> 5.2%), every one verified byte-identical. The 400-sample rate was optimistic (28.5% vs the sweep's 1.9%) because the sample's VA window is full of small clean accessors; large functions rarely match for unrelated reasons. The convention now lives in exactly one place, automatch.eff_addr(hi, off, is_addiu), used by both m2c_one and tools/jumptables.py.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_one.py

<a id="f324"></a>
### #324 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x004e52b0 — FUN_00204840

The switches' jump tables are recovered and emitted: tools/jumptables.py finds 140 functions / 180 tables / 2,694 entries from the dispatcher shape, m2c_one.asm_for emits them as a jtbl_* symbol in a.section.rodata block, verify.py compares the compiled.rodata against the retail bytes (and fails an object that has.rodata with no recorded table), and layout.py/linkgen.py place it as obj(.rodata) at the retail table address.

**Notes:** WHAT WAS BUILT. (1) tools/jumptables.py detects a dispatcher backwards from the indirect jump: find 'jr $Rs' (Rs != $ra), find the load that defines Rs and its base register, follow that register's definitions back through the addu/sll chain to the unique lui/addiu pair, take the entry count from the bound guard 'sltiu Rd,\<index>,N' (whose destination cannot be pinned — MWCC puts it in $at or any free register: FUN_00109410 uses 'sltiu $v0,$s2,5'), and accept only if every entry lands inside the function. The table's low half can also sit in the LOAD's displacement rather than an addiu ('lui v1,0x4d; lw v1,0x41d0(v1)' = 0x4d41d0, FUN_0012c858's 89 cases), which is the second-most-common form for the big ones. Result: 140 functions, 180 tables, 2,694 entries, 10,776 table bytes, 11,044 bytes of span (18 functions have 2-9 tables; MWCC 16-byte-aligns each, the inter-table padding is zero and every first table is 16-aligned — checked). Written to config/SLUS-20836/jumptables.txt as 'fn_va jtbl_va count'. (2) tools/m2c_one.asm_for(jtables=...) names the base pair jtbl_\<addr>, seeds a.L\<case> label at every entry target and appends the '.section.rodata / jtbl_x: /.word.Lcase' block m2c's own tests/end_to_end/got/manual.s shows; m2c now parses the switch instead of failing with 'Unable to determine jump table for jr instruction'. (3) tools/verify.py compares the compiled.rodata against the retail bytes at the retail table address, relocation-aware (R_MIPS_32 is REL, so the addend is the word itself; the dispatcher's HI16/LO16 reference MWCC's ANONYMOUS local table symbol — '@21' — which is resolved by placing our.text at the function VA and our.rodata at the table VA). An object that carries.rodata for a function with NO recorded table is now a hard FAILURE, which is the guard the task needed: otherwise a switch would 'verify' on its code alone and the relink would drop the table. (4) tools/layout.py carves each function's table span out of the enclosing data unit as a 'jtbl' unit (owner recorded), and tools/linkgen.py places that unit's bytes as obj(.rodata) of the owning function's compiled object (or the retail bytes when the function has no C), requiring the sizes to agree. make link stayed BYTE-IDENTICAL through the layout change (28,122 units), and it is still byte-identical with the three switch functions compiled from C, i.e. the compiled tables really do land at 0x4eb250/0x4ea200/... in the relinked section.

**Evidence:** tools/jumptables.py

<a id="f325"></a>
### #325 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0024a700 — FUN_0024a700

Jump tables emitted: 3 of the 140 switch functions match (408 code bytes); the remaining 135 are 122 compile-fail / 14 mismatch / 2 m2c-fail. The table fixed m2c's PARSE, not the match — the next wall is m2c's type model (107/138 contain MMI lq/sq/ld/sd, 89 declare void* temps). The void*->int experiment converted 8 compile failures to mismatches and 0 matches, so it was not kept.

**Notes:** MEASURED YIELD. 140 switch functions = 371,284 code bytes = 11.6% of the 3,193,800 code bytes. Re-running tools/m2c_batch.py --jtbl-only over all of them: 3 matched (408 bytes) — FUN_0023cd00 (60 B, 13 cases returning 0/1), FUN_00204150 (232 B) and FUN_0024a700 (116 B, hand-corrected). The other 135 split 122 compile-fail / 14 mismatch / 2 m2c-fail (m2c still cannot parse two of them). So the jump table was the BLOCKER for the *parse*, not for the match: with the table visible m2c produces a complete, readable switch, and the next wall is the same type model that blocks the rest of the corpus (finding #320). Composition of the 138 candidates: 107 contain lq/sq/ld/sd (MMI 64/128-bit moves), 89 declare a 'void *temp_*' local, 8 use m2c's s128 type. Compile errors, censused by first error: 28 'illegal implicit conversion from int to void *' (a void*-declared temp assigned a word load), 25 'undefined identifier' (mostly s128, which the cleaner's type map lacks — and it cannot be mapped to any MWCC-PS2 type without changing codegen), 24 'identifier redeclared as char[]' (the arrayize_globals trick colliding with m2c's own extern), 13 compile, then a long tail of illegal type / pointer-array required. ONE MECHANICAL EXPERIMENT, REPORTED AS A NEGATIVE: rewriting every m2c 'void *temp_*'/'void *var_*' local to 'int' (the values are register-width and all their uses already carry explicit casts) took the tier from 122 compile-fail to 114, i.e. it converted 8 compile failures into mismatches and produced ZERO additional matches — the same measured behaviour as #320, so it was not kept. KEEPING: the one near-miss that a source idiom explains. m2c's 'switch (arg0)' over cases 32..41 compiles its index adjustment as 'addi v0,a0,-32' where retail has 'addiu' (0x2482ffe0) — MWCC lowers a bare switch over int constants to the SIGNED subtract even when the parameter is unsigned. Writing the adjustment explicitly, 'switch ((unsigned)(arg0 - 32))' with cases 0..9, reproduces retail exactly (src/third_party/lua/lcode.c). That idiom is specific: only 1 of the 14 mismatches differs in the index opcode.

**Evidence:** build/m2c_jtbl_worklist.tsv

THE TOP COMPILE-FAILURE BLOCKER WAS A DUPLICATE GLOBAL DECLARATION FROM THE CLEANER: clean emits 'extern char DAT_x[]' for a numeric address while m2c's own declaration for the same address (through %hi/%lo) is arrayized to 'extern void *DAT_x[4]', and MWCC rejects the pair ('identifier DAT_0055f5d0 redeclared / was declared as: char[] / now declared as: void *[4]'). <a id="f332"></a>
### #332 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00113038 — FUN_00113038

THE R5900 C CALLING CONVENTION PASSES THE FIRST EIGHT INTEGER ARGUMENTS IN a0-a3 THEN t0-t3, NOT THE O32 FOUR. Measured directly: MWCC compiles 'void f(int a,int b){ X(a,b,2,3,4); }' to 'li a2,2; li a3,3; j X; li t0,4' - the fifth argument lands in t0. This is what the frame-keeping wrapper family's t-register setups mean (FUN_00113038 is 'move t0,zero' plus 'move a3,sp' before the jal), and it is why 55 wrappers that set t-registers reproduce once the enclosing signature declares those parameters. tools/automatch.py's new 'wargs' template decodes the whole argument setup (constant, pass-through, field load, global address, parameter address) from the retail shape and emits the wrapper as C++; 55 verified byte-identical (658 candidate compiles, 8%).

**Evidence:** tools/automatch.py

THE 480 FUNCTIONS THAT REACH DATA THROUGH gp COULD NOT MATCH BECAUSE THE SYMBOLIZER LEFT disp(gp) NUMERIC. Retail's small-data access is lbu $v1,-32476($gp) (gp = 0x0055c870 from.reginfo, so the object is at 0x00554994); fed numerically, m2c models $gp as a saved-gp pointer and emits saved_reg_gp->unk-7EDC, which does not compile (265 rows' first error). tools/m2c_one.asm_for now rewrites a load/store whose base is $gp to %gp_rel(GPR_\<addr>)($gp).
