<a id="f338"></a>
### #338 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00000018 — tools/m2c_loop.py

TWO HARNESS BUGS FOUND WHILE TRIAGING. (1) tools/m2c_loop.py --sample N --status compile selected NOTHING: sample required 0 <= diffs, but the 'queue made honest' fix (3d3b2c15) records diffs = -1 for a candidate that does not compile, which is every compile-tier row — the tracker's own triage command was a no-op. The cap now applies only to rows that have a diff (diffs < 0 is 'not comparable'). (2) verify.solve_gp read the retail immediate at an object-side relocation offset and raised struct.error when the object was LARGER than the retail function, aborting an entire 15-minute sweep; it now skips relocations past the retail buffer, so the candidate fails as a mismatch instead.

**Notes:** Both surfaced in this session's triage run; the second killed the first gp sweep at candidate 250.

**Evidence:** tools/verify.py

<a id="f339"></a>
### #339 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x005187b8 — beNDMWMdlPlayer BKCHIP* properties

THE TWO SAVESTATES DIFFER IN EXACTLY ONE STRUCTURED STATE RECORD: THE PLAYER MODEL'S SAVE/CHECKPOINT ('BKCHIP') PROPERTY SET IS UNINITIALISED IN no-deaths AND POPULATED IN 1-death. BKCHIP* are properties of the player model class (the static name table at 0x5187b8..0x5187f8 sits beside the 'Gap::Bec::beNDMWMdlPlayer2' RTTI name): - BKCHIPSTAGE: NULL -> pointer to the interned 'stag0101' (Death Valley) - BKCHIPNAME: NULL -> 'p_dorumon' - BKCHIPPOS: 12 zero bytes -> 3 floats (465.09, 45.39, 84.40) - BKCHIPDATA: 8 zero bytes -> 19 00 00 00 00 00 00 00 - BKCHIPBITEXP: identical in both. In each state the four differing slots live in the SAME holder objects (e.g. BKCHIPSTAGE's 4-byte buffer is 0x6EE7A0 and its holder is 0x79E560 in both), so this is one record being written, not relocated. Everything else that differs between the two RAM images is heap churn (world/asset/entity structures reallocated at different addresses - the live GAMERAM property-pointer array is at 0x7E53A0 and its values are stable) or volatile scratch (timers, RNG, the render/particle buffers), and the engine's current-stage field 0x6FF148 is 'stag0101' in BOTH. The two other real deltas are small: GAMERAM property BIT 25 -> 0 (the player's bits, i.e. the death penalty) and save flag 508 set -> clear (a lobby quest flag touched only by 01-lobby-hub/05-lobby-quests_e820/010-npc-digilfm-8.lua, so a side effect of play, not of the death). READING: the death/restart path returns the player via the checkpoint stage, so with BKCHIPSTAGE empty (fresh session, nothing ever written to the chip) a death in an area the game cannot resolve has no valid return point -> hang; after the Death Valley death wrote stage+pos, the same death in the Goblin Fortress guard room returns to Death Valley instead. That is exactly the reported repro: die once before the skip and later deaths stop softlocking.

**Notes:** CORRECTED BY #340 (dynamic repro): the two states' difference stands, but the reading was wrong — the guard-room death DOES write the whole BKCHIP record ~2 s after the KO (with the CURRENT stage, stagGF01), so an empty chip is not the cause of the softlock. REPRO: p2s_ram extracts; live checks via pi-pine. State files: the no-deaths and 1-death savestates; guard-room repro: the no-deaths-in-guard-room savestate. Folded into #340, the current answer for Guard Room death writes the BKCHIP record; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** Offline savestate read of the no-deaths and 1-death savestates

<a id="f340"></a>
### #340 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x005187b8 — flag-408 death chunk (every stage)

DYNAMIC REPRO (the no-deaths-in-guard-room savestate, live emulator): THE DEATH IN THE GUARD ROOM DOES WRITE THE WHOLE BKCHIP RECORD, ~120 FRAMES (2 s) AFTER THE KO — finding #339's static-only inference that it is never written was a timing artifact (a read 1.5 s after the KO still showed NULL). Measured on the live VM: pre-death BKCHIPSTAGE = NULL; at the KO still NULL; ~120 frames later BKCHIPSTAGE = 'stagGF01' (the CURRENT stage — not Death Valley), BKCHIPPOS = (78.20, 10.0, -75.55), BKCHIPNAME = 'p_dorumon', BKCHIPDATA = 0x19. Structurally identical to the Death Valley death, which records stag0101 + its own position. AFTER THE WRITE THE GAME STALLS: the ENEMY and PLAYER2 controllers go off (the enemy drops to idle, the player model and the entire HUD disappear) and neither the SQ_GAMEOVER fade nor the dialogue box that normally appears within ~5 s ever happens — the scene stays that way over 6 s and over a 10 s run (softlock). Seeding the block beforehand with exactly the post-Death-Valley values (stag0101 + the DV position + name + data, written into the live holders 0x6EE7A0/0x7A0390/0x7DABB8/0x7A03F0) does NOT prevent the stall, and the death overwrites it with stagGF01 regardless — so the block's content is not what makes the 'die once before the skip' workaround work. THE HANDLER IS THE SHARED flag-408 CHUNK (byte-identical in 02-valley-overworld/01-stag0101-valley-entrance_e614/028-flag-408.lua and 12-goblimon-fortress/{01,02,03,04}-*/...flag-408.lua): first it runs ENEMY/PLAYER2 'OFCTRL' (which is exactly the state we see), then GENERATEs the i_fadewn01 fade 'SQ_GAMEOVER' and waits 'ISRUN == 0', then opens i_messwn and shows message mswk62009 with a SELECTCTRL wait, then PLAYERPOINT SETGENERATEPOS I<0>,0 + STAGECTL NEXTSTAGE 'stagML11' (return to the hub). So the stall is inside the scripted handler (after OFCTRL, at/before the fade+message), not in the checkpoint data. Setting flag 408 directly in the Death Valley state (live, 0x7BA8D0) did NOT trigger the chunk — it is not a plain flag-value watch — so the trigger is engine-side and still unidentified.

**Notes:** Method note: pi-state/pi-pine savestates written while the VM is paused came back STALE (guardroom-death.p2s held the pre-death state, guardroom-stuck.p2s held a later Death Valley state) — verified by the embedded screenshots and by md5 against the slot files. Live pi-pine reads are the ground truth for this finding; the static before/after diffs from those snapshots were discarded. Addresses 0x6EE7A0/0x7A0390/0x7DABB8/0x7A03F0 are per-session heap buffers; flag array base 0x7BA738, current-stage field 0x6FF148. Topic head for Guard Room death writes the BKCHIP record: also read #339 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** Running-game read of the BKCHIP record across the KO, from the no-deaths-in-guard-room savestate

<a id="f341"></a>
### #341 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001e0410 — FUN_001e0410

m2c WAS FED THE WRONG MIPS ABI, AND IT WAS THE LARGEST PIPELINE BUG LEFT: its `mips-mwcc-c` target selects MipsArch, whose integer argument slots stop at a0-a3, but the R5900 passes arguments 5-8 in t0-t3 (finding #332). The fix is to run m2c's EE arch instead — `mipsee-mwcc-c` (MipseeArch) names the extra slots a4-a7 — AND to rename the hardware t0-t3 to a4-a7 in the assembly, because MipseeArch does not list t0-t3 at all (without the rename they are not even parsed as registers). tools/m2c_one.asm_for(ee_abi=True) does the rename; m2c_batch.run_m2c and m2c_loop/m2c_one now default to `mipsee-mwcc-c`. PROOF: FUN_001e0410 retail calls FUN_00306e50(a0, 7, DAT_004e4880, &GPR_00554bac, GPR_00554bac, 1) with six argument registers; the old target produced a four-argument call, the EE target+alias produced the six-argument call and verify.py matched it byte-for-byte (symbol mode). MEASURED: a 400-function stratified A/B of the unmatched tier gave 7 matches under the EE pipeline, 0 under the old, 0 regressions; the full 11,034-row sweep banked 179 (18,592 bytes), taking the tree 4,820 -> 4,999 / 15,974 (31.3 %) with `make link` byte-identical. 168 of the 179 are 100-149 bytes and call FUN_002fe9c0 — the per-class MetaObject registrar trampolines (T1; 1,075 `jal FUN_002fe9c0` sites in retail), whose registration call takes 11 arguments, of which the four-register model could see four. 484 of those registrar callers are still unmatched.

**Notes:** TRIAGE OF THE RESIDUAL after the EE switch (all measured, so nobody re-chases them): (a) the old `subroutine_arg0`/`subroutine_arg1` family (103 + 24 rows) did NOT survive as a cleaner bug — under MipseeArch it is renamed `sp0`/`sp4` (82 + 22 rows) and is m2c modelling the outgoing-argument stack slot as a *value* while retail passes its *address* (`move a1,sp`) and reloads it. Ten hand-written variants (int/unsigned/void*/array/float/2-local/init/return-deref) all compile to the same shape and all differ from retail by the same four words: the local sits at sp+28 with ra at sp+0 where retail has the local at sp+0 and ra at sp+16. Not mechanical. (b) `bitwise` (98 candidate files, 23 as first error) is m2c's type-pun pseudo-C `(bitwise int)expr`; a union spelling compiles but MWCC then uses `dsll32/dsrl32` for `& 0x7FFFFFFF` where retail used `lui/ori/and`, and `fabsf` tail-calls, so it needs the original union recovered per function. (c) `s128` (34) is the MMI tier, already no-route. (d) bare `saved_reg_\<reg>` reads are functions whose source stores the `gp` value (or an unset register), not expressible in C. NEXT: the 484 remaining registrar-trampoline callers, then the ~2,500-row type model.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f342"></a>
### #342 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001e0410 — FUN_001e0410/FUN_001d81e0

THE gp SYMBOLIZER HANDLED ONLY `disp($gp)` LOADS/STORES; an `addiu $r,$gp,disp` that COMPUTES a small-data object's ADDRESS was left numeric, so m2c emitted `saved_reg_gp - 0x7CC4` and the candidate did not compile. tools/m2c_one.asm_for(gp=...) now also rewrites `addiu $r,$gp,D` to `addiu $r,$gp,%gp_rel(GPR_\<gp+D>)`, which m2c models as `&GPR_x`. PROOF: FUN_001e0410 retail has `addiu a3,gp,-31940` = &GPR_00554bac (gp 0x55c870 - 0x7cc4); the old pipeline emitted `saved_reg_gp - 0x7CC4`, the new one `&GPR_00554bac`. Six candidate files carried `saved_reg_gp - 0xN`; FUN_001e0410 (88 B) and FUN_001d81e0 (136 B) became comparable (8 / 16 diffs) and both were then matched by the EE-ABI sweep (#341); the other four still fail on the type model. This is the "addiu computes a gp address" case the earlier doc said did not exist. Bare `saved_reg_gp` (13 reads) is a different, unfixable kind: those functions store the gp *value* (e.g. DAT_0055cf84[0] = saved_reg_gp), which has no C spelling.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_one.py

<a id="f343"></a>
### #343 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001d4480 — FUN_001d4480

THE CLEANER TURNED m2c's UNKNOWN-WIDTH TYPE `?32` INTO AN UNDECLARED `int32`. m2c writes an unknown type with a known size as `?\<bits>` (m2c types.py: an any-reg TypeData with size_bits set). fix_unknown_type replaced the `?` with `int` but left the digits, so `?32 sp3C;` became `int32 sp3C;` — undeclared and uncompilable, and the file's real errors were hidden behind it. It now consumes the digits and maps 8/16/32/64 to char/short/int/long long (an unmapped width degrades to int), while leaving a ternary `? N:` untouched (which is the whole reason the `?` rule detects ternaries by the preceding token). Measured: 24 candidate files / 98 occurrences carried `int32`, all in the compile tier (168-8,608 bytes, median 280 B); the fix converted them to comparable mismatch candidates (e.g. FUN_001e14d0, FUN_001d4480) but banked 0 matches on its own — finding #320's law again.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f344"></a>
### #344 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0034cf40 — FUN_0034cf40 / FUN_002fe9c0 registrar family

TWO CLEANER RULES COMPLETED THE CLASS-REGISTRAR FAMILY (315 more, 4,999 -> 5,314 / 33.3 %). The 108-116-byte registrars but not the 100-byte ones pass a FUNCTION POINTER as the 9th argument to FUN_002fe9c0. m2c, seeing a `FUN_\<addr>` symbol used only as an address and never called, declares it `extern int FUN_x;` — a small-data object — so MWCC reaches `&FUN_x` gp-relative (`addiu r,gp,imm`) where retail uses absolute `lui`/`addiu`; and it casts the 32-bit function address to `long long`, so MWCC zero-extends it with `dsll32`/`dsrl32` before the `sd`, while retail stores the sign-extended 32-bit pointer directly. Two mechanical cleaner rules fix both: (1) `extern T FUN_<8hex>;` -> `T FUN_<8hex>;` (a FUN_ name is always a function, so `&FUN_x` is an ordinary function pointer); (2) `(long long) &FUN_<8hex>` -> `&FUN_<8hex>`. Measured: of the 316 remaining registrar candidates, 315 matched (the 316th is FUN_002fe9c0 itself), in 28 s. The registrar family is now complete: 1,075 `jal FUN_002fe9c0` sites = 484 distinct caller functions, 483 decompiled, the last being the registrar's own body. `make check` 5,314/15,974, `make link` byte-identical.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f345"></a>
### #345 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001301d0 — registrar callers in Ghidra gaps

THE 592 REMAINING REGISTRAR CALLERS ARE NOT IN THE SYMBOL TABLE AT ALL — CODE-SHAPED GHIDRA GAP LABELS. Mapping every `jal FUN_002fe9c0` site (1,075) to its enclosing symbol gives only 484 distinct caller functions (483 now decompiled); the other 592 sites have NO enclosing symbol, and each sits alone in a distinct unclaimed gap of 116-404 bytes. Example: the real registrar at 0x001301d0 is a 112-byte function calling FUN_002fe9c0 at 0x130230, inside the gap 0x1301cc..0x130270. `data/functions.json` has no function at 0x1301d0 — but Ghidra DOES have a label `LAB_001301d0` in `data/symbols.json` (as it does for the sibling stubs at 0x130250 and 0x130260). So the functions were *discovered* (as labels / branch targets) and never promoted to function objects, most likely because nothing takes their address as code (no xref Ghidra followed). ROUTE: promote code-shaped `LAB_` labels that fall in an unclaimed gap to functions in `tools/split.py` as a third derivation rule (the delay-slot and jump-table-body rules are the precedent), size them by the next start / trailing `jr ra`+delay, then regenerate `config/SLUS-20836/symbols.txt` + `asm/` and confirm `make link` stays byte-identical. The same rule reaches far beyond the registrars: 1,429 `addiu sp,sp,-N` prologues sit in >=8-byte gaps.

**Notes:** RESOLVED by #346: the gap-label promotion in tools/split.py promoted 883 code-shaped LAB_ labels (592 of them registrars) and 600 matched; make link byte-identical. Folded into #346, the current answer for Registrar callers as Ghidra gap labels; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** data/symbols.json

<a id="f346"></a>
### #346 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001301d0 — tools/split.py promote_gap_labels

THE GAP-LABEL PROMOTION: 883 FUNCTIONS GHIDRA ONLY LABELLED, 600 OF THEM MATCHED. tools/split.py gained a third derivation rule (after the delay-slot and jump-table-body rules): promote a `LAB_\<addr>` label that falls in an unclaimed gap to a function when it looks like a function start — a non-leaf frame prologue `addiu sp,sp,-N` that is not reachable by fallthrough (the preceding word is padding, or the previous function's `jr ra` / its delay slot) — with the size running to the word after the last `jr ra` before the next label. The bound is the next label of any kind, not the next promoted start, or a sibling stub after the function is swallowed. 883 promoted: 15,974 -> 16,857 functions, +131,532 code bytes, including ALL 592 remaining registrar trampolines. Byte-identity is untouched — nothing moves, the newly-claimed bytes were already linked as raw data — and `make link` stays byte-identical; the split round-trips 16,857/16,857. Decompiled: 600 matched (all 592 registrars, so the registrar family is complete at 484/484 caller functions, plus 8 others), taking the tree to 5,914 / 16,857 functions (35.1 %) and 299,860 / 3,325,332 code bytes (9.0 %). The 291 non-registrar promotions are real functions, not data (0x10a418 calls 0x10a558; 0x1308c0 calls the reflection driver 0x2fdf40), but only 8 match yet. One further cleaner rule came out of the 64 registrars the first pass missed: the `(long long)` cast also needs dropping on a DATA address (`(long long) &DAT_x[0]`), not just a function address; generalised to any address-of, it took the registrars from 528/592 to 592/592.

**Notes:** Topic head for Registrar callers as Ghidra gap labels: also read #345 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** tools/split.py

<a id="f347"></a>
### #347 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00133770 — FUN_00133770 / promoted vtable-filling functions

A FUNCTION ADDRESS STORED INTO A WORD SLOT NEEDS THE (int) CAST BACK once `FUN_x` is declared a function (finding #344). m2c emits `*(int *)((char *)temp_v0 + 52) = &FUN_002d1870;` for a vtable/thunk table entry, and with the function declaration that is `int (*)` -> `int`, which MWCC rejects. The cleaner now rewrites such a store to `= (int)&FUN_x`. Measured on the 30 promoted functions whose first error was that message: 20 were blocked only by the cast and now compile (the other 10 also carry m2c's malformed `FUN_x*(int *)...` juxtaposition, an m2c parse failure). Over the whole 883-function promotion the compile tier fell 80 -> 66 and the mismatch tier rose 203 -> 217 — 14 rows made comparable, ZERO new matches, finding #320's law again. Kept because it is correct (the cast is required C and the codegen is unchanged), not because it yields.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f348"></a>
### #348 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001c59e0 — tools/m2c_batch.py clean/rewrite_members

THE MEMBER-ACCESS REWRITE WAS SILENTLY MIS-COMPILING CHAINS AND CALL RESULTS, AND THE WHOLE FIX BANKS 73 FUNCTIONS (5,924 -> 5,997 / 35.6 %), every one a previous compile failure. Three symptoms, one cause: the clean member pass rewrote identifiers only and scanned the base against the *original* text. (1) CHAINS: m2c's `arg0->unk8->unk8` became `**(int *)((char *)(((char *)arg0 + 8)) + 8)` — a load from arg0+16, the inner deref lost; the identifier pre-pass rewrote the inner access and the general scan then folded the outer access onto the *cast argument*. (2) CALL RESULTS: `FUN_x(a, b)->unkC` came out as `FUN_x*(int *)((char *)((a, b)) + 12)` — the call's parens and argument list destroyed. (3) SLOT CALLS: `slot->unk28(args)` lost the `((int (*))…)` wrapper because the pre-pass had already consumed the `->unk28`; a call through a *dot* member (`DAT_x.unk0(...)`) never got one either. Two more gaps sat beside them: `(p + N)` on a declared `void *` ("illegal type", 751 rows of the bucket table), including the bare `temp = arg0 + 0x3E8` form, and `.unkN` value members on an unknown-type global ("not a struct/union/class"). The rewrite is now one left-to-right pass that folds the **innermost** member first and rescans, so the outer base is the deref it just emitted: `_primary_start` recognises an identifier, an array element (`DAT_x[0]`), a call result (`f`), a parenthesised expression and a deref-cast (`*(int *)(...)`); the narrow-width cast lands on the outermost access only; and `fix_void_arith` casts pointer arithmetic on a declared `void *` to `char *`. Two sweeps over the unmatched set: the first banked **69** (50 a `->unkN->unkM` chain, 11 a member access on a call result, 7 `void *` arithmetic, 1 a slot call), the second caught the bare `arg0 + 0x3E8` and the dot-member call and banked **4** more (all `void *` arithmetic). Tier **4,548 -> 3,913 compile / 6,265 -> 6,827 mismatch (838 within 3 instructions)**. `make check` 5,997/16,857 (35.6 %) 0 mismatching, `make link` byte-identical. Finding #320's "mechanical fixes yield 0 matches" does NOT hold when the fix is a CORRECTNESS bug rather than a spelling: a follow-up that only re-typed the loads (`void *temp = *(int *)…` -> `*(void **)…`) made 83/150 compile failures comparable and matched 0.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f349"></a>
### #349 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00270db0 — tools/m2c_one.py asm_for %hi/%lo symbolizer

THE %lo SYMBOLIZER PAIRED A lui WITH ONLY ITS FIRST USE, SO A SECOND USE AFTER A BRANCH KEPT A BARE DISPLACEMENT AND m2c ADDED IT ON TOP OF THE NAMED BASE — an address that was too low, and no C could match it. A register loaded by `lui $r,HIGH` keeps that high half until it is written again, and **every** load/store that uses it completes the `%lo` of the object being addressed. The pass searched only a 4-instruction window and stopped at the first use, so FUN_00270db0's `sw $a0,-28036($v0)` at 0x270dcc — the same DAT_004a927c, reached across the `bnez` — stayed numeric; m2c then emitted `DAT_004a927c.unk-6D84`, 0x6D84 below the real 0x4a927c. A detector over the whole unmatched set found such a use in **130 of 10,740 functions (1.2 %)**. The pass now tracks `hi_by_reg` for the register's lifetime: each later base use completes a `%lo`, and any write to the register clears it. Two ordering details fell out while validating it: the first-use search has to recognise the `addiu $dst,$dst,lo` completion **before** treating that same instruction's write as a kill (testing the write first left every jump-table base numeric — 133 functions became m2c "unable to determine jump table" failures), and it has to tolerate `addu $dst,$dst,$idx` between the `lui` and the `lw %lo($dst)` (the classic indexed jump-table form; 13 more). With both, m2c failures are back to 0 new, the missed- detector reads 0, and the fix banks **18 functions** (5,997 -> 6,015 / 35.7 %), all previous compile failures. It also exposes a latent crash in the vendored m2c — `fold_shift_bgez` computes `1 << (31 - shift)` with no bounds and raises `ValueError: negative shift count` on a `bltz` whose operand shifts by 32+ — so `tools/m2c_compat.py` guards that fold in memory (m2c is fetched, not committed, so the fix cannot live in its source); without it the naming change alone turned 133 working functions into aborts.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_one.py

<a id="f350"></a>
### #350 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001c59e0 — tools/m2c_batch.py --jobs / verify.py caches

THE BATCH SWEEP IS PARALLEL NOW — `m2c_batch.py --jobs N` — 4x on 8 threads with byte-identical worklists. It is not CPU-serial: the work is mostly `wibo`+`mwccps2` subprocesses, which release the GIL, so a ThreadPoolExecutor scales it (16 threads gave no more than 8). Two unobvious things had to be fixed first. (1) **A stdout race**: `m2c.main` is not thread-safe, and the capture used `contextlib.redirect_stdout`, which rebinds the *global* `sys.stdout` — a progress `print` from the aggregating thread landed inside a worker's StringIO and was spliced into the m2c draft, so 1-2 rows out of ~1,100 flipped to `compile` non-deterministically. A thread-local `sys.stdout` proxy (`_ThreadStdout`, with `_capture_stdout`) makes each worker's capture private; m2c itself stays serialised by `_M2C_LOCK`. (2) **The GIL-bound half dominated**: `verify_one` re-read and re-parsed the 4.5 MB ELF, the 16k-line config/SLUS-20836/symbols.txt and config/SLUS-20836/jumptables.txt on every call (up to 3 per candidate), which is why a naive thread pool only reached 1.5x. `functools.lru_cache` on `read_elf`/`load_symbols`/`load_jumptables` cut the sequential sweep itself and took the parallel one to **4.0x** (3,000 candidates: 149 s -> 37 s, identical worklist at jobs 1/8/16, repeated). `verify.TMP_TAG` also had to become a function (the module constant froze the importing thread's id, so every worker wrote the same scratch path). No worklist changes: `map` preserves candidate order and a worker only returns, never appends.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f351"></a>
### #351 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00112ca0 — tools/m2c_batch.py rewrite_members

m2c SPELLS A NEGATIVE MEMBER OFFSET `EXPR->unk-8` / `EXPR.unk-8` (a field before the base, e.g. a container-of access), and the member scan required a hex digit immediately after `unk`, so those accesses were never rewritten and stayed uncompilable C. 634 of the 10,722 unmatched functions' raw drafts contained at least one `unk-N`; for ~24 of them it was the sole blocking error (most carry a second one, which is why the compile tier only fell by that much). The scan now admits an optional sign and the rewrite emits `*(int *)((char *)(base) - N)` (and `... &(base) - N` for a value member), so the machine's `lw r,-8(base)` is spelled correctly. Measured: 1 new match (FUN_0022a740), compile tier 3,866 -> 3,842, mismatch 6,856 -> 6,879, tree 6,015 -> 6,016 / 16,857 (35.7 %). Kept as a correctness fix with a small yield, the same shape as finding #348 but narrower.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f352"></a>
### #352 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x0055c870 — igBinary hdr[4] object-data region (T2 step 3)

T2 FIRST LOOK AT hdr[4] — AN ALIGNMENT LEAD, THE OBJECT ENCODING IS STILL UNREAD. NDMW.AFS 0x6300 pack (file 0x1055b000, hdr[4]=0xf86bc): the region is u32-aligned and opens with a small-integer run — 1, 33119, 23, 20, 1, 1, 32560, 134, 20, 3, 3, 32888, 18, 40, 32602, 225, 0, 0, 0x00010001, 0xffffffff x3 — and then a second, differently-shaped run at +0x58 (22, 20, 1, 1, 33086, 135, 20, 5, 5, 32896, 24, 100, 4, 0, 0xffffffff, 2, 32830, 0x3f800000 …). IEEE-754 floats really are present (0x3f800000 = 1.0f), which is consistent with the vec3f/vec4f reflection fields, but no record boundary, type index or field count is identified yet, and the 0x7f30..0x815f values are unexplained. AREA.AFS 0x12a8 pack (file 0x72ed000, hdr[4]=0x34420): **the u32 stream is one byte late**. Read at hdr[4], the words are 0x140a00, 0x1500, 0x1400, 0x100, … (every value is the real one shifted left by 8 bits, and 0xffffff00 stands where 0xffffffff belongs); read at hdr[4]+1 they are the documented 21, 20, 1, 1, 4404, 21, 20, 0, 0, 0xffffffff, … So either hdr[4] points at a 1-byte tag that precedes the stream, or the AREA object data is not u32-aligned to the blob — the earlier note "begins with a repeating {21,20,…} run" is exactly the +1 reading and only that shift reproduces it. Next: dump both regions with the AREA +1 applied, look for a repeated record header whose first field is a small type index (<135, the 0x6300 directory's record count) and whose stride matches a class's declared size from data/reflection.json, and correlate with the hdr[5] index table.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

### #354 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00371c70 — src/ translation units + per-function matching build

SRC/ IS TRANSLATION UNITS NOW, AND THE BUILD STILL MATCHES PER FUNCTION. The repo source was one file per function (a pipeline artifact); it is now organised the way the game was built, and the build no longer keys on the filename. Two new tools: tools/units.py scans a file for its top-level definitions and can emit the file with all but one blanked out (includes/prototypes/externs survive), which is how check.py, linkgen.py and verify.py compile exactly one function at a time; tools/merge_units.py folds per-function files into a unit in retail address order, hoists the declarations every user agrees on, and keeps a symbol two functions declare differently (m2c's int[4] vs char[] for DAT_0050dde8) inside each user as a block-scope extern, since a declaration has no effect on codegen. Pilot: the 116 Lua real implementations are one contiguous run 0x371c70..0x375d90 (every gap <=16 bytes of alignment padding -- contiguity in the binary is what identifies the original file), folded into src/lua/api.cpp (47 functions) plus three C++-mode siblings. make check 6,016/16,857 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. progress.py had to count definitions, not filenames, or the 47 functions would have counted as one.

**Evidence:** src/lua/api.cpp

<a id="f355"></a>
### #355 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003a4910 — src/ module placement of the named vtable units

THE 813 FLAT NAMED UNITS ARE PLACED AND FOLDED INTO MODULES. Every named unit sat in src/ root because its name carried no folder; the namespace does. tools/place_named.py groups them by class and folds each group with tools/merge_units.py: 813 files -> 508 units under src/game (483), src/movie (11), src/audio (8), src/engine (5), src/menu (1), e.g. Gap__Bec__beCameraMode__vfunc_08 + __20 + __23 + __24 + __25 + __26 + __27 -> src/game/beCameraMode.c, and Gap::Core::igObject's 40 vfuncs -> src/engine/igObject.c. A class whose methods need both C mode and C++ mode keeps two files: a C-mode method does NOT match compiled as C++ (measured 0 of 25 mixed classes), so the split is real. Three merge/compile gotchas found and fixed: (1).cpp units wrap their prototypes in an `extern "C" {... }` block, and the line-based merge localized the braces into a function body -- merge now treats a balanced brace block as ONE hoisted chunk; (2) a file that is only a provenance comment plus the function had that comment inside the definition span, so the merge duplicated it -- the span now starts at the declaration, not at the comment; (3) MWCC refuses a leading-dot filename (it reads the extension from the first dot: 'unrecognized file contents or filename extension') and truncates paths past ~80 chars, so the one-function split is now dw4u_\<pid>_\<n>.c in the unit's own directory. make check 6,016/16,857 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL.

**Evidence:** tools/place_named.py

THE REFLECTION REGISTRARS AND FIELD INITIALISERS ARE NAMED -- 1,736 functions, named coverage 1,496 -> 3,232. data/reflection.json records, for all 1,075 MetaObjects, the class name plus register_site (the address of the `jal igMetaObject_RegisterClass` inside that class's static-init registrar) and post_init (the function that adds the class's fields). BUG FOUND AND FIXED: split.py merged the overlay inside load_functions, i.e. BEFORE promote_gap_labels -- but 592 of these registrars are not in data/functions.json at all (Ghidra never promoted them; they are the #345 gap labels). RESOLVED -- THE +0x5c READER IS THE REFLECTION STRUCT ITSELF. data/reflection.json's `igMetaObject` class (25 named fields) names every argument of the registrar: t0 -> `+0x54 _getMetaObjectFunction` (so v54 IS the accessor, exactly as named), t2 -> `+0x48 _sizeofSize`, t1 -> `+0x1c _name`, t3 -> `+0x5c _vTablePointer`. ### #360 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002e0200 — class hierarchy from the reflection registrars + method names from assert strings

THE CLASS HIERARCHY IS RECOVERED, AND THE BINARY'S ASSERT STRINGS YIELD REAL METHOD NAMES. (1) HIERARCHY: the RTTI `+4` word points at igObject for 578 of 579 records, so it carries no immediate-base information (docs/engine/rtti-and-vtables.md). The reflection registrars do: the a3 (fn2) argument returns the BASE class's MetaObject and is fed straight to igMetaObject_InheritFromBase. fn2(X) is a small getter (`lui at,hi; lw v0,lo(at)`, sometimes behind a `j` thunk), so the handle it returns maps back to a class through data/reflection.json. That resolves 1,074 of 1,075 classes (only igObject, the root, has none); max depth 6; scripts/gen_class_hierarchy.py writes data/class-hierarchy.tsv. Examples: igViewportAttr -> igVisualAttribute -> igAttr, igObjectList -> igDataList, beCameraMode -> igViewMode. (2) IT DOES NOT RESOLVE THE 47 AMBIGUOUS VFUNCS: computing the lowest common ancestor of each shared default's referring classes gives igObject for all 47, because the true owners are template instantiation bases (igTObjectList<...>, igTDataList<...>) which have an RTTI record but NO MetaObject, so they are absent from the reflection hierarchy too. The slot naming is therefore as precise as the data allows; the ambiguity is real, not a tooling gap. (3) ASSERT STRINGS ARE THE ONLY PLACE A METHOD NAME SURVIVES: strings like `igObject::release`, `igArenaMemoryPool::checkHeap(E70)` and `igLuaState::runFile(%s) failed` have a code xref from the function that logs them, so the containing function IS that method. 17 functions matched; 15 are named in config/SLUS-20836/names/Methods.tsv (igLuaState_RunString/RunFile/RunBuffer, igObject_Release, igIGBFile_ReadFile, igArenaMemoryPool_DisplayOutstandingAllocationsBlock/CheckHeap/Trim/TrimArenaBlock/SetPoolOptionFlags/MemoryOperation, igMallocMemoryPool_Dump, igPageMemoryPool_MemoryOperation/GetBlockSize, igMovieRenderer_InitializeDrawing). Two are left unnamed on purpose: igIGBFile::readFile and igArenaMemoryPool::checkHeap each have two functions referencing the same assert (0x2e25a0 vs 0x2e95e0, 0x3125d0 vs 0x312790) and picking one would be a guess. This is why semantic method naming is bounded: the binary is stripped of names except in these asserts. make check 6,016/16,857 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL.

**Evidence:** config/SLUS-20836/names/Methods.tsv

That builder creates the instance itself -- FUN_002befe0 = igMetaObject_CreateInstance(uRam00585d60 = the igMetaEnum handle) after RunStaticInit(igMetaEnum_Register 0x2bf1a0) -- then interns param_1 as _name and fills _names (an igStringRefList, class handle 0x5858d8, via FUN_002b90e0) and _values (an igIntList, 0x586880, via FUN_002caa70) from the two input arrays. So 0x2fbc70 is the factory every lazily-initialised enum accessor calls: e.g. FUN_00131f80 is 'if (!*(0x56ed40)) *(0x56ed40) = igMetaEnum_Create(0x4d4d08, 0x485bf0, 0x485c00, 3);', which builds the 'ApplyType' enum from the rodata {NOT_SET=0, CUBE_MAP=1, SPHERE_MAP=2}. The two string helpers take the enum last: 0x312020 appends the name of every value whose bit is set in a mask, 0x312140 copies one value's name into a size-bounded buffer (both used by the debug printers 0x3121c0/0x3245f0). One correction to #363: the mask joiner is 0x4f9950 = a single blank ' ', not '|'. (2) THE +0xc8/+0xcc SLOTS ARE WriteRawFieldMem / ReadRawFieldMem, AND THE DIRECTION IS NOW SETTLED. The argument order is identical for both -- (field, field_memory=param_2, stream=param_3) -- and only the copy direction differs: the base +0xc8 body 0x2fc830 is memcpy(param_3, param_2, _size) (field -> stream) and the base +0xcc body 0x2fc7c0 is memcpy(param_2, param_3, _size) (stream -> field). The engine's own name survives in the +0xc8 override 0x305620's log format 0x4fafd0/0x4fb050: '%s->%s(%s)->writeRawFieldMem0x%x[0] = 0x%x,Not in directory\nDid you modify the object after adding it to the directory ?' -- and that override does exactly a pointer->directory-index conversion (FUN_002d7c50, writing 0xffffffff when the ref is not in the output directory), which is why the message is a write-side assert. The two array-class overrides loop the same operation over _num four-byte slots (0x2fa800/0x2fa760); 0x2fa700 (the array's +0xd0) is left unnamed -- it calls the global field 0x585d88's own +0xd0 with elemSize*_num, which does not agree with the base body's fixed _size and needs its own investigation. (3) THE _resetMethod PUZZLE IS CONFIRMED, NOT RESOLVED. igMemoryRefMetaField_Init 0x2fb930 really does store 3 at +0x24 _resetMethod, and igMetaField_Reset 0x2fc500 really does dispatch 0 -> +0x70, 3 -> +0x74, 2 -> +0x78; the ref class's +0x74 is the base 'jr ra' no-op 0x2fc5c0, so Reset leaves a memory-ref value alone and the refcounted +0x70 SetToDefault 0x2facc0 (which honours _releaseOnReset +0x41) must be reached by a direct +0x70 dispatch. That caller is still unidentified: 0x2facc0 has no direct jal anywhere, so it is one of the ~123 vtable[0x70] dispatch sites. The candidate is the per-field object initialiser; FUN_0030d970 is the only direct caller of the base +0x70 body 0x2fc580 but it reaches it through its own +0x38/+0x3c function-pointer pair, not a MetaField vtable. (4) THE GAP-BODIES ARE STILL UNNAMEABLE: 0x2fa8b0/0x2fa9d0 (+0x64), 0x2fd170/0x2fd1e0 (+0xd0/+0xd4), 0x2fc2a0/0x2fc2b0, 0x2fd070/0x2fd080, 0x2fc9d0-0x2fca40 and the 0x3054d0-0x305ce0 igRefMetaField virtuals are all LAB_ labels with no function object -- they are leaf stubs with no frame prologue, so tools/split.py's promote_gap_labels (which requires addiu sp,sp,-N and size >= 16) does not reach them. ### #365 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002fc2b0 — igMetaField_GetSize

THE VTABLE-REFERENCED LEAF LABELS ARE FUNCTIONS -- +1,804 PROMOTED (16,857 -> 18,661), AND THE OBJECT MODEL'S +0x64 IS GetSize. (1) THE RULE. promote_gap_labels recognises a function by its frame (addiu sp,sp,-N), which every non-leaf function has and no leaf one does, so a whole class of real code stayed behind: the leaf stubs the class vtables are full of -- 8-byte jr ra; li v0,N getters, 8-byte jr ra; nop defaults, lui at,hi; jr ra; lw v0,lo(at) MetaObject accessors, and 12-byte bodies that tail-call a shared implementation with j. Ghidra labels each LAB_ but never makes it a function because nothing *branches* into it; only a vtable holds its address, and Ghidra does not follow a data reference. The evidence is mechanical and needs no prologue: the label sits in an unclaimed gap, its address appears as a 4-byte word in the image (so something holds it as a code pointer), the preceding word is padding or a jr ra/its delay slot (so it is not fallthrough), and the bytes from it form a leaf body -- EXACTLY ONE terminating j/jr (a jr of any register, so jr t9 tail calls count too, as does a plain j), never a prologue anywhere in the body. Its size runs to that terminator's delay slot, and the bound is the next *plausible start* rather than the next label of any kind, because a function with an internal loop carries branch-target labels of its own and those must not cut its body short (that is what was hiding igMetaField's +0xd0 byte-swap 0x2fd170). tools/split.py gained promote_data_labels (second pass, after promote_gap_labels and after extend_jump_table_bodies, so jump-table case bodies are already claimed). MEASURED: 1,804 promoted, 42,872 bytes, 16,857 -> 18,661 functions; verified 1,804 added / 0 removed / 0 changed / 0 overlaps with an old function or with one another, so every claimed byte was already linked as raw data. make check split round-trips 18,661/18,661 and keeps 6,016 matched C byte-for-byte; make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. The coverage percentage therefore FALLS (35.7% -> 32.2%) because the denominator grew by real functions; the matched count is unchanged. (2) THE NAMES THIS UNBLOCKED -- +8, 4,800 -> 4,808 (25.8% of 18,661). The decisive one is the +0x64 slot: the field constructor 0x2fc630 computes _size (+0x14) as `*(u16*)(field+0x14) = vtable[0x64]` (asm: lw t9,100(t9); jalr; sh v0,16(s0)), and igMetaField_AllocateArray 0x2fb190 divides an allocation by the same slot read from the memory type (_memType at +0x3c), so +0x64 is the field's byte size: the base 0x2fc2b0 returns 0, igRefMetaField 0x3054f0 and igMemoryRefMetaField 0x2fa9d0 return 4 (one pointer), igMemoryRefArrayMetaField 0x2fa8b0 returns (_num*4) & 0xffff (one pointer per inline element) -- all named GetSize. The ref class's +0x84 override 0x305500 corroborates it: memset(instance + _offset, 0, GetSize). Also named: igMetaField_SetToDefault 0x2fc580 (the base +0x70 body the ref class overrides, memcpy of _default +0x20 into the value); igMetaField_SwapBytes 0x2fd170 (the +0xd0 technique byte-reverses each _size-byte element of a buffer -- for each element swap byte i with byte _size-1-i -- the endian conversion WriteRawFieldMem/ReadRawFieldMem run on the destination after copying) with its in-place form igMetaField_SwapValue 0x2fd1e0 (vtable[+0xd0]\(field, instance + _offset, 1)); and igRefMetaField_ReadRawFieldMem 0x305580 (igRefMetaField's +0xcc override -- the same index->pointer resolution as igMemoryRefMetaField_ReadRawFieldMem 0x2fb690 without the _memType learning). (3) LEFT NAMED-BUT-UNCALLED, deliberately: the +0x6c slot (base 0x2fc2a0 returns -1, igRefMetaField 0x305ce0 returns 4) is NOT the alignment the memory-type's +0x6c is in AllocateArray -- a caller in the pool region (0x2f0bc0) uses its result as a predicate, so the analogy fails and it stays unnamed; likewise the +0x84/+0x88 base no-ops (the ref overrides 0x305500/0x305540 zero the value), the +0x90/+0x94/+0x98 three identical memcpy technique bodies selected by igMetaField_CopyByTechnique 0x2fc8e0 (0 -> +0x90, 1 -> +0x94, 2 -> +0x98, 3 -> +0x9c = Reset, 4 -> +0xa0 = no-op), the +0xb0..+0xc0 compare family (igRefMetaField's +0xb0 0x305bd0 returns *(a2+off) - *(a1+off)), the +0xd8..+0xf0 block, the igRefMetaField +0xf0 0x305cd0 (returns +0x34 _construct), and the +0xe0/+0xe4 0x3054d0/0x3054e0 (return 0 / clear 4 bytes) -- all trivial bodies whose slot roles are not yet pinned. make check 6,016/18,661 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL.

<a id="f366"></a>
### #366 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00130060 — igVisualContextAttrDefaultManager_GetMetaObject

THE REGISTRAR'S MetaObject POINTER IS SOMETIMES A `j` THUNK, NOT THE ACCESSOR -- 574 more names, 4,808 -> 5,382 (28.8 %). (1) THE FINDING. `igMetaObject_RegisterClass`'s `t0` is stored at `metaobj+0x54` `_getMetaObjectFunction` and is the class's lazy get-or-register accessor -- the body verified here for three unrelated classes: `if (handle == 0 || (handle->flags & 4) == 0) \<Class>_Register; return handle;` (0x130060/igVisualContextAttrDefaultManager reading 0x55ebc0, 0x1311b0/igTextureUnloadAttr reading 0x55ec90, 0x130490/igViewportAttr reading 0x55ebd8; each handle matches data/reflection.json). But for 572 of the 1,075 classes the registrar passes a **two-instruction `j \<accessor>; nop` thunk**, not the accessor itself: e.g. igVisualContextAttrDefaultManager's t0 resolves to LAB_00130250 whose single word is `j 0x130060` + nop, and the accessor it jumps to is a separate function. Because the thunk is a gap label with no symbol, `scripts/seed_names_metaobject.py` -- which names `t0`'s target `\<Class>_GetMetaObject` -- counted it a "gap thunk" and skipped it (finding #358 recorded 592 of them; 2 had meanwhile been promoted and named). The pointer is registered *by immediate*, so `split.py`'s two promotion rules never claimed the thunk (they need a prologue or a data-word reference) and the accessor stayed `FUN_`. (2) THE FIX is in the generator, not the tree: when `t0` has no symbol, decode a `j \<target>; nop` body and use the target. Regenerating gives 485 direct + 572 via-thunk = **1,057 distinct accessor functions** -- and **no target is shared by two classes**, so the generated names cannot collide (the generator asserts uniqueness). 574 overlay entries are new: the 572 resolved accessors plus the 2 (igTimeAttr 0x1310c0, igColorAttr 0x139080) that the leaf promotion (#365) had already made nameable. 18 classes remain unresolved (their t0 is neither a symbol nor a `j` thunk to one). (3) MEASURED. `tools/apply_names.py --apply --check`: 574 renames, 557 units rewritten, **550 units moved** out of `src/unmatched/` into `src/engine/` (the re-sweep had banked them), 574 stale asm units pruned. Named coverage **4,808 -> 5,382 / 18,661 (28.8 %)**; the matched set is unchanged at 7,381 (4,391 address-named + 2,990 named), and the named-but-no-C queue goes 2,368 -> 2,392. `make check` 18,661/18,661 round-trip and 7,381 matched 0 mismatching; `make link` BYTE-IDENTICAL. This is the accessor half of the family #358 opened; the 572 thunks themselves are still unclaimed gap bytes (8-byte `j \<accessor>; nop`), so they have no symbol to name -- promoting them (a `j`+nop rule) is the next step if their bytes are ever wanted as units.

**Notes:** Follow-up (#389): the 572 thunks themselves are now promoted and named thunk_\<accessor> (613 j-thunk stubs total, 18,664 -> 19,277), and the generator resolves t0 from the bytes so it is idempotent.

**Where:** 0x002fd050 — igMetaField_ConstructValue

THE igMetaField SLOT BODIES WHOSE SUBCLASS OVERRIDE IS ALREADY NAMED, PLUS THE SLOT MAP THE REST IS BLOCKED ON -- 8 more names, 5,382 -> 5,390 (28.9 %), and the reason ~25 slot bodies cannot be named yet. (1) THE PRINCIPLE. A virtual is identified by its *slot*, not by its body: if one class's entry at `+0xNN` is already named, the same slot in another class's vtable is the same virtual, so the base body takes the base prefix. The sweep banked most of these bodies (they are tiny), so naming them also moves matched units out of `src/unmatched/`. NAMED: `igMetaField_ConstructValue` 0x2fd050 and `igMetaField_DestructValue` 0x2fd060 (base `jr ra; nop` no-ops at +0x7c/+0x80 whose `igMemoryRefMetaField_ConstructValue`/`DestructValue` overrides 0x2faa10/0x2faae0 do the real work), `igMetaField_ReleaseElements` 0x2fd040 (base no-op at +0xa8, ref override named), `igMetaField_Write` 0x2fcea0 (+0xac -- the base returns 0 when `_persistent` and -1 otherwise, the body #363 decoded; the ref class replaces it with the IGB writer 0x2fb3d0), `igMetaField_IsConstruct` 0x2fd230 and `igRefMetaField_IsConstruct` 0x305cd0 (+0xf0: the base returns 0, the ref class returns its `_construct` at +0x34, which reflection.json declares -- so the virtual is the construct flag), and the destructor-slot thunks `igRefMetaField_Destruct` 0x305cc0 (`j igMetaField_Destruct`) and `igMemoryRefMetaField_Destruct` 0x2fba20 (`j 0x305cc0`), which are the +0x08 entries of those classes' own vtables. `apply_names --apply --check`: 8 renames, 10 units rewritten, 7 moved out of `src/unmatched/`; `make check` 7,381/18,661 0 mismatching, `make link` BYTE-IDENTICAL. Named coverage 5,382 -> 5,390; address-named matched 4,391 -> 4,384. (2) WHY THE REST OF THE SLOT BODIES IS NOT NAMEABLE THIS WAY, recorded so it is not re-investigated. (a) THE METHOD NAMES ARE TAKEN BY THE DISPATCH THUNKS. The copy and alike slots cannot take their method names because `igMetaField_DeepCopy` (0x2fc9b0), `igMetaField_ShallowCopy` (0x2fc9a0), `igMetaField_ConstructCopy` (0x2fc9c0), `igMetaField_IsAlikeExactly/Deep/Shallow` (0x2fcf60/70/80) are already the *entry-point thunks* that load the method number and jump to the dispatcher (igMetaField_CopyByTechnique 0x2fc8e0 / IsAlikeByTechnique 0x2fcec0). The slots they select are technique slots, not methods: +0x90/0x94/0x98 (three byte-identical memcpys `memcpy(dst+_offset, src+_offset, _size)`, 0x2fc9d0/0x2fca10/0x2fc9f0), +0x9c (`j igMetaField_Reset`, 0x2fca30), +0xa0 (no-op, 0x2fca40); and +0xb4 (byte compare over `_size`, 0x2fcf90), +0xb8/+0xbc (both tail-call +0xb4, 0x2fcfe0/0x2fcff0), +0xc0 (returns 1, 0x2fd000). Naming them by technique (`igMetaField_CopyByTechnique_0`...) would be accurate but mechanical, and would contradict the ref class's technique-0/2 overrides that ARE named `DeepCopy`/`ShallowCopy` -- so they stay FUN_ and are recorded here. (b) TWO SLOTS SHARE ONE BODY AND ROLE: the base `+0x84`/`+0x88` (0x2fd070/0x2fd080) are no-ops and the ref/array overrides (0x305500/0x305540) are *identical* `memset(instance + _offset, 0, GetSize)` bodies, so which is which cannot be recovered. (c) NO NAMED COUNTERPART: `+0x6c` (base 0x2fc2a0 returns -1, refs 0x305ce0 return 4 -- a caller in the pool region at 0x2f0bc0 uses its result as a *predicate*, so the memory-type "alignment" analogy fails), `+0x60` 0x2fd130 (`memcpy(arg2 + _offset, arg3, _size)` -- installs a value into the instance; no override names it), `+0x5c` 0x2fc620 (returns the global 0x587190, which the field constructor 0x2fc630 mines for `_typeIndex` at +0x12), `+0xc4` 0x2fc8a0 (returns `(_size + 3) & ~3` iff `_persistent && !_isRunTime`, else 0 -- a "persistent size", but no override names it), `+0xd8`..`+0xec` (0x2fcdb0 dispatches to +0xe0 when the value is non-null; 0x2fcdf0 forwards to another object's +0xe4; 0x2fce10 tail-calls 0x12a78c with the value; 0x2fce20 is a frame body; 0x2fca80 concatenates the string elements and 0x2fcce0 sums their sizes via `GetSize` +0x68 -- so +0xe8/+0xec are array loops over +0xe4/+0xe0, but the +0xe0/+0xe4 direction is not pinned). (d) THE SHARED DEFAULTS (T7/#283): igMetaObject's +0x98 (0x142a60) and +0xa8 (0x13c180) are two of the 47 bodies shared by 70 vtables whose owner is a template base with no emitted vtable -- the decompiler's own comment says "left unnamed on purpose: any name here would duplicate the widest group's". (e) THE PER-CLASS OVERRIDES CANNOT BE ENUMERATED SAFELY YET: a script that reads a class's vtable out of its `\<Class>_GetVTable` body picks up the *base* class's vtable literal (igVec3fMetaField_GetVTable yields 0x52f230, igMetaField's), so an automatic `\<Class>_\<method>` generator over the 47 MetaField classes would mis-attribute; that is the real obstacle to the ~25 orphan overrides (e.g. igTrackedElementMetaField's +0x64/+0x70/+0xa4/+0xc8/+0xcc/+0xd0/+0xd4).
