### #368 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0018e460 — igVec3fMetaField_GetSize

A VERIFIED CLASS-VTABLE EXTRACTOR, AND THE 22 MetaField SUBCLASS OVERRIDES IT NAMES FROM THE SLOT THE ROOT CLASS ALREADY NAMES -- 5,390 -> 5,412 (29.0 %). THE RULE: `igMetaField` is an abstract reflection type, so a field type (`igVec3fMetaField`, `igLongArrayMetaField`,...) overrides the slots whose behaviour depends on its element type. Its vtable entry at the same slot as a named `igMetaField` method IS that method's override, so it is named `\<Class>_\<Method>`; nothing is read from the body. NAMED (generator `scripts/seed_names_metafield_slots.py` -> `config/SLUS-20836/names/MetaFieldSlots.tsv`, 22 names over 13 classes): GetSize 11 (`igVec2f` 0x18e2e0, `igVec3f` 0x18e460, `igVec4f` 0x18e660, `igVec4fArray` 0x18e4f0, `igMatrix44f` 0x18c630, `igLong` 0x2ebbf0, `igLongArray` 0x2ebb10, `igFloatArray` 0x2e36b0, `igDoubleArray` 0x2d9410, `igUnsignedChar` 0x30e740, `igUnsignedCharArray` 0x30e650), SwapBytes 10, and `igVirtualCFuncMetaField_SetToDefault` 0x30ef90. apply_names --apply --check: 22 renames, 11 units rewritten and 11 moved out of src/unmatched/; make check 7,381/18,661 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. INDEPENDENT CHECK THAT THE RULE IS SOUND, and the reason to trust the other 20: the generated GetSize bodies return exactly the element sizes their class names imply -- `igVec2fMetaField` `li v0,8`, `igVec3fMetaField` `li v0,12`, `igVec4fMetaField` `li v0,16`, `igMatrix44fMetaField` `li v0,64`, `igLongMetaField` `li v0,8`, and the array types `(_num << 4) & 0xffff` (Vec4fArray) / `(_num << 3) & 0xffff` (LongArray) = `_num * elemSize`. THE EXTRACTOR (the asset here) reads each class's vtable out of its `\<Class>_GetVTable` function, which builds a temporary and stores the class's vtable as its first word. THREE MEASURED TRAPS, each of which silently mis-attributed before being fixed: (1) the tempting shortcut -- the first `lui`/`addiu` pair whose result is in the vtable range -- fails because the pair is often split by other instructions (igTimeAttr_GetVTable) and because every body ALSO materialises the base class's vtable for an identity test, which attributed 26 classes to igMetaField's own vtable; the fix is to take the first rodata-range value actually stored to a stack slot, with registers tracked (980 of 983 classes resolve; 3 materialise the pointer from a global). (2) counting matching slots to test descent fails because the embedded igObject interface block (+0x08..+0x58) sits at the same offsets in every igObject subclass, so all 980 vtables "matched" and beDemoManager was offered a `_DestructValue`; the fix is to count only the 18 named MetaField slots, where the histogram is cleanly bimodal (25 classes at 16-18, every other class at <=4). (3) a body used by more than one class's vtable cannot be attributed (the T7 shared-default problem) and is skipped -- ten classes (igIntMetaField, igBoolMetaField,...) share igMetaField's vtable whole, and aliased class groups (beCameraMode/igPickMode/igViewMode on 0x542960, igTransform/beTransformSync/... on 0x5284f0) are excluded by trap 2. WHAT REMAINS IN THE OBJECT MODEL, measured: of the 980 extractable class vtables, 478 classes already carry RTTI-derived vfunc names and 512 have none; the ref-family roots name too few slots to reach their descendants (igRefMetaField names 4, and no class shares them), which is why this batch is 22 and not more. The extractor itself is reusable for anything that needs class -> vtable -> slot body (resolving indirect calls in decompiled code, or attributing future slot work).

**Evidence:** scripts/seed_names_metafield_slots.py

<a id="f369"></a>
### #369 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00100008 — m2c_errors.py

THE 3,913-FUNCTION COMPILE TIER IS DOMINATED BY ONE TYPE-MODEL GAP, NOT BY 3,913 SEPARATE PROBLEMS -- so `--context` / the recovered type model is the M7 lever, and it is now measured rather than assumed. build/errors_compile_tier_final.log (the m2c_errors.py summary over the compile tier) reports 162 distinct messages over 3,913 failures, and the head of that distribution is entirely int-vs-pointer typing: `illegal implicit conversion from 'int' to 'void *'` 1,826 messages / 292 functions, `'int' to 'void **'` 339/228, `'char *' to 'int *'` 291/208, `'int *' to 'int'` 199/212, `'int' to 'int *'` 125/240, then `'void *' to 'int'`, `'int (*)(...)' to 'int'`, `'int' to 'int (*)(int)'` and the rest of that family -- roughly 3,300 of the messages are the same thing: m2c emitted `int` for a value the binary uses as a pointer, because the struct field or argument has no type. The genuinely different causes are small: `undefined identifier 'bitwise'` 23, `'sp0'` 91, `'sp4'` 22, `'s128'` 45 (stack-slot naming), `';' expected` 19, `not a struct/union/class` 55 + 34. WHY THIS MATTERS FOR THE MATCHED COUNT: the compile tier holds 3,933 functions, 698 of them already NAMED, and a named function that compiles is a function whose C can be banked -- the named-but-no-C queue is 2,404, of which 698 are in this tier (1,593 are in mismatch, 74 sdk, 0 already matching). Supplying field and argument types (data/reflection.json's structs, the igObject/ObjectModel field maps, the #367/#368 vtable slot signatures) is a single systematic change that addresses the top bucket rather than one function at a time; `bitwise`/`sp0`/`sp4`/`s128` are separate, small, mechanical tool fixes.

**Notes:** Refuted by #370: the buckets were real, the diagnosis was not. The int/pointer errors were not missing parameter types (nothing in the repo records those, and m2c's own recovered types were already declared correctly in the drafts) but clean's blanket rewrites between pointer and int that ignored the lvalue. Fixing them (fix_pointer_types) banks +81 and takes the compile tier 3,913 -> 1,258, so m2c --context was never the lever here -- the cleaner's type coherence was. The measured int-vs-pointer concentration in this row stands and is what pointed at the right place. Folded into #370, the current answer for Compile-tier int/pointer errors come from the cleaner; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** build/errors_compile_tier_final.log

<a id="f370"></a>
### #370 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00277fa0 — fix_pointer_types

THE COMPILE TIER'S `int`/POINTER BUCKETS WERE THE CLEANER'S OWN CASTS, NOT m2c's TYPES -- and fixing them banks +81 and collapses the tier from 3,913 to 1,258 functions. #369 measured that ~3,300 of the compile tier's first errors were `int` to `void *` and friends and read that as a missing type model; the drafts say otherwise. m2c recovers types correctly and DECLARES them: `void *temp_a0;`, `int *temp_a1;`, `void *arg0`, `int (*temp_v1)(int, int, int);`. What disagreed was `clean`, which wrote every unknown-width access as a bare `int` and stripped `void *` from both directions with two blanket rules (`= (void *)` -> `= (int)`, `*(void *)` -> `*(int *)`). Neither looks at the lvalue, so `void *temp_a0_2 = *(int *)((char *)(arg0) + 88);` is `int` -> `void *`, `*(int *)((char *)(arg0) + 0) = &DAT_00532a50[0];` is `int *` -> `int`, `int *temp_a1 = (char *)temp_v1 - 8;` is `char *` -> `int *`, and `DAT_00569238[0] = &FUN_001f4100;` is `int (*)` -> `void *` (DAT_x is arrayized to `void *DAT_x[4]` so its element is a `void *`). THE FIX is one pass, `fix_pointer_types`, at the end of `clean`: it reads the types m2c already declared (pointer locals, function-pointer locals, parameters, `void *DAT_x[4]` element type, callee return types -- and scalars, so it can tell which side of an assignment is which) and then makes each assignment's value cast agree with its target -- a pointer target gets `(T)(expr)`, an `int` target (including the cleaner's own `*(int *)(...)` store) gets `(int)(expr)`. Every rewrite is a cast between a 32-bit pointer and a 32-bit integer, so the instructions cannot change (`lw`/`sw` either way); the 64-bit types are deliberately excluded, because casting a `long long` to a pointer would truncate. MEASURED, over the whole compile tier (11,280 candidates swept, 360 s): 78 matched and were written, the tier fell 3,913 -> 1,357, and the function-pointer extension for `int (*)(...)` targets added 3 more (tier 1,357 -> 1,258, `int` to `int (*)(int)` 68 -> 0). `make check` 7,462/18,661 0 mismatching, `make link` BYTE-IDENTICAL. THE RATE IS THE POINT: 2,655 compile failures became comparable and 81 matched = 3.05 %. `docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md`'s "Measured negatives" table already carried this shape as refuted -- "re-typing a load to match its local, 83 of 150 became comparable, 0 matched" -- and at a 3 % conversion rate a sample that size yields 0 about 8 % of the time, so THAT MEASUREMENT WAS UNDER-POWERED, not wrong: #320's law ("a spelling fix does not match") is a statement about a low rate, not about impossibility, and a 150-row stratifed sample cannot separate 3 % from 0. The whole-tier sweep can. (Caveat: the pass is broader than that one shape -- it also fixes stores, pointer arithmetic and function-pointer targets -- so the +81 is the pass's combined yield, not that shape's alone.) WHAT IS LEFT in the 1,258, re-bucketed with m2c_errors.py (144 distinct messages): `illegal type` 235, `pointer/array required` 206+19, `illegal operand` 141, `undefined identifier 'sp0'` 116 and `'sp4'` 33, `'s128'` 62, `int` to `void *` 54, `'bitwise'` 27. Two of these are now understood and are NOT a missing declaration: `sp0`/`sp4` are m2c's names for STACK SLOTS in the frame (`FUN_00277fa0`'s retail body is `move a1,sp; jal; lw v0,0(sp)`, so m2c's `sp` is the frame base used as a value and `sp0` the word at frame offset 0 -- declaring `int sp0;` alone cannot place it, the frame has to be modelled), and `s128` (62) is m2c's 128-bit SIMD TYPE, not a variable: it appears only as a cast, `(s128) *(int *)(...)`, for the `lq`/`sq` MMI values. The `bitwise` bucket is m2c's pseudo-operator for an instruction it could not model. MMI 64-bit/128-bit modelling is the next real block; it is not a typing pass.

**Notes:** Topic head for Compile-tier int/pointer errors come from the cleaner: also read #369 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f371"></a>
### #371 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00128598 — declare_simd_types

THE 128-BIT `s128` TYPE IS FIXABLE BUT BANKS NOTHING, AND IT IS NOT THE MMI BLOCK -- both spellings of m2c's 128-bit SIMD type were implemented and measured over the whole compile tier, and neither matched a single function. WHAT m2c EMITS AND WHY IT HURTS MORE THAN IT LOOKS: `s128` appears as a declaration (`s128 spF0;`), a pointee (`s128 *p;`), an arrayized global (`extern s128 DAT_x[4];`) and a scalar cast (`(int)((s128) *(int *)(X))`), over 94 candidate drafts. An *undefined* type name is worse than a wrong one: MWCC's C parser recovers so badly that every declaration after it in the same scope is reported too, so FUN_001ebc10's `s128 spF0;` produced undefined-identifier errors for a dozen plainly-declared locals that followed it (`spCC`, `spC8`, `spC4`,...). That cascade is why the tier's `undefined identifier` buckets (sp0 116 + sp4 33 + s128 62 + more) looked like missing declarations. WHAT MWCC CAN DO (measured with the real compiler, not assumed): there is no 128-bit *scalar* -- `(s128)x` from an int is `illegal explicit conversion from 'int'` -- but a 16-byte struct is the type MWCC does have: `typedef struct { unsigned long long lo, hi; } s128;` satisfies `sizeof(s128) == 16` (checked with a negative-array-size constraint), accepts `s128 *p`, accepts struct assignment, and **a struct copy compiles to `lq a1,0(v1)` / `sq a1,0(a2)`** -- the MMI sequence the code is made of. C99 compound literals (`(s128){x,0}`) also compile, so a scalar cast has a spelling. THE TWO VARIANTS, MEASURED with `m2c_batch.py` aimed at the tier (`--only` each of its 1,258 names, no `--write`, 109 s each): (a) `typedef unsigned long long s128;` -- a scalar: every cast m2c emits becomes legal with no rewriting, the tier falls 1,258 -> **1,212** compile, **0 matched**; (b) the 16-byte struct plus the two cast rewrites (a word store `*(int *)((char *)&(X) + 0) = (int)(E)` for `X = (s128) E`, and dropping `(s128)` elsewhere): the tier falls to only **1,235**, **0 matched**. Variant (b) is *worse* for reach because its extra rewriting leaves more drafts failing, so both were reverted -- `tools/m2c_batch.py` is byte-identical to HEAD. THE RETAIL EVIDENCE for the arg-slot shape (`sp40 = (s128) var_a5;`, 11 occurrences) is FUN_00128598, which stores a **word** into that slot (`sw a1,0(sp)`) even though m2c types the slot as a 128-bit quadword -- so m2c's model of the outgoing argument area is a quadword slot holding a word, and the frame layout that follows from it is wrong independently of how `s128` is spelled. CONCLUSION: typing `s128` is not a route to the MMI block. The MMI bytes are in the *accesses* (`lq`/`sq`/`ld`/`sd` over quadword slots and the register pairs m2c splits), and m2c's own 64/128-bit modelling is what has to be right; the doc's existing claim ("`s128` has no MWCC-PS2 spelling that keeps the codegen") is refuted in the narrow sense that a spelling exists (`long long lo, hi` + lq/sq), but not in the sense that matters -- none of them matches.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f372"></a>
### #372 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00128598 — dedupe_switch_labels

THE ILLEGAL-SWITCH-LABEL FAMILY IS TWELVE FUNCTIONS, NOT ELEVEN PERCENT OF THE CODE -- AND THE MEASUREMENT THAT SAID OTHERWISE WAS WRONG TWICE OVER. WHAT IS REAL: m2c reconstructs a switch from the jump table, one table value can appear at several indices, and it emits the label once per index, so drafts carry `case 0: case 0:` and `default:` twice, plus runs of labels m2c leaves behind when it abandons a dispatch (`} else { default: default: case 1: }` with nothing between them). MWCC rejects both (`case constant defined more than once`, `illegal use of keyword`). A cleaner pass was written that merges an *adjacent* repeat (labels with nothing between them name the same position, so dropping the repeat is positional and cannot change what runs) and drops a label that sits outside any `switch` (illegal there, and a label only names a position, so the statements after it are unaffected). MEASURED, over the whole compile tier: the tier falls 1,258 -> 1,246 (twelve functions made comparable) and the full sweep banks **0 matches** (11,199 candidates, 385 s; mismatch 9,759 -> 9,771). Reverted -- tools/m2c_batch.py is byte-identical to HEAD -- because a twelve-row legality fix with no matches does not earn a permanent cleaner rule, and because for the *stray* case the honest reading is that m2c's switch reconstruction is broken (the 16,352-byte NDMW save-interface function that motivated this still mismatches by 3,870 instructions after being made legal), so leaving it as a loud compile failure is better than turning nonsense C into plausible C. THE TWO MEASUREMENT BUGS, which are the durable part of this finding: (1) the population was wrong. The first count (103 drafts with duplicate labels, 370,092 bytes, read as 11.0 % of all code bytes) scanned `build/m2c/*.c` -- but that directory is *scratch*, holding whatever the last `prepare`/`--only` run happened to leave, and in this case the 30 largest compile-tier functions from a previous measurement. Any census taken over `build/m2c/` is a biased sample of whatever was prepared last; measure a tier by preparing the tier (the batch regenerates every draft, and `loop.prepare` returns the cleaned source as `res[6]`, which is what the honest census used, over every 8th of the 1,258 rows). (2) the detector had the same scope bug the implementation later had: `switch (x) {` puts the brace on the keyword's line, so arming the scope *after* walking the braces leaves the switch unopened, every label then looks like it belongs to the previous switch's scope, and labels from different switches are counted as duplicates of each other -- that produced the "1,825 separated repeats over 148 functions, 13.3 % of the code" figure, which is an artifact: with the scope tracker fixed, an unbiased sample of 158 compile-tier functions finds **zero** drafts carrying an illegal label at all. CONCLUSION: the illegal-label family is 12 of 1,258 compile-tier functions (0.96 % of the tier's rows; the byte weight of those twelve is small once the big-function bias is removed), and the compile tier has no remaining systematic family -- its bytes are concentrated in 767 functions at >=256 B (28.5 % of all code) that fail for individual, long-tail reasons.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f373"></a>
### #373 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00521750 — datamap.py

THE TWENTY BIG DATA REGIONS ARE FIVE KNOWN THINGS, NOT PADDING -- the unclassified data is 76 % concentrated in 20 regions (880,360 of 1,162,652 bytes), and identifying them by composition, pointer targets and embedded strings names all five of the biggest. 0x52xxxx 0x521750 (209,840 B) is the VTABLE REGION: 6,613 runs of code pointers, most of length 21-23, each preceded by a `[rtti_pointer, 0]` header -- the same layout as the 478 vtables in data/vtables.json, which came from RTTI records, so this region holds the vtables of the classes that have NO RTTI (my #368 extractor found 980 class vtables). Its first 828 words are not data at all: real code (see #374). 0x4833c8 (321,816 B) is SONY SDK LIBRARY METADATA PLUS EMBEDDED VU1 MICROCODE: 44 % of its words are pointers into.data, 33 % are zero, and its strings are the SDK's own version strings (`PsIIlibcdvd 2800`, `PsIIlibpad 2800`, `PsIIlibsdr 2800`, `PsIIlibmc 2810`) beside VU program metadata (`EXECUTE_PATH;1073741824;;131077;;;;.EXECUTE_RENDERLIST;...`, `vu1_entry_UCODE_BEGIN;0;vu1_entry_EXECUTE_PATH;16;vu1_end_EXECUTE_PATH`) and `Error decoding compressed text`. 0x467108 (110,536 B) is more of the same SDK area -- `PsIIlibgraph 2800`, `PsIIlibdma 2800`, `PsIIlibipu 2800`, `PsIIlibkernl 2800` -- but its body is packed records, not pointers: 4,465 words of 0x2ff and 3,515 of 0x8000033c (the shape of SDK library descriptor / relocation entries), with no embedded ELF (an IRX image would carry the 0x7fELF magic; the whole image has exactly one, outside these regions, so the IOP modules are NOT stored here -- only their library version strings are). 0x50005c (54,724 B) is REFLECTION METADATA: 1,688 ASCII runs including `Gap::igAdxFile`, `Gap::Core::igNamedObject`, `_ptNumber`, `_fileNumber`, `igAdxFile::3 read cancel` -- the class/field name and descriptor arrays behind the MetaObject field tables (#T1). 0x4d7020 (30,720 B) is the ENGINE STRING TABLE plus IOP MODULE NAMES (`sio2man.irx`, `padman.irx`, `mtapman.irx`, `supportMultitap`, `IG_GFX_INDEX_SIZE_16_BIT`, `error creating visual context`, 893 ASCII runs). METHOD, so it can be repeated: composition by word class, pointer targets classified against data/strings.json and the section ranges, and printable runs >=6 chars -- tools/datamap.py does the accounting, this is the identification step above it. WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE DATA NUMBER: three of the five are recoverable mechanically (the vtable region by enumerating `[rtti|0, 0, codeptr…]` runs, which would extend data/vtables.json beyond its RTTI-only 478; the reflection metadata from reflection.json's own tables; the strings from strings.json), so the 21.2 % identified figure is a floor set by the dumps, not by what is known.

**Evidence:** tools/datamap.py

<a id="f374"></a>
### #374 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x001024d0 — split.py

THERE IS UNCLAIMED CODE INSIDE THE "DATA" REGIONS, AND THE PROMOTION PASSES CANNOT SEE IT -- because they need a Ghidra label and Ghidra never disassembled it. EVIDENCE: two of the twenty biggest data regions begin with real code. `0x521750`, the start of the 209,840-byte vtable region, disassembles cleanly (`lui at,0x59; sw zero,-14736(at)` guard, then a tail `j 0x100c20` with a0/a1/a2 set -- the static-initialiser idiom), and the symbol table jumps from `FUN_00521730` (ends at 0x521750) straight to `data_00521750`. Verified leaf functions found the same way: `0x1006c0` = `lui v0,0x4d; jr ra; addiu v0,v0,0x1c18` (`return &DAT_004d1c18;`), `0x1024d0` = the same shape for DAT_004d1e60, `0x13c410` = `jr ra; sw a1,12(a0)` (a one-instruction setter -- `obj->unkC = v`), and 0x13b7a0/0x13c1c0 are prologue-shaped framed functions in the ig*Attr cluster. WHY THEY SURVIVED: `0x1024d0` DOES appear as a 4-byte word in the image -- once, at `0x5224ac` inside the vtable region -- so this is not dead code. `split.py`'s two promotion passes (`promote_gap_labels` by frame prologue, #346; `promote_data_labels` by data reference, #365) both iterate the gap `LAB_` labels Ghidra produced; for an address Ghidra never disassembled there is no label to iterate, so a function reachable only through a pointer table is invisible to both. MEASURED SIZE, whole image: 8,679 distinct 4-byte words point into a *data* region, of which 43 form a self-contained leaf body (a `jr ra` in the first 16 bytes, no prologue) and **145 are prologue-shaped** -- about 188 functions, plus the 1,143 data regions that contain a `jr ra` at all (503 of them <=24 bytes; some of those are the 80 known SDK syscall stubs, which sit in data regions because they are deliberately excluded from the split). THE FIX IS A THIRD PROMOTION PASS, keyed on the data words rather than on Ghidra's labels: for every 4-byte word that lands in an unclaimed data region and whose target decodes to a plausible body (a prologue, or a leaf ending in `jr ra`/`j` with no fallthrough), promote it -- the same shape as the two existing passes, with the *reference* as the signal instead of the label. CAVEAT, since it changes a published number: adding ~188 unmatched functions LOWERS the coverage percentage (7,462/18,849) until they are decompiled; the matched count is the number that matters (#365 made the same point), and these are trivial functions (`return &DAT_x;`, one-instruction setters) that m2c handles well.

**Evidence:** config/SLUS-20836/layout.txt

<a id="f375"></a>
### #375 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00481c20 — sdk-boundary

THE SDK LIBRARY METADATA IS INLINE DESCRIPTORS WITH CODE POINTERS BESIDE THEM -- THE FIRST STEP OF THE SDK BOUNDARY MAP (tracker T9). WHAT THE EIGHT LIBRARY STRINGS ARE: `PsIIlibgraph2800` 0x481c20, `PsIIlibdma 2800` 0x481c60, `PsIIlibipu 2800` 0x481dc0, `PsIIlibkernl2800` 0x481e40, `PsIIlibcdvd 2800` 0x483540, `PsIIlibpad 2800` 0x4850f8, `PsIIlibsdr 2800` 0x485148, `PsIIlibmc 2810` 0x485180 -- eight library descriptors, in the data region that #373 identified from these very strings. THEY ARE REFERENCED BY NOTHING: no 4-byte word in the image points at any of them, and no `lui`+`addiu`/`ori` immediate pair computes their address either (scanned with register tracking over the whole code range), and not gp-relative either (gp is 0x55c870, far above them). The reason is that they are **inline in the descriptor**, not pointed at -- which is exactly how the vendor's tables are laid out. THE DESCRIPTOR SHAPE, read off `PsIIlibgraph` at 0x481c20: three code pointers just before the name (`0x001006f0`, `0x00100ba0`, `0x00100b70`, separated by zero words and a `0x00000001`), then the name+version inline, then a run of u16 pairs (`0x00020001`, `0x00030001`,...) which are the IOP library's function numbers -- i.e. the EE-side binding descriptor: the library's name and version, its function-number list, and the stubs that call it. Neighbouring descriptors show the other shapes: `PsIIlibpad` is followed by a run of pointers into 0x4d2a78/0x4d2a70/0x4d2a60 (the engine string table), and `PsIIlibcdvd` sits among zero words and 0xFFFFFFFF entries (a handler table with invalid markers). FIRST COUNTS, over the metadata window 0x481000..0x4d1c80: 5,282 pointer words with 957 distinct targets in the code range, of which 862 are anonymous `FUN_` functions (the SDK shape -- the symbol map has 15 `sce*` names and no library functions at all) and 50 are named game/engine functions (callbacks registered with the SDK -- precisely the kind of thing a port must reconnect). CAVEAT, so this is not overread: the window is a mixed data region, some of those values are misaligned non-pointers (0x130503, 0x150015) and the most-referenced target (0x2bce90, 1,278 sites) is a shared body rather than 1,278 entry points, so the SDK entry-point count needs the descriptor walked properly rather than a window swept. NEXT: pin the descriptor layout for one library, enumerate all eight, then label the SDK functions (excluding them from decompilation priority) and count the game-> SDK call sites -- that count is the port's shim surface.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/data-regions.md

<a id="f376"></a>
### #376 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100008 — sdk-boundary

THE PLATFORM BOUNDARY: 56 FUNCTIONS TOUCH EE HARDWARE, AND THE SDK IS ONE ANONYMOUS BLOCK AT THE FRONT OF THE IMAGE WITH A 132-ENTRY CALL SURFACE (tracker T9, port goal). METHOD: `tools/platform_map.py` scans for `lui rt,hi` and follows rt for up to eight instructions -- `addiu`/`ori rt,rt,lo` or a load/store with rs==rt gives the address exactly, `addu`/`or` with a second register leaves the hi<<16 page (enough to name the block when the whole page is inside one), a call/jr or any write to rt ends the search; KSEG0/KSEG1 aliases are normalised first. TWO MEASUREMENT TRAPS, both of which made the first version see a quarter of the truth: (1) the window -- a four-instruction window that breaks on a second `lui` and on any SPECIAL opcode resolved ~60 addresses, while an eight-instruction window with a proper writes-rt predicate resolves 329 of the 402 hardware `lui` in the image (the compiler separates a base from its use with nops and `lui at,0x57` delay slots); (2) the page is not the address -- MWCC reaches 0x1000F180 as `lui 0x1001` plus a negative offset, and requiring the *page* to be MMIO discarded every one of those sites. RESULT: 56 functions touch a memory-mapped register -- 19,956 bytes, 0.59% of the code, all 56 anonymous FUN_ -- by block: ipu 23 functions/5 registers, timer 21/15, gs 4/15, dma_spr 3/12, vif1 3/11, vu1_regs 3/14, dmac_ctrl 3/9, dmac 2/15, intc 1/1. Cross-checked by hand: FUN_001f8190 is a switch over a register id whose arms are 14 `lui at,0x1200` GS accesses (a GS register writer), FUN_001f00e0 forms 0x10008000/0x10009000/0x1000a000 (DMA channels). THE SDK BLOCK: reading the image from the front, the first 177 functions are anonymous and contiguous -- 0x100008..0x10aaa8, 41,432 bytes, 1.2% of the code -- and the very next function is `SetGsCrt`, an already-recovered GS-library name, so the boundary between SDK and named game code is sharp; 24 of the 56 MMIO functions are inside it, and **756 `jal` sites from 633 outside functions enter it, reaching 132 distinct entry points** (FUN_00100610 alone, a lazy-init/iterate helper, is called from 548 places). That call surface is the port's shim size: a port must provide those 132 entry points, and the game logic above them is free of platform detail. PLATFORM CODE RECURS OUTSIDE THE BLOCK (the link interleaves it): 0x1146a8..0x1150b0 (9 timer functions), 0x1efb40..0x1f8350 (5 functions across dmac/dma_spr/dmac_ctrl/vif1/vu1_regs/gs -- the DMA/VIF/GS driver), 0x20cd10..0x20e080 (7 timer/vif1), 0x2a9210 (ipu), and 0x482ec0..0x4831e8 (timer, INSIDE a data region -- #374's phenomenon). OPEN: the GS needs only four EE functions because this game renders through DMA chains into the VU1 microcode, so a port's renderer depends on the VU1 program and packet formats rather than on EE register code; the timer cluster may be game profiling rather than SDK; and the 132 entry points are not yet named -- the eight library descriptors of #375 are where to start.

**Notes:** Folded into #378, the current answer for SDK region is the EE runtime (T9); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f377"></a>
### #377 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0010aae0 — sdk-boundary

T9 CORRECTION AND EXTENSION: THE SDK REGION IS THE EE's OWN RUNTIME (84 KB, 410 FUNCTIONS) AND THE GAME->SDK SURFACE IS 127 ENTRY POINTS, NOT 132; THE EE KERNEL API IS ALREADY NAMED AND IS THE PORT's OS SHIM LIST. CORRECTION TO #376: the figure "132 entry points" counted every distinct `jal` target in the block, including the 99 that are only reached from *inside* it. Called from outside, the anonymous head `0x100008..0x10aae8` has **33 entry points over 756 sites from 633 functions**. THE BLOCK IS NOT THE WHOLE SDK, and its end is not a boundary with game code: the next function after it is `SetGsCrt`, and what follows is the **EE kernel library already named** -- `_Exit`, `_LoadExecPS2`, `AddIntcHandler`, `AddDmacHandler`, `CreateThread`, `WaitSema`, `SignalSema`, `FlushCache`, `sceSifSetDma`, `_InitTLB`, `GetMemorySize` (65 functions, 0x10aae0..0x10b360) -- then **168 more anonymous functions** (0x10b360..0x1137a8, 33,864 bytes) which are the same runtime's unnamed body, then the platform timer cluster at 0x1146a8; the first name that is NOT platform code is `igStrCmp` at 0x12a4a4. With the whole region `[0x100008, 0x1137a8)` (84 KB, 410 functions) the game->SDK surface is **127 entry points, 1,364 call sites, 866 calling functions**, and the named half of it is exactly what a port replaces first because the OS API is visible by name: `SignalSema` 56 sites, `ChangeThreadPriority` 28, `WaitSema` 24, plus `sceSifSetDma`, `FlushCache`, `AddIntcHandler`, `CreateThread`. NOT ALL OF THE BLOCK IS PLATFORM CODE: `FUN_001007e0` and `FUN_00101380` materialise the typeinfo names `std::bad_alloc` and `std::bad_exception`, so the block also holds the **C++ runtime**, which a port replaces wholesale instead of shimming; 150 of the 177 head functions have no string evidence at all, so the rest is unmeasured by this method. NAMING (new generator `scripts/seed_names_sdk_strings.py` -> `config/SLUS-20836/names/Sdk.tsv`): the SDK reports errors as `sceXxx:...` from inside `sceXxx`, so a function whose only string is its own name *is* that function -- six recovered: **sceMcUdCheckNewCard 0x11a170, sceMtapPortOpen 0x225540, sceMtapPortClose 0x2255b0, sceMtapGetConnection 0x225620, sceMtapChangeThreadPriority 0x225690, sceMtapGetModVersion 0x225700** (apply_names --apply --check: 6 renames, 0 units moved, make check green, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL). The same generator reports the second class -- functions naming a *library* without naming themselves -- as findings rather than symbols: libpad 0x117b60/0x117c78/0x117ed8, libmc 0x1194c0, libmtap 0x225300, libdma 0x102c98/0x102d08, the IOP-module loaders 0x1485c0/0x32dd80/0x332860, and the CRI file layers. THIRD MEASUREMENT TRAP (now in `tools/mips_literals.py`, shared by both tools): **the low half of an address usually lands in a call's delay slot** -- `lui a0,0x4e` / `jal f` / `addiu a0,a0,lo` is how every SDK error string is materialised, because the delay slot always executes; a scan that treats a call as "control flow leaves" sees the `lui` and none of its uses. The string miner found 6 self-named functions before that fix and 12 library attributions after it (was 8); the platform map was unaffected (hardware bases are formed inline). The `libdma` binding block also carries the EE DMA channel bases (0x10008000, 0x10009000,..., 0x1000d400) beside its name. OPEN: the 0x10b360..0x1137a8 stretch is assumed SDK, not proven (no single piece of evidence), and the surface count depends on it (33 vs 127).

**Notes:** Folded into #378, the current answer for SDK region is the EE runtime (T9); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f378"></a>
### #378 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00114698 — sdk-boundary

T9 SETTLED: THE FRONT REGION IS THE EE RUNTIME (CRT + C++ RUNTIME + LIBC + LIBKERNEL), 410 FUNCTIONS / 75,268 BYTES AT 0x100008..0x1137a8, AND 41 OF ITS 127 EXTERNALLY-CALLED ENTRY POINTS ARE KERNEL SYSCALL STUBS. This closes the open question #377 left, and it does so with evidence rather than shape: (1) `tools/syscalls.py`'s stubs (config/SLUS-20836/syscalls.txt, 83 of them) span 0x100008..0x114698 -- the first syscall stub IS the entry point -- so the front of the image is kernel/runtime code spread across the whole region, and the platform timer cluster starting at 0x1146a8 continues it; (2) the disputed stretch 0x10b360..0x1137a8 (168 anonymous functions, ~34 KB) is the C runtime: FUN_0010d108, its busiest entry with 60 external call sites, saves a1,a2,a3,t0,t1,t2 into a stack buffer and installs that buffer in a global (lw/sw at 0x481e64) -- the va_list prologue of the printf family; FUN_0010e2a8 (59 sites) is a second variadic formatter. (3) The block also carries the C++ runtime: FUN_001007e0 and FUN_00101380 materialise the typeinfo names std::bad_alloc / std::bad_exception (#377). OVER THE WHOLE REGION the call surface from outside is **127 entry points, 1,364 call sites, 866 calling functions**; **41 of those entry points are kernel syscall stubs already listed in config/syscalls.txt**, and **39 are already named**, most of them the OS API: CreateThread, WaitSema, SignalSema, CreateSema, AddIntcHandler, AddDmacHandler, ChangeThreadPriority, EnableCache, ExitDeleteThread, FlushCache, sceSifSetDma. That named subset is precisely the part a port must *implement*; the rest is the C runtime below it, which a port replaces with the host's libc/libstdc++ and never shims -- so the two halves of "the shim surface" are now distinguishable in kind, not just in count. HONEST LIMITS: (a) of the 1,364 call sites into the region, only **2 come from functions with a recovered engine name** (ig*/Gap*/be*) -- the rest come from functions that are still anonymous -- so the surface measures the game's dependency but does not yet separate the engine's own calls from the SDK libraries' internal ones (the CRI file/audio layers are themselves callers); (b) the ~86 non-syscall entry points are split between libc / C++ runtime (replace, never shim) and SDK library internals (must shim), and only shape-level identification can separate them, since address and strings do not. Naming unchanged from #377: 6 recovered from self-naming strings, 12 attributed to a library.

**Notes:** Topic head for SDK region is the EE runtime (T9): also read #376, #377 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f379"></a>
### #379 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002716f8 — sdk-boundary

T9 INVENTORY: THE PLATFORM-SIDE COMPONENTS OF THE IMAGE, EACH WITH ITS EVIDENCE, AND WHAT CAN AND CANNOT BE DELIMITED. (1) EE RUNTIME -- CRT + C++ runtime + libc + libkernel: 410 functions / 75,268 bytes at 0x100008..0x1137a8; extent proven three ways (the kernel syscall stubs of config/SLUS-20836/syscalls.txt span 0x100008..0x114698, the C++ typeinfo std::bad_alloc/std::bad_exception lives in it at 0x1007e0/0x101380, and the printf va_list prologue is at 0x10d108); 41 of its 127 externally-called entry points are syscall stubs and 39 are named OS functions. (2) SONY EE SDK LIBRARIES -- libgraph, libdma, libipu, libkernl, libcdvd, libpad, libsdr, libmc: evidenced by the eight binding descriptors at 0x481000+ (#375: name, version, function-number list, stub pointers; libdma's block also carries the EE DMA channel bases 0x10008000/0x10009000/.../0x1000d400) plus 12 functions attributed to a library by their own strings (libpad 0x117b60/0x117c78/0x117ed8, libmc 0x1194c0, libmtap 0x225300, libdma 0x102c98/0x102d08, the IOP loaders 0x1485c0/0x32dd80/0x332860/0x3ad5b0) and 6 recovered names (sceMtap*, sceMcUdCheckNewCard). (3) CRI MIDDLEWARE -- SRD (file/stream), HTCI, DVCI, PS2RNA, ADXM (ADX sound): a THIRD platform component that is neither Sony SDK nor game code; evidenced by 51 `SRD:`, 48 `HTCI:`, 36 `DVCI:`, 9 `PS2RNA:`, 6 `ADXM:` error strings, and the 41 functions referencing them cluster at 0x2716f8 (39 within 0x2716f8..0x27b188, ~39 KB), with the sound layer at 0x37e070. (4) THE IOP SIDE -- 11.irx modules plus IOPRP280.IMG, loaded by name from the loader functions: sio2man/padman/mtapman (0x1485c0), libsd/igFileIO (0x32dd80), cri_adxi/sdrdrv/sndfi (0x332860), mcman/mcserv (0x3ad5b0), igAudio (0x1439d0); the modules themselves live on the disc, not in the image. (5) HARDWARE: the 56 MMIO-touching functions (#376). (6) THE GAME: 1,744 `Gap::` RTTI strings and the recovered ig*/be* names -- the decompilation target. A LIMIT WORTH STATING PLAINLY: the *extent* of any single SDK library cannot be delimited from outside, because anonymity is not a boundary signal (13,249 of the 18,661 functions are anonymous FUN_, so any contiguous-anonymous-run heuristic swallows the whole image: the gap-expansion test run for this finding returned 3,012 functions / 424,904 bytes for the CRI cluster, which is meaningless) and strings mark a library only where it reports errors. Whole-library extents need the #375 binding tables decoded into function->library maps; the counts above are floors over evidence, not totals. This is the quantitative end of T9: which parts of the image are platform, with the OS/hardware shim surface named.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f380"></a>
### #380 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001001d0 — memory-map

THE RUNTIME MEMORY MAP, MEASURED: THE SPACE ABOVE THE IMAGE IS THE C HEAP (heap_start = 0x0058d280 = _end, size -1 = everything left), AND IT IS IN USE FROM ITS FIRST BYTE -- BUT THE HEAP BASE IS AN ARGUMENT IN CODE THIS REPO RE-LINKS, SO IT IS MOVABLE. The CRT startup (0x100198..0x1001e8, in the section) makes two EE kernel calls whose arguments are immediates: syscall 60 `SetupThread` with gp=0x0055c870, stack 0x01F00000, size 0x00100000 (the main thread's stack is the LAST MEGABYTE of RAM, 0x01f00000..0x02000000, and the call's result is moved straight into `sp`), then syscall 61 `SetupHeap` with heap_start=0x0058d280 (the byte after BSS) and heap_size=-1 ("everything left"). The numbers are pinned by the repo's own config/SLUS-20836/syscalls.txt, which lists EndOfHeap as 62; 60 and 61 are the two immediately before it. LIVE CONFIRMATION (in-world save state, pi-pine): 0x0058d280 holds small allocator words; the first 8 MB above the image is 57.8% non-zero with live data reaching 0x00d77d31 (~8.3 MB of heap in use); the stack's live frames sit just below 0x02000000 (0x01fff000 holds return addresses into the loaded section) while 0x01e00000..0x01f00000 is untouched. CONSEQUENCES FOR MODS, which is what this was measured for: (1) the space above the BSS is NOT free -- it is the game's working memory from its first byte, so the earlier hypothesis that new code could simply live past 0x0058d280 is refuted; (2) but heap_start is an argument to a call the repo re-links, so a mod region CAN go at 0x0058d280..0x0058d280+N with SetupHeap given 0x0058d280+N and segment 0's FileSz/MemSz grown by N to make the region file-backed -- the cost is N bytes off a heap that has ~8.3 MB live against ~26 MB of address space; (3) taking that route means the CRT startup must be built from source rather than linked as the committed disassembly (ordinary decompilation work). UNRESOLVED: two addresses in that range recur as code immediates (0x008ba000, 0x00979680) and appear nowhere as data -- hardcoded pool bases or bit patterns is not determined, and it matters only if a mod region is megabytes deep. Method note: the constants were found by resolving lui/addiu pairs across the whole code range; most of the 52 "heap address" hits were masks (0x01010101, 0x007effff), which is why only the three that also appear in the startup and the two recurring pool-shaped ones were pursued.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/binary.md

<a id="f381"></a>
### #381 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00130060 — typed-coverage

TYPED COVERAGE, MEASURED AND MOVED: 24.25% OF FIELD ACCESSES IN THE MATCHED C NOW USE A RECOVERED TYPE (550 OF 2,268), UP FROM 0 (tracker T10). The metric: `tools/typed_coverage.py` classifies every field access in src/ -- a member of a recovered class against an offset cast (`*(int *)((char *)p + 36)`). It is the inward metric the port needs: m2c's C reproduces retail instruction-for-instruction while encoding 4-byte pointers and hand-computed offsets, which no other compiler can use. Published in PROGRESS.md and docs/progress.json beside matched bytes and identified data; page docs/engine/typed-coverage.md. HOW THE TYPES ARE RECOVERED: `scripts/gen_type_headers.py` turns data/reflection.json (659 classes / 3,680 fields) into include/dw4/reflected.h, with two rules that keep it honest -- (1) **the offsets are the authority, not the type map**: a field's size comes from the distance to the next field, because igMetaField has `_persistent` (a bool) at +38 and `_shallowCopyMethod` (a char) at +39, so a bool is one byte and a map that said `int` there would shift every later field (the first version emitted 23 of igMetaObject's 25 fields for exactly that reason); (2) **a disagreement is printed, not hidden**: 223 fields are inline aggregates (vectors, matrices, arrays) emitted as arrays of the element type, and the 72 where the metadata's scalar size contradicts the offsets are reported. THE FIRST BATCH: tools/automatch.py emits a layout with no meaning in 550 files -- `struct G { char pad[36]; int f; }; extern struct G *DAT_0055ebc0[4];` -- and every fact needed to say it meaningfully was already recovered: the global is a **reflection class handle**, so what it holds is an `igMetaObject *` (the invariant the object-model work established), reflection names the field at +36 `_validity`, and that accessor is the lazy get-or-register path the repo already described (#358: `if (!*handle || flags & 4 == 0) register;`), so `_validity & 4` is read rather than guessed. `scripts/retype_metaobject_idiom.py` rewrote those 550 files -- only files whose shape is exactly that one, field used only through that pointer, a real class handle, and a reflected field of the same width at that offset. VERIFIED: make check stayed **7,462/18,661 matched, 0 mismatching** and make link is **BYTE-IDENTICAL**, so the typing is codegen-identical (typing that changed codegen would be a bug and the build is the test); the number went 0 -> 24.25%. TWO PROCESS FACTS WORTH KEEPING: the 550-file batch first failed make check down to 6,912 matched because the inserted include was written with escaped quotes (`#include \"dw4/reflected.h\"`) so those units did not compile -- caught by the gate, fixed, re-run from a clean tree; and the metric's own parser had two bugs that made it report 0 recovered accesses (a base-expression detector that picked the *cast type* rather than the root identifier, and a declaration regex that required an uppercase class name so every `ig*`/`be*` type was skipped) -- both found by printing what the parser actually saw rather than trusting the number. THE MEASURED CEILING, which is the actionable part: only **1.4%** of accesses are typeable from the receiver class alone, because most matched C is address-named (`FUN_00130fb0(void *arg0)` cannot say what arg0 points at) -- so **typed coverage advances with naming**, and the two loops are the same work seen from two sides; the remaining 1,718 casts each need the identity of the object they point into.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/typed-coverage.md

<a id="f382"></a>
### #382 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00481c20

The SDK library descriptors are decoded (T11): 8 records, each a 16-byte header of a 12-byte space-padded library name and a 4-byte ASCII version -- PsIIlibgraph 0x481c20, PsIIlibdma 0x481c60, PsIIlibipu 0x481dc0, PsIIlibkernl 0x481e40, PsIIlibcdvd 0x483540, PsIIlibpad 0x4850f8, PsIIlibsdr 0x485148, PsIIlibmc 0x485180. The version is real and per-library: mc is "2810" (SDK 2.8.1) and the other seven are "2800" (2.8.0), the same 280 as the disc's IOPRP280.IMG. Re-verified unreferenced: no word anywhere in the image holds any of the eight addresses, so these are linker-emitted metadata the game never reads. The payload is library-specific data, not a uniform binding table: libdma carries the ten EE DMA channel register bases (0x10008000..0x1000d400) followed by a 15-entry function table; libgraph two u16 pair tables; libipu the MPEG-2 DCT quantisation tables (an intra zig-zag matrix and the all-16 non-intra default) plus microcode; libkernl the C heap base 0x0058d280 -- the same value the CRT passes to syscall 61 (finding #380) -- one code pointer and a long 0xffffffff run; libpad eleven pointers into the shared string pool, eight of them the status names ERROR/STABLE/EXECCMD/FINDCTP1/DISCONNECT/BUSY/FAILED/COMPLETE; libmc driver structures on an 88-byte stride; libcdvd and libsdr no pointers at all. LIMIT (proven, not suspected): only libdma yields a function->library map, so per-library extents for the other seven cannot be had this way -- T9's stated limit stands. SPLIT ORACLE: libdma's table names 3 addresses the function tiling missed -- 0x104478 and 0x1044f0 inside FUN_00104258, which is therefore three functions (0x104258..0x104478,..0x1044f0,..0x104588, every boundary clean), and 0x105050, which begins exactly where FUN_00104f30 ends and starts with MMI ops rather than a prologue. Promoting them takes the tree 18,661 -> 18,664. Tool: tools/sdk_bindings.py -> data/sdk-bindings.json.

**Notes:** Promoted ( follow-up). All three split-oracle starts are now functions: FUN_00104258 (0x220) + FUN_00104478 (0x78) + FUN_001044f0 (0x40), and FUN_00105050 (0x114) at FUN_00104f30's old end. Tree 18,661 -> 18,664; make check 18,664/18,664 round-trip + 7,462 matched (40.0%); make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. The decoder bug that blocked the run is fixed: tools/sdk_bindings.py's _late_code branch continue'd before va += 4, so the decode loop re-appended the same word forever (unbounded memory -> OOM kill).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f383"></a>
### #383 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00491910

The VU1 microcode's own symbol table is in game.elf's rodata at 0x491910 (a "name;offset;name;offset;..." string, ~150 vu1_entry_*/vu1_end_* pairs running from vu1_entry_UCODE_BEGIN;0 to vu1_end_UCODE_END;109184, i.e. 109,184 bytes of microcode). It is the VU1 assembler's symbol table left in the image, and it is the complete function map of the renderer's microcode -- every EXECUTE_*/TRANS_*/LIGHT_*/CLIP_*/TEXGEN_*/FOG_*/PARTICLE_*/MULTIPASS_*/COMPRESS_*/BLEND_* entry with its byte offset, including the shared segments (LIGHT_SHARED, CLIP_SHARED, MULTIPASS_SHARED1..8). A second table at 0x48dd60 lists the same technique names with their parameter/flag words ("EXECUTE_PATH;1073741824;;131077;;;;", "TRANS_MVP;264192;;17;;;;"), which is the dispatch state the renderer builds. Found while decoding the library descriptors (T11); it belongs to the port's renderer sub-project rather than to the decompilation, but it is a fact about this binary's rodata. The 0x491910 string is also what the porting plan's "VU1 microcode with its embedded vu1_entry_* symbol table" refers to.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f385"></a>
### #385 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0011a170

Where the SDK's function names are, and are not (T11 follow-up, measured across everything shipped). In game.elf: 6 functions name themselves in their own error strings (sceMcUdCheckNewCard, sceMtapPortOpen/PortClose/GetConnection/ChangeThreadPriority/GetModVersion -- finding #377); the other 21 sce* strings are call-site messages that name the callee, e.g. CRI SRD's "SRD: sceCdBreak is fail. %d" names sceCdBreak but sits in SRD's wrapper, so they are evidence about call sites rather than about the containing function. In IOPRP280.IMG (269,889 bytes, 16 embedded IOP modules): 16 sceCd* names, all from the cdvdman module -- the only embedded module that keeps a name table; the rest are stripped. In the 11 extracted MODULES/*.IRX: zero -- every one has no symbol table (mipsel-linux-gnu-readelf -s reports 0 FUNC symbols), and their export tables carry numbers, not names. CONCLUSION: apart from those 6 + 16, the SDK's function names are not in the shipped data at all -- they exist only in Sony's SDK headers/documentation, so naming SDK functions beyond the self-naming ones would be documentation, not recovery, which the repo's naming rule does not allow in config/SLUS-20836/names/. What is recoverable is ATTRIBUTION (which library a function belongs to), and the one bounded naming route left is the cdvd join: the 16 sceCd* names are cdvdman's exports by number (in the ROM), and game.elf's EE-side libcdvd wrappers call them by RPC command number, so the two can be matched by number. That is new item T12.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f386"></a>
### #386 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00532bb0

THE ENGINE HAS A NAMED PLATFORM SEAM, AND IT IS THE PORT'S REAL BOUNDARY: exactly 32 classes named igPsx2* implement PS2-specific behaviour, and 32 of 32 derive from a non-PS2 base class -- igPsx2File->igFile, igPsx2Folder->igFolder, igPsx2Media->igMedia, igPsx2Controller->igController, igPsx2ControllerManager->igControllerManager, igPsx2AudioContext->igAudioContext, igPsx2Thread->igThread, igPsx2Semaphore->igSemaphore, igPsx2ThreadManager->igThreadManager, igPsx2LongTimer->igLongTimer, igPsx2CallStackTracer->igCallStackTracer, igPsx2VisualContext->igVisualContext, igPsx2Window->igWindow, igPsx2Image->igMemoryImage, igPsx2Clut->igClut, igPsx2ImageConvert->igImageConvert, igPsx2IndexArray->igIndexArray, igPsx2VertexArray1_1/igPsx2VertexArray2/igPsx2VertexStream, and the shader-extension family (BlendEquationExt, DecalExt, DisableExt, EnvironmentMapShader2, MultiTextureExt, ParticleArray, PointSpriteExt, ScissorExt, UserUCodeExt, plus AudioSourceList and LongList under igDataList). The one exception is igPsx2IopRpcConnection, whose base is igObject -- it IS the EE<->IOP RPC seam rather than an implementation of a generic interface. Class hierarchy from data/class-hierarchy.tsv (T1 / the registrar's fn2), so this is recovered, not inferred from names. CONSEQUENCE FOR A PORT, which is why it was measured: the game and the engine talk to the generic bases, so a port implements those 32 interfaces natively (pthreads, POSIX file I/O, an ISO reader, SDL2 pad/audio, a new renderer) and the engine's own logic runs unchanged -- the port seam is ~32 classes wide, not 18,661 functions, and not the 127 SDK entry points either. It also says what the SDK is used FOR, by area: threading and synchronisation, long timers, files/folders/CD media, controller input, audio, and graphics (visual context, window, images, CLUTs, vertex/index arrays and the shader-extension set). And it reframes the naming question: naming SDK functions would not help a port much, because the port replaces the platform classes rather than the SDK functions they call -- facts #382-#385 measured that the SDK's names are not in the shipped data, and this measurement shows they are not needed at that layer.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

<a id="f387"></a>
### #387 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001041d8

LIBDMA'S FUNCTION TABLE IS REACHED BY INTERIOR INDEXING -- NO POINTER TO IT, NO jal TO ITS ENTRIES. Scanning every lui/addiu pair in the image for an address inside libdma's descriptor block finds exactly three, and one of them is live: at 0x1041d8 the code does "lui a1,0x48; addiu a1,a1,0x1cb8; sll v0,v0,2; addu v0,v0,a1; lw a2,0(v0)" -- i.e. it indexes the table through a base anchored at 0x481cb8, which is the table's NINTH entry, not its start (0x481c98). The table's own start address is formed nowhere in the image, each of the sixteen entries has exactly one reference (its own word in the table), and a scan of all jal instructions finds ZERO call sites into any of the sixteen. So three independent xref methods -- stored pointer, computed base, direct call -- each miss all or most of it, and the table looked like dead code until the arithmetic was resolved. METHOD WARNING worth keeping for this binary: a function table can be dispatched through a base computed to an interior entry, so "no data word points at it and nothing calls it directly" does NOT mean unreachable; resolve lui/addiu pairs before concluding. What the three "new starts" are, confirmed by disassembly rather than by the pointer alone: 0x104478 begins immediately after FUN_00104258's stack restore (addiu sp,sp,176; nop) with a0-relative descriptor loads (lw a1,20(a0); lw a2,24(a0); lw a3,8(a0)), and uses mtsab/lq -- unaligned quad access, the MMI idiom for a block move; 0x105050 begins immediately after a jr ra; nop with an MMI mask idiom (pnor/psrlh/psllh) and the same a0-relative loads. Both are argument-taking MMI block-copy kernels characteristic of a DMA driver, not data tables, so the descriptor's table really is libdma's function list and the split oracle stands.

**Notes:** Confirmed split oracle and now used: its interior-indexed table named 0x104478, 0x1044f0 and 0x105050, all promoted to function starts in the T11 follow-up (18,661 -> 18,664). Details in #382.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/platform-boundary.md

**Notes:** SUPERSEDED AND FIXED by #389. The naive pass's two defects were the literal window (scan_from_lui default 8 < the registrar's 11-instruction gap) and the generator's at(v) is None gate; both fixed, it now promotes 613 stubs and the generator is idempotent. Kept for the record: 208/591 promoted, 383 left unclaimed, 206 accessor names dropped on regeneration. <a id="f389"></a>
### #389 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001310c0 — igMetaObject_RegisterClass t0 thunks

All 572 registrar 'j \<accessor>; nop' thunks promote and name as thunk_\<accessor> once the literal search window covers the registrar's nine-argument setup, and resolving the thunk unconditionally makes seed_names_metaobject.py idempotent.

**Notes:** Two fixes. (1) tools/split.py's literal_code_addresses used scan_from_lui's default window of 8 instructions, but a registrar builds all nine arguments at once, so lui t0,hi and its completing addiu t0,t0,lo sit up to 11 instructions apart (no write to t0 in between). window 9 -> 16 resolves 20,044 literal addresses (was 17,185), and promote_j_thunks promotes 613 stubs (all 572 MetaObject thunks + 41 others), 18,664 -> 19,277; window >= 12 saturates. make check 19,277/19,277, 7,462 matched (38.7%), make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. (2) scripts/seed_names_metaobject.py resolved t0 to the accessor only when at(v) is None, so once the thunks had symbols at their own addresses the generator named the thunk and dropped 206 accessor names; it now resolves the thunk from the bytes and emits both the accessor and its thunk, so apply_names.py can move a name off a thunk. That corrected the 2 names #365/#366 had placed on thunks instead of accessors: igTimeAttr_GetMetaObject 0x1310c0 -> 0x130fb0 and igColorAttr_GetMetaObject 0x139080 -> 0x138f70 (the real accessors, whose.cpp units were already decompiled), with the thunks named thunk_igTimeAttr_GetMetaObject / thunk_igColorAttr_GetMetaObject. Supersedes the naive pass refuted in #388. Topic head for Registrar j-thunk promotion: also read #388 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** config/SLUS-20836/names/MetaObject.tsv

<a id="f390"></a>
### #390 · refuted

**Where:** 0x002fe290 — FUN_002fe2ac

The last 8-byte candidate (thunk_FUN_002fe2ac, a 'b 0x2fe2ac; nop') is an m2c failure that can be banked once m2c handles it.

**Notes:** Refuted, and it is a split defect rather than an m2c gap. 0x2fe290's only instruction is 'b 0x2fe2ac; nop'. The target 0x2fe2ac is Ghidra's start for FUN_002fe2ac, but that body opens 'bnez a0,0x2fe298' and branches backward into 0x2fe298, which tools/layout.py classifies as data (data_002fe298, 0x14 bytes): Ghidra split one linked-list walk -- while (a0 != 0) { if (a0 == a1) return 1; a0 = *(int *)(a0 + 56); } return 0 -- at its loop condition. It is the only 'b'-thunk in the tree (all 688 other 8-byte j/b thunks are 'j'; exactly one is 'b'), so a merge rule for a single function is not worth adding. C cannot branch into another function's interior: 'void f(void) { FUN_002fe2ac; }' compiles to 'j', not 'b'. Related: symbols.txt overlaps here -- FUN_002fe2ac is listed 0x24 bytes (ending 0x2fe2d0) past igMetaObject_GetChildCount at 0x2fe2c0, and layout truncates it to 0x14. It stays in asm/ and make check still round-trips. A real fix is to merge 0x2fe290 + the 0x2fe298 gap + 0x2fe2ac into one function and re-decompile.

**Evidence:** config/SLUS-20836/symbols.txt

<a id="f391"></a>
### #391 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0013b7f0 — FUN_0013b7f0

The 109 near-miss 8-byte functions are two m2c weaknesses, not one-off oddities: m2c widens a narrow store and drops a tail call's delay-slot argument setup, and both are template-expressible.

**Notes:** Two classes. (a) WIDENED NARROW ACCESS: for a void *-typed base m2c emits a word store, so 'sb a1,12(a0)' becomes '*(int *)((char *)arg0 + 12) = arg1;' and MWCC emits dsll32/dsra32/sw (16 bytes, size mismatch) instead of a single sb; the fix is the access width, and 'ld v0,OFF(a0)' will not compile as an int return. (b) DROPPED DELAY-SLOT SETUP: m2c decompiles 'j X; move a0,a1' as 'X' with no argument, losing the setup the delay slot performs; same for j+addiu/li/lw/move/store-before-jump. Both are now templates in tools/automatch.py: setter (sb/sh/sw/sd + zero + the width from the mnemonic), fset (swc1 $f12), fget (adds ld), rbase (jr ra; addiu v0,aN,IMM) and tsetup (the general j + delay-slot setup, using the EE a4-a7 argument names). Measured on the tree: the extended pass covers 95 of the 109 (22 before), and on the unmatched set it produced 0 new matches -- expected, since the 109 were exactly its domain and are now banked. Kept so a later promotion that adds these shapes is caught by the template pass instead of a manual fix.

**Evidence:** tools/automatch.py

<a id="f392"></a>
### #392 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00141400 — FUN_00141400

The cleaner's default 4-byte width for an untyped member access causes the sb/sh/ld/sd/float near-misses; feeding the retail instruction's width back into the candidate fixes them, and verification decides.

**Notes:** m2c emits an untyped member (arg0->unk20 = 0) whenever it had no cast to carry the width, so the cleaner defaults to int and a byte store becomes a sw (finding #391). tools/m2c_batch.py fit_widths now reads the memory ops of the function own retail words and rewrites the simple *(int *)((char *)(argN) +/- OFF) accesses to the width retail uses (char/short/long long/float), and both m2c_batch and m2c_loop.prepare verify the original and the fitted spelling and keep the one that matches. Measured: the <=64-byte tier banked 119 and the whole unmatched set banked 30 more, 149 together, tree 8,184 -> 8,333 (43.2%), make link BYTE-IDENTICAL; the <=3-diff tier is 643 within <=64 bytes and 927 over the whole tree. The residual is now measured and is not width: 322 frame/tail-call in the <=64-byte tier (retail keeps a frame -- addiu sp,sp,-N; sd ra -- where our C compiles to a j; a naive.cpp retry does not help: of 669, 312 fail to compile as C++ and the other 357 still differ, so it needs a C++-aware cleaner) and 263 register-allocation (same instruction, different register, e.g. retail lw v0,(a0) vs ours lw v1,(a0)).

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f393"></a>
### #393 · refuted

**Where:** 0x001147f0 — FUN_001147f0

The 322 frame/tail-call near-misses are MWCC C-vs-C++ frame behaviour (finding #314), so recompiling m2c candidate as C++ would keep the frame and match.

**Notes:** Refuted by measurement, and the real cause is m2c dropping a delay-slot instruction. (1) A.cpp retry of the cleaned candidate matches 0 of 669 <=64-byte near-misses: 312 fail to compile as C++ and 357 compile yet still differ, so the language is not the lever. (2) Only 4 of the 324 <=64-byte frame/tail-call cases are the single-call wrapper_shape the wargs/wcall templates target; 261 keep a frame and 68 are frame-less tail calls ending in j. (3) The dropped instruction is visible in m2c C: FUN_001147f0 lui a0,0xB000; j X; ori a0,a0,0x1000 becomes the constant 0xB0000000 (the delay-slot ori is dropped); FUN_00109910 sw zero,2144(a0); j X; li a0,1 loses the li a0,1 (the hand fix FUN_00105228(1) matches); FUN_00100290 dsll32 a0,a0,0; j X; dsrl32 a0,a0,0 loses the dsrl32 (ours dsra32). A general frame-less tail-call template over the whole setup (automatch.jargs, tried and reverted) matched only 3 of 1077 candidates and 0 unbanked, because MWCC materialises a recovered literal through a scratch (lui v0; ori a0,v0) where retail writes the destination directly (lui a0; ori a0,a0). So the group needs an m2c/symbolizer delay-slot fix (cf. #349), not a language change or a template.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f394"></a>
### #394 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001308c0 — igVertexBlendStateAttr_InitFields

The \<Class>_InitFields reflection field initialisers are a 2-instruction near-miss: m2c types the loaded MetaObject handle as int, so MWCC loads it into the first argument register and saves it afterwards, while retail loads it into the saved register.

**Notes:** m2c emits `int temp_s0; temp_s0 = DAT_x[0];...`; OURS compiles `lw a0,DAT; move s0,a0` (load fused into the first call argument, then saved) while RETAIL is `lw s0,DAT; move a0,s0` (loaded into the saved register, then moved to the argument). Declaring the local `void *` and casting the load — `void *a; a = (void *)DAT_x[0];` — makes MWCC keep the value in s0 and reproduce retail. Measured: 141 of the 142 <=3-diff InitFields match, and a full sweep banked 141, tree 8,333 -> 8,474 (44.0%), make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. The transform lives in tools/m2c_batch.retype_global_loads and is tried as an extra variant by both m2c_batch and m2c_loop.prepare; it is generic (any `NAME = DAT_x[0];` local declared int), so verification, not the heuristic, decides.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f395"></a>
### #395 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00133ac0 — igShadeModelAttr_InitFields

The larger \<Class>_InitFields functions are register-swap near-misses: MWCC assigns saved registers by declaration order, and retail keeps the field pointer in s1 and the count in s2, so the declarations must be ordered pointer-first.

**Notes:** m2c declares `int temp_s2;` (the field count) before `void *temp_v0;` (the field pointer), so MWCC puts the count in s1 and the field in s2 -- the reverse of retail, which is 6 instructions of pure register swap (move s2,v0 vs move s1,v0 etc.). Ordering pointer-typed locals before integer locals reproduces retail: 96 of the 237 remaining 4-12-diff InitFields match. tools/m2c_batch.order_pointer_decls applies it (and it is tried after retype_global_loads from #394, since the larger initialisers need both). A full sweep banked 103, tree 8,474 -> 8,577 (44.5%), make link BYTE-IDENTICAL. With #394 the InitFields family is ~244 functions, most of the family; the rest have other issues.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

A byte-level scan of all 1.07 M instructions of game.elf for the plain folded tail (addu X,X,sp; lw X,D(X)) finds 345 *_GetVTable sites plus only 4 non-GetVTable sites (FUN_0024bda0 v0/16, FUN_00447ed0 v1/80 and v1/48, Gap__Bec__beNDMWShopCtrl10__vfunc_21 a1/112) -- so the plain form really is ~345 and the rest differ. igVisualContextAttrDefaultManager_GetVTable (0x1300b0, 140 B) shows what the tail is: the same 60-byte core, then a null-checked refcount decrement (lw 4(a0); addiu -1; sw; dsll32/dsrl32; bnez) and a call to 0x2ff820 (a release/destructor) that is dead in this instance (the pointer tested was just zeroed). <a id="f398"></a>
### #398 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00123538 — FUN_00123538

The m2c diff count under-reports when our reconstruction is shorter, because verify.py compares only over min(len(ours), len(retail)); a large part of the apparent near-miss tier is structural failure, not a near miss.

**Notes:** Measured. verify.py line ~412: bad = [i for i in range(min(len(words_a), len(words_b))) if words_a[i] != words_b[i]], so when ours is shorter every extra retail instruction is invisible and only the size line hints at it. Of the 937 unmatched functions the worklist reports at 1-3 diffs, **379 (40%) have len(ours) != len(retail)** and are really m2c structural failures. The biggest of these is a tail-call collapse: retail FUN_00123538 is 20 B with a redundant frame (addiu sp,sp,-16; sd ra,0(sp); ld ra,0(sp); j 0x11acc0; addiu sp,sp,16) while m2c emits void f(void){g;} -> 8 B (j; nop), and MWCC will not re-emit the frame from ANY source form tried (void/int callee, return value, dead local, volatile local, address-taken local: all 8 B). Detecting the truncation is what separates a real near miss from a wrong reconstruction, so any future near-miss count must carry the size pair (tools/queue_diag.py now records retail and ours size and flags size!).

**Evidence:** tools/queue_diag.py

The defaulted-int store width is fixed by decoding m2c local names (temp_sN) to their register, not only argN; this is the store-width class (#392 handled loads).

The unmatched queue measured: 10,700 functions, 8,571 unnamed and 2,129 named; m2c can bank 0 more of them unaided, the true same-size near-miss tier is 558, and its largest single cluster is a store width.

<a id="f401"></a>
### #401 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001109f0 — FUN_001109f0

The cleaner symbolized a numeric address even when it was an operand of a bitwise operator, turning m2cs uncached-alias constant `0x5586C0 | 0x20000000` into `char[] | int`, which MWCC rejects.

**Notes:** Confirmed cleaner bug, fixed in tools/m2c_batch.py clean: the numeric->symbol substitution in symbol/hybrid mode emitted a bare `DAT_x` (declared `extern char DAT_x[]`) wherever an address sat inside a cast, but `*(0x5586C0 | 0x20000000)` has the constant as a *bitwise operand*, so naming it produced `char[] | int` -- MWCC `illegal operand`. The PS2 uncached alias `0x5586C0 | 0x20000000` is a real constant m2c legitimately builds with `|`, and retail materialises it as `lui v0,0x2000` then `or` with a register holding 0x5586C0 (shared with a call argument, so it is not folded). The fix keeps a bitwise-context address an int value: emit `(int)DAT_x` when the constant is followed by `|`/`&`/`^`/`<<`/`>>` or preceded by `|`/`&`/`^`. Also extended fix_void_arith to the three further syntactic forms of the same machine arithmetic (`p += N` / `p -= N`, `N + p`, and a `(void *)` cast used as an arithmetic base), each of which MWCC rejects with a DIFFERENT message (illegal type / illegal operand / pointer-array required), which is why the failure taxonomy never grouped them as one bucket. Measured over the whole tier: **compile 1,261 -> 1,063 (-198, -15.7 %)**, mismatch 9,257 -> 9,240, and **0 banked** -- the #320 law in its purest form (a compilability fix makes functions comparable, it does not make them match).

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f402"></a>
### #402 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002e24e0 — FUN_002e24e0

MWCC rejects `void *` arithmetic in every syntactic form, so the cleaners `(char *)` cast is load-bearing (not redundant), and the same machine arithmetic fails with three different MWCC messages.

**Notes:** Probed directly with verify_one on `void FUN_0010db08(void *p){...}`: `p = p + 4`, `p += 4`, `p = p + n`, `p = 4 + p`, `p = n + p` and `*(int *)(p + 4) = 1` ALL fail to compile (MWCC rejects void* arithmetic outright), so fix_void_ariths cast is required, not cosmetic -- an earlier reading of this function as "MWCC accepts void* + int, just not int + void*" was wrong (the probe that suggested it had counted `!!` lines without checking the compile verdict; a compile failure reports zero `!!` lines, which reads as "0 diffs" if you only count them). FUN_002e24e0 itself is the useful specimen: before the fix it was a compile failure; after it, it is **1 diff** -- `addu v1,v1,a0` (retail) vs `addu v1,a0,v1` (ours), i.e. the addend order inside `*(int *)((arg1 * 4) + (char *)arg0)`. MWCC canonicalises `X + (T *)Y` to `(T *)Y + X`, so the cast placement, not the expression order, decides the addu operands.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f403"></a>
### #403 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00123538 — FUN_00123538

Some MWCC optimisation flag reproduces retails framed tail call (the 20-byte shape: addiu sp,sp,-16; sd ra,0(sp); ld ra,0(sp); j target; addiu sp,sp,16), which would explain the largest near-miss class mechanically.

**Notes:** REFUTED: no flag reproduces it. Fifteen combinations measured on the same source (`void FUN_00123538(void){ FUN_0011acc0; }`): -O3, -O3,p, -O3,s, -O4, -O4,p, -O4,s, and -opt speed/space/full/size all emit the **8-byte frameless tail jump** (j; nop); -O2, -O2,p, -O1, -O0 and -opt off emit a **32-byte function with the frame AND a real jal** (no tail call). Retails 20-byte framed tail jump sits between the two and is produced by none of them. So the class is not an optimizer-flag artifact: either the source is not `void f(void){ g; }` (something makes MWCC allocate the frame yet still tail-convert), or the retail objects came from a different code path. Consequence for the queue: the largest structural class (~379 of the <=3-diff rows) is a correctness problem in the reconstruction, not something a flag change reaches, and m2cs output (which simply omits the dead frame) is *semantically* right while being byte-wrong.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f404"></a>
### #404 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0013c260 — FUN_0013c260

The game is C++, and a virtual call must be *written* as a C++ virtual call: MWCCs C front end emits `jalr v0` for an indirect call while its C++ front end emits the MIPS C++ ABI sequence `lw t9,0(a0); lw t9,K(t9); jalr t9`. That is a structural class of the remaining queue, not a cleaner detail.

**Notes:** CONFIRMED with a byte-identical reconstruction. FUN_0013c260 (0x13c260, 80 B) is a call through a function pointer stored in the object at +88 (the igObject interface block), then the result is passed to igMetaObject_CountInstances, then Gap::Core::igObject::vfunc_10(arg0). Compiled as C (the pipeline default) the same source is 3 instructions off, all three in the call: retail lw t9,0(a0) / lw t9,88(t9) / jalr t9, ours lw v0,0(a0) / lw v0,88(v0) / jalr v0. Written as C++ with a real virtual call it matches byte-for-byte: declare `class C { public: virtual void v0;... v19; virtual int vX; };` (20 preceding virtuals place the target at byte 88 -- MWCC reserves 2 vtable slots), then `if (igMetaObject_CountInstances(((C *)arg0)->vX, 1) == 0) {... }` with `arg0` the OBJECT (a single level: retail loads the vptr straight from a0), compiled as a.cpp with `extern "C"` around the declarations (a plain C++ declaration mangles the symbol, so the file must keep `extern "C"` and enough prototype detail for the C++ front end to accept the calls). Measured population: **1,449 of the 10,700 unmatched functions (13.5 %) contain a `jalr t9`, and every one of them has the vtable-call idiom (`lw t9,K(t9)` immediately before the `jalr t9`)** -- and **zero matched functions contain a `jalr t9` at all**, because the C front end can never emit one. 211 of the 1,449 are already named. Next: recognise the pattern `((int (*))*(int *)((char *)(X) + K))` (what the cleaner makes of m2cs `X->unkK` vtable call) and re-spell it as a synthesized C++ virtual call, with the file emitted as.cpp -- a variant the sweep can try, verify-gated like every other.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/m2c-bulk.md

<a id="f405"></a>
### #405 · refuted

**Where:** 0x0013b6f0 — FUN_0013b6f0

A register-allocation near miss can be steered by reordering the local declaration block, generalising #395s pointers-before-ints rule to register-name, reversed and scalars-first orders.

**Notes:** REFUTED: 0 of 279 register-class near-misses became exact. The 279 are the same-size, <=3-diff rows whose every differing instruction is the same mnemonic with a different register. #395 banked 96 by ordering *pointer* locals before *int* locals in the reflection initialisers, which is a type-class rule; generalising it to `reg` (sort by the register m2cs own name implies), `rev`, and `int` (scalars first) as extra sweep variants produced no match at all, so the remaining swaps are not a declaration-order effect. The tooling was therefore reverted rather than kept: the extra variants cost throughput in the 10,485-candidate sweep and buy nothing. What actually differs in these functions is visible in the examples -- FUN_00242560 is one `li a1,1` vs `li a0,1` (which argument register a constant lands in), FUN_0013c260 was the `jalr t9`/`jalr v0` class (see the C++ finding), i.e. ABI/argument-setup differences, not allocator steering.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_loop.py

<a id="f406"></a>
### #406 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0013e9e0 — FUN_0013e9e0

A sweep-time transform that re-spells m2cs vtable call as a C++ virtual call in a `.cpp` variant banks matches, not just comparable code — the C front ends `jalr v0` is the only thing standing between those functions and retail.

**Notes:** CONFIRMED: **+50 banked** (8,792 -> 8,842 / 19,277, 45.9 %), make check 8,842/19,277 **0 mismatching**, make link **BYTE-IDENTICAL**. Implemented as tools/m2c_batch.virtualize_calls, tried as a `+vcpp` variant by both m2c_batch.score_candidate and m2c_loop.prepare and banked as a `.cpp` (the `--write` destination extension is chosen from the winning variant tag). What it does: find the cleaners rendering of a vtable call, `((int (*))*(T *)((char *)(BASE) + K))(ARGS)`, recover the object *address* from BASE (which m2c writes either as `(*E)` or as a word load `*(int *)((char *)(E) + 0)`, both meaning "load the word at E"), and re-spell it as `((DW4VC_K_N *)(E))->vX((int)(A1),...)`. The generated file is wrapped in `extern "C"` and gets a synthesized class with `K/4 - 2` leading virtuals (MWCC reserves two vtable slots). Three implementation traps, each found by failing: (1) the callee declarations must become **variadic** (`int f(...);`) because C++ reads `int f;` as taking no arguments, so the cleaners argument-less prototypes make every call a type error; (2) the functions own forward declaration must carry its real signature inside `extern "C"` or the definition is mangled and verify.py cannot find the symbol; (3) the scan has to keep the text *before* each match (an early version dropped the whole preamble, which reads as "no rewriteable call" because the name never appears in the rebuilt body). Yield is 50 of the 1,449-function population, so the mechanism is proven but the transform only covers a subset of the shapes: it fires when m2cs base is a plain word load, and functions whose call sits inside a narrower cast (e.g. `*(short *)`) or whose argument setup differs still miss. Next: broaden the base shapes and the argument casts, and measure again.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

<a id="f407"></a>
### #407 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001bb510 — FUN_001bb510

The C++ virtual-call transforms coverage gap was not the base shapes but two bugs and two C++-strictness traps; fixing them more than doubles the yield.

**Notes:** CONFIRMED: **+124 banked** in one sweep (8,842 -> 8,966 / 19,277, 46.5 %, 13.1 % of code bytes; make check 8,966/19,277 **0 mismatching**, make link **BYTE-IDENTICAL**). Four fixes, and the first is the reason the earlier coverage read as "partial": (1) **_vcall_objs regex order was wrong** -- the generic `^\\*(.+)$` branch matched `*(int *)((char *)(s0) + 0)` and returned the *cast text* instead of the load address, so the member-load base shape (`X->unkK` where m2c already loaded the object) **silently never fired**; the function now parses the leading `*`, then an optional `(T *)` cast, and returns what follows as the address, so `(T *)A` -> A and `(E)` -> E. The earlier reading of "the rewrite fires on all 120 sampled" was itself wrong for the same reason -- it only firing on the `(*E)` shape. (2) **C++ passes the object as `this`**, so an explicit first argument that *is* the object was being passed twice (retail has no second `move a1,a0`); the transform now drops it. (3) The cleaners **`(int (*))` casts must become `(int (*)(...))` in C++**, because `T (*)` means a pointer to a function taking *no* arguments -- this is what `function call does not match prototype` was (the vtable calls nested in another calls argument list). (4) `declare_stack_placeholders` now declares m2cs `sp\<hex>` slots (`spD0`, `sp14`,...), not just bare `sp` -- in C an undeclared identifier is an implicit int, but in C++ it is `undefined identifier`, which is how the C++ path surfaced it. Fixes (3) and (4) are compilability fixes on their own (they took the whole compile tier 1,063 -> 855 and banked nothing), and the measured composition of the remaining population says where the rest is: of the 1,399 left, 712 are over 256 B (too much else wrong), and of the 351 at <=128 B there are 96 at <=3 diffs (27 %), whose remaining signatures are now the virtual call *plus* an argument-setup difference.

**Evidence:** tools/m2c_batch.py

**Where:** 0x0013b6f0 — FUN_0013b6f0

The C++ virtual-call rewrite only claimed the OUTERMOST vtable call: a vtable call nested inside another call's argument list was left a plain indirect call, and MWCC compiles a plain indirect call 'jalr v0' where retail has the C++ ABI's 'jalr t9'. Rewriting the argument list recursively (and splitting arguments at top-level commas, since a nested call contains its own) closes the class: FUN_0013b6f0 went 8 diffs -> 2 and a targeted sweep of all 1,276 unmatched jalr-t9 functions banked 5 (FUN_0013ca60, FUN_0013cb50, FUN_001403e0, FUN_001d33a0, FUN_00330d30), tree 8,966 -> 8,971 / 19,277 (46.5%), make link byte-identical.

Population after the fix: 235 compile / 1,035 mismatch / 1 m2c-fail, 89 at <=3 diffs. Tool: tools/m2c_batch.py _vc_rewrite/_vc_split_args.

### #409 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0013e6a0 — FUN_0013e6a0

The residual of the C++ virtual-call class is the STRUCT-RETURNING virtual call: when the slot's method returns a class by value, MWCC passes the hidden return buffer in a0, so 'this' shifts to a1 and the vtable is loaded from a1 -- 'lw t9,0(a1); lw t9,K(t9); jalr t9; addiu a0,sp,N'. That is why the 129 a1-object functions were 0-of-129 banked while a0 (84/684), s0 (45/196) and v0 (40/122) banked: the int-returning rewrite '((C*)obj)->vX(args)' correctly emits 'move a0,a1', which retail does not have. Proven against the compiler: a class method returning any struct (even 4 bytes) emits exactly the a1 shape, and FUN_0013e6a0 reproduces BYTE-IDENTICALLY as '*(int *)((char *)(arg0) + 12) = ((DW4VC_280_1 *)(arg1))->vX.w0;' with 'struct DW4SR1 { int w0; };' -- two spellings match (temporary-derived and local-assigned); a 16-byte struct fails by 2 diffs, so the buffer SIZE decides the frame offset.

**Notes:** Register census over the 1,450 jalr-t9 units: a0 684 (84 banked), s0 196 (45), a1 129 (0), v0 122 (40), s1 85 (4), s2 75 (1), s3 52 (0), s4 42 (0), s5 38 (0), s6 7 (0), v1 10 (0), a2 3 (0), a3 3 (0) -- banked 174 = the #406/#407 yield, and a1/s3+/a2/a3 are exactly the never-claimed registers. NEXT INCREMENT (scoped, not done): the rewrite is expression-level, so this needs a statement-level transform -- retype the local m2c already declared ('int sp2C;' -> 'struct DW4SR_K sp2C;'), turn the call statement 'X(&sp2C);' into 'sp2C = ((C*)obj)->vX;', rewrite the local's other reads to '.w0', and add the class with 'virtual DW4SR_K vX;'. The yield is bounded now: 89 of the 1,276 are at <=3 diffs and the a1 subset of the <=3 tier is ~11 rows. Reproduce with sret_test.py-style single compiles (mwccps2 -c -O3).
