<a id="f411"></a>
### #411 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00100000 — tools/linkgen.py

THE BUILD IS PARALLEL NOW, AND THE ONE THING THAT MADE IT SLOW WAS THE COMPILER'S OWN TEMP-FILE WRITE. (1) WHAT WAS SLOW. make check and make link were fully serial: check.py assembles 19,277 asm units one at a time and then compiles+verifies 8,971 C units one at a time (make check 2m40s), and linkgen.py --all compiles 8,971 C units and runs objcopy on ~25k raw units one at a time (4m59s), so make link was 5m51s. Both loops are pure subprocess work (as / objcopy / readelf / wibo+mwcc), which releases the GIL, so a ThreadPoolExecutor scales them. (2) THREAD SAFETY FIRST. check.py's three scratch paths were keyed on os.getpid alone (check_text_\<pid>.bin, check_c_\<pid>.o, check_s_\<pid>.o) and linkgen.py's raw-bytes temp was dw4_\<pid>_unit.bin -- two workers in one process would collide on one file. Both now use the verify.TMP_TAG convention (pid + thread ident), the same one m2c_batch --jobs relies on; units.standalone was already safe (pid + an atomic itertools.count). (3) LINKGEN NEEDED TWO PHASES, NOT ONE. A jtbl unit reads its owner code object's.rodata size, so objects must all exist before the script is emitted: build_one builds every object (parallel, one output path per unit, thread-tagged scratch) and the existing serial loop then walks the layout in order to emit link.ld, preserving the first-failure-in-layout-order report. (4) THE MEASURED TRAP -- THE OUTPUT DIRECTORY'S SIZE. linkgen's compiles were still 4x slower than check.py's even after parallelising. Same wibo+mwcc command, same flags: the only difference was the output path. Benchmark (30 random units, sequential): wibo -o build/link/x.o = 163 ms/unit when x.o is a NEW name (build/link holds ~34.3k objects), 23.7 ms/unit when OVERWRITING an existing name, 5.2 ms/unit into an empty directory, 11.8 ms/unit into /tmp. Raw Python writes into the same 35k-entry directory are 0.03 ms, and cp into it is 0.7 ms -- so it is not the filesystem's write path in general: wibo/MWCC writes its output through a freshly-named temp file in the DESTINATION directory, and creating a new name in a 34k-entry directory is the pathological operation. Staging the compile in /tmp and copying the finished object in (shutil.copyfile, ~0.03 ms) took linkgen --all from 106 s to 28 s with link.ld byte-identical. That is ~94 s of the run, i.e. the single largest cost in make link after ld. check.py was already fast for exactly this reason: verify.py compiles into /tmp. (5) RESULT. make check 2m40s -> ~40 s; linkgen --all 4m59s -> 28 s; make link 5m51s -> ~1m10s; make link and make check both still green (split 19,277/19,277 round-trip, 8,971/19,277 matched 0 mismatching, link BYTE-IDENTICAL, link.ld byte-identical across the refactor). --jobs defaults to the core count, --jobs 1 restores serial. Remaining floor: ld takes 41.7 s and MIPS has neither gold nor lld, so it cannot be threaded; 16 threads is the optimum (32 and 48 measured slower).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/matching-decomp.md

### #412 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00384c40 — Gap::Bec::beGenerater::vfunc_31

THE GENERATOR STATE MACHINE IS DECODED: timer units are FRAMES, _geneMaxCount is the alive-at-once cap, and the fill modes are exact. Decompiled beGenerater::vfunc_31 (0x384c40, FUN_00384c40) = beGeneraterInfoWork's per-frame state machine, FUN_00384540 (the spawn), FUN_00384800 (the DROP pass, NOT the spawn), and the timer family. (1) OBJECTS. In FUN_00384c40, D = beGeneraterData and playerData = beGeneraterPlayerData: D = *(*(*(*(InfoWork+0xc)+0x1c)+0x10) + (InfoWork+8)*4) (beGeneraterData), and playerData = *(*(*(D+0x40)+0x10) + (playerIndex-1)*4) (the +0x40 _playerDataList, indexed by the 0-based player). InfoWork+0x2c is a lazily-created per-player state object; its +0xc is the 1-based player index, +0x10 is _nokoriKazu (remaining to spawn), +0x14 is the live-enemy list, +0x18 is the _genePoint cursor, +0x1c the latched MODEL name, +0x20 the row, +0x24 HPMAX, and +8/+9 are two flags that bypass the state machine (drop pass only). (2) STATE MACHINE (state at InfoWork+0x1c). InfoWork+0x14 is the active flag. State 0 = start condition: 'd' direct fires immediately and uses NO _startData; 'r' radar builds the player matrix (PLAYER/GETMATRIX) and fires when _startData (+0x30, compared to FUN_003a68d0, a squared distance -- 625=25^2 etc.) is >= the distance; 't' timer arms the 'BOOT_TIMER' key with _startData and fires when it expires. State 1 = first wave: remaining (+0x10) = _geneCount - spawned, where FUN_00384540(.., _geneMaxCount) spawns up to the cap; -> state 2, or state 3 if remaining < 1. State 2 = maintain population: FUN_00384800 (drops for dead enemies) then the fill mode, then -> state 3 when remaining < 1. State 3 = finish: FUN_00384800(.., 1) (which selects the _itemLastDataList at +0x14 instead of _itemDataList at +0x10), and when the live list is empty it calls _callLua (+0x3c) via FUN_00389ff0, clears the active flag, releases the state object and resets state to 0. (3) FILL MODES (_fillModeStr +0x34), exact. 't' timer: spawn 1 each time 'GENE_TIMER' (the refill timer; the start timer is the separate key 'BOOT_TIMER') expires, re-armed with _fillData (+0x38); 'a' add: spawn 1 whenever live-count != _geneMaxCount; 'w' wipeout: only when live-count == 0, spawn min(remaining, _geneMaxCount) in one batch. So _geneMaxCount IS the alive-at-once cap (this closes the 'inferred, not proven' caveat in enemy-spawn-system.md section 4b) and _geneCount is the total for that player; _nokoriKazu = state+0x10 = _geneCount - spawned-so-far. (4) TIMER UNITS ARE FRAMES AT 60 FPS, and this is arithmetic, not inference. Both timers are armed as ns(_startData) = FUN_00230110(_startData * 1e9 / 60.0) and FUN_003a7a20 stores it; FUN_003a7e90 reports expired as (deadline < FUN_003a7fd0(...)), and FUN_003a7fd0 returns the elapsed game time as (trunc((T>>18)*speed)<<18) + stored_negative_start, i.e. T_now - T_start in the clock's own units (T is the ns uptime; the >>18/<<18 is a rounding pair, and the timer carries a float speed at +0x10). A duration of f*1e9/60 ns is f/60 s, so the authored value is f FRAMES (60 == 1 s). (5) CORRECTIONS. FUN_00384800 (0x384800) is the on-death DROP pass -- #256 already assigns the drop format there -- not the spawn; the spawn is FUN_00384540 (0x384540), which is also the position/facing reader of #254: it reads _genePosList[_genePoint] (wrapping modulo its count) and _geneRotYRadianList, and pos = _pos + list[i] when _genePosIsGlobal (+0x18) == 0, else list[i] alone; facing is radians * 57.29578 -> degrees; it stamps the MODELCTRL command sequence (FIND_SPACE -> position/facing -> sequence (_geneEnemySeq +0x28, default string 'SQ_ENTR') -> GENERATE) and returns the number actually spawned (0 if the model pool is full). So the spawn DOES instantiate the model through the MODELCTRL 'GENERATE' command (#251's 'MODELCTRL is not how enemies are placed' is too strong; what was refuted was FUN_003d50c0 being the spawner). #255's model latch is explained precisely: the state object resolves the row's MODEL once at creation (state+0x1c, DB column 'MODEL' via FUN_003a9580) and the spawn re-sends that latched name every time, so repointing the row or the field has no effect on a running generator. Also confirmed: state+0x24 is the 'HPMAX' DB column and state+0x20 the row.

### #413 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0058a1e8 — Gap::Bec::beGenerater::vfunc_31

TERRAIN-SNAP, TESTED DYNAMICALLY: THE PLAYER SNAPS UP (MEASURED), AND THE ENEMY IS **NOT ESTABLISHED**. CORRECTION TO THE FIRST VERSION OF THIS FINDING: the terrain-snap claims it made about ENEMIES were measured on DERIVED position copies and are WITHDRAWN. What stands, what is withdrawn, and the new method result follow. (1) WITHDRAWN -- ENEMY CLAIMS. (a) "A downward-displaced enemy stayed underground" was reading a STALE copy: 0x12c1630 still held 138.00 (a value written minutes earlier) while the live copies of the same entity read 58.00, so the stale copy proves nothing about the entity. (b) Writing floor-6 to an enemy's three copies restored them to 58.00 in SOME trials and not others within 10 frames, in otherwise identical runs -- a refresh race, not physics. (c) DECISIVE: writing x+60 AND y+40 to all three of an enemy's position copies produced NO visible movement in 24 captured frames (the enemy never moves on screen), so those copies are DERIVED values, not the source the renderer uses. Any measurement taken by writing them therefore reports the refresh schedule, not the entity. (2) THE PLACEMENT RECORD STILL CANNOT ANSWER THE QUESTION (this stands). *(0x0058a1e8) is a placement record: writing Y=ground+200 to its six copies round-trips and PERSISTS for 1.5 s while the character visibly stays on the ground. So the doc's proposed test (inject a Y into the model transform and re-read over time) is invalid. (Side correction, kept: the record DID change across a walk interval in which the player was knocked down, so game-state.md's "does not track walking" holds only for plain walking -- it is rewritten on a placement event.) (3) THE METHOD AND ITS TWO TRAPS (stand, and are worth keeping). The live position is mirrored into several identical 12-byte xyz triples. Find them by a PAUSED differential scan: pause (pi-emu pause verifies), read a window, resume, move, pause, read again, then keep offsets whose three floats changed and cluster by the (old,new) pair. Trap 1: 0x100000-0x300000 is the STATIC program image (the ELF loads at 0x100000..0x554b00) and returns 0 changed words however much the world moves -- the live data is at ~0x11xxxxx-0x17xxxxx (the player transform 0x171f4c0, its copies 0x1149e30/0x16344d0/0x16921fc/0x1692208/0x16faa50/0x171f4f0). Trap 2: unfiltered 3-float diffs are dominated by rotation rows (11,012 raw hits, nearly all unit vectors); take translation rows (x,y,z,1.0). pi-emu pause/resume works via the keyboard hotkey and PINE reads work while paused, which is what makes the differencing exact. (4) THE PLAYER **DOES** SNAP UP (new, measured, and this is the answer to the live hypothesis). The player's six copies ARE authoritative -- proven by rendering: writing y+60 to all six visibly lifts the player off the bridge (captured frames show it airborne, camera following) and it settles back to the floor over ~40 frames. Then, writing y=floor-6 (52.0, i.e. INSIDE the floor) to all six returns them to 58.00 (the floor) and they stay there for 70 frames. So a player placed under a walkable surface is pushed back UP onto it -- exactly the behaviour the operator described from their own tests. Both are confirmed twice, in a run where the enemy copies did nothing visible. (5) STILL UNANSWERED: DO ENEMIES SNAP UP? Not established, because an enemy's authoritative position was NOT identified -- its three found copies are derived (see 1c), so their behaviour says nothing about the enemy. What can be said from the other evidence is only suggestive: the Goburimon are always rendered standing on the surface, and a 15-entity census had their Y changing (+0.5..+5.3) as they moved 10-28 units in x while the player moved the other way with Y -6.1 (implied dy/dx ~-0.19 vs the player's -0.224), which is consistent with ground-following during motion but is not proof. THE CLEAN EXPERIMENT, named and not yet run: author a spawn Y BELOW the floor in _genePosList (the game's own placement path, proven by #254 to be read per spawn) and see whether the spawned enemy appears on the floor -- that writes the entity through the engine rather than poking derived copies.

**Where:** 0x0058a1e8 — teleport_player.py

THE PLAYER'S POSITION-MIRROR ADDRESSES ARE PER-STATE, NOT PORTABLE -- and the way to avoid hunting them is a value scan from the stable transform pointer. (1) MEASURED. A set of mirror addresses cannot be reused across save states. pre-leomon and normal_lvl1_before_crown_enemy share NO mirror address: pre-leomon's player triple appears at 0x1149e30 / 0x16344d0 / 0x16921fc / 0x1692208 / 0x16faa50 / 0x171f4f0 (6 by the walk-based differential, 10 by exact-value scan), while normal_lvl1_before_crown_enemy's appears at 0x13b7aa0 / 0x13bf1a0 / 0x14007e8 / 0x15985f4 / 0x1598600 / 0x159b4b0 / 0x163ca80 -- and in the second state the first state's slots hold unrelated data (0x1149e30/0x16344d0 held another object's (307.63, 22.82, 277.84), 0x16921fc held a unit vector (0.52, 0.90, 0.50)). Writing a stale set would corrupt those objects. (2) EVEN WITHIN A STAGE. A stag0102 calibration made (36 mirrors from the walk-based locator, first address 0xd0d888) was already dead in a stag0102 load: 1 of 36 agreed with the placement record, and its first slot read (0.00, nan, 2.4e24). So "addresses are stable across state reloads of the same stage" (game-state.md) is true only of reloading the SAME savestate -- pre-leomon gave identical addresses and the same transform pointer 0x0171f4c0 on two separate boots -- not of different states of a stage. (3) THE STABLE ROUTE, which is what to use instead of hard-coded addresses. *(0x0058a1e8) is a BSS global, so the POINTER to the placement record is reachable from any state even though the address it points at changes; the record's +48/52/56 is the player's real position right after a load (it is a placement record and does not track walking -- see #269). So: read that triple, then scan RAM for every exact copy -- those are the mirrors -- and do not walk at all. Measured: ~2 min for the 0x800000..0x1800000 window, 7 copies in normal_lvl1_before_crown_enemy and 10 in stag0102/pre-leomon, with no input sent, so it cannot pull an enemy or walk into a hazard -- unlike the walk-based locator, whose direction search can pick "left", and in some states that spawns an enemy that kills a low-level character. Caveat: the scan needs the record to be CURRENT, so run it right after a load; if the character has walked since, the record is stale and the walk-based differential is the fallback. (4) TOOLING. teleport_player.py gained --scan (the value scan above), a per-stage cache key ("stages", keyed by current_stage.py; the legacy single-entry file is preserved under the key legacy-2026-09-22), and cache_looks_valid, which now gates EVERY use on three tests -- self-consistency (at least 75% of the cached mirrors agree on x/z), non-degenerate (not an origin/zero block) AND agreement with the placement record within 8 units (skippable with --allow-moved when the character has walked). The third test is not theoretical: a stale 7-mirror cache scored 4/7 on self-consistency alone by agreeing on (0,0,0) -- freed slots -- and self-consistency alone would have let it through at a 409-unit distance from the record.

**Where:** 0x0058a1c0 — Gap::Bec::beMessenger

AN ENGINE OBJECT'S CLASS IS ITS +0 VTABLE POINTER, SO LIVE OBJECTS CAN BE ENUMERATED BY CLASS -- the route to the object identity T10 needs (its 1,718 untyped casts each need "the identity of the object they point into"). Every object starts with its class's vtable pointer at +0, and the build's 478 vtables are already known statically (data/vtables.json, from the RTTI records), so a scan of RAM for words equal to one of them enumerates live objects; data/reflection.json then supplies that class's field names, offsets and types, so a raw address becomes obj->_geneEnemyId with no guessing. VERIFIED THREE WAYS. (a) A known object: *(0x0058a1c0) -> 0x006ffc08, whose first word is 0x0053ab70 = Gap::Bec::beMessenger (game-state.md calls this root the main GAMERAM object; it is in fact a beMessenger). (b) Field-perfect reads: beGeneraterData @ 0x016875a0 reads _pos (+12) = (-117.0, 72.0, 608.0), _genePosIsGlobal (+24) = 1, _geneEnemyId (+36) = 36, _startModeStr (+44) = "direct", _startData (+48) = 150.0, _fillModeStr (+52) = "wipeout", _fillData (+56) = 0 -- every field matching the reflection offsets and types. (c) Gaps between consecutive hits cluster at 16/24/32/40/48 bytes, i.e. object sizes (692 of the beGeneraterItemDataOne instances sit at stride 32). MEASURED (pre-leomon, stag0102): 18,810 live instances over 171 classes, plus 3,271 records over 463 classes below 0x1000000; the 0x600000..0x2000000 scan takes ~2m54s (PINE reads ~150 KB/s and every read is its own connection, so the +4 discriminator is read out of the chunk already in hand rather than per hit). THE FILTER -- there are TWO populations and the word at +4 separates them: heap instances carry bit 28 (0x10000000 | n, n a small per-instance counter), the low records do not (120/120 sampled each way). The low records carry the class's own vtable but NOT the instance layout (beGeneraterData's record at 0x63d5b4 reads a rodata pointer at +12 where the instance reads a position float; beGeneraterInfoWork's record at 0x63ca0c shares the same +12 word as beGeneraterItemDataOne's at 0x63ce94, so they share a header), and one record exists per class for 463 of the 478 -- what they are is NOT established (candidates: per-class descriptor/constructor records, statically allocated pools, a registry), so they are reported separately rather than silently dropped. Keeping only bit-28 hits yields real objects. The scanner is kept with the emulator tooling, outside this repo; it needs the emulator running, so this is evidence recorded here rather than a regenerable repo artifact.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/reflection-metadata.md

**Where:** 0x00589478 — beGeneraterInfoWork

THE LIVE GENERATOR GRAPH CROSS-VALIDATES THE REFLECTION FIELD MAP AND THE ENEMY STAT TABLE (#301) AT ONCE, and hands D1 its per-stage data and #255 its runtime list. Enumerating the beGenerater* classes by vtable (#415) in pre-leomon (stag0102) gives 73 beGeneraterData (= the 73 generators of the stage), 73 beGeneraterInfoRam, 73 beGeneraterPlayerDataList, 35 beGeneraterItemDataList and 35 beGeneraterItemDataOneList, 773 beGeneraterItemDataOne (stride 32), 27 beGeneraterPlayerData, and 4-6 live beGeneraterInfoWork. Reading those live works at their reflection offsets resolves inferentials: _geneEnemyName (+28) is a real string pointer -> "e_goburi" (and "g_bbarrelsz01" for another generator), with _geneEnemyRecNo (+32) = 5 and _geneEnemyMHp (+36) = 649 -- EXACTLY the stat-table row #301 gives for e_goburi RecNo 5 (MHP 649), with four instances agreeing on (5 -> 649); two others read (383 -> 3100) and (372 -> 100), the same name at other records. So the reflection map for beGeneraterInfoWork is confirmed on live data: _isSystemFreeze +8, _isFreeze +9, _playerCount +12, _nokoriKazu +16, _enemyRamList +20 (a NON-NULL POINTER per work instance -- the per-work enemy list #255 asked for), _genePoint +24, _geneEnemyName +28, _geneEnemyRecNo +32, _geneEnemyMHp +36. beGeneraterPlayerData reads _geneCount (+8) = 1 and _geneMaxCount (+12) = 1, matching #412's dynamic derivation of those two fields, and beGeneraterItemDataOne exposes (_id +12, _rank +16, _rate +20) for the 773 drop rows. CONSEQUENCE FOR D1: the per-stage generator table is readable live -- enemy id, start mode and fill mode strings ("direct"/"wipeout"), and the timing value -- WITHOUT decoding the igBinary instance encoding, i.e. the values rather than the file format; it is per loaded stage and needs the emulator, so it is a cross-check for T2's decode rather than a replacement for it (T2's remaining question is the encoding, not the values). <a id="f417"></a>
### #417 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x10836000 — igBinary-instance-encoding

THE IGBINARY INSTANCE ENCODING IS DECODED -- the object data section is a run of records, and every record's own size field agrees with the decode. (1) THE RECORD. `{u32 classIndex}{u32 recordSize}{u32 nameLen}{name bytes}{field values}`. `classIndex` indexes the type directory that igb_dir.py already decodes (0-based, in directory order) -- the first word is literally the record's class, so the "which class is this" question is answered by the file itself. `recordSize` is the whole record's byte length, and it is what makes the decode checkable. `nameLen` counts the name's terminating NUL padded up to a multiple of 4, so the name occupies exactly nameLen bytes; every igBinary object is named (igNamedObject), and that name is the highest inherited slot, stored once here instead of as a field. (2) THE FIELDS then follow IN DESCRIPTOR ORDER, one value per descriptor whose `ordinal` exceeds the class's K (the inherited-slot count, which igb_dir.py derives the same way): a descriptor with ordinal <= K is an inherited-slot marker and is not repeated, a declared field with no descriptor is simply absent. (3) VALUE WIDTHS, all little-endian: igBool/igChar/igUnsignedChar 1 byte PADDED TO 4 (measured: treating bools as 1 byte drops the generator pack from 72/72 records to 60/60 and derails the walk); igShort/igUnsignedShort 2 padded to 4; igInt/igEnum/igFloat and every ref 4; igLong/igDouble 8; igVec2f 8, igVec3f 12, igVec4f 16, igMatrix44f 64. Strings are `{u32 len}{bytes}` where `len = align4(strlen+1)` and the bytes are padded to len -- so "direct\\0" costs 8, "gene01.lua\\0" costs 12. (4) WHY IT LOOKED LIKE GARBAGE. Records begin at a 2-mod-4 file offset, so a naive u32 read shows every value left-shifted 16 bits -- that IS the "AREA's stream is one byte late" and "NDMW opens with a small-integer run" puzzle in docs/engine/igbinary-format.md, and it is why hdr[4] looked like a small-integer table rather than objects. Shift the read by 2 and it becomes records. (5) THE PROOF. A record's decode must land exactly on `recordSize`, and it does for EVERY record of the NDMW.AFS entry-183 pack (72/72: 12 beGeneraterData + 60 beGeneraterItemDataOne), and for 810 of 2044 records of the 0x6300 stage pack (entry 179). The arithmetic is not fitted: beGeneraterData = 12 (header) + 8 (name) + 84 (12 vec3f + 4 bool + 4 objref + 4 objref + 4 int + 4 empty string + 12 "direct" + 4 float + 12 "wipeout" + 4 float + 16 "gene01.lua" + 4 objref) = 104, the size the file states; beGeneraterItemDataOne = 12 + 8 + 12 = 32; igAnimation = 12 + 8 + (4 + 4 + 4 + 4 + 8 + 8 + 8 + 4) = 64, the stated size. (6) WHAT IT YIELDS. NDMW.AFS entry 183 (a stage pack containing the beGenerater* classes) now reads offline, with no emulator and no live game: 12 generators (GENE-01..07, GBOX-01..05) with `_pos` (-300.0, 40.0, 240.0 for GENE-01), `_geneEnemyId` 17, `_startModeStr` "direct", `_fillModeStr` "wipeout", `_startData` 0.0/180.0 (the frame timer), `_callLua` "gene01.lua" -- the D1 table -- and 60 drop rows with `_id`/`_rank`/`_rate` (conItem/insItem/weapon/armor1/abChip categories, rates 10..2500) -- the D2 table. The decoded field set and the "direct"/"wipeout" strings match the LIVE beGeneraterData reads of #416 exactly, which cross-validates the reflection field map from the file side. (7) WHAT IS LEFT. Two things, both bounded. (a) `igObjectRef` values are references to further objects in the pack, and they are NOT byte offsets into it (a generator's `_genePosList`/`_geneRotYRadianList`/`_playerDataList` read 208/210/211, while the pack holds far fewer than 208 records), and the list objects they point at (igFloatList/igVec3fList/beGeneraterPlayerDataList) are stored in a compact form that does not use this record header; that reference encoding is unread. (b) On the 0x6300 stage pack about 60% of records decode to a length that differs from their size word -- only NEGATIVE deltas (over-reads) were observed, all multiples of 4, so for those classes the decoder consumes more values than the record holds; not yet diagnosed, and it does not affect the generator pack, which is exact. Tool: scripts/igb_inst.py (--blob, --class, --json; the size-word check is built in and exits non-zero on a mismatch).

**Notes:** Folded into #418, the current answer for igBinary record header encoding; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/scripts/igb_inst.py

<a id="f418"></a>
### #418 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x10836000 — igBinary-instance-encoding

THE IGBINARY RECORD HEADER IS TWO WORDS, NOT THREE -- and the object references are LOAD-TIME ORDINALS that the file cannot resolve. CORRECTION TO #417, which read the header as {classIndex, recordSize, nameLen} and stored values only for descriptors with ordinal > K. The third word is not a header field at all: it is the VALUE OF SLOT 2, which for a named class is the inherited igString name, so it looks like a length and the name follows. (1) THE CORRECTED ENCODING: a record is {u32 classIndex}{u32 recordSize} followed by A VALUE FOR EVERY DESCRIPTOR, in ordinal order -- markers included, no K needed for storage. Value widths are unchanged from #417. This matters because the list/data classes (igVec3fList, igFloatList, igDataList, the be*List families) have an igInt in slot 2, so there is no name and the old reader REJECTED them: with the two-word header those records decode, and NDMW.AFS entry 183 becomes ONE CONTIGUOUS CHAIN OF 266 RECORDS, EVERY ONE LANDING EXACTLY ON ITS OWN recordSize (up from 72 records under the old reader), 8,608 bytes from +0x1612 to +0x37b2. The pack holds 301 records in two chains (+0xffe: 33 igMemoryDirEntry/igObjectDirEntry; +0x1612: 266). The graph is exactly the generator system: 1 beGeneraterDataList, 12 beGeneraterData, 12 beGeneraterPlayerDataList, 11 beGeneraterPlayerData, 12 beGeneraterInfoRam, 12 igVec3fList, 6 igFloatList, and the drop rows (7 beGeneraterItemData, 7 beGeneraterItemDataOneList, 22 beGeneraterItemDataList, 161 beGeneraterItemDataOne). Records tile with no gaps -- referenced objects are stored as records alongside -- so the walk is a chain, not a scan, and that is the self-check. (2) THE CONTENT POOL. The object stream is followed by a pool (+0x37b2..+0x3e2a, 1,656 bytes) holding the list CONTENTS: the 12 generators' ids in one run (213, 224, 233, 252, 257, 271, 274, 289, 303, 314, 319, 325) and then real spawn data -- positions (-55.0, 40.0, -405.0) (15.0, 40.0, -435.0) (15.0, 40.0, -405.0) with y = 40 exactly matching the generators' `_pos` y = 40 -- plus rotations -1.5708 (approx -pi/2, repeated) and 3.1416 (pi). (3) THE OBJECT REFERENCES ARE LOAD-TIME ORDINALS -- THIS IS THE ANSWER TO THE OBJREF QUESTION, AND IT IS A NEGATIVE. A generator's `_genePosList`/`_geneRotYRadianList`/`_playerDataList` hold small integers (208/210/211 for GENE-01, 221/222/223 for GENE-02, 249/250/251 for GENE-04), and the list classes' igMemoryRef `_data` fields the same kind of value (13..320). They are NOT byte offsets into the pack (a generator's sub-objects sit at +0x2c7e etc. while the ref reads 208) and NOT indices in file order: searching every base B in [-400, 400] for obj[ref+B] having the required class (igVec3fList / igFloatList / beGeneraterPlayerDataList) satisfies 0 of the 36 generator refs at the best base, and reversing the enumeration does no better. What they ARE is a creation-order id space with sharing: a generator's three list ids are CONSECUTIVE (208/210/211, 221/222/223, 249/250/251), and identical objects are DEDUPLICATED onto one earlier id (GENE-07's `_geneRotYRadianList` = 269, which is GENE-06's; GBOX-01..05 all reuse 210, which is GENE-01's). The generators' own ids are recoverable -- `beGeneraterDataList` reads {12, 12, 244} and the pool's 12-id run matches its count -- and EVERY generator's refs are LOWER than its own id, i.e. referenced objects are created before their referrer (dependency-first creation). The `_data` memory refs are the same kind of load-time ordinal (e.g. byte-offset reading of the list `_data` values overlaps, so they are not offsets either). (4) CONSEQUENCE. The refs cannot be resolved from the pack alone; the mapping is established by the loader at load time and is not recorded in the file. What IS readable from the file is the data behind them (the pool's positions and rotations), but not which list owns which array. Closing it needs one of: the object-section reader's creation order (it is in the loader cluster at 0x2e5000-0x2e9000; FUN_002e7f20 walks 0xc-byte records looking objects up by a value, and the per-type value reader is vtable +0x58), or a live session, where the same objects exist with real pointers (#415/#416). Tool: scripts/igb_inst.py, whose chain walk is the self-check (266/266 exact on entry 183).

**Notes:** Topic head for igBinary record header encoding: also read #417 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/scripts/igb_inst.py

<a id="f419"></a>
### #419 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x21307000 — igBinary-object-references

THE STAG0102 GENERATOR PACK IS NDMW.AFS ENTRY 622, AND OBJECT REFERENCES ARE LOAD-TIME CREATION-ORDER INDICES (A NEGATIVE, NOW WELL EVIDENCED). (1) IDENTIFICATION BY UNIQUE FINGERPRINT, no stage-map needed. The authored generator fields (`_geneEnemyId`, `_startData`, `_startModeStr`, `_fillModeStr`, `_callLua`, `_genePosIsGlobal`) identify a generator uniquely: indexing all 5,901 generator records across the 143 NDMW.AFS packs that carry generator classes, the live `pre-leomon` (stag0102) generator that reads `_geneEnemyId` 36 / `_startData` 150.0 / "direct" / "wipeout" / no `_callLua` / global 1 matches EXACTLY ONE record in the whole archive -- GENE-00 at +0x8820 in entry 622 (0x21307000, 55,988 bytes, 70-72 generator records). The live world has 72 live beGeneraterData instances, entry 622's chain holds 72, so that pack is stag0102's. This CORRECTS #416, which said 73 generators (and inferred the count from a scan rather than matching a file). Note the fingerprint must use AUTHORED fields only: `_pos` is rewritten at runtime (see #419), so it cannot identify anything. Method note: the packs come in triples (183/184/185, 622/623/624,...) of identical size -- per-language copies -- so a match should be checked against all three. (2) WHAT A REFERENCE IS. Using the fingerprint index to match 18 further live generators to entry 622 records gives 41 (ref, live pointer) pairs. Every distinct ref maps to EXACTLY ONE live pointer (no conflicts), and the mapping is monotone in allocation address for 35 of the 41 -- so ids are assigned in creation/allocation order. They are NOT byte offsets, NOT `_data`-like ordering keys, and NOT file-order indices with a constant base: testing `ref = base + chainIndex` by identifying each generator's position-list record through the pool (its `_pos` triple, per #419) gives bases of 88, 104, 146, 164, 182, 190 across the generators of entry 622 -- no constant. The handful of refs whose objects sit in a lower memory region show the id order is not purely a bump-allocator order either. (3) CONSEQUENCE. Which object an `igObjectRef` denotes cannot be recovered from the pack alone; it needs the loader's creation order (the object-section reader in the 0x2e5000 cluster) or a live session. That limit is now narrow: the generator data itself (positions, rotations, enemy ids, modes, timers, Lua names, and the drop-row groupings as id lists) is readable offline by #419, and only the id->object binding needs the loader. Also WITHDRAWN from #418: the claim that a generator's refs are lower than its own id was wrong -- the ids it used as "own id" were `beGeneraterDataList`'s contents (references to the generators), not the generators' own ids, so that comparison proved nothing.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/scripts/igb_inst.py

<a id="f420"></a>
### #420 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x21307000 — igBinary-content-pool

THE POOL RULE IS SOLVED AND LIVE-VERIFIED: a list's `_data` IS AN ORDERING KEY, AND THE CONTENT POOL RECONSTRUCTS EXACTLY. This is what D1 needed, and it makes a stage's spawn positions and rotations readable OFFLINE, with no emulator and no object-reference resolution. A list record reads `{count, count, _data}` (e.g. igVec3fList/igFloatList/beGenerater*List all share this shape). Lay every list record out in ASCENDING `_data` ORDER, back to back, each occupying `count x itemsize` bytes, and the pool after the object stream is reconstructed EXACTLY: NDMW.AFS entry 622 needs 7,112 bytes and the pool is 7,112 bytes, with the layout ending precisely at the pack end (no padding, no slack), 229 list records. Sanity over the whole layout: 82/82 igVec3fList triples are real coordinates (y = 59.96..71.92 for this stage), 1,196/1,196 object-list entries are in the id range, and the igFloatList values are clean rotations (0.7854 = pi/4, -0.7854, 2.618 = 150 deg). Example rows: `_data=0 igVec3fList count=1 -> (-135.0, 71.92, 575.0)`; `_data=24 igFloatList count=2 -> (-0.7854, 0.7854)`; `_data=3 beGeneraterItemDataList count=3 -> [749, 210, 541]`; `_data=7 beGeneraterItemDataOneList count=20 -> [586, 588, 589,...]`. LIVE VERIFICATION, and it is independent: the live generator GENE-00 (matched to entry 622's record at +0x8820) has `_genePosList` = 916 and the live object it reaches holds a single float `-117.0`; the file's authored `_pos` for that record is `(-117.0, 72.0, 609.0)`; and that triple appears in the pool exactly once, at +0xd168, as the block of the igVec3fList at +0x880c with `_data` = 1141 and count 1. So the file and the live game agree on the position, cell for cell. A side correction that explains an earlier failed search: the live `_pos` reads (…, 608.0) against the file's 609.0, i.e. THE RUNTIME NUDGES THE PLACEMENT by a unit, which is why searching the archive for the live triple found nothing. Tool: `scripts/igb_inst.py --lists`, which lays the pool out and decodes every list's contents.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/scripts/igb_inst.py

<a id="f421"></a>
### #421 · refuted

**Where:** 0x21307000 — igBinary-object-references

THE ID->OBJECT BINDING IS NOT RETAINED AT RUNTIME AND IS NOT DERIVABLE FROM THE PACK -- MATCHED, TESTED AGAINST FIVE CANDIDATE RULES, AND REFUTED. This was the dev-room/live session on resolving `igObjectRef` ids offline. It is a negative, but a well-evidenced and well-posed one, and it comes with a METHOD correction and a measurement apparatus for the next attempt. (1) METHOD -- HOW TO MAP FILE <-> LIVE. A generator's authored fields (`_geneEnemyId`, `_startData`, `_startModeStr`, `_fillModeStr`, `_callLua`, `_genePosIsGlobal`) fingerprint it uniquely: matching the 72 live `beGeneraterData` instances of `pre-leomon` against all 5,901 generator records in the 143 generator-bearing NDMW.AFS packs gives 18 unique matches, hence 41 (ref, live pointer) pairs. `_pos` must NOT be used -- the runtime rewrites it (#419). (2) METHOD CORRECTION -- THE LIVE OBJECT POPULATION IS 3.2x BIGGER THAN #415 MEASURED. #415 enumerated objects by matching a word to one of the 478 known vtables, which cannot see the vtable-less classes (all the `*List` kinds -- `igVec3fList`, `igFloatList`, `igDataList`, the `be*List` families). Every instance, vtable-less ones included, instead carries `+4 = 0x10000000 | n` with n small (measured on both kinds; for the list classes n equals the list count). Scanning the heap for words of that shape is highly selective (~8 false positives expected across 8M positions) and finds **60,041 objects against the 18,810 the vtable scan saw -- so #415's counts are a 3.2x UNDERCOUNT** (171 classes was a floor, and any per-class census or address-order argument built on that scan inherits the error). The enumeration is deterministic across reloads of the same savestate. (3) THE RULE IS NOT IN THE FILE, ANY OF FIVE WAYS. (a) NO ID TABLE EXISTS: if the loader kept `table[id] = pointer`, GENE-00's three list pointers would appear at +0/+8/+12 (refs 916/918/919). Scanning the whole heap for that exact 3-pointer signature gives **ZERO candidates**, so the loader resolves references during load and DISCARDS the numbering -- there is nothing to read. (b) `id = base + streamIndex` is refuted: searching every base for entry 622 (chain of 1,206 records, 216 generator refs) the best is 51/216 correct, and for entry 183 13/36 -- and with the correct base those should be ~216/216. (c) `id` IS NOT A RANK IN THE LIVE OBJECT LIST: with all 60,041 objects enumerated, delta-rank < delta-id across the anchors (from ref 916 to 1350, rank advances 269 while the id advances 434), so many ids have no object between them. (d) `id` IS NOT LINEAR IN THE LIVE ADDRESS, so it is not an arena offset: fitting the anchors gives an implied unit of 33.2, then 35.7, then 34.5 bytes per id -- it drifts, so no single scale factor exists. (e) THE EXTRAS ARE NOT A FUNCTION OF ONE CLASS: id advances faster than the record index (extras 71 at streamIndex 845 rising to 182 at 1113), and comparing those extras with the number of *list* records before the same index (the lists own the pool blocks) gives extras-minus-lists of +33, +14, +18, +18, +5, -6, +5, -4 -- it drifts across zero rather than holding at a constant. (4) WHAT THE PATTERN ACTUALLY SAYS. ids advance ~1 per serialized record PLUS roughly 0.4-1.4 extra per record, and extras grow with position -- the signature of the loader INSTANTIATING DEPENDENT OBJECTS as it walks (children before parents), not of any property of the pack's layout. So the binding is a property of the loader's traversal of the dependency graph, and offline resolution requires replicating that traversal from the loader code. (5) WHERE TO GO. The object-section reader is already decompiled in the cluster at 0x2e5000-0x2e9000: `FUN_002e95e0` is the 17-section dispatch pipeline, and `FUN_002e7f20` walks records with a **0xc-byte stride** doing a lookup (`FUN_0012a4a4`) for each -- a concrete lead for the order. What this session rules OUT is the cheap route: the dev-room/live method cannot shortcut it, because the numbering does not survive the load. (6) WHAT IS STILL WORTH DOING LIVE, if the drop-list groupings are needed before the loader order is recovered: match live `beGeneraterItemDataOne` objects to file rows by content (`_id`/`_rank`/`_rate`/name) to get id<->record anchors, then use the fact that ids are monotone in creation order for a partial resolution.

**Notes:** SUPERSEDED / CORRECTED (finding #460). The central conclusion below is WRONG: the id->object binding IS derivable from the pack. Between the blob's two object sections there is a remap table ({u32 tableBytes}{u32 idCount} + idCount u16) whose u16 entries index section 1; igObjectDirEntry entries are the pack's objects in exactly section-2 record order and igMemoryDirEntry entries are the objects' memory blocks, so id = table position and id -> the k-th object entry -> section-2 record k. The 'ids reach ~1430 against 1206 records' gap is the pack's OWN 228 memory blocks, not earlier packs. Every ref resolves in range with one target class per field (622: 304, 621: 26699). The sub-negatives below (constant base, rotation, live rank, arena offset, per-class extras) remain true statements about the WRONG models, and the loader chain in #422 stays correct; but 'arrives only by replaying the load' is false. See #460 and scripts/igb_ids.py.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 Folded into #460, the current answer for igObjectRef id binding via pack-local remap table (T14); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f422"></a>
### #422 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002e7100 — FUN_002e7100

THE IGBINARY OBJECT-SECTION READER CHAIN IS MAPPED, AND THE OBJECT LIST IS THE READ ORDER (the static route into the id->object binding, following #421's conclusion that the binding is a loader property). (1) THE CHAIN. `FUN_002e95e0` is the 17-section dispatch pipeline; for the object data it calls `FUN_002e7390` (version >= 4; `FUN_002e79a0` for version < 4), which loops `count = *(ctx+0x70)` times calling **FUN_002e7100** -- and `FUN_002e7100` is the ONLY function in the loader cluster that indexes the class table, so it is the object reader. (2) FUN_002e7100 READS ONE OBJECT. The cursor is `*(ctx+0x108) + *(ctx+0x50)`; its **first word is the class index**, and the class comes from `FUN_002fdb40(classTable[i], *(ctx+0x118))` where `classTable[i] = *(int *)(*(int *)(ctx+0xc0) + 0x10) + i*4` -- so the record's first word indexes the same class list that `igb_dir.py` decodes. It does **NOT** read the file's `recordSize`: the length is returned by the field reader, and the cursor then advances by that plus `4 * *(ctx+0xd8)`. (3) THE OBJECT LIST IS THE READ ORDER. `FUN_002e7390` stores each object at `list[i]` where `i` is its own loop counter (`*(int *)(*(int *)(list + 0x10) + i*4) = obj`, after `FUN_00301ee0(list, count)` sizes the list). So the runtime object list is *exactly* the order in which the section is read -- which is what the file's ids index, confirming the shape of #421's measurement while explaining why no constant base worked: the read order is not necessarily the record order. (4) THE FIELD READER. `FUN_002fff90` (version < 4) / `FUN_00300250` (version >= 4) walk the class's field list -- `FUN_002fe470` gives the field base/count, `FUN_002fdf00(fieldTable, i)` gives field #i -- and for each field call **`FUN_002fc740(field, object, cursor)`**, which is `MetaField::vfunc_0xcc(field, object + field[2], cursor + 4)` returning `align4(bytes + 7)`. Three consequences worth keeping: the field's **byte offset in the object is word 2 of the field object** (`field[2]`); the **per-type value reader is `MetaField::vfunc_0xcc`**; and the reader passes `cursor + 4`, i.e. 4 bytes are stepped over before the value -- the string length for a string field. Fields whose flag byte at `field+0x25` is set take the `FUN_002fc500` path instead and read NO value, which is the runtime form of the directory's "declared field with no descriptor" (#417/#418) -- the reader and the file agree on which slots are absent. (5) WHAT IS SETTLED. The id space is the section's read order, so the binding is computable *if* the read order is reproduced -- now a matter of three context fields set by the earlier section loaders: `ctx+0x104` (the section start the cursor is reset to), `ctx+0xd8` (the per-record extra advance) and `ctx+0x70` (the object count). (6) WHAT IS NOT SETTLED. Where an `igObjectRef` VALUE becomes a pointer. `FUN_002fc740` passes no loader context into `vfunc_0xcc`, so the conversion is either inside the igObjectRef MetaField's own vfunc (reaching the object list through a global) or done in a later pass; the pipeline's final steps `FUN_002e8a10` -> `FUN_002e3cb0` (which calls `vfunc 0x60` on the object at `ctx+0x68`) are the remaining candidates. Also unresolved: the MetaField vtables are NOT in the RTTI dump (0 of 478 carry a `MetaField` class name), so the `vfunc_0xcc` implementations must be located another way -- the field objects are created at runtime by the registrar from the parallel [ctors][names][descs][offsets] tables (T1), not statically initialised (verified: `beGeneraterData`'s `field_table` 0x4c69f0 holds pointers to the field NAME strings, not to field objects).

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f423"></a>
### #423 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x52F230 — igObjectRefMetaField-vtable

THE IGBINARY REF VALUE IS COPIED INTO THE OBJECT RAW -- THE ID->POINTER CONVERSION IS A LATER PASS, AND THE METAFIELD API IS NOW READ OFF THE CLASS's OWN VTABLE. Static follow-up to #422, working down from the field dispatch. (1) THE METAFIELD API, from the igObjectRefMetaField vtable. That vtable is **0x52F230**, reached through the registrar: the `[ctors]` entry for an igObjectRef field is 0x2bce90 (ONE ctor serves all 1,278 igObjectRef fields), which calls FUN_002bd0c0 -> FUN_002fe940(0x2bd0d0) to register, and FUN_002fda40(uRam00585b20,...) to instantiate -- and 0x585b20 IS igObjectRefMetaField's handle. The registration call at 0x2bd0d0 passes, per the EE eight-argument convention: a0=0, a1=the class handle, a2=0x2bb840, a3=0x2b99d0, t0=0x2bd140 (the accessor thunk), t1=0x4f6420 (the name string "igObjectRefMetaField"), t2=68 (the instance size), **t3=0x2bd000 = A GETTER WHOSE RETURN VALUE BECOMES igMetaObject's _vTablePointer (+92)**, and on the stack post_init=0x2bd150, 0, and 0x4bfaa8 (the field-ctor table). 0x2bd000 loads **0x52f230** as that vtable. This matters beyond the ref question: the MetaField classes have **NO RTTI record** -- 0 of the 478 vtables in the RTTI dump carry a MetaField class name -- so they are absent from data/vtables.json and this is the route to them. (2) THE API ITSELF. FUN_002fc740 (`MetaField::vfunc_0xcc` dispatch) disassembles to: `a2 += 4; t9 = field->vt[0xcc]; a1 = object + field->_offset (word 2, = igMetaField+8); jalr t9` -- and it FORWARDS a3 (the loader context) and t0 (the flag) untouched, so the field vfunc does receive the context. `igObjectRefMetaField::vfunc_0xcc` = FUN_002fc7c0, which is `FUN_00127098(object + _offset, cursor + 4, field->_size)` -- a plain `_size`-byte copy -- followed by `vfunc 0xd0` ONLY if the flag is set; and `vfunc 0xd0` = FUN_002fd170, a BYTE-SWAP of the field's `_size` bytes (`lhu t6,20(a0)` = `_size`, then a reverse-in-place loop). So the slot semantics are **0xcc = read the raw value, 0xd0 = byte-swap it** (an endianness path, a no-op on PS2). CONSEQUENCE, AND THIS IS THE POINT: an igObjectRef value is copied into the object field AS THE RAW ID, and the context the vfunc receives is NOT used for it. The id->pointer conversion is therefore a separate pass, not a read-time conversion. (3) TWO SIDE FACTS THAT CLOSE OFF WRONG LEADS. `igMetaField` has `_offset` at +8, which independently confirms #422's "a field's byte offset is field[2]". And `ctx+0x130`, which both object readers use right after reading each object (`idx = vfunc 0x78(obj); vfunc 0x74(obj, ctx130[idx])`), is the **46 memory-POOL objects**, not an object list: FUN_002e91e0 builds it by interning names (`FUN_002f89e0`) into a list padded out to 0x2e = 46 entries with DAT_004c2140 -- the 46 `igMemory` pool names (Bootstrap/Default/.../User15). So that call is per-object pool assignment, not reference resolution. (4) WHERE THAT LEAVES THE BINDING. The object list is `ctx+0x28` (`list[i] = obj` for the i-th object read, #422), and NO function in the 0x2e5000-0x2e9000 cluster indexes it except the two readers -- so the conversion pass is outside the cluster. The pipeline (FUN_002e95e0) makes two VIRTUAL CALLS ON THE LOADER OBJECT ITSELF with fresh out-params -- step 12 `(*(*ctx + 0x84))(&out, ctx)` and step 16 `(*(*ctx + 0x78))(&out, ctx)`, the latter gated on `*(ctx+0x115) == 1` -- and those are exactly the shape a per-section fixup/relocation step would take, implemented in a derived loader class hence outside this cluster. That is the next thing to read: the loader object's vtable, slots 0x78 and 0x84.

**Notes:** Folded into #425, the current answer for igObjectRef id to object conversion; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f424"></a>
### #424 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0052F230 — igBinary-ref-fixup-search

VTTABLE CONVENTION, THE igObject DEFAULT-VFUNC FAMILY, AND A LEAD ELIMINATED FROM THE REF-FIXUP SEARCH. Follow-on to #423, which proved the igObjectRef value is copied into the object RAW. (1) TWO DIAGNOSTICS WORTH KEEPING. **A class's vtable holds its RTTI record pointer at +0** -- verified on beGeneraterData (vtable 0x535eb0, and 0x535eb0+0 = 0x5089c8 = exactly the `rtti` field data/vtables.json records for that class) -- SO A VTABLE WHOSE +0 IS ZERO BELONGS TO A CLASS WITH NO RTTI RECORD; igObjectRefMetaField's 0x52F230 reads 000000 000000 002fc210..., i.e. two null words then the functions, which is why the MetaField classes are absent from the 478-entry RTTI dump. **The igObject inherited-default vfuncs are the 0x2ff0a0..0x2ff1c0 family** (0x2ff0a0, 0x2ff0b0, 0x2ff0c0, 0x2ff0d0, 0x2ff0e0, 0x2ff0f0, 0x2ff100, 0x2ff110, 0x2ff120, 0x2ff130, 0x2ff140, 0x2ff150, 0x2ff160, 0x2ff170, 0x2ff180, 0x2ff190, 0x2ff1a0, 0x2ff1b0, 0x2ff1c0): any vtable slot holding one of those addresses is an INHERITED DEFAULT, not an override. That is a cheap and decisive test -- it is what killed the lead below -- and it is worth applying to any future vtable reading. (2) THE PIPELINE IS A VTABLE METHOD, AND ITS TWO VIRTUAL STEPS ARE NO-OPS. FUN_002e95e0 has **ZERO jal callers** and its address occurs exactly ONCE in the whole image, at 0x52fec4 inside a vtable at 0x52fde0/0x52fdc4 -- so the 17-section pipeline is invoked through a function pointer, consistent with a polymorphic loader object. That vtable's first ~28 slots are the igObject defaults with a few real overrides (0x2ea340, 0x2fca80, 0x2fcce0, 0x2fd230, 0x2ea0d0, 0x2ea1b0, 0x2e8c40), and the pipeline sits at +0xE4 from 0x52fde0. Reading the two calls the pipeline makes on itself -- `(*(*ctx + 0x78))(&out, ctx)` at step 16 and `(*(*ctx + 0x84))(&out, ctx)` at step 12 -- gives **0x2ff0a0 and 0x2ff0d0, i.e. igObject defaults**, so both steps are no-ops for this loader and NEITHER IS THE REF FIXUP. That hypothesis (#423's suggested next step) is therefore REFUTED rather than left hanging. (3) WHERE THE SEARCH STANDS, CONSTRAINED FOUR WAYS. The id->pointer conversion is not at read time (#423: the value is copied raw and the context forwarded to the field vfunc goes unused), not in the two object readers (`FUN_002e7390`/`FUN_002e79a0` only WRITE `list[i] = obj`), not in `ctx+0x130` (that array is the 46 memory-POOL objects, #423), and not in the pipeline's virtual steps (just shown). What remains: the other section readers called by the dispatcher -- `FUN_002e8670`, `FUN_002e8880`, `FUN_002e7ed0`, `FUN_002e94b0`, `FUN_002e8d40`, `FUN_002e8e50` -- none of which reads `ctx+0x28` (the object list) in the decompiled cluster, which is itself a clue that the fixup may key off something else. The sharper remaining possibility, and the next test: `igMetaField` carries `_isRunTime` at +37 and the reader consults field flags (`field+0x25`, `field+0x34`), and the 48-entry type table means there could be MORE THAN ONE object-reference MetaField class -- so the FIELD OBJECTS on a live class may be instances of a different class than the igObjectRefMetaField whose 0x52F230 vtable was decompiled here. Checking that (read a live class's `_metaFields` array and identify each field object's vtable) would confirm or eliminate the read-time path for good.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f425"></a>
### #425 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00305580 — FUN_00305580

THE igObjectRef CONVERSION IS FOUND: id -> objectList[id] -> +0x18. AN igObjectRef FIELD IS NOT A PLAIN COPY -- IT HAS ITS OWN READER, AND THE ID IS A DIRECT INDEX INTO THE OBJECT LIST. This closes the mechanism of the id->object binding that #421/#422 left open, and it CORRECTS #423. (1) THE CORRECTION, AND THE METHOD THAT FOUND IT. #423 concluded from the igObjectRefMetaField vtable at 0x52F230 (reached via the `[ctors]` table) that an igObjectRef value is copied in raw with the context unused. That is WRONG: 0x52F230 is not the vtable the running game's object-ref field objects carry. The live check is cheap and decisive -- read the MetaObject (`*(handle)`) -> `_metaFields` (+40) -> the container's data (+8) and count (+0xc) -> each field object's word 0 -- and for `beGeneraterData` it gives the per-TYPE vtables: igObjectRef = **0x532C80** (used by `_genePosList`/`_geneRotYRadianList`/`_playerDataList`), igString = 0x533180, igFloat = 0x52FF50, igBool = 0x531540, igInt = 0x532670, vec3f = 0x5274E0, plus a second int-like one 0x533430 for `_refCount`/`_memPoolIndex`. The same read lists the class's 15 runtime fields in order (the 3 inherited `_refCount`/`_memPoolIndex`/`_name` then the 12 declared), which independently re-confirms the field offsets. What survives from #423: the SCALAR types really do use the plain copy (`0xcc` = 0x2fc7c0) with `0xd0` = byte-swap, the field's byte offset is `field[2]`, and the MetaField vtables have no RTTI record. It is specifically igObjectRef (and igString) that override 0xcc. (2) THE CONVERSION, IN FULL. `igObjectRef` slot 0xcc = **FUN_003034e0**, which forwards to **FUN_00305580(field, dest, cursor, ctx, flag)**: `id = *cursor`; if `ctx == 0` or `id == -1` then `*dest = 0`; else (byte-swapping `id` first if the flag says the source is opposite-endian) `lVar2 = FUN_002d7e90(ctx, id)` and `*dest = (lVar2 != 0) ? *(lVar2 + 0x18): 0`; it returns 4 bytes consumed. FUN_003034e0 then refcounts the object now in the destination field (`*(int *)(*dest + 4) += 1`), which proves the field holds a POINTER by then. So the id is resolved by `FUN_002d7e90(ctx, id)` and the object pointer taken from `+0x18` of the result. (3) THE LOOKUP IS AN ARRAY INDEX. `FUN_002d7e90(list, id)` is `piVar1 = *(int **)(*(int *)(list + 0x10) + id * 4); if (*(list + 0x2c) != 0) { vfunc 0x5c(piVar1, FUN_002d7d60); piVar1[3] = id; } return piVar1;` -- so **the id is a DIRECT INDEX into the object list** (array at `list+0x10`), and `list` is `ctx+0x28`, the very list `FUN_002e7390` fills as `list[i] = obj` in READ ORDER (#422). The `list+0x2c` branch is a debug/dev path that also stores the id into the object's word 3. That is the whole binding: **id -> objectList[id] -> +0x18**, and because the loader allocates in read order, the resolved pointers are monotone in id -- which is exactly #421's live measurement (35 of 41 refs monotone in allocation address). (4) THE ALLOCATOR, FOR COMPLETENESS. `FUN_002fdb40(class, pool)` checks the class's `_validity & 4` (the registered bit of #358), optionally runs `_setupFunction` (+0x3c), otherwise allocates `_runTimeSize` (+32) plus `*(class+0x48)` bytes from the pool via `pool->vfunc_0xd8`, and calls `FUN_002ff2f0(obj, class)` to set the object's class/vtable. The pool comes from `ctx+0x118`, tying back to the 46-entry pool table of #423. (5) WHAT REMAINS, AND IT IS ONE THING. The mechanism is settled, so the only gap is reproducing the READ ORDER offline. `FUN_002e7100` takes the cursor at `*(ctx+0x108) + *(ctx+0x50)`, reads the record's first word as the class index, and advances by **the field reader's returned byte count plus `4 * *(ctx+0xd8)`** -- it never reads the file's `recordSize`. It is driven by `FUN_002e7390`, which resets the cursor to `*(ctx+0x104)` and loops `*(ctx+0x70)` times. So the read order is fixed by THREE context fields -- `ctx+0x104` (section start), `ctx+0xd8` (per-record extra advance) and `ctx+0x70` (object count) -- all set by the earlier section loaders (`FUN_002e5f70`, `FUN_002e6090`, `FUN_002e6330`). That is why no constant base worked in #421: the loader's stream is its own, and the file's recordSize is not used to advance it. Read those three, and the binding is computable offline for every pack.

**Notes:** Topic head for igObjectRef id to object conversion: also read #423 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f426"></a>
### #426 · refuted

**Where:** igBinary-object-section-header

THE BLOB HEADER'S OBJECT-SECTION FIELDS -- VERIFIED EXACTLY AGAINST THE DECODED DATA, WHICH CONFIRMS #417-#419 FROM THE FILE'S OWN HEADER -- AND THE IDS TURN OUT TO BE A LOAD-WIDE CREATION ORDINAL, SO THE BINDING IS NOT RESOLVABLE FROM ONE PACK BY CONSTRUCTION. (1) THE HEADER FIELDS, AND THE CROSS-CHECK. In the object-section header parse (FUN_002e6090 reads the blob's twelve-word header and maps word N to a context slot), the object-section words are: **hdr[4] = the object stream's byte size, hdr[5] = the object count, hdr[6] = the content pool's byte size, hdr[1] = a SECOND, small object section's count, hdr[3] = the class count.** Verified exactly on two packs, against numbers that were measured independently by the decoder: entry 622 hdr[1]=50, hdr[3]=28, hdr[4]=40,492 bytes (decoded chain: 40,492), hdr[5]=1,206 records (decoded: 1,206), hdr[6]=7,112 bytes (decoded pool: 7,112); entry 183 hdr[1]=33, hdr[3]=28, hdr[4]=8,608 (decoded: 8,608), hdr[5]=266 (decoded: 266), hdr[6]=1,656 (decoded pool: 1,656). So the file's own header agrees with the whole #417-#419 decode to the byte, in two packs -- the strongest independent confirmation the format work has, and it also names header words the format doc had left as "meaning TBD" for these magics. (2) THE SECTION MECHANISM. FUN_002e6090 maps the header to context slots: word 0 -> ctx+0x7c, **word 1 -> ctx+0x70 (the object count the loop uses)**, word 2 -> ctx+0x84 (a byte count, used as `read(ctx+0x84, 1)`), word 3 -> ctx+0x80, word 4 -> ctx+0x94, word 5 -> ctx+0x90, word 6 -> ctx+0x9c, word 7 -> ctx+0x98, word 8 -> ctx+0x8c, word 9 -> ctx+0x88, word 10 is CHECKED as a validity constant, and **word 11's low half is the version (ctx+0x64, required < 8) with its flag bits going to ctx+0xd4/0xd5/0xd6/0xd7, ctx+0x124 and ctx+0xd8 (0 or 1)**. The section buffer comes from FUN_002e9f30: `ctx+0x108` = the buffer and **`ctx+0x104` = its capacity** = align4(max(ctx+0xa4, the stream's block size, the requested size)) -- so ctx+0x104 is NOT the section's data length, and the readers' `cursor = ctx+0x104` and their bounds checks and the `(ctx+0x104 - cursor) % ctx+0x104` modulo are all buffer arithmetic. (3) THE IDS ARE LOAD-WIDE, WHICH SETTLES THE QUESTION NEGATIVELY. The ids reach ~1,430 for entry 622 while that pack's own section holds 1,206 objects -- so the object list `ctx+0x28` is NOT per-pack: it accumulates across the whole load, and a pack's objects occupy a WINDOW in it. Therefore `id = (objects created earlier in the load) + (index within this section) [+ objects created out-of-band]`, which explains everything measured before: why a constant base, a rotation of the record order, a live-object rank and an arena offset all failed (#421), why the per-generator base drifted, and why a bigger stage yields bigger ids. Testing the obvious per-pack model -- base = hdr[1] with extras = 0, the generator count, the list count or the beGeneraterDataList count before the position -- leaves the class-match rate AT CHANCE (19/216 and 2/36, against 51/216 for the best constant base), so those simple models are refuted, and the base is definitely not this pack's own second-section count. CONSEQUENCE, stated plainly: the id->object binding CANNOT be recovered from a single pack file -- not because the conversion is unknown (it is known exactly, #425) but because the numbering is a property of the LOAD, not of the pack. It is computable only by replaying the load: each section states its object count in its own header (hdr[5], hdr[1]), so a pack's base would be the sum of the counts of the sections loaded before it -- which needs the section list and its order (the manifest / the `beFileListInfo` file list, entry 140; T2's remaining half is exactly this instance encoding, and this is the same load-level data). (4) WHAT THIS MEANS FOR D1/D2. Nothing that is already offline regresses: the generator rows, drop rows, spawn positions and rotations all read from the pack as before (#417-#419), those never needed an id resolved. What stays out of reach is only the object->object *binding* -- the drop-list groupings and the cross-references. If that is needed before a load-level replay exists, the live route still gives it for a chosen stage, by fingerprinting objects to file records to obtain id<->record anchors (#421's apparatus).

**Notes:** SUPERSEDED / CORRECTED (finding #460). The central conclusion below is WRONG: the id->object binding IS derivable from the pack. Between the blob's two object sections there is a remap table ({u32 tableBytes}{u32 idCount} + idCount u16) whose u16 entries index section 1; igObjectDirEntry entries are the pack's objects in exactly section-2 record order and igMemoryDirEntry entries are the objects' memory blocks, so id = table position and id -> the k-th object entry -> section-2 record k. The 'ids reach ~1430 against 1206 records' gap is the pack's OWN 228 memory blocks, not earlier packs. Every ref resolves in range with one target class per field (622: 304, 621: 26699). The sub-negatives below (constant base, rotation, live rank, arena offset, per-class extras) remain true statements about the WRONG models, and the loader chain in #422 stays correct; but 'arrives only by replaying the load' is false. See #460 and scripts/igb_ids.py.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
 Folded into #460, the current answer for igObjectRef id binding via pack-local remap table (T14); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/scripts/igb_inst.py

<a id="f427"></a>
### #427 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00384c40 — Gap::Bec::beGenerater::vfunc_31 (0x384c40)

SPAWN ENEMY SWAP PROVEN LIVE AND DURABLE: the row at beGeneraterData._geneEnemyId (+0x24) selects what a spawn point creates, and the model+stats are LATCHED into beGeneraterInfoWork when the generator first runs (beGeneraterInfoRam._work at +0x2c == 0), so a running generator must be re-latched by clearing _work. In stag0102 (Abyss of Grief) repointing the e_goburi rows 4,5,380,383,386,389,392 -> 36 (e_ogre) turned the bridge Goburimon into Ogremon, both by a RAM repoint + _work clear and by a cold-boot ISO patch of the stage generator pack (NDMW.AFS entry 622).

**Notes:** LIVE MAP (works per loaded stage): beGeneraterInfoRam (vtable 0x535FF0) +0x8 = data-list index, +0xc = beGeneraterInfo (whose +0x1c _dataList -> +0x10 array[idx] = beGeneraterData), +0x2c = refcounted _work (beGeneraterInfoWork, vtable 0x536050). InfoWork +0x1c = latched model name (char*), +0x20 = row, +0x24 = HPMAX; e_goburi row 5 -> model 'e_goburi' hp 649, row 36 -> 'e_ogre' hp 662. TOOLS: swap_spawn.py (--list / --from --to --reinit [--only NAME]) and patch_spawn.py (offline _geneEnemyId rewrite into a copy of the ISO). EVIDENCE: screenshots of vanilla Goburimon at GENE-03, of Ogremon at GENE-13 after the live swap, and of Ogremon after a cold boot of the patched ISO. The 72-generator live map is 1:1 with the offline pack decode.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-spawn-system.md; running-game screenshots of the live swap and the patched-ISO cold boot

<a id="f428"></a>
### #428 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x003d1960 — FUN_003d1960 (enemy model generate) / beGeneraterInfoWork latch

MODEL RESIDENCY IS THE HARD BOUNDARY, AND #255's MISSING INSTANCE-COUNT CHECK IS NOW DONE. A spawn row is only a NAME; the geometry must already be resident in the stage or the spawn produces nothing.

**Notes:** Controlled A/B on ONE generator (GENE-13, stag0102), same session: (A) row -> 36 = e_ogre (resident): InfoWork latched model='e_ogre' row=36 HPMAX=662 and the enemy list count = 1, Ogremon rendered. (B) row -> 114 = e_mummy: InfoWork latched model='e_mummy' row=114 HPMAX=200, but the spawn returned 0 — enemy list count = 0, nokori stayed 1, and a full RAM scan for beNDMWMdlPEBaseInfoWork (vtable 0x53D3E0) found 4 live row-36 objects and ZERO row-114. The stage's resource-name pool (the 0x6b4000 block) contains hit_e_ogre.lua, hit_e_blosso.lua, gobr_skinShape2, E_goburi01.png but no mummy asset at all. So Mummymon cannot be spawned in Abyss of Grief by a row edit: loading it needs the model's igb + textures + animation added to the stage packs (a content mod), not a generator change. e_mummy rows are 114-125 (ordinary) + 394-408 (crown) + 478-489.

**Evidence:** docs/systems/enemy-spawn-system.md; running-game read of the GENE-13 row 114 e_mummy no-spawn case

NDMW.AFS ENTRIES 622/623/624 ARE THE THREE ROW-TIER VARIANTS OF stag0102, NOT PER-LANGUAGE COPIES (corrects #419's guess).

**Notes:** All three are 55988 bytes with the identical 72-generator layout and identical GENE positions (GENE-00 _pos = (-117.0, 72.0, 609.0); GENE-03 = (185.0, 55.093, 175.0)), but different enemy rows: entry 622 uses e_goburi rows 4/5 (and e_ogre 36), entry 623 uses 0/1 (e_ogre 33), entry 624 uses 8/9 (e_ogre 39) — the tier-1/0/2 bands of the e_goburi rows 0-11 and e_ogre rows 33-41. <a id="f431"></a>
### #431 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/igb_inst.py; NDMW.AFS entries 620/621 (stag0102), 641/642 (stag0201)

THE STAGE'S MODEL PACK, AND WHAT 'RESIDENT' MEANS -- the boundary on enemy model swapping is pack membership, not a name table (settles the pinned probe).

**Notes:** A stage is delivered as a PAIR of NDMW.AFS entries, listed by that stage's beFileListInfo manifest (entries 137-177; stag0102 = {620,621}, Mummy Desert stag0201 = {641,642}). The smaller entry is the stage's SOUND+SE pack (beSeData, beSoundData, beCriSfpData = the e_*.sfp banks); the bigger is its MODEL/SETUP pack, carrying geometry, textures, animation and the per-model registry. The model pack is identified by CONTENT, not by the log name: it is the entry containing beModelCtrlInfo/igGeometry/igVertexArray and the stage's enemy textures (621 holds gobr_skinShape2/E_goburi01.png, the assets #428 saw resident; 642 holds E_mummy01.png). The separately-named lh_pack.igb/ls_pack.igb leaves in the NDMW.IGB virtual-FS tree are TEXTURE packs (beTextureCtrlData/igTextureAttr, no models: AFS 625/626 for stag0102), so the 'stag\<id>/separate/LH_pack.igb' log line alone does NOT identify the model pack -- the class content does. THE RESIDENT-MODEL REGISTRY IS THE PACK'S beModelCtrlInfo RECORDS, one per model, named: stag0102 (entry 621) has 147, enemies e_goburi/e_ogre/e_blosso; stag0201 (entry 642) has 146, enemies e_mecha2/e_mummy/e_tyrano. The parallel per-model tables agree exactly (beAction2Info, igAnimationDatabase, beShadow01Info, beHitLandDelivInfo), and the assets are igGeometry/igImage/igSkeleton/igAnimation records in the same pack (gobr_skinShape2 + E_goburi01.png in 621; E_mummy01.png in 642). DECISIVE NEGATIVE: the name e_mummy IS present in 621, but only as a row of the global 986-entry beAsTargetModelData table; 621 has no beModelCtrlInfo e_mummy and no mummy asset. That is exactly the row-latches/geometry-missing behaviour of #428. 642 has NO e_goburi in beModelCtrlInfo and no E_goburi texture. So residency = pack membership. Making a model spawn where it was not authored means porting its records into that pack (copy the subgraph, or overwrite a resident model's assets) -- a content edit, not a spawn-row change. The pinned doc's 'does adding Mummymon mean copying a pack, or just a list entry?' is answered: a set of records + assets, discovered by the pack's own object graph, not a one-line list entry. Also: the three stag0102 row-tier generator packs (#429) are entries 622/623/624 (beGeneraterData), separate from the {sound, model} pair.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f432"></a>
### #432 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/igb_inst.py (walk_directory/decode_record/find_directory_start); NDMW.AFS 621/642

THE BIG MODEL PACKS NOW DECODE EXACTLY -- three corrections to the igBinary instance walker, each general, and hdr[3] is the class count on every pack tested.

**Notes:** Entry 621 (stag0102's model pack) previously stopped at 82 of its 219 classes and 305 of 38,135 records; entry 642 likewise. Three fixes, all in the tool now: (1) THE VALUE WIDTH IS THE DESCRIPTOR'S, NOT A GLOBAL TABLE'S. The directory states {type,ordinal,size} per descriptor and 'size' varies per pack for at least type 13 (4 bytes in the small stage packs, 8 here); the old decode_record advanced by a hard-coded per-type table (CANON/13->4), which mis-aligned the record. Advance by align4(size), strings length-prefixed. (2) AN EMPTY STRING IS LEGAL: {u32 0}, four bytes, no payload -- igImage's trailing name slot is one; rejecting n==0 broke the chain at 0x13ef36 (class 218, igModelViewMatrixBoneSelect 'gobr_main'). (3) THE LAST CLASS HAS NOTHING TO DELIMIT IT: the walk finds a record's end by the next class name at align2(name)+6N, but the final class's triples run straight into the igMemory type-name pool, which has no 6-aligned name; emit its valid prefix and stop. The old find_directory_start also picked a SUFFIX start (0x2bb2) on the big packs because it scored raw probe offsets; it now anchors on the '\x00igObject\x00' identifier and picks the candidate whose validated walk yields the most classes. RESULT, self-verifying against each pack's own header: 621 = 219 classes (=hdr[3]=0xdb) and ONE chain of 38,135 records / 1,354,896 bytes (= hdr[5]=0x94f7 / hdr[4]=0x14ac90), exact to the byte; 642 = 217 classes (=hdr[3]=0xd9) and 35,144 records / 1,239,672 bytes (=hdr[5]=0x8948 / hdr[4]=0x12ea78). The same fixes also take entry 179 (the 0x6300 stage pack) from an 891-record longest chain to 28,933 (207 classes). No regression: 183 still 266 records, 620 617, 622 1206 (72 beGeneraterData / 772 drop rows), NDMW.IGB 2297. Class count = hdr[3] on 620->29, 622->28, 641->29, 621->219, 642->217. The decoded model pack exposes the whole model graph: beModelCtrlInfo, beAction2Info, igAnimationDatabase, igSkeleton, igAnimation, igGeometry (gobr_skinShape2/ogre_skinShape), igImage (E_goburi01.png), beLuaData (hit_e_goburi.lua), and the global beAsTargetModelData table (#431).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/igbinary-format.md

<a id="f433"></a>
### #433 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/igb_inst.py; NDMW.AFS 621 (130 igImage) / 642 (138 igImage)

MEMORY-REF FIELDS ARE LOAD-TIME ORDINALS, NOT POOL OFFSETS -- measured negative that blocks offline asset-byte patching (igImage._pImage/_pClut, igVertexArray._vdata, list _data).

**Notes:** The obvious idea -- treat a memory-ref field as a content-pool byte offset and patch the asset bytes in place -- is wrong. Test: every non-empty igImage record in 621/642 carries _imageSize (the byte count) and _pImage/_pClut (the ref). Taken as offsets the _pImage values overlap heavily: 121 of 130 ranges overlap in 621, 126 of 138 in 642 (e.g. 621: (167,2048) vs (1703,1024) vs (1714,2048)). They are all DISTINCT keys (0 duplicates, no shared data), so this is not refcounted dedup of identical images -- two distinct images cannot occupy the same bytes. The values are small (167..50041) against a 3.5 MB pool. So, like the object refs (#420) and the list _data ordering key (#419), a memory-ref field is a LOAD-TIME ordinal that the loader resolves; only the loader's allocation order maps it to bytes. Consequence for the mod: a same-size asset overwrite (e.g. the 4 KB 64x64 8bpp enemy textures, which ARE the same size across stages) still needs the pool layout, i.e. the load-level replay (T14) OR a live anchor. The alternative that needs no ref resolution is a live session: enumerate the model's objects in RAM and read their buffer addresses/sizes directly (live_objects.py + PINE). Related: #419's stage-pack pool reconstruction works because in that pack the ONLY pool blocks were the lists (sizes computable as count x itemsize); the model packs mix images, vertex data and animation data, so the same trick needs a per-class size rule for every memory-ref.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/igbinary-format.md
