### #434 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0037f220 — Gap::Bec::beFileListInfoManager vfunc_20..27 (0x37f220..0x37fa30, 0x381990); beFileListInfo::vfunc_20 0x36f250; NDMW.AFS entries 137-177

THE STAGE FILE LIST IS A RUNTIME OBJECT (beFileListInfo / beFileListInfoManager), AND A SAME-SIZE PACK-INDEX SWAP SHOULD ADD A STAGE'S MODEL PACK -- experiment prepared, not yet booted.

**Notes:** CONFIRMED LIVE (patched ISO, dev stage-select, fresh disc load). The stage manifest IS the runtime pack list: NDMW.AFS entry 140 = stag0102.igb = Gap::Bec::beFileListInfo (_section + _fnames), managed by beFileListInfoManager (assert strings _readFileList_/_freeFileList_/_fileList_ at 0x501cf0/0x502bf0/0x5052f0; twelve vfuncs 0x37f220..0x37fa30 plus the 0x381990 singleton accessor for the global at 0x5996f8; reflection fields _system/_messenger/_sceneInfoManager/_isReadOnetime/_onetimeCount/_fnames/_infoList/_dirList/_readInfoList/_importantInfoList/_loaded/_proc; vfunc_26 0x37f3f0 is the release loop). Its _fnames are '[0.620]/s' (sound pack) and '[0.621]' (model pack). A same-size 3-digit index swap (patch_stage_manifest.py; NDMW_LBA 252450) changes which entries the stage loads -- proven by swapping '[0.621]'->'[0.642]' (Mummy Desert's model pack) and loading stag0102 FRESH from the disc (the dev stage-select, because a savestate carries the already-loaded world): after the load RAM at 0x600000 held 'E_mummy01.png' x1 and 'mummy_skinShape' x1 but 'E_goburi01.png' x0 and 'gobr_skinShape2' x0 -- i.e. Mummymon's assets became resident and Goburimon's did not. So the runtime reads the stage manifest's pack list and residency is exactly pack membership (#431). NOTE the swap's cost is in #435 (the model pack also carries the stage's level geometry, so a swap corrupts the level; loading both packs crashed PCSX2).

**Evidence:** Running-game fresh disc load of stag0102 from a patched ISO through the dev stage-select, with RAM reads at 0x600000

<a id="f435"></a>
### #435 · confirmed

**Where:** NDMW.AFS 140 (stag0102.igb), 621 (its model pack), 642 (stag0201's model pack)

THE STAGE'S MODEL PACK ALSO CARRIES ITS LEVEL GEOMETRY, SO A MANIFEST SWAP IS NOT A USABLE MOD -- and loading two model packs at once crashes PCSX2.

**Notes:** Follow-on to #434 (the manifest IS the runtime pack list). Two measured limits. (1) THE PACK IS THE WHOLE STAGE'S VISUALS, NOT JUST ENEMIES. After swapping stag0102's '[0.621]'->'[0.642]' and loading the stage fresh, the loaded-stage strings were 'Stage0102' x0 and 'Stage0201' x40 in RAM at 0x600000 -- i.e. entry 621 carries the stage's own level/setup geometry ('Stage0102_polySurfaceShape295' etc.) as well as its enemy models, and swapping it replaced the LEVEL with Mummy Desert's. The stage then rendered as a corrupt hybrid even though the engine considered itself in stag0102 and Mummymon was resident. So a manifest swap is a diagnostic, not a mod. (2) LOADING BOTH PACKS CRASHED. The other variant swapped the SOUND pack instead -- fnames {642,621} -- so stag0102 would keep its own model pack AND load Mummy Desert's; PCSX2 died during the load with a stream of TLB misses (pc=0x80000180, wild addresses) and the window vanished, while the same recipe on the unpatched disc loaded stag0102 and ran normally (baseline capture of the stag0102 bridge). The cause is not isolated: candidate is EE memory (two ~5 MB packs plus the level), or a conflict from the shared/global objects both packs define (both carry 'g_000access', 'g_000armor', 'i_*' etc. -- the beAsTargetModelData table alone is 986 entries in each). CONCLUSION for the mod: the correct route is to MERGE Mummymon's enemy records+assets into stag0102's own pack (keeping Stage0102's level assets), i.e. the subgraph port that needs T14 (load-level id renumbering) or a live anchor -- not a pack swap. Tooling: patch_stage_manifest.py (index swap), patch_mummymon_demo.py (manifest swap + 156 spawn-row edits -> e_mummy row 114).

**Evidence:** Running-game fresh load of stag0102 from a patched ISO: screenshot of the corrupt hybrid stage

<a id="f436"></a>
### #436 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/retype_receiver_class.py; tools/units.py definitions; tools/typed_coverage.py

RECEIVER-CLASS RETYPING: 46 offset casts become member accesses from the function's own name (595 recovered, 550 -> 595), and an #include before a unit's first function silently broke tools/units.py.

**Notes:** The general form of the #381 batch. A matched function named \<Class>_\<Method> (or Gap__Bec__\<Class>__vfunc_N / thunk_...) carries its receiver's class in its name, so its first argument IS that class and *(T *)((char *)(arg0) + N) can be spelled arg0->_field. scripts/retype_receiver_class.py applies it with three guards, each of which was needed: (1) the field comes from the reflection metadata and its WIDTH must equal the cast's width, read from the generated include/dw4/reflected.h rather than the MetaField->C map -- igMemoryRefMetaField._releaseOnCopy is a bool (1 byte) and the code does a byte store, which the map alone calls int, so using the map would have changed codegen; (2) only the receiver (arg0/this) may be a base, because a temporary usually holds a DIFFERENT object -- an _InitFields body's temp_s2 is the field object returned by igMetaObject_GetFieldAt, not the class the function is named for (mapping it with the name's class would invent names that compile but are wrong); (3) both the definition and any extern-C forward declaration are retyped together, or C++ sees two different functions. RESULT: 46 accesses in 31 files, recovered 550 -> 595 of 4,732 (12.6%), make check 8971/19277 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL, baseline re-recorded. THE TOOL BUG IT EXPOSED: adding the reflected.h include to src/game/beNDMWItemDisk.c made check.py report 8957 decompiled and 1 mismatching (Gap__Bec__beNDMWItemDisk__vfunc_20, 18 bytes vs 12) -- tools/units.py definitions builds a statement from the previous top-level;/} and rejects it when it starts with '#', so a unit whose first function follows an #include was never recognised as a definition, extract did not blank it, and the object's first symbol was the wrong function. Fixed by resetting stmt_start at a top-level preprocessor line (a newline at depth 0 whose statement starts with '#'). The 550 #381 files were unaffected only because they all carry an extern declaration ending in; before the function. THE MEASURED NEXT MULTIPLIER: of 1,389 offset casts inside class-named matched functions, only 46 are on the receiver; ~1,343 are on temp_sN holding a call result, so a recovered SIGNATURE table (return/param types) is what unlocks the next batch -- port/docs/project/tasks.md P1.2.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/typed-coverage.md

<a id="f437"></a>
### #437 · confirmed

**Where:** config/SLUS-20836/signatures.tsv; scripts/retype_initfields.py; tools/typed_coverage.py; 416 \<Class>_InitFields units

SIGNATURE RECOVERY + THE _InitFields FAMILY: the biggest typed-coverage batch so far, 595 -> 1312 recovered accesses (27.7%), 717 across 310 files.

**Notes:** SIGNATURE RECOVERY + THE _InitFields FAMILY: the biggest typed-coverage batch so far, 595 -> 1312 recovered accesses (27.7% of 4732), 717 across 310 files. NEW ARTIFACT config/SLUS-20836/signatures.tsv: recovered engine signatures as name/return/params/evidence, seeded from the object model -- igMetaObject_GetFieldAt -> igMetaField *, GetFieldCount -> int, CreateFields/NameFields -> void, GetChildAt -> igMetaObject *, GetChildCount -> int, igMetaField_Init/igMetaObject_Init -> void. It exists because the receiver rule (#436) is bounded by the caller's own name: measured, of 1389 offset casts inside class-named matched functions only 46 apply the offset to the receiver, the other ~1343 apply it to a temp holding a CALL RESULT, so the callee's return type is the missing fact. scripts/retype_initfields.py applies it to the 416-function \<Class>_InitFields family, whose shape is fixed: temp_s0 = igMetaObject_GetFieldCount(metaObject); igMetaObject_CreateFields(metaObject, ctors, N); temp = (void *)igMetaObject_GetFieldAt(metaObject, temp_s0 + k); *(int *)((char *)temp + 56) = X. Because temp_s0 is the class's own field count and CreateFields creates the class's own declared fields, GetFieldAt(_, count + k) is the MetaField object for reflection[Class].fields[k], and ITS CLASS is that field's declared type. That fixes the offsets per field: igGeometryAttr's 5 object-ref fields (k=0,1,5,7,8) get igObjectRefMetaField._metaObject at +56 and its one enum field (k=2) gets igEnumMetaField._getMetaEnumFunction at +52 -- exactly the 5+1 accesses in the file; beAction2InfoRam's 4 object-ref fields get +56 and its +52 char stores (igRefMetaField._construct) are correctly left raw. THE MAPPING IS VALIDATED, NOT ASSUMED: the CreateFields count equals len(reflection[Class].fields) in 416 of 416 files, 0 mismatches -- a wrong k would pick another field's MetaField class and write a wrong struct name that still compiles, so make check cannot catch it and this count check is what does. The rewrite adds an explicit cast around the temporary ( ((igObjectRefMetaField *)temp_s2)->_metaObject = FUN_x;) rather than retyping the declaration, so it is one expression and codegen-identical: make check 8971/19277 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL, baseline re-recorded. A TOOL GAP FELL OUT: tools/typed_coverage.py recognised x->field only when x was a word, so ((T *)x)->field was counted as neither a cast nor a member and the 717 conversions vanished from the total (595 recovered of 4015); the scanner now has CAST_MEMBER_RE and the total is back to 4732 with 1312 recovered. NEXT: the remaining raw accesses in this family are INHERITED MetaField fields -- igObjectRefMetaField's generated struct starts at +56 (char _pad0[56]), so the +52 char stores are igRefMetaField._construct (unsigned char) and need base-class fields merged from data/class-hierarchy.tsv; the 81 "width char cast vs int field...__refCounted" skips are the generator's own offset/width disagreement at igObjectRefMetaField+60 and are correctly left raw. Port task: P1.2 in port/docs/project/tasks.md.

<a id="f438"></a>
### #438 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/retype_initfields.py; data/class-hierarchy.tsv; 167 \<Class>_InitFields units

INHERITED FIELDS ARE REACHED THROUGH THE STRUCT THAT DECLARES THEM (+359, 1312 -> 1671 recovered, 35.3%).

**Notes:** INHERITED FIELDS ARE REACHED THROUGH THE STRUCT THAT DECLARES THEM. The \<Class>_InitFields family (#437) also writes fields its MetaField object INHERITS: igObjectRefMetaField's generated struct is `char _pad0[56]` then `_metaObject/_refCounted/_poolName`, so the `+52` char store is igRefMetaField._construct and the `+38` char store is igMetaField._persistent. scripts/retype_initfields.py now merges each class's ancestors' fields (from the generated data/class-hierarchy.tsv, itself recovered from the registrar's base MetaObject) into one offset map, so those offsets resolve: beAction2Data's `*(char *)((char *)(temp_s1) + 52) = 1;` becomes `((igRefMetaField *)temp_s1)->_construct = 1;` and beBaseInfoRam's `+38` store becomes `((igMetaField *)temp_s1)->_persistent = 0;`. THE TRAP, measured: merging in Python is not enough to compile -- the generated structs are SEPARATE, so `((igObjectRefMetaField *)p)->_construct` is invalid C (`_construct` is declared only on igRefMetaField), and the first attempt failed compilation on every affected unit (igSwitch_InitFields, igSkeleton_InitFields, igVertexArray2_InitFields,...). The fix is the fact worth keeping: carry each merged field's DECLARING class and cast to that, which is also the honest C -- the field really is the base's. RESULT: +359 accesses across 167 files, 1,312 -> 1,671 of 4,732 (35.3%), make check 8971/19277 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL, baseline re-recorded. The remaining raw accesses in this family are the generator's own offset/width disagreements at +60 (igObjectRefMetaField._refCounted is declared `int` because the generator pads to the next field, while the code does a char store) and +53 (igRefMetaField._destruct declared int though it is 1 byte) -- fixing those means correcting gen_type_headers.py's size rule, not the pass. Port task: P1.2 in port/docs/project/tasks.md.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/typed-coverage.md

<a id="f439"></a>
### #439 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/gen_type_headers.py SCALAR table; include/dw4/reflected.h; scripts/retype_initfields.py

A SCALAR'S SIZE COMES FROM THE METADATA, NOT THE OFFSET GAP -- gen_type_headers.py's size rule fixed, removing the last raw accesses in the _InitFields family (+93, 1671 -> 1764 recovered).

**Notes:** A SCALAR'S SIZE COMES FROM THE METADATA, NOT THE OFFSET GAP -- gen_type_headers.py's size rule fixed, which removed the last raw accesses in the _InitFields family. The generator followed "layout from the offsets, type from the metadata", but for a scalar MetaField it let the DISTANCE TO THE NEXT FIELD set the size. So igObjectRefMetaField._refCounted (an igBoolMetaField at +60, next field _poolName at +64) was emitted as `int`, and igRefMetaField._destruct (an igBoolMetaField at +53, the LAST field, so no next offset to bound it) was emitted as `int` too. The matched C writes both with 1-byte stores (`*(unsigned char *)((char *)p + 60)`, `*(char *)((char *)p + 53)`), so no member access could ever be typed there and the generator reported 72 fields as offset-vs-metadata disagreements. FIX: a new SCALAR table in scripts/gen_type_headers.py maps each MetaField that has a FIXED C type to (ctype, size) -- igBool->unsigned char 1, igChar->char 1, igUnsignedChar->unsigned char 1, igShort->short 2, igUnsignedShort->unsigned short 2, igInt->int 4, igUnsignedInt->unsigned int 4, igEnum->int 4, igFloat->float 4, igLong->long long 8, igUnsignedLong->unsigned long long 8, igDouble->double 8 -- plus the 4-byte REFS (igObjectRef, igString, igRawRef, igMemoryRef are inline handles, not inline data). For those the field takes the scalar's size and the rest of the gap is emitted as explicit `char _padN[K]`; aggregates (vectors, matrices, arrays) keep the distance-to-next-field rule. RESULT: the generator's mismatch report goes from 72 to 0 -- those were a generator artefact, not a fact about the metadata. Two structs now say what the code does: `unsigned char _refCounted; char _pad61[3]; int _poolName;` and `unsigned char _construct; unsigned char _destruct;`. TWO SIDE EFFECTS worth stating because they change a recovered type: igMetaField._isAlikeShallowMethod was `char[4]` and is now `char` + 3 pad -- correct, its siblings (_isAlikeDeepMethod +43, _isAlikeExactlyMethod +42) are single chars and its type is igCharMetaField, not igCharArrayMetaField; and `unsigned short _size[1]` is now `unsigned short _size`. Re-running scripts/retype_initfields.py then typed the accesses the rule had blocked: +93 across 63 files (e.g. ((igObjectRefMetaField *)temp_s1)->_refCounted = 0). make check 8971/19277 0 mismatching and make link BYTE-IDENTICAL, so no already-typed access changed width. Typed coverage 1,671 -> 1,764 of 4,732 (37.3%). Port task P1.2.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/typed-coverage.md

<a id="f440"></a>
### #440 · confirmed

**Where:** scripts/retype_initfields.py; data/reflection.json field order; 454 _InitFields units

THE _InitFields FIELD INDEX IS REGISTRATION ORDER, AND IT CAN BE HEX -- two bugs in reading GetFieldAt(metaObject, count + k) (+141, 1764 -> 1905 recovered, 40.3%).

**Notes:** THE _InitFields FIELD INDEX IS REGISTRATION ORDER, AND IT CAN BE HEX -- two bugs in reading GetFieldAt(metaObject, count + k), found by asking why a family that should be uniform still had raw accesses. (a) HEX: the index expression is sometimes hex (temp_s0 + 0xA, + 0xB,...), and scripts/retype_initfields.py's regex captured decimal digits only, so libBecRuntimePlugin_InitFields -- 41 object-ref fields, all hex-indexed -- was skipped entirely; accepting 0x... added 113 accesses across 16 files. (b) REGISTRATION ORDER: reflection.json's `fields` array is registration order, NOT offset order, and the two disagree for 55 of 470 classes. igCollisionGrid is the clearest: given order is _cells, _activeCells, _processedCells, _checkedGroups, _dynamicCollisions, _staticCollisions, _lostGroups, _sizeX, _sizeY, _sizeZ, _maxRadius, _bound, _deltas, _rate, _maxPriority, while offset order interleaves the ints at +16..+32. The pass sorted by offset, so for those 55 classes it indexed the wrong field. It produced NO wrong member names -- a wrong k selected a field type (e.g. igIntMetaField for _sizeX) with no field at the accessed offset, so the access was skipped rather than mistyped -- but it silently MISSED the fields that had moved: +28 accesses, 10 of them igCollisionGrid's object-ref fields (+56 _metaObject and +52 _construct). The pass now uses the given order, and gained a --check that re-derives every emitted member from the mapping through the field's declared MetaField class and fails on any disagreement: 1,310 emitted accesses checked, 0 inconsistent. That check is the thing that matters, because a wrong k would put a wrong struct name in the C and still compile byte-identically. RESULT: 1,764 -> 1,905 of 4,732 (40.3%), make check 8971/19277 0 mismatching, make link BYTE-IDENTICAL, baseline re-recorded. THE MEASURED CEILING, which redirects the plan: of the 2,826 raw casts now left, 2,621 are in unnamed FUN_* units, and of the 1,830 that apply the offset to arg0, 1,777 are in functions whose name-implied class is not a reflected class at all -- only ~40 are call results any signature could type. So the next lever is NAMING (P1.3), not more signatures; config/SLUS-20836/signatures.tsv should still grow (it is seeded with the object model only) but it is no longer the biggest.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/typed-coverage.md

**Notes:** THE igBinary READER'S INTERNALS ARE NAMED, AND TYPED COVERAGE IS NOW NAMING-GATED WITH NO SYSTEMATIC SOURCE LEFT. Two results. All five are asm-only matched (no src unit), so apply_names --apply --check rewrote 5 asm labels, 0 units, and make link stayed BYTE-IDENTICAL; the value is comprehension and the port's asset loader (P3.3), not a typed-coverage jump. (2) THE MEASUREMENT THAT REDIRECTS P1: after the typed-coverage batches (#381/#436-#440, 1,908 of 4,732 = 40.3%), the receiver rule is EXHAUSTED for named reflected classes -- of 6,411 raw arg0/this casts, 6,361 are in functions whose name implies no recovered class at all, 41 have no reflected field at the offset, 2 are width mismatches, and exactly 7 were convertible (3 now done: ParticleArray::vfunc_38's _continuousTime/_hasTextureCoordinates, igMemoryRefMetaField's _refCounted, igRefMetaField's _destruct). Two routes were measured and are dry: (a) VTABLE MEMBERSHIP -- only 28 distinct FUN_* symbols appear in the 478 vtables (427 slots reference them) and their owner is ambiguous by construction (the shared default bodies of T7/#410), and no decompiled FUN_* unit is a vtable slot; (b) CALL-SITE PROPAGATION -- scanning every call to a FUN_* callee for an argument whose class is known found 0 votes, because the callers' own locals are untyped m2c temporaries. So further typed coverage needs either manual module archaeology (read a decompiled body, name it, cite a finding -- the 'decoded semantics' row of the naming table) or the LIVE route (P1.4: #415's vtable scan gives objects' classes at runtime, but attributing a static function's arg0 to a runtime class needs more than the scan). P1 HAS HIT AN EVIDENCE WALL: five routes to more typed coverage were measured and are all dry; 1,908 of 4,732 accesses (40.3%) is the mechanical ceiling until FUN_* functions are named by hand.

**Notes:** P1 (TYPED, NAMED CODE) HAS HIT AN EVIDENCE WALL: FIVE ROUTES TO MORE TYPED COVERAGE WERE MEASURED AND ARE ALL DRY. State: 1,908 of 4,732 field accesses use a recovered type (40.3%), up from 550, across the #381 (automatch synthetic layouts), #436 (receiver-class retyping), #437-#438 (signature table + the 416-function _InitFields family + inherited fields cast to the declaring struct) and #439-#440 (the generator's scalar size rule; the registration-order/hex field index) batches. What is left is 2,826 raw casts, and every mechanical route to them has now been measured: (1) RECEIVER RULE (#436) -- of 6,411 raw arg0/this casts, 6,361 are in functions whose name implies no recovered class at all, 41 have no reflected field at the offset, 2 are width mismatches, and only 7 were convertible (3 applied). (2) VTABLE MEMBERSHIP -- only 28 distinct FUN_* symbols appear anywhere in the 478 decoded vtables (427 slots reference them) and their owner is ambiguous by construction (the shared default bodies of T7/#410); no decompiled FUN_* unit is a vtable slot, because the other 1,333 vfunc addresses are already named. (3) CALL-SITE PROPAGATION -- scanning every call to a FUN_* callee for an argument whose class is known (a typed local, an arg0 of a class-named caller, or a cast) gave 0 votes, because the callers' own locals are untyped m2c temporaries; the typed set is still only the families the passes have touched. (4) OBJECT-REF CHAINS -- a new static source was built and tried: the \<Class>_InitFields bodies assign ((igObjectRefMetaField *)field)->_metaObject = \<accessor>, and the accessor's class handle (DAT_xxx, resolved against data/reflection.json's per-class handle) recovers a (class, field) -> referenced-class map of 485 pairs over 196 target classes (e.g. beTargetObj._enemyPosList -> igVec3fList, beFileListInfo._fnames -> igNamedObjectList, beMatCtrlData._chkBox -> igAABox). Using it to type the object-graph walk -- *(T *)((char *)(arg0->_field) + N) -> ((DeclaringClass *)arg0->_field)->_member, where the target class's merged reflection fields (bases from data/class-hierarchy.tsv; igVec3fList has no OWN fields, so _count/_capacity/_data at +8/+12/+16 come from igDataList) supply the name -- yields 2 accesses. The reason is structural, not a bug: the functions that walk object graphs are the unnamed FUN_* units, because the named reflected classes are the reflection/object-model families that do not. (5) ASSERT/LOG STRINGS -- the binary's own Class::method strings are the Methods.tsv source; re-deriving it against the current symbols leaves 14 (string, containing function) pairs and only 2 unnamed functions, one of which is the ambiguous twin Methods.tsv deliberately declined (FUN_002e25a0 alongside igIGBFile_ReadFile), so the source is used up. Also checked and empty: reflection.json's register_site/post_init sites (all applied; the 52 post_init addresses that are not function starts are 4-instruction leaf fragments in unclaimed gaps, e.g. igWindow's at 0x00145e80 is `lw v1,0x56f5d8; *(v1+60) = 0x145ea0; jr ra` -- not _InitFields bodies, and promoting them is a layout change), data/functions.json's signature field (Ghidra inferred types for 1 of 15,974 functions), and the per-class ctor_table (an array address, and the array's contents are not a MetaObject-ctor list). CONCLUSION: further typed coverage is manual per-module archaeology -- read a decompiled FUN_* body, recognise it against data/reflection.json, name it with a finding, then the receiver rule types it for free -- or the live route (P1.4), and P1.4 x typed coverage is not automatic either (#415's vtable scan gives runtime OBJECTS' classes; attributing a static function's arg0 to one still needs a trace, not a scan). Recorded so none of these five is retried.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/typed-coverage.md

THE GENERATED REFLECTION HEADER IS NOW COMPLETE: every class with fields has a struct, including those whose fields are all inherited (659 -> 1,036 structs).

**Notes:** THE GENERATED REFLECTION HEADER IS NOW COMPLETE: EVERY CLASS WITH FIELDS HAS A STRUCT, INCLUDING THE ONES WHOSE FIELDS ARE ALL INHERITED (659 -> 1,036 structs, 3,680 -> 9,127 fields). scripts/gen_type_headers.py emitted each class's OWN fields only (with char _padN for the gaps), so a class whose fields all come from an ancestor had no struct at all: igVec3fList (whose _count/_capacity/_data are actually igDataList's, at +8/+12/+16) could not be declared, so `igVec3fList *p; p->_count` did not compile -- which is what a port needs and what typing an object-graph walk needs. The generator now merges each class's ancestors' fields (data/class-hierarchy.tsv, itself recovered from the registrar's base MetaObject) into its struct. The LAYOUT DOES NOT CHANGE: a base sits at offset 0, so an inherited field keeps its absolute offset, and the two rules that were already there still apply (offsets are the authority and gaps become explicit padding; a scalar's size comes from the metadata, #439). Verified: make check 8971/19277 0 mismatching and make link BYTE-IDENTICAL, so no already-typed member access changed width or offset; tools/typed_coverage.py is unchanged at 1,908 of 4,732 (40.3%), which is the expected outcome -- this batch adds usable TYPES, not accesses. One trap: reflection.json keys by handle and one name (igModelViewMatrixBoneSelectList, two handles, no own fields) appears twice, so the naive merge emitted the tag twice and every *_GetMetaObject.cpp unit failed to compile with "struct/union/enum/class tag redefined"; the generator now skips a duplicate class name. The change is also what makes the object-ref-chain route (#442) expressible at all -- with the merged structs, `((igVec3fList *)arg0->_playerPosList)->_count` compiles -- even though that route's own yield is only 2 accesses.

### #444 · confirmed

**Where:** NDMW.IGB beNDMWAfsSetupData/igAdxAfsFile; scripts/afs_manifest_names.py; NDMW.AFS

NDMW.IGB IS THE VIRTUAL FILESYSTEM: its igAdxAfsFile records name every AFS entry (2,080 names; pt 0 = NDMW.AFS, pt 1 = AREA.AFS).

**Notes:** NDMW.IGB IS THE VIRTUAL FILESYSTEM: ITS igAdxAfsFile RECORDS NAME EVERY AFS ENTRY. NDMW.IGB (106,350 bytes) is not just a container -- decoded as igBinary it is a 20-class, 2,297-object schema, and two of those classes carry the whole filesystem: beNDMWAfsSetupData has _name ("ndmw.afs" / "area.afs") and _fileCount (972 / 1382), and igAdxAfsFile (2,080 records) has _name, _ptNumber and _fileNumber. So _ptNumber 0 = NDMW.AFS and 1 = AREA.AFS, and _fileNumber is the ENTRY INDEX in that archive: a name resolves to an (archive, entry) pair with no guessing. Coverage: the manifest names 2,080 files -- ALL 1,382 AREA entries and 697 of NDMW's 972. TWO ANCHORS CHECK IT against facts already recorded: (pt 0, entry 140) is "stag0102.igb", and finding #434 had independently identified AFS entry 140 as the stag0102 manifest; (pt 1, entry 0) is "aeu_opening.sfd", and AREA entry 0 starts 00 00 01 ba, an MPEG pack, exactly as afs-archives.md records. The descriptor ordinals are part of the recovered schema (igAdxAfsFile's base is igFileName, so _name is the inherited igNamedObject slot 2; _ptNumber/_fileNumber are slots 4/5, both igShort). WHAT THE NAMES REVEAL ABOUT THE LAYOUT: the stage manifests are a contiguous run (stag0000.igb = entry 137, stag0101 = 139, stag0102 = 140, stag0103 = 141,...), and after them NDMW's entries repeat in GROUPS OF SEVEN -- generatorh.igb, generatorn.igb, generatorv.igb, lh_pack.igb, ls_pack.igb and TWO UNNAMED entries. The two unnamed ones per group are the packs a stage manifest reaches BY INDEX, which is exactly the [0.620]/[0.621] pair of findings #434/#435 (stag0102's sound and model packs are NDMW entries 620 and 621 -- numbers that appeared throughout the manifest work as bare indices with no name). The 275 unnamed NDMW entries are the 0..136 lead-in (textures/models) plus those two slots in every later group. Reproduce with scripts/afs_manifest_names.py (new): `--entry 140` prints stag0102.igb, `--grep stag01` lists the stage manifests, no arguments prints name\<TAB>archive\<TAB>entry for all 2,080. The port's C reader (port/src/manifest.c) decodes the same table and cross-checks it against that script on all 2,080 names, and `find stag0102.igb` round-trips to the bytes of NDMW.AFS entry 140. Documented in docs/engine/afs-archives.md.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/afs-archives.md

**Notes:** A STAGE MANIFEST NAMES ITS PACKS AS [\<ptNumber>.\<fileNumber>] -- THE SAME PAIR THE ROOT MANIFEST USES. A stage's file list holds the literal strings "[0.620]/s" and "[0.621]" (NDMW.AFS entry 140 = stag0102.igb; the strings sit at entry-relative 0xc42 and 0xcb2). The notation is the SAME (part, entry) pair that finding #444 recovered from NDMW.IGB: _ptNumber 0 = NDMW.AFS, _fileNumber = the entry index. So "[0.621]" reads as "NDMW.AFS entry 621", and the bracket is not an opaque id. THAT CLOSES THE LOOP on findings #434/#435: the root manifest names all 1,382 AREA entries and 697 of NDMW's 972, and the 275 unnamed NDMW entries are the 0..136 texture/model lead-in plus TWO SLOTS IN EVERY LATER GROUP OF SEVEN -- and those two slots are exactly the ones a stage manifest reaches by [p.e]: stag0102's "[0.620]"/"[0.621]" are NDMW entries 620 and 621, the sound and model packs whose swap #435 measured live. It also explains why those pack indices appeared throughout the modding work as bare numbers nothing resolved: they are addresses, not names, and the manifest deliberately leaves them unnamed because they are reached positionally from the stage file. The "/s" suffix on "[0.620]/s" is the one difference between the two strings -- a flag or variant marker in the file list, not part of the pair. Verified without an emulator, by reading the strings out of the extracted AFS entry.

RESOLVED IN THE PORT (port/src/mount.c, the 'refs' subcommand): the notation now resolves end to end, so the question the mod tooling had been asking with bare indices has an answer. stag0102.igb -> [0.620] = ndmw.afs entry 620 (494,084 bytes) and [0.621] = ndmw.afs entry 621 (5,060,706 bytes); stag0201.igb -> [0.641] = entry 641 (346,670 bytes) and [0.642] = entry 642 (5,173,220 bytes). The sound/model pair per stage is therefore (base+0, base+1) in NDMW.AFS, and 642 is exactly the pack the live model-residency experiment of #435 swapped into stag0102. A full sweep of the mounted tree also confirms 2,079 of the 2,080 named files resolve into their archive, the 2,080th being the manifest own NONE sentinel (pt=0 entry=-1).

<a id="f446"></a>
### #446 · confirmed

**Where:** igImage/_pImage/_pClut; igMemoryDirEntry; NDMW.AFS entry 179 and bectext.igb entry 79; port/src/texture.c

THE ASSET LAYER STOPS AT THE ORDINAL BOUNDARY: texture bytes are fully described by an igImage record but not locatable offline (no igMemoryDirEntry in the image-bearing chain; no live igImage/igClut).

**Notes:** THE ASSET LAYER STOPS AT THE ORDINAL BOUNDARY: TEXTURE AND SOUND BYTES ARE DECODABLE BUT NOT LOCATABLE OFFLINE. The port's C asset layer now reads every container level exactly (DW4_Port: AFS, igBinary class directory + instance records, the NDMW.IGB filesystem, the mount table) and cross-checks all of it against the repo's Python decoders, but the one thing it cannot do is find a buffer, and the measurement says that is #433's fact reaching all the way up. (1) TEXTURES -- AN igImage CARRIES EVERYTHING NEEDED TO DECODE AND NOTHING THAT LOCATES. A live-decoded igImage record (NDMW.AFS entry 179 = blob 0x1055b000) reads: _px 32, _py 32, _bitsIdx 8, _bytesPerRow 32, _imageSize 1024, _pfmt 65536, _pImage 211, _pClut 1315. So the format is fully specified (32x32, 8 bits per index, 32-byte rows = 1024 bytes, palette object 1315) and the buffers are two load-time ordinals. That is not fixable by ordering the pack's own blocks: entry 179's chain holds 116 igImage records and ZERO igMemoryDirEntry records, so the block list lives in a different section of the pack and only the loader's replay maps ordinal -> bytes. A directory-style pack (NDMW entry 79 = bectext.igb, 306,654 bytes, 27 classes) does carry igMemoryDirEntry blocks (sizes 4, 19360, 262144, 1024, 8) and its header word hdr[6] is the content-pool size (301,908, pool starting at 0x128a), but laying those blocks out in directory order and decoding the 262,144-byte block as a 512x512 8bpp image with the 1,024-byte one as its palette gives a plausible index histogram and a NON-palette (every entry 239..249 in all four bytes), i.e. the order is wrong -- consistent with #433 rather than contradicting it. (2) A LIVE STAGE IS NO SHORTCUT FOR TEXTURES. live_objects.py scans a loaded pre-leomon save to 18,812 live instances over 171 classes, and NONE of them is igImage or igClut; the game layer's texture controllers are live (beTextureCtrlData 46, beTextureCtrlInfo 33, beTextureCtrlAttr 96, beTextureCtrlWork 1) and beTextureCtrlData._tex (+12) reads 0x0173f928, an object whose vtable 0x00522c60 is NOT among the 478 recovered vtables -- so the pixel buffers are not identified by class yet. (3) CONSEQUENCE FOR P3. The port's asset work is complete below the ordinal boundary and blocked above it on T14 (the load-level replay): containers, the filesystem table, path -> bytes, the media path and the texture DEcode are done and verified; locating an igImage's or igClut's bytes needs either T14 or a live anchor that follows beTextureCtrlData._tex to a recognised buffer.

FOLLOW-UP MEASUREMENTS FOR WHOEVER TACKLES T14 (all on NDMW.AFS entry 179). (a) THE BLOCK LIST DOES EXIST in the image-bearing pack -- it is just in other chains: entry 179 has 627 igMemoryDirEntry and 264 igObjectDirEntry records, and the two counts sum to exactly 891, which is the length of the pack second chain (chains are 28933 + 891 + small ones). So the in-memory directory is a 891-entry chain and the images are registered there. (b) EVERY IMAGE SIZE IS A BLOCK SIZE: all 116 igImage records have an _imageSize that appears among the blocks _memSize values (blocks have 328 distinct sizes over 627 records, 211 of them unique-occurrence). So the images ARE blocks -- what is missing is which block each _pImage names. (c) THE ORDINAL IS NOT A FILE-ORDER INDEX: taking the id as an index (0- or 1-based) into the block list ordered by file offset matches 0 of 116 by size; nor is it an index into the main chain (id 211 -> igEnbayaTransformSource, id 1315 -> igEnbayaTransformSource, while the igClut records sit at chain positions 100, 911, 2828,... and id 1315 should have been a CLUT). The ids span 211..36417 for only 116 images and 627 blocks, so the id space is larger than the pack object count and is assigned by the loader, which is exactly T14. The useful part for T14 is (b): a size match plus the directory chain ordering is a strong constraint, so a replay that creates the 891 directory objects in the order the loader does should let every _pImage be matched to its block by _imageSize.

RESOLVED FOR TEXTURES BY THE LIVE ROUTE (finding #448): the chain beTextureCtrlData._tex -> igTextureAttr._image -> igImage gives ordinary runtime POINTERS for _pImage and _pClut, so the pixels are readable without the loader replay; the palette is 32-bit R,G,B,A, textures are bottom-up and unswizzled, and 46 textures dump cleanly with their real asset names. The offline blocker described here is unchanged -- it is what a texture extraction WITHOUT a running game would have to solve (T14).

**Evidence:** port/README.md

<a id="f447"></a>
### #447 · confirmed

**Where:** NDMW.IGB media names; AREA.AFS entries 0..2; ffprobe; DW4_Port media command

THE THREE.sfd MOVIES ARE MPEG-1 VIDEO + ADX AUDIO IN A CRI SOFDEC CONTAINER, AND THERE IS NO ADX-IN-AFS (the 79 (c)CRI hits are pack-header author strings, not ADX files).

**Notes:** THE THREE.sfd MOVIES ARE MPEG-1 VIDEO + ADX AUDIO IN A CRI SOFDEC CONTAINER, AND THERE IS NO ADX-IN-AFS. The root manifest NDMW.IGB names exactly three media files -- aeu_opening.sfd, ajp_opening.sfd, aus_opening.sfd, all in AREA.AFS (entries 0, 1, 2, each 40,091,648 bytes) -- and the extension count over all 2,080 names is 2,076.igb + 3.sfd + 1 NONE, so the movies are the only standalone media in either archive. ffprobe on AREA.AFS entry 0 (via the port's mount layer) reports duration 1:04.05, "Video: mpeg1video 640x480 29.97 fps" and "Audio: adpcm_adx 48000 Hz stereo 432 kb/s". TWO CORRECTIONS TO WHAT THE DOCS ASSUMED: the video is MPEG-1, not MPEG-2 (afs-archives.md said "MPEG-2 pack start" from the 00 00 01 ba prefix, which both share), and the audio is CRI ADX carried inside the Sofdec container, so no separate audio decode is needed for the movies. Both streams are handled by stock ffmpeg (adpcm_adx is a built-in decoder), so the media path needs no reverse engineering: the port's "media convert" produces an h264/AAC clip that ffprobe accepts. SOUND EFFECTS AND MUSIC ARE NOT IN THE ARCHIVES AS ADX: a full byte sweep of NDMW.AFS (727 MB) finds the string "(c)CRI" exactly 79 times and AREA.AFS (511 MB) exactly 3 times, and every one of the 79 is at offset 0x22 of a pack header (the CRI author string), not an ADX file header -- so SE/BGM is either inside a pack's memory blocks (the same unresolved layout as textures, #446) or IOP-side behind SNDFI/LIBSD. The IOP-side modules are out of scope by the settled scope decision, so the remaining audio work is the in-pack path, i.e. T14. correction (#777): the no-ADX-in-AFS conclusion was false. Standalone ADX entries exist at NDMW 0..78; title metadata resolves ST_CHARA/AM_TITLE/AM_START to 53/19/18. The Sofdec MPEG-1/ADX finding remains valid.

**Evidence:** port/README.md

<a id="f448"></a>
### #448 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0173f490 — igTextureAttr._image; igImage._pImage/_pClut; igClut._pData; live_textures.py; port/src/texture.c

P3.4 IS SOLVED VIA THE LIVE ANCHOR: the live igImage/igClut layout is confirmed, the palette is 32-bit R,G,B,A, textures are bottom-up, and 46 textures decode cleanly (verified by eye).

**Notes:** P3.4 IS SOLVED VIA THE LIVE ANCHOR: THE igImage/igClut LAYOUT IS CONFIRMED ON A LIVE OBJECT, THE DECODE IS VERIFIED BY EYE, AND 46 TEXTURES DUMP CLEANLY. The offline path stays blocked (#433/#446 -- buffers are load-time ordinals), but the runtime holds ordinary pointers, and the whole chain is now implemented (live_textures.py, feeding the port's decoder in port/src/texture.c). THE CHAIN: a live beTextureCtrlData (46 instances) has _tex (+12) -> an igTextureAttr, whose _image (+48) is an igImage; that object's _pImage (+52) is the pixel buffer and _pClut (+68) the igClut object, whose _pData (+20) is the palette. THE igImage LAYOUT IS CONFIRMED FIELD-FOR-FIELD on a live object at 0x0173f490: _px 256, _py 128, _pz 1, _ps 1, _order 102, _pfmt 0x00010000, _imageSize 32768 (= 256*128, i.e. 8 bits per index), _pImage 0x0175bdc0 (a resolved pointer), _pClut 0x0173f8f8, _bitsIdx 8, _bytesPerRow 256, _compressed 0, and _pNameString 0x006a6854 -> "stmojipnl01.png". Its igClut reads _fmt 7, _numEntries 256, _stride 4, _pData 0x0173f4f0, _clutSize 1024 -- so the palette is 32-bit, 4 bytes per entry, memory order R,G,B,A (that is the layout that decodes; PS2 CSM1 16-bit does not fit _stride 4 / _clutSize 1024). Two more facts the pixels settle: TEXTURES ARE STORED BOTTOM-UP (the atlas and the text textures read correctly only after a vertical flip), and _pfmt 0x00010000 with _bitsIdx 8 is the whole format -- all 46 reachable textures share it, and their _bytesPerRow equals _px, so the source is a plain linear 8-bit indexed image, not a swizzled one. VERIFIED BY EYE, not by a checksum: stmojipnl01.png is the status-screen label atlas (Strength/Defense/Wisdom/Spirit/Speed/LV/Max MP/Max HP/Tech/Skill/EXP, the 1-4 digits, fire/skull/crown/star/sword/lightning/key/eye icons, arrows and a colour bar), stcngmoji.png reads "Go to the destination." / "Stay here.", P12W00.png is a smooth radial gradient and FD01BJ05.png a set of colour ramps -- all correct, which is what makes the palette order and the flip settled rather than guessed. A side effect worth having: the images carry their real asset names (base01.png, num02.png, TA01BJ10.png, P12W00.png, stmojipnl01.png,...), so a texture can be identified without the pack. One correction for the port's RTTI note: igImage and igClut do NOT appear in the 478 recovered vtables, and a live igTextureAttr's vtable is 0x00522c60, which is not among them either -- that is why the live object scan (which keys on those 478) reports zero of each, and why the chain above had to be followed from beTextureCtrlData instead. Tools: live_textures.py --list/--dump/--all; the 46 textures were decoded to PNGs.

**Evidence:** port/README.md

<a id="f449"></a>
### #449 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00526590 — igVertexArray._vdata/_numVerts/_vtype; igIndexArray; beModelCtrlNode2; live heap scan

MODELS (P3.5) NOT SOLVED: the live route finds 28 large {ptr,count,type,flags} objects (vtable 0x00526590, counts 107..711) but their buffers are small integers (index-like), and igIndexArray/igVertexArray reflection offsets do not match the live layout.

**Notes:** MODELS (P3.5): THE LIVE ROUTE REACHES ARRAY OBJECTS BUT NOT A VERTEX POSITION BUFFER, AND THE REFLECTION OFFSETS DO NOT MATCH THE LIVE LAYOUT. Attempted after the texture win (#448) and NOT solved. What is measured. (1) A SHAPE TEST FINDS THE RIGHT-LOOKING OBJECTS: scanning the heap (0x1000000..0x1F00000) for a 24-byte header {code-pointer, instance-word-with-bit-28, heap-data-pointer, count 100..200000, small type, flags} finds 28 objects, all of one class (vtable 0x00526590), with counts 107..711 (largest 711, then 592, 541, 468, 426, 420, 406, 350, 331, 326,...). A graph walk from a live beModelCtrlNode2 finds the same shape at smaller counts (4..51). So the class is real and locatable. (2) BUT THE BUFFER IS NOT A POSITION ARRAY: for the 426-count instance the buffer is 426 words of SMALL INTEGERS (values 3 and 4 dominate, plus 6, 8, 10, 11), not floats -- and for the small instances it is a run like 13,14,13,14,...,29,29, which is a triangle-strip index list. A per-vertex position buffer cannot look like that; these read as INDEX arrays. (3) THE REFLECTION OFFSETS DO NOT FIT THE LIVE OBJECT: igIndexArray is reflected as _memPool(+8, igObjectRef), _indexData(+12, igMemoryRef), _numIndices(+16), _dataSize(+20), _usageFlags(+24) -- i.e. a data pointer at +12 -- but the live objects hold a DATA POINTER at +8 (verified: 0x013f9e98 holds 13,14,13,14,... with no vtable, so it is not the pool object the reflection claims is there) and a count at +12. igVertexArray is reflected as _vdata(+8), _numVerts(+12), _vtype(+16), _usageFlags(+20) -- which fits the header shape, yet the same class's buffers are index-like, so the two classes' live layouts cannot be told apart from reflection alone. (4) THE ENGINE'S ig* CLASSES ARE NOT IN THE RECOVERED TABLE: igVertexArray/igIndexArray/igGeometryAttr have no entries among the 478 vtables and a vtable-keyed live scan reports zero instances of each; a live igImage's vtable (0x00525630) and a live igTextureAttr's (0x00522c60) are likewise absent (#448). So an object's class cannot be looked up by vtable here and must be recovered per class. WHAT WOULD UNBLOCK IT (for whoever continues): the class identity of vtable 0x00526590 (index array is the leading candidate) and of the vertex-array class -- the discriminator to look for is a buffer whose stride is 16 or 32 bytes and whose words parse as packed PS2 vertex data (or as floats in a plausible coordinate range), which none of the 28 current candidates is. The `igNode` chain from a live beModelCtrlNode2 also has to be mapped to whatever object owns the render list (igGeometry._renderList, +40), since the node's own reflected fields (+12 _bound, +16 _parentList, +28 _childList, +100 _mvm, +104 _transformInput, +136 _overrideAttrList) carry no attribute set. REFUTED (#455): the live route DOES yield usable geometry. The 28 {ptr,count,type,flags} objects are not the mesh; the mesh is igGeometryAttr1_5 -> igVertexArray (12-byte vec3f positions) plus a strip-length array, and 669 models extract from the loaded scene with passing self-checks. Folded into #455, the current answer for Live model geometry chain; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/model-format.md

<a id="f450"></a>
### #450 · confirmed

**Where:** NDMW.AFS entry 620 (stag0102 sound pack); dw4asset sfp; port/tools/verify_sound.py

A STAGE'S SOUND EFFECTS ARE CRI ps2_ BANKS INSIDE ITS SOUND PACK, AND THE VAG ADPCM DECODES EXACTLY OFFLINE (no load-time ordinal to resolve).

**Notes:** A stage is delivered as a PAIR of NDMW.AFS entries (#431): for stag0102, the smaller entry 620 (494,084 bytes) is the sound+SE pack, the bigger 621 (5,060,706 bytes) the model pack; stag0201 = 641/642. The sound pack is an igBinary container (magic 0x40c, version 38, 29 classes, 617-record chain at +0x18a0..+0x7748) whose CONTENT POOL (hdr[6] = 463,548 bytes, +0x7748..end) is almost entirely six CRI sound banks: a 580-byte u32 sound-ID list, then bank0 @0x798c (49,176 B), bank1 @0x139a4 (51,200), bank2 @0x201a4 (41,548), bank3 @0x2a3f0 (22,688), bank4 @0x2fc90 (89,460), bank5 @0x45a04 (208,896). Each bank is a self-contained.sfp file with three chunks: ps2_DTPK at +0x000 (16-byte header: pcm_size +0x0c, a field +0x10, vag_size +0x18, header_size +0x1c = 0x800), ps2_TBLD at +0x060 (decode tables plus a trailing 16-byte-per-sound record table {u16 x, u16 rate, u32 a, u32 end, u32 0}), and ps2_VAGD at +0x800 (0x20-byte header then the ADPCM). The pack's six beCriSfpData records NAME the banks (e_goburi.sfp, e_ogre.sfp, common_blst.sfp, scannon.sfp, stag0102.sfp, e_blosso.sfp) and their _pcmSize values match the DTPK size field EXACTLY (49152/51200/40960/20480/88064/208896), so the name set is recovered, not guessed. The record table's `end` fields tile the VAGD chunk exactly (entry i = [end_{i-1}, end_i), starting at 0x20), giving 5/7/4/2/6/9 sounds per bank. The ADPCM is Sony VAG with three details a stock decoder gets wrong, each pinned by matching ffmpeg adpcm_psx sample-for-sample: byte 0 is shift(low)/filter(high); byte 1 is a CONTROL byte so the 28 samples are the low-nibble-first nibbles of bytes 2..15; control 0x07 silences the frame and resets the predictor (the end-of-sound padding), 0x00 normal, 0x01 on an entry's last audio frame; the predictor feeds back the UNCLAMPED sample and the prediction is a truncating /64. Coefficients are the standard VAG pairs. Rate 24000 Hz for all banks. VERIFIED: all 6 banks, 773,472 samples, 0 differences against ffmpeg (port/tools/verify_sound.py, wired into the port's make check). The negative that made this look IOP-side is #447: there is no ADX-in-AFS (the 79+3 (c)CRI hits are pack-header author strings). OPEN: the entry->name map -- and the meaning of the record x/a fields. CONSEQUENCE: the port's sound path (P3.6) is a REGULAR asset loader with no live anchor or load-level replay; unlike textures (T13/live anchor) and models (blocked, #449).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/sound-format.md

<a id="f451"></a>
### #451 · unconfirmed

**Where:** pack 620 beSoundData records (slot 6); reflection field _memoryIdx +20 (int)

beSoundData._memoryIdx IS A PACKED 4-BYTE FIELD, NOT A PLAIN OFFSET: over the 101 records of NDMW.AFS entry 620 its low byte is 0xa9 (or 0xffff), the next byte is 0..3, and its high 16 bits are 0..19 (or 0xffff).

**Notes:** Measured by decoding entry 620's 101 beSoundData records (dw4asset records --class beSoundData). Slot 6 is _memoryIdx (reflection: +20, igIntMetaField; _type/_fnameIdx/_channel/_volume/_priority decode as slots 4/5/7/8/9). Values are bytes [a9][b][lo][hi] little-endian: e.g. cmFIRE a9 00 0e 00, WINDOW_CLOSE2 a9 02 08 00, mlGATE01 a9 03 06 00, OBJ_DARKTOWER_DEAD a9 03 03 00, foot_lp1 a9 01 00 00, foot_lp2 a9 01 01 00; the BT_*/AM_*/ST_* records are 0xffffffff (unset). So low byte 0xa9 is a constant tag, the second byte ranges 0..3, and the top 16 bits range 0..19 (the number of entries in the largest bank, e_blosso, is 9, and the banks hold 5/7/4/2/6/9 sounds -- so 'top16 = entry index' is a hypothesis, not established). NOT yet the entry->name map: whether byte 1 is a bank (only 0..3 of 6) or something else, and which bank order it indexes, needs the EE-side sound setup code. Recorded so the shape does not have to be re-derived. The rest of the sound path is solved (#450).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/sound-format.md

### #452 · confirmed

**Where:** NDMW.AFS entry 620 beSoundData/beCriSfpData records; live beSoundInfo._fnameList (pre-leomon); port/src/sfp.c dw4_sfp_bank_names/dw4_sfp_sounds

beSoundData._fnameIdx selects the bank via beSoundInfo._fnameList; _memoryIdx selects a resident sequence group/index, not the waveform directly (#783). beCriSfpData._pcmSize names each bank.

**Notes:** THE SOUND NAME -> (BANK, ENTRY) MAPPING IS RESOLVED. beSoundData.name is the sound name; beSoundData._fnameIdx selects a bank through the stage's beSoundInfo._fnameList, and beSoundData._memoryIdx's HIGH 16 BITS is the entry index within that bank (the low byte is a constant 0xa9 tag, the next byte unchecked; 0xffffffff = unset). MEASURED LIVE (pre-leomon/stag0102): the beSoundInfo object 0x0111b208 has _fnameList (+28) -> list object 0x01122770 {_count 11, _capacity 11, _data 0x01123bf0}; the 11 entries resolve to: [0] sound/sfp/PS/allse.sfp, [1] sound/sfp/PS/COMMON_BLST.sfp, [2] [0.55], [3] [0.30], [4] [0.33], [5] [0.23], [6] sound/sfp/PS/STAG0102.sfp, [7] sound/sfp/PS/scannon.sfp, [8] sound/sfp/PS/e_ogre.sfp, [9] sound/sfp/PS/e_goburi.sfp, [10] sound/sfp/PS/e_blosso.sfp. The four [pt.entry] entries are the notation of #445; allse.sfp is a 64-sound GLOBAL bank that is NOT in the stage pack. CONSISTENCY: the 101 beSoundData records of pack 620 group by _fnameIdx into sizes 64/4/1/1/1/1/6/2/7/5/9, and the six stage banks' entry counts (from the packed TBLD record table) are 4/6/2/7/5/9 -- a UNIQUE 1:1 match, which is how the offline reader recovers the mapping without the live list: each _fnameIdx group is matched to the one bank whose entry count equals the group's size, and a group matching no bank (allse, 64) stays external. The bank NAMES come from the pack itself, exactly: beCriSfpData._pcmSize equals the bank's DTPK size field (49152/51200/40960/20480/88064/208896 -> e_goburi/e_ogre/common_blst/scannon/stag0102/e_blosso). RESULT (port/src/sfp.c + dw4asset sfp, finding-grade verified): the 33 stage entries are all named and semantically consistent with their bank -- go*=e_goburi (goDEAD01, goLDMG01, goNATK01/02, goSDMG01), og*=e_ogre (7), bl*=e_blosso (9), common_blst 4 blast sounds, scannon 2, stag0102.sfp 6 stage-object sounds (TARU_BLST01, OBJ_TORCH_CRASH, drOFF2ON/drON2OFF, OBJ_GLASS01, OBJ_TREE01). tools/verify_sound.py asserts the names. OPEN: allse.sfp's 64 sounds live in a bank outside the stage pack (not located); its own name->entry needs allse.sfp found and read. correction (#783): _memoryIdx high16 is a sequence index, not a VAG waveform index. Low16 selects the sequence group. Resolve sequence note through tone table; previous direct waveform numbering is superseded. Filename/model-action recovery remains valid.

<a id="f453"></a>
### #453 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0140f908 — live pre-leomon; segment table 0x00944b38; beModelCtrlInfoRamData 0x01263fd0 (vtable 0x537bb0); GetVTable 0x14a860/0x14a560; vtable 0x00526590 (GetVTable 0x14be30)

LIVE MODEL GEOMETRY IS A PACKED vec3f ARRAY (12-byte stride) in the heap (0x0140f908: 1,821 vertices, character-scale bbox), reached through a per-mesh segment table at 0x00944b38 -- the #449 probe's 16/32-byte-stride assumption was the blocker.

**Notes:** THE #449 PROBE IS ANSWERED: LIVE MODEL GEOMETRY IS A PACKED vec3f ARRAY (12-BYTE STRIDE), NOT 16/32-BYTE -- which is why the earlier probe missed it. MEASURED (pre-leomon/stag0102, paused-free live RAM): (1) vtable 0x00526590 has 19 live heap instances with the shape {vt, 0x10000001, ptr(+8), count(+12), 0x20(+16), flags(+20)}; their +8 buffers are NOT floats -- the first words are 3s/4s (index-like) then object pointers ({00531c90,10000002} etc.), i.e. a list/index structure, so this is not the vertex class. Its GetVTable is 0x14be30 (stores 0x526590) and its registrar is 0x14be80 (calls igMetaObject_RegisterClass 0x2fe9c0 with MetaObject handle 0x55fb00 = igPrimLengthArray1_1). (2) igVertexArray2's GetVTable (0x14a860) returns vtable 0x526410 and igVertexArray2Helper's (0x14a560) returns 0x5262a0 -- but 0x526410 appears ONLY at 0x5eb66c in RAM (no live instances), and 0x5262a0 nowhere. So the live geometry is not in an igVertexArray2. (3) An exhaustive float scan of the 8 MB heap (strides 12/16/24/32/36/48) found a contiguous triangle list of vec3f positions at 0x0140f908: 1,821 vertices (21,852 bytes = 1,821*12 + 4 pad), bbox x[-9.93,7.25] y[-0.99,6.93] z[-4.09,2.95] (~17 wide, ~8 tall -- character scale), 1,510 UNIQUE positions with vertices repeated in fan/strip order. Sample: (6.576,4.313,-3.987) (5.637,4.358,-3.460) (6.499,3.571,-3.955) (5.571,3.750,-3.430)... (4) The segment's start is referenced twice: by a pointer TABLE at 0x00944b38 whose entries are 0x0140f908, 0x01414e68, 0x0141a3c8, 0x0141dcb8, 0x0141f588,... (stride 0x5560, 0x5560, 0x38f0, 0x18d0, 0x18d0, 0x1090 -- a per-mesh segment list), and by the word at 0x01263ff0 (object at 0x01263fd0, vtable 0x537bb0 = beModelCtrlInfoRamData). CONSEQUENCE FOR P3.5: the live route to geometry exists and needs no loader replay -- read the segment table (or the per-model RamData), take each segment's first array as vec3f positions. OPEN (next probe): the table's owner and lifetime, whether it is per-stage or per-model, how to walk from a specific beModelCtrlNode2 to its segment, and the layout of the bytes AFTER the positions in each segment (normals/uvs/indices -- the segment 0x0140f908..0x01414e68 is 0x5560 bytes, of which only the first 0x5554 are positions). CORRECTED (#455): the 1821-vertex vec3f buffer at 0x0140f908 is real geometry, but it belongs to geometry attr 0x01261ac8 (one model), not to beModelCtrlInfoRamData -- see #455 for the class chain and the strip/list connectivity. Folded into #455, the current answer for Live model geometry chain; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/model-format.md

<a id="f454"></a>
### #454 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00798c — live pre-leomon/stag0102; beModelCtrlInfo 0x537c10, beModelCtrlNode2 0x537f40, beModelCtrlInfoRamData 0x537bb0; model classes 0x5284f0/0x528e70/0x528dd0/0x5294b0

THE LIVE MODEL GRAPH: named beModelCtrlInfo registry (g_*), a G_*/M_* node tree, and per-instance beModelCtrlInfoRamData holding 12-byte-stride vec3f positions+normals and an igPrimLengthArray (strip lengths 3/4/7/10/12) -- but NOT the index array, so triangle connectivity is still unresolved.

**Notes:** THE LIVE MODEL GRAPH IS MAPPED; THE VERTEX DATA IS LOCATED, THE TRIANGLE CONNECTIVITY IS NOT. STEP 1 -- the named registry: the 148 live beModelCtrlInfo objects (vtable 0x537c10) are the per-model registry, named by _name(+8): g_000bcaps, g_000bit, g_barrelshotz01, g_torchz01, g_bush01z01, g_rock01z01, g_rock02z01, g_wall02z01, g_security, g_woodencasez01... Each has _dataList(+20) = the per-model control data (beModelCtrlInfoDataHit 'HIT', beModelCtrlInfoDataSeq 'SEQ') and _ramList(+24) = a list of beBaseInfoRam (vtable 0x539e90). STEP 2 -- the node tree: beModelCtrlNode2 (vtable 0x537f40) is an igTransform/igGroup; its _childList(+28) walks to named nodes: a group 'G_BK17v1_barrelm' (vtable 0x5284f0) and a mesh 'M_BK17v1_barrelmShape' (vtable 0x528e70), whose children include named model objects 'g_bbarrelmz01', 'g_bbarrelgz01', 'g_bbarrelsz01', 'g_000armor', 'g_boxfield',... (vtable 0x5294b0), and 'GM01'/'M01' nodes (0x528dd0/0x5294b0). So the engine's ig* model classes are 0x5284f0/0x528590/0x528dd0/0x528e70/0x5294b0/0x534210 etc. -- none among the 478 recovered RTTI vtables (#449 confirmed). STEP 3 -- the geometry: a beModelCtrlInfoRamData (vtable 0x537bb0, _name 'ONE') references raw 12-byte-stride vec3f buffers: positions at 0x140f908..0x1414e68 (1,821 verts, bbox x[-9.93,7.25] y[0.62,6.93] z[-4.09,2.95]) and NORMALS at 0x1414e68..0x141a3c8 (1,821 verts, bbox exactly [-1,1]^3 -- a unit sphere, decisive). A third buffer 0x141a3c8 (1,214 vec3, bbox [0,1]^3) is a colour/weight array. The object also references an **igPrimLengthArray** at 0x1593ba0 with 118 entries whose values are {3,4,7,10,12} -- STRIP lengths, not a plain triangle list. NEGATIVE (the render proves it): treating the 1,821 positions as a consecutive triangle list produces a shredded mesh (long triangles span the model) even with a z-buffer and backface culling, and the same for a strip. The positions are therefore a VERTEX BUFFER, and the primitives need an igIndexArray (reflection: _indexData+12, _numIndices+16, _dataSize+20, _usageFlags+24, _memPool+8) that has NOT been located -- the RamData's other refs are objects, not an index array. The 85% agreement between supplied smooth normals and geometric face normals is NOT evidence of correct connectivity (nearby vertices make that test weak). EVIDENCE: renders of the model positions (structured point cloud, x/y, x/z, y/z) and of the positions with their normals. NEXT PROBE: find the igIndexArray for a sub-mesh -- scan the heap for an object with 12 <= _numIndices <= 100000 and _indexData pointing at a u16/u32 array whose max < the igVertexArray's _numVerts and whose count equals sum(prim lengths); or resolve vtable 0x5294b0/0x528e70's class from its GetVTable registrar and walk its children. Then the model extracts and renders; until then P3.5 is 'geometry located' not 'extracted'. ADDITIONAL NEGATIVES (second pass): (a) igVertexArray, igVertexArray1_1 and igIndexArray are ABSTRACT -- their igMetaObject_RegisterClass registrars (0x14ba30, 0x14b720, 0x14d8a0) all pass t3=0 (no GetVTable); only igVertexArray2 (vtable 0x526410) and igVertexArray2Helper (0x5262a0) are concrete, and NEITHER has a live instance (0x526410 appears only at 0x5eb66c). (b) A heap scan for objects with a vtable in 0x524000-0x528000 found 8,319 live objects over 28 vtables (0x524420 x2686, 0x524950 x1699, 0x525190 x851, 0x5277e0 x830, 0x524880 x587, 0x526590 x454,...) -- but NONE has +8 pointing at a float array. The only array-shaped class there is 0x526590 = igPrimLengthArray1_1 (buffers of small ints, vtype 32). So the vertex data is held as DATA inside beModelCtrlInfoRamData, NOT as live igVertexArray/igIndexArray objects. (c) A heap scan for prim-length-like runs (u32 in 3..16, >20 long) found 8 candidates but NONE whose sum matches the 1,821-vertex buffer (nor sum+2n, nor 3n). So the 1,821 positions have no (locatable) matching primitive/index array. CONCLUSION: the RamData's per-sub-mesh record (which maps a positions buffer to its prim/index array) is the missing decode; the RamData at 0x1263fd0 has fields at +0x20 (positions 0x140f908..0x1414e68), +0x4c, +0x78, +0x84, +0x100 (prim array 0x1593ba0, count 118) -- several sub-meshes, and only one prim array. NEXT: decode that record (probably {positions, normals, prim_len_ptr, prim_len_count, index_ptr, index_count} repeated per sub-mesh), or find the renderer function that consumes it. RESOLVED (#455): the RamData record was a red herring -- beModelCtrlInfoRamData is a 24-byte, 2-field class (_node+16, _work+20) and the geometry is NOT in it. The connectivity is igGeometryAttr1_5 -> igVertexArray + igPrimLengthArray1_1, with the vertex buffer walked in order (no index array needed). The object at 0x1260d88 is the barrel geometry attr; 0x1260dc0 is the next attr in the same allocation, which is why reading past +0x38 looked like an embedded field.

<a id="f455"></a>
### #455 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x005238c0 — igGeometryAttr1_5

MODEL GEOMETRY: igGeometryAttr1_5 (vt 0x5238c0) -> igVertexArray (0x525190) -> igPrimLengthArray1_1 (0x526590); strips/lists walk the vertex buffer in order so there is no index array

**Notes:** MODEL GEOMETRY SOLVED. The chain is igGeometryAttr1_5 (vtable 0x005238c0, 0x38 bytes): +0x0c _vertexArray -> igVertexArray (vtable 0x00525190; _vdata+0x08, _numVerts+0x0c); +0x10 _indexArray (NULL on 1005 of 1006 live attrs); +0x14 _primType; +0x18 _numPrims; +0x30 _primLengths -> igPrimLengthArray1_1 (vtable 0x00526590; _vdata+0x08, _count+0x0c). TWO EASY TRAPS: igVertexArray._vdata points at a {start,end} descriptor whose end is a BLOCK end that overshoots the data by 8 (end-start == 12*numVerts+8) and is sometimes 0, so size the read from _numVerts; igPrimLengthArray1_1._vdata is the lengths themselves, no descriptor. Also _primType sits at +0x14 (not +0x20, which base-class reflection alone would suggest) because igGeometryAttr1_5 inserts two fields before _primLengths (+0x30), and objects are allocated back-to-back so over-reading returns the next attr. TWO REPRESENTATIONS, BOTH WALKING THE BUFFER IN ORDER (hence NO index array): _primType 4 = triangle strip -- _primLengths gives strip length L, strip i owns [off,off+L) with off += L and yields L-2 triangles, so sum(L)==numVerts; _primType 3 = triangle list -- no prim array, _numPrims triangles read in triples, 3*numPrims==numVerts; _primType 0 = empty/recycled slot (_vertexArray 0; freed slots self-point at +0x38). LIVE CENSUS: 1006 attrs = 454 strip + 337 list + 215 empty. VERIFIED BY RASTERISING, not by field names: rendering the vertices as a plain list or ignoring strip boundaries shreds the mesh even with a z-buffer, while the rules above produce recognisable objects (barrel with staves and spout, claw, ring, box). 669 distinct models extracted from the loaded stag0102 scene, every one passing its self-check. CORRECTION to #453: the 1821-vertex buffer at 0x0140f908 is one model's vertex data (geometry attr 0x01261ac8), NOT beModelCtrlInfoRamData -- that class has only 2 fields (_node+16, _work+20) and is 24 bytes. STILL OPEN: the offline form (the same geometry ships in the stage model pack, NDMW.AFS 621 for stag0102; that layout is undecoded, task T14); model names (reached through the scene graph, not from a node field); normals/UVs (the shipped arrays are 12-byte positions only). OFFLINE BRIDGE (same session): the geometry ships UNCOMPRESSED in the stage model pack -- a model's vertex block and its strip-length block are byte-identical in NDMW.AFS entry 621 (stag0102), and 446 of 668 live models were located there by content alone (searching the pack for the block's full 5928-byte vertex data matches exactly). No stored offset points at a block: the loader reconstructs the pool, so a u32 search for the block offset finds nothing, and the mapping is NOT a linear copy of the pack (live address order is monotone in pack offset for 405 of 445, blocks are shared -- several live arrays resolve to one pack offset, one resolves to none). So the offline reader needs T14's load-level replay; until then the extractor annotates each model with its pack offset per session (--pack AFS:621 --pack-tsv). Topic head for Live model geometry chain: also read #449, #453 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** docs/engine/model-format.md

<a id="f456"></a>
### #456 · refuted

**Where:** 0x00528e70 — M_*Shape

MODEL NAMES + NORMALS: normals are _vdata[1] (a {positions,normals} pair, not a descriptor); an M_*Shape object (vt 0x528e70) carries the DCC part name at +0x08 and owns its geometry attrs via +0x1c (wrapper embedding the attr at +0x18) and the +0x20 igAttrList chain

**Notes:** SUPERSEDED / CORRECTED (findings #457/#458). The two named facts stand: normals ARE
_vdata[1] and the DCC part name IS at M_*Shape+0x08. The STRUCTURE below is wrong: the +0x20
igAttrList is NOT the head of a 0x18-stride chain (the lists are a packed shared arena, so the
next 0x18 is the NEXT shape's list -- 637/752 attrs were over-claimed and 'font000' swallowed
177), and the +0x1c wrapper is not the geometry link (it is the shape's first attr of ANY
subclass and usually duplicates the +0x20 entry). Correct rule: one list at +0x20 -> 680 attrs,
0 collisions, 599/669 models named; 539 of 669 meshes have NO normal buffer at all; the buffer
after the last 12-byte stream is the UV (uv = align8(pos/nrm + 12*nv)), now confirmed. #457/#458.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
NORMALS + MODEL NAMES (second hunt, paused live game; emulator stopped mid-way).

(A) NORMALS ARE IN THE VERTEX ARRAY'S SECOND WORD. `igVertexArray._vdata` (+0x08) is NOT a {start,size} pair -- it is a {positions, normals} pair of heap pointers:
 _vdata[0] -> positions, 12-byte vec3f x _numVerts
 _vdata[1] -> normals, 12-byte vec3f x _numVerts, all unit length (0.02 tol)
Tested across the live scene: 128 attrs have unit-length normals at _vdata[1], 2 do not. This also explains the "descriptor span = 12n+8" the earlier pass reported: for those models _vdata[1] happened to sit just past the position block. Where _vdata[1] == 0 (539 of the ~790 loaded attrs; 221 strip + 318 list) the normals are NOT in that block and were not located -- that is the open half of the normals question. `igPsx2VertexArray1_1` (vtable 0x00525190, which is the real runtime class of _vertexArray) also has `_pBlendedVertsList`+0x30 and `_pBlendedNormsList`+0x34, both 0 here, and `_textureCoordCompiled`+0x48 -- so a blended/normal path exists that these objects do not populate.
 UV CANDIDATE (unverified): an 8-byte-stride float pair immediately after the normals (normals + 12*numVerts + 8). For 101 of the 128 normal-bearing attrs both components land in [0,1] with dyadic values (a barrel's first UVs are 0.53125, 0.703125 = 17/32, 45/64); the other 27 exceed [0,1] (up to 11.4), which is plausible for tiling. Nothing found so far *references* that buffer, so it is a hypothesis, not a fact -- verify either by finding the referring field or geometrically (a cylinder's u should track atan2 around its axis).

(B) MODEL NAMES ARE RECOVERED (the link is found). Model part names come from the DCC source (Maya/3ds Max) and live on the M_*Shape object:
 M_\<model>Shape object, vtable 0x00528e70 (826 live), +0x08 = name string pointer
 examples: "M_BK17v1_barrelmShape", "pCubeShape5", "polySurfaceShape38", "group2", "barrelbody"
 +0x1c -> wrapper object (vtable 0x0052ab20) which EMBEDS a geometry attr at +0x18
 (its own +0x0c is that attr's _vertexArray, +0x30 its _stripLengths)
 +0x20 -> chain of igAttrList objects (vtable 0x00524420), each {_count +0x08, _array +0x10}
 with the next list embedded at +0x18; the arrays hold attr object pointers, and the
 entries whose vtable is 0x005238c0 are the geometry attrs that extract_live_models.py reads.
So a geometry attr is named by the M_*Shape whose attr list contains it. Also found: the 0x92a000..0x92bbfc region is a flat array of ~1792 object pointers (the load-time object registry), which is why a "table of geometry attrs" there gives no names -- it is not a name index.

BLOCKED / NEXT: the mapping tool (the project's scripts/name_live_models.py, kept outside this repo because it needs a running game) walks each of the 826 shapes with many small PINE reads, i.e. tens of thousands of round trips, and did not finish in 15 minutes. Fix that the way extract_live_models.py already does -- bulk-read one big block and slice it (the whole shape block is contiguous) -- then re-run and map all attrs. Live facts above were taken with the VM PAUSED via pi-emu: the heap mutates every frame otherwise and reads are not reproducible (and the -lm emulator is now stopped). Reusable artefacts (outside the repo): scripts/class_vtables.tsv, a class -> vtable map extracted from the 1,075 igMetaObject registrars (652 vtables resolved) -- that is what identified 0x00525190 as igPsx2VertexArray1_1, 0x00528e70 as the M_*Shape class, and 0x005238c0 as igGeometryAttr1_5. TOOLING UPDATE (so the next session can pick this up): the name-mapper was rewritten to read EE RAM ONCE into a local buffer and resolve every pointer offline -- the first version issued one PINE round trip per deref (tens of thousands over 826 shapes) and never finished. It now has --dump (write the buffer) and --from (work from a saved dump with no emulator), plus testing/test_name_live_models.py, an offline self-test that builds a synthetic heap (shape + wrapper + two-link igAttrList chain) and passes. NOT YET RUN against a live dump -- that single command is the resume point. Reminders that cost time here: the VM must be PAUSED (the heap mutates every frame, so unpaused reads of the same address disagree), and the reusable artefacts are scripts/class_vtables.tsv (1,075 registrars -> 652 class/vtable pairs; this is what identified 0x00525190 as igPsx2VertexArray1_1, 0x00528e70 as the M_*Shape class and 0x005238c0 as igGeometryAttr1_5) and the offline analysis of the vertex array: igGeometryAttr1_5 adds _stripLengths at +0x30 (the base igGeometryAttr's _primLengths is +0x20 and stays NULL). Folded into #457, the current answer for Model part names via shape+0x20 igAttrList; this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

<a id="f457"></a>
### #457 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00528E70 — M_*Shape vt 0x00528e70; scripts/name_live_models.py

MODEL PART NAMES: a shape's geometry attr is the single igAttrList at shape+0x20 (not a 0x18-stride chain); +0x08 is the DCC name. 681 of 826 shapes named -> 680 attrs, 0 collisions, 599 of 669 extracted models named; the 145 null-name shapes (106 attrs, mostly font/UI glyphs) are left un-renamed.

**Notes:** MODEL PART NAMES -- the link is ONE igAttrList at shape+0x20, not a 0x18-stride chain (corrects #456).

CHAIN: M_*Shape (vt 0x00528e70) +0x08 = DCC name string; +0x20 -> exactly one igAttrList (vt
0x00524420, {_count+8, _array+0x10}); the array's entries whose vtable is 0x005238c0 are that
shape's geometry attribute. Measured on a paused pre-leomon/stag0102 dump:
826 shapes, all 826 +0x20 pointers distinct, 786 shapes own exactly 1 geometry attr, 40 own none,
681 shapes carry a name -> 680 attrs named with ZERO attr claimed by >1 shape and ZERO by >1 name,
357 distinct DCC names. Of the 669 extracted models, 599 are named this way.

THE 0x18-STRIDE CHAIN IS AN OVER-CLAIM (refutes the reading in #456). An earlier version walked the
object 0x18 bytes past each list as its "next list". The igAttrList objects are a packed shared
arena: the object after one shape's list is simply the *next shape's* list (and the shape's own data
array can sit inline there too). Evidence: the first-list addresses are all distinct, yet 14,288 of
the stride-walk's extra lists are other shapes' first lists, and the shape at 0x15bde48 is 'matz00'
while the stride-walk from 'polySurfaceShape1' (0x15bdd28) reached it. With the stride walk, 637 of
752 attrs were claimed by >1 shape and 'font000' swallowed 177 attrs; the single-list rule removes
every collision. The +0x1c wrapper attr is NOT the geometry link in general -- it is the shape's
first attr of ANY subclass (on ~1100 other objects it is class 0x529750, not 0x5238c0) and normally
duplicates the +0x20 entry, so it is used only as a fallback.

UNNAMED: 145 of the 826 shapes have a NULL name pointer (+0x08==0) and own 106 attrs, 65 of them in
the extracted set. They are mostly font/UI glyph sub-meshes (their geometry is small -- 24-vert/8-tri
quads -- and content-unique: none matches any named attr's vertex block), so they cannot be named
from a shape and are left as model_\<addr>.obj rather than mislabelled with a neighbour's name.

TOOL: scripts/name_live_models.py (rewritten to one bulk EE-RAM read; --from reuses a dump) writes
a model-names table and renames the extracted OBJ models. Topic head for Model part names via shape+0x20 igAttrList: also read #456 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** scripts/name_live_models.py; docs/engine/model-format.md

<a id="f458"></a>
### #458 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00525190 — igVertexArray vt 0x00525190; scripts/extract_live_models.py

MODEL NORMALS + UV: _vdata is a {positions, normals} pair; 130 of 669 meshes carry unit normals, the other 539 ship NO normal buffer (the next buffer is the UV). The UV is a real 8-byte {u,v} stream at align8(last 12-byte stream + 12*numVerts), confirmed geometrically on a cylinder (corr(u,atan2)=0.998).

**Notes:** MODEL NORMALS + UV (answers the open half of #456).

NORMALS: igVertexArray._vdata (+0x08) is a {positions, normals} pair -- [0] positions (12*nv bytes),
[1] normals (12*nv) or 0. It is NOT a {start,end} descriptor (end is 0 or overshoots). Measured on
the paused pre-leomon dump: of 791 live loaded attrs, 130 carry unit-length normals at _vdata[1];
539 have _vdata[1] == 0 and NO normal buffer anywhere -- the buffer layout is
[positions][4-byte tag + pad][optional normals][4-byte tag + pad][uv], so for those meshes the buffer
right after the positions is the UV, not the normals. A search of every buffer near 40 sampled
no-normal attrs found no 12*nv unit-length block, so the normals are genuinely absent (not relocated).
Their names are the 2D/unlit family -- font*/font0Shape (205), polySurfaceShape, CF_*, pPlane,
pCylinder, iconShape, sen, mpnm/lvnm/hpnm, win0Shape -- while the 130 WITH normals are effect/part
meshes (M_BK_op_0Shape 27, M_BK_p0Shape 9, expnum/bitnum/kouka) plus the barrel (0x1260d88). This is
consistent: text/UI meshes need no lighting normal, and the game's character/prop meshes that need
lighting do carry one.

UV -- CONFIRMED, and it is a real stream (the #456 "candidate" is now a fact): the UV buffer is the
8-byte-stride {u,v} float pair after the last 12-byte stream, at
 uv = align8((normals if present else positions) + 12*numVerts)
where align8(x) = (x + 4 + 7) & ~7 (the 4-byte gap is a pool tag, then 8-byte alignment). Each entry
is 2 floats; the buffer is 8*numVerts bytes, followed by the next tag+alignment exactly (checked on
both a normals-bearing and a normal-less mesh against the buffer-pointer pool at 0x949xxx/0x94a3xx).
GEOMETRIC PROOF, not a field name: for pCylinderShape3 (attr 0x111ca30, 55 verts, no normals) the u
component tracks atan2(z,x) with correlation 0.998 and all 55 pairs lie in [0,1] with dyadic values
(e.g. 0.53125=17/32, 0.703125=45/64); the barrel's first pair is likewise 0.53125,0.703125. Nothing
in the object points at the UV buffer, which is why it was only a candidate before.

TOOL: scripts/extract_live_models.py now reads the normals and UV streams and writes standard OBJ
vn/vt (faces v/vt/vn), and gained --from so extraction runs offline from a saved EE-RAM dump. The
regenerated OBJ models carry them: 669 files, 669 with vt, 130 with vn.

**Evidence:** scripts/extract_live_models.py; docs/engine/model-format.md

<a id="f459"></a>
### #459 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002e6090 — FUN_002e6090 (header->ctx) / FUN_002e7390 (object reader, loops ctx+0x70); scripts/igb_sections.py

T14: each igBinary blob has TWO object sections (hdr[1] and hdr[5] records, file order); the loader reads hdr[1] via ctx+0x70. id-recordIndex is a deterministic monotone function of the read position (equal for equal records), but the per-record object count is NOT a per-class constant.

**Notes:** T14 ADVANCE -- the blob has TWO object sections, and the id is a deterministic function of the read
position (not of the pack alone).

(1) TWO OBJECT SECTIONS PER BLOB. An igBinary blob carries two record chains, and the header states
both counts: hdr[1] and hdr[5]. They are not "the object count" and "a small side section" -- both are
real object chains, in file order, and a pack's id window spans their concatenation. Verified exactly
against the decoded chains on five blobs (scripts/igb_sections.py):
 entry 622 (stag0102 generators) hdr[1]=50 + hdr[5]=1206
 entry 183 hdr[1]=33 + hdr[5]=266
 entry 621 (stag0102 models) hdr[1]=1122 + hdr[5]=38135
 entry 140 (beFileListInfo) hdr[1]=6 + hdr[5]=5 (here the first is the larger)
 NDMW.IGB hdr[1]=11 + hdr[5]=2297
So entry 622's own id window is 1,256 objects, not 1,206.

(2) THE LOADER MAPPING, READ OFF THE DISASSEMBLY (FUN_002e6090; this corrects #426's slot list only in
emphasis, its mapping was right). The 12-word header is read from the FILE at offset 0 (proved by the
validity word: hdr[10]=0xfada at s1+0x28 is compared to ctx+0x60), and copied to:
 hdr[0]->ctx+0x7c hdr[1]->ctx+0x70 hdr[2]->ctx+0x84 hdr[3]->ctx+0x80
 hdr[4]->ctx+0x94 hdr[5]->ctx+0x90 hdr[6]->ctx+0x9c hdr[7]->ctx+0x98
 hdr[8]->ctx+0x8c hdr[9]->ctx+0x88 hdr[10]=validity hdr[11]=version+flags
The object reader FUN_002e7390 loops **ctx+0x70 = hdr[1]**, i.e. it reads the hdr[1] section (entry
622's 50-record chain); the hdr[5] section is read by another reader in the 17-section pipeline (the
one that uses ctx+0x90 is not yet pinned). This corrects the tracker's "hdr[5] is the count the loop
uses" reading.

(3) id - recordIndex IS DETERMINISTIC AND MONOTONE. Matching each beGeneraterData's authored `_pos`
to the count-1 igVec3fList record whose pool content is that position (pool laid out by ascending
`_data`; content cell-for-cell as in #419) gives, for entry 622, B = id - recordIndex:
 index 895 -> 21 922 -> 24 941 -> 31 960 -> 40 990 -> 59 1023 -> 65
 index 1048 -> 76 1073 -> 91 1096 -> 106 1118 -> 114 1163 -> 132 1182 -> 140
and B depends ONLY on the record: seven generators whose target is record 1118 all give B=114, seven
at 1163 give 132, seven at 1182 give 140. So the id is a well-defined deterministic function of the
read position -- the numbering has no per-ref noise, and #421's "extras drift" is really "a cumulative
count that grows with position". What is missing is the number of objects created per record.

(4) REFUTED: THAT COUNT IS NOT A PER-CLASS CONSTANT. Treating it as "each record of class C creates
one extra object" and solving for the class set misses the anchor increments by 43 (16 classes, 35
interval equations); no single class x1..3 and no two-class x1..2 combination makes B minus the count
constant. The extra objects are therefore created per record by something not keyed to the record's
class alone -- candidates are the list backing stores (the `_data` block of a list record) and
dependency instantiation -- which is the next thing to trace.

(5) NEGATIVE KEPT: hdr[0] (1,432 for 622, just past the largest id) is a candidate for the cumulative
object count after the blob, but it does NOT hold on entry 621 (hdr[0] 32,196 < hdr[1]+hdr[5] 39,257),
so it is something else.

METHOD NOTE: a few anchors are wrong because several count-1 lists hold the same authored position
(GENE-19/29 matched a duplicate); those give B far outside the monotone run and are dropped.

(6) WHAT THE TWO SECTIONS ARET14 ADVANCE -- the blob has TWO object sections, and the id is a deterministic function of the read
position (not of the pack alone).

real object chains, in file order, and a pack's id window spans their concatenation. Verified exactly

 entry 183 hdr[1]=33 + hdr[5]=266
 entry 621 (stag0102 models) hdr[1]=1122 + hdr[5]=38135
 entry 140 (beFileListInfo) hdr[1]=6 + hdr[5]=5 (here the first is the larger)
 NDMW.IGB hdr[1]=11 + hdr[5]=2297
So entry 622's own id window is 1,256 objects, not 1,206.

(2) THE LOADER MAPPING, READ OFF THE DISASSEMBLY (FUN_002e6090; this corrects #426's slot list only in

 hdr[4]->ctx+0x94 hdr[5]->ctx+0x90 hdr[6]->ctx+0x9c hdr[7]->ctx+0x98
 hdr[8]->ctx+0x8c hdr[9]->ctx+0x88 hdr[10]=validity hdr[11]=version+flags

uses" reading.

(3) id - recordIndex IS DETERMINISTIC AND MONOTONE. Matching each beGeneraterData's authored `_pos`
to the count-1 igVec3fList record whose pool content is that position (pool laid out by ascending

 index 895 -> 21 922 -> 24 941 -> 31 960 -> 40 990 -> 59 1023 -> 65
 index 1048 -> 76 1073 -> 91 1096 -> 106 1118 -> 114 1163 -> 132 1182 -> 140
and B depends ONLY on the record: seven generators whose target is record 1118 all give B=114, seven

constant. The extra objects are therefore created per record by something not keyed to the record's
