### #468 · unconfirmed

**Where:** 0x001f911 — NDMW.AFS 625; #467; texture-pack pool rule; next step = the descriptor walk order

Texture-pack pool analysis (entry 625): palettes are packed 1024-byte blocks (often +0x400 apart); index grids are packed but unaligned (gaps are sums of sizes); no stored offset points at either block, so the loader lays the pool out while walking the descriptor table. Ground truth for 24 textures recorded (both buffers located exactly).

**Notes:** TEXTURE-PACK GROUND TRUTH (NDMW.AFS entry 625) -- palette and index-grid byte offsets for 24 of the
46 live-dumped textures, each located EXACTLY (the palette by matching bytes 1..3 of its 4-byte
entries against the live PNG's colours; the grid by mapping every live pixel through that palette to
its index and searching for the resulting byte string):

 name w h palette grid name w h palette grid
 BJGR01.png 64 64 0x1bd0c 0x1f911 TA01BJ13.png 64 32 0x1c10c 0xb409
 FD01BJ03.png 64 32 0x0ed08 - TA01BJ16.png 64 32 0x1b90c 0x10109
 FD01BJ05.png 64 64 0x15104 - TA01BJ17.png 64 32 0x0ac08 -
 FD01BJ07.png 64 64 0x0c008 0x15909 TA01BJ18.png 64 32 0x0b004 -
 FD01BJ09.png 64 64 0x0e904 - TA01GM07.png 128 64 0x30110 -
 FD02BJ07.png 32 32 0x0dd08 - TA01SP99.png 128 128 0x1e510 0x16909
 FD02BJ15.png 64 64 0x1e110 - TA02BJ10.png 32 32 0x1ed10 -
 FD04BJ06.png 32 32 0x0d908 - TA02BJ16.png 64 32 0x1e910 0xd109
 P10G00n.png 128 64 0x02c08 - P12G00n.png 256 128 0x03408 0x23911
 P10G00s.png 128 64 0x2bd10 - P12G00s.png 128 128 0x2b910 0x3809
 TA01BJ10.png 128 64 0x3050c - P12W00.png 64 64 0x09804 -
 sfZ0SY10.png 16 16 0x1cd0c - (the other 22 live names are ramps/atlases whose
 palette is generated, not stored, or in entry 626)

WHAT THE OFFSETS SAY ABOUT THE POOL:
 * palettes are 1024-byte blocks and they are PACKED: many consecutive ones sit exactly +0x400 apart
 (0x1b90c, 0x1bd0c, 0x1c10c; 0x1e110, 0x1e510, 0x1e910, 0x1ed10; 0x2b910, 0x2bd10;...), so several
 palettes run back-to-back with no index buffer between them.
 * index grids are NOT aligned (offsets end 0x09 or 0x11) and their gaps are sums of block sizes
 (0x1000, 0x3000, 0x4800, 0x7c00, 0x9008,...), i.e. buffers are packed, not fixed-stride.
 * NO stored offset points at either block: the values 0x1bd0c / 0x1f911 appear nowhere as u32, and
 the descriptor table's fields are counts/sizes/dimensions (0x100 palette entries, 0x400 palette
 bytes, w, h). So the loader lays the pool out as it walks the descriptors -- the same *kind* of
 implicit layout as the object pack's #464, but the walk order/entry order is not the name order
 (the names repeat and their palette offsets are not monotone with it).
 * the low byte 0x09/0x11 of every located grid suggests the block is preceded by an 8-or-16-byte
 header that the loader skips.

NEXT: recover the descriptor entry structure and its walk order; the table above is the known-answer
set to check it against (a correct rule must reproduce every offset in it).

**Evidence:** scripts/igb_pool.py

<a id="f469"></a>
### #469 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001c85 — NDMW.AFS 625; #467/#468; texture-pack descriptor table; next = the pool order key

Texture-pack descriptor entry recovered: {u32 1}{u32 0}{u32 C}{u32 bitsIdx=8}{u32 DIM=width}{u32 0}{u32 0}{u32 namelen}{name padded}{variable post fields} in NDMW.AFS entry 625, 26 entries at odd offsets. The pool is packed but its order is neither the physical entry order nor sorted by C.

**Notes:** TEXTURE-PACK DESCRIPTOR ENTRY (NDMW.AFS entry 625) -- structure recovered for 26 entries.

Entry shape (variable length; fields little-endian u32):

 {u32 1}{u32 0}{u32 C}{u32 8}{u32 DIM}{u32 0}{u32 0}{u32 namelen}{name, namelen bytes, NUL-padded}
 {variable post fields}

 * `DIM` = the texture's width for every entry checked (BJGR01 64x64 -> 64; sfZ0SY10 16x16 -> 16;
 TA01BJ10/TA01BJ11/TA01SP99 128 -> 0x80; FD02BJ07/TA02BJ10 32 -> 0x20).
 * the constant 8 is `_bitsIdx` (every located texture is 8-bit)
 * `C` is a small id (3..188) -- it is NOT the pool order (see below)
 * the post fields carry the palette entries (256) and bytes/entry (4) and palette size (1024) for
 entries that have a palette, plus `_pfmt = 0x00010000` and a trailing 0xffffffff; their count
 varies per entry (BJGR01 22 u32, sfZ0SY10 27), so the post is itself variably shaped.
 * the entries are found at odd offsets (the table is not 4- or 2-aligned) -- that is why earlier
 even-step scans found nothing.

WORKED EXAMPLES
 BJGR01.png @0x1c85 C=39 DIM=64 pal 0x1bd0c grid 0x1f911
 sfZ0SY10.png @0x1d09 C=92 DIM=16 pal 0x1cd0c grid (256 B, not located)
 TA01BJ10.png @0x13e9 C=188 DIM=128 pal 0x3050c grid (8192 B, not located)

THE POOL IS PACKED (blocks tile: consecutive palette blocks are exactly +0x400 apart, and a grid
often starts where the previous block ended, +-1 byte). But the pool order is NEITHER the physical
entry order NOR sorted by C -- checked both ways against the 25 ground-truth palette offsets. So the
order comes from somewhere else (a list the loader walks: a `beTextureCtrlData`/`igImageMipMapList`
order, or an index stored in one of the post fields).

NEXT: the ground truth is [`../testing/texture-pack-groundtruth.tsv`]\(../testing/texture-pack-groundtruth.tsv);
the entry structure above plus that table is enough to identify the missing order key (a correct rule
must reproduce every offset in the table).

**Evidence:** testing/texture-pack-groundtruth.tsv

<a id="f470"></a>
### #470 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001ca5 — NDMW.AFS 625; #467/#468/#469; texture-pack sub-records; pool order

Texture-pack records are interleaved sub-records: clut [11,28,7,256,4,X,1024] and image [10,108,W,H,1,1,100,0,0,0,0,65536,SIZE,Y,-1]; the sub-records are the pool blocks (SIZE = W*H = grid size, 1024 = palette). A texture's sub-records are NOT adjacent to its name descriptor (BJGR01's 64x64 image sits in TA02BJ16's span), so the pool is ordered by the sub-record sequence, not by the name descriptors.

**Notes:** TEXTURE-PACK RECORDS ARE INTERLEAVED SUB-RECORDS, and the pool blocks match their sizes.

The bytes between two {...,name} descriptors are not one blob: they are a sequence of SUB-RECORDS,
recognisable by their shape and directly readable:

 clut record [11, 28, 7, 256, 4, X, 1024]
 = 256 palette entries, 4 bytes each, 1024 bytes (+ a leading 11,28,7 tag, and X)
 image record [10, 108, W, H, 1, 1, 100, 0,0,0,0, 65536, SIZE, Y, -1]
 = W x H, _pfmt 65536, _imageSize = SIZE = W*H, and Y

Both are the igImage / igClut field sets seen in the object packs (#419): `100` is `_order`,
`65536` is `_pfmt`, `SIZE` is `_imageSize`, `256`/`4`/`1024` are `_numEntries`/`_stride`/`_clutSize`.

Crucially the sub-records of one texture do NOT sit next to its own name descriptor: e.g. BJGR01's
64x64 image record is the last sub-record of TA02BJ16's span, while BJGR01's own span opens with a
clut record and ends with a 16x16 image record that belongs to sfZ0SY10. So the pack is a flat pool of
interleaved image/clut sub-records, with the {...,name} descriptors appended separately.

That is why the pool order resisted the descriptor fields: the pool is ordered by the SUB-RECORD
sequence (each image contributes its grid, each clut its palette), not by the name descriptors. The
ground-truth offsets (palette 0x400 apart, grids packed) fit a straight concatenation of the
sub-records in file order -- the missing piece is exactly which sub-records are emitted and in what
file position, which the interleaving now explains.

**Evidence:** testing/texture-pack-groundtruth.tsv

<a id="f471"></a>
### #471 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001bd0c — saved EE RAM dump of a paused pre-leomon stag0102; igClut/igImage runtime objects; texture-pack pool order

Dynamic ground truth for the texture pack: the RAM dump's addresses are dump+0x100000; entry 625's blob region 0x10940..0x22b80 is copied to RAM verbatim (base 0x8af45f, zero u32 diffs); the loader converts palette entries FF R G B -> R G B 80, and inverting that gives 9 EXACT file palette offsets whose runtime addresses are monotone in file offset (so the loader walks the pool in file order). The offset-accumulation rule itself is still open.

**Notes:** ADDENDUM: 183 igClut-shaped runtime structures; the field just after `_clutSize` is a small id (5..95 or -1, e.g. 41 at file 0x1bd0c) -- not the descriptor C, not the pool index, not monotone with the offset. No RAM field or table stores the source offset (0x1bd0c as a u32 appears only in unrelated byte data), so the loader computes it.
------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
DYNAMIC GROUND TRUTH (from a saved EE RAM dump of a paused pre-leomon stag0102).

ADDRESSING. The dump is 31 MB (0x1F00000) and its addresses are shifted: true RAM address =
dump offset + 0x100000. Proven: the exact FILE-form palette bytes (below) turn up at dump
0x7cb16b, and the only pointer to the runtime palette is the u32 0x17c5018, which lands on its
converted copy.

THE PACK IS COPIED, UNPATCHED. NDMW.AFS entry 625's blob region 0x10940..0x22b80 is present in RAM
verbatim at true base 0x8af45f (74,304 bytes, byte-identical; a u32-by-u32 diff of the whole copied
region against the file finds ZERO differences). So the loader does not write computed offsets into
the pack.

THE LOADER CONVERTS. A palette entry in the file is `FF R G B`; the loader's runtime palette is
`R G B 80` (0x80 = the GS's opaque alpha). Inverting that conversion -- reading a runtime 1024-byte
palette, rewriting `(r,g,b,80)` to `FF r g b`, and searching the pack -- yields an EXACT 1024-byte
file match for **9 palettes**:

 runtime 0x17b3168 -> file 0x00b008 runtime 0x17c5018 -> file 0x01bd0c
 runtime 0x17b4528 -> file 0x00c008 runtime 0x17c5420 -> file 0x01c10c
 runtime 0x17b7700 -> file 0x00ed08 runtime 0x17c79c0 -> file 0x01e510
 runtime 0x17d52d8 -> file 0x02b910 runtime 0x17d5730 -> file 0x02bd10
 runtime 0x17da040 -> file 0x030510

Sorted by runtime address the file offsets increase monotonically, so **the loader processes the
palettes in file-offset order** -- i.e. it walks the pool in file order and converts as it goes. That
also gives the exact palette block starts: 0xb008, 0xc008, 0xed08, 0x1bd0c, 0x1c10c, 0x1e510, 0x2b910,
0x2bd10, 0x30510 (all 4-aligned, 1024 bytes each; several are exactly +0x400 apart, confirming palettes
run back to back).

RUNTIME OBJECTS. Each converted palette has an owning igClut: e.g. at true 0x17b35d0 a vtable
0x00526a60 followed by `_fmt`=7, `_numEntries`=256, `_stride`=4, `_pData`=the runtime palette,
`_clutSize`=1024, then 256 and 41. igImage objects carry `_pfmt`=65536, `_imageSize`, `_pImage`.

WHAT IS STILL OPEN. The pack holds no stored offset, the loader walks in file order, so the pool
layout must be reproducible by accumulating the descriptor table's sub-record sizes in some order -- but
the sub-records taken in raw file order (clut 1024, image w*h, or w*h+1 for a 1-byte grid header) do
NOT reproduce the nine exact palette offsets, and neither does the descriptor's physical order, nor its
`C` field order, nor the sub-record order read the other way. The order key is the last unknown; the
nine offsets above are the regression set a correct rule must reproduce.

**Evidence:** testing/texture-pack-groundtruth.tsv

<a id="f472"></a>
### #472 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002c08 — texture packs; igClut/igImage sub-records; pool layout; NDMW.AFS entry 625

Texture-pack pool order SOLVED: the pool is the igClut/igImage data blocks in ascending object-id order (clut id = word after _stride; image id = word after _imageSize), size 1024 for a clut and W*H for an image; offset(id) = pool_base + sum(smaller-id sizes). An image block has a 1-byte prefix (grid at block+1). Pool base = end of the pack's object data (0x2c08 for NDMW.AFS entry 625), with two 4-byte pads after ids 129 and 140. Reproduces all 26 textures of entry 625 exactly.

**Notes:** THE POOL ORDER KEY (texture packs) -- SOLVED.

The pool is the igClut/igImage data blocks laid out in ASCENDING OBJECT ID ORDER. Each sub-record
carries the id of the memory block its data lives in:

 clut: [.., 28, 7, N, 4, id, N*4 ] (id = the word after _stride)
 image: [.., 104|108, W, H, 1, 1, 100, 0,0,0,0, 65536, W*H, id, -1 ]
 (id = the word after _imageSize)

A clut contributes N*4 bytes (1024 for a 256-entry palette); an image W*H. Therefore

 offset(id) = pool_base + sum(sizes of blocks with a smaller id)

An IMAGE BLOCK HAS A ONE-BYTE PREFIX, so its grid data starts at block+1; a palette has none, so its
entries start at the block. The pool begins immediately after the pack's object data (for NDMW.AFS
entry 625 that is 0x2c08, right after a trailing 4-byte-strided list of small ids), and a few 4-byte
PADS interrupt the run -- two in entry 625, after ids 129 and 140. Nothing is stored in the pack: the
loader accumulates the sizes as it walks the sub-records.

VERIFIED ON ENTRY 625. 52 blocks (26 clut + 26 image) tile 0x2c08..0x30910 (file size 0x30911, one
trailing byte), and all 26 textures decode pixel-for-pixel against the live-extracted PNGs:
 P10G00n(21/163) P10G02n(26/160) P12G00n(27/168) P12G00s(175/29) P10G00s(177/31) P12W00(32/34)
 T1BJ13a1(74/83) FD01BJ03(86/139) FD01BJ05(111/94) FD01BJ07(52/117) FD01BJ09(84/129)
 FD02BJ07(80/58) FD02BJ15(147/146) FD04BJ06(79/115) TA01BJ10(189/179) TA01BJ11(49/107)
 TA01BJ13(135/42) TA01BJ16(133/97) TA01BJ17(36/155) TA01BJ18(37/102) TA01GM07(187/186)
 TA01SP99(150/124) TA02BJ10(153/73) TA02BJ16(152/78) BJGR01(134/157) sfZ0SY10(140/69)
(clut id / image id). The full derived layout is committed as
testing/texture-pack-625-layout.tsv, and testing/verify_texture_pack.py re-runs the check.

WHAT MADE IT POSSIBLE. Finding #471's dynamic ground truth pinned nine palette offsets exactly (the
loader's runtime palettes are the file palettes with `FF R G B` -> `R G B 80`, and inverting that finds
an exact 1024-byte file match), and the runtime addresses of those nine plus nine grid buffers are
monotone in file offset -- i.e. the loader walks the pool in file order. Locating the remaining grids
by decoding each live PNG through its palette to an index grid (row-reversed, as stored) then gave a
full content map, which the id-sort rule reproduces exactly.

PITFALL. A palette entry is `A R G B` with a VARYING alpha: many textures have a transparent or
partly-transparent entry 0 (values like 0x00, 0x66, 0xb6, 0xda), so a palette block must not be
recognised by testing bytes for 0xFF -- that heuristic finds only 9 of 26.

**Evidence:** testing/texture-pack-625-layout.tsv; testing/verify_texture_pack.py

<a id="f473"></a>
### #473 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002c08 — texture packs; pool layout; igBinary header; NDMW.AFS 625/626

The texture pack's header states the pool layout exactly: hdr[6] == sum(block sizes) + 4*(nclut+2) and hdr[7] == nclut+nimg+3, so pool_base = EOF-1-hdr[6] + 4*nclut and pad_total = hdr[6]-4*nclut-sum == 8 (constant). Holds with no exception on all 44 NDMW.AFS texture data packs and reproduces the verified 54-row entry-625 fixture exactly.

**Notes:** THE POOL BASE AND THE PADS COME OUT OF THE PACK HEADER (texture packs).

The texture pack is an igBinary-ish container and its header (12 u32; hdr[10]=0xfada the
validity word, hdr[11] the version) states its own layout. Two of its words are exact
functions of the sub-records:

 hdr[6] == sum(block sizes) + 4 * (nclut + 2)
 hdr[7] == nclut + nimg + 3

where `sum` is the sum of the N*4 / W*H block sizes. From those:

 table_start = EOF - 1 - hdr[6] -- the trailing table of nclut 4-byte ids
 pool_base = table_start + 4*nclut
 pad_total = hdr[6] - 4*nclut - sum == 8 (a constant, 4-byte slots)

VERIFIED: all 44 texture data packs of NDMW.AFS satisfy both header identities and give
pad_total == 8, with no exceptions. The derived pool_base for entry 625 is 0x2c08, the
value the live-PNG ground truth pins independently, and the full derived layout (base +
pad search) reproduces the committed 54-row fixture docs/engine/texture-pack-625-layout.tsv
exactly, so scripts/igb_texpack.py and scripts/verify_texpack_layout.py no longer need a
handed-in base.

THE PADS. Each pad is a 4-byte slot, so it can only sit in a gap of the id sequence; the
placement is chosen to line up the most palettes (a palette block starts at a 4-byte entry
whose index-0 RGB is black in pack 625). That resolves 625 exactly, but the score is not
decisive on every pack -- entry 626's palettes are a different set and the search cannot pin
its pads -- so the pad POSITIONS remain inferred while the base and the pad TOTAL are exact.

NOT YET: AREA.AFS's packs (51 cluts but hdr[6]-sum in the 10^5 range) are multi-asset -- their
pool carries block types beyond igClut/igImage, so `sum` undercounts and the identity does not
hold. The 44 NDMW packs are pure texture packs.

CONTEXT (finding #472): the pool is the igClut/igImage data blocks in ascending object-id
order, a clut N*4 bytes and an image W*H, with the image's grid data at block+1. Because an
image's data is one byte longer than its block, its last byte lands on the next block's first
byte -- which is why a palette's entry-0 alpha byte is unreliable (it is the preceding image's
last grid byte) while its RGB is the palette's own index-0 colour.

**Evidence:** scripts/igb_texpack.py; scripts/verify_texpack_layout.py; docs/engine/texture-pack-625-layout.tsv

<a id="f474"></a>
### #474 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001c85 — texture packs; record stream; texture->image link; NDMW.AFS 625

In a texture pack the igImage sub-record (15 u32 = 60 bytes) sits immediately before its texture's descriptor entry, so descriptor_offset-60 is the image record and its id field is the image block's id. Verified 5/5 on NDMW.AFS 625. The igClut link is NOT positional (the clut records are interleaved from other textures) and the descriptor's C field is not the clut id, so the palette link is still open. The pack also has no T14-style {tableBytes,idCount} remap table.

**Notes:** THE TEXTURE -> IMAGE LINK IS POSITIONAL (texture packs).

A texture pack's record stream is a global object list, but the pairing between a texture and
its image is structural: the igImage sub-record

 [10, 104, W, H, 1, 1, 100, 0,0,0,0, 65536, W*H, id, -1] 15 u32 = 60 bytes

sits IMMEDIATELY BEFORE the texture's descriptor entry

 [1, 0, C, bitsIdx, DIM, 0, 0, namelen, name] 32 + namelen bytes

so `descriptor_offset - 60` is the image record, and its `id` field is the image block's id in
the pool (finding #472). Verified 5/5 on the descriptors NDMW.AFS entry 625 carries
(P12G00n, P10G00n, P10G02n, P12G00s, P10G00s): each one's preceding record is an igImage whose
id equals the id the verified pool layout assigns to that texture's grid.

So the image half of a texture needs no reference field: walk the record stream, and every
descriptor is preceded by its own igImage.

STILL OPEN -- the clut half. The igClut records are NOT adjacent to their texture's
descriptor, and the descriptor's `C` field is not the clut's block id (P12G00n: C=20, clut id
27; P10G00n: C=165, clut id 21). The object list interleaves igClut records from different
textures (P12W00's descriptor is followed by its clut 32, but P12G00n's descriptor is followed
by clut 84), so "the clut in front" / "the clut behind" are each right only about half the
time. A texture→palette link therefore still needs either the loader's object-construction
order or the beTextureCtrlAttr reference field.

ALSO: the pack is NOT an igBinary with the T14 remap table -- a scan for the
`{u32 tableBytes}{u32 idCount}` + idCount u16 shape (tableBytes == 2*idCount, values < idCount,
monotone) finds nothing in entry 625. Whatever maps the pack's object ids is not that table.
MEASURED DATA FOR THE OPEN CLUT LINK (entry 625). Both orders are known exactly, so the
missing piece is only the correspondence between them.

The 26 textures in DESCRIPTOR order, with the (clut id, image id) pair the content-derived
pool layout assigns to each:

 P12W00(32,34) TA01BJ10(189,179) P12G00n(27,168) T1BJ13a1(74,83) FD01BJ09(84,129)
 FD02BJ07(80,58) FD01BJ03(86,139) FD01BJ05(111,94) TA02BJ16(152,78) BJGR01(134,157)
 sfZ0SY10(140,69) FD02BJ15(147,146) TA01BJ11(49,107) TA01BJ17(36,155) TA01BJ16(133,97)
 FD01BJ07(52,117) FD04BJ06(79,115) TA01BJ18(37,102) TA01SP99(150,124) TA01BJ13(135,42)
 TA02BJ10(153,73) TA01GM07(187,186) P10G00n(21,163) P10G02n(26,160) P12G00s(175,29)
 P10G00s(177,31)

The igClut records, in FILE order, are ids:
 32 27 84 134 49 111 80 133 52 79 150 86 74 152 140 37 36 153 147 135 187 26 21 177 175 189

-- a permutation of the clut ids that is neither the id order (which is the pool order) nor
the descriptor order. Expressed as descriptor positions it is
 0 2 4 9 12 7 5 14 15 16 18 6 3 8 10 17 13 20 11 19 21 23 22 25 24 1
which is likewise neither sorted nor a rotation of anything obvious (it is not a bit-reversal,
a heap/BST in-order, or the descriptor order reversed).

Also ruled out, each measured: "the clut is the record right after the descriptor" (2/26
exact; for others it is a different texture's clut), "the clut is the record right before the
image" (no match), "the clut id is stored near the name" (4 coincidental hits out of 52 name
occurrences), and "the igImage is followed by its texture's clut" (the image is followed by its
*descriptor*, which is what made the image link work).

What is left is the loader's own object-construction order -- the same question the object
packs answer with the pack-local remap table, which this pack does NOT carry (finding #474).

**Evidence:** scripts/igb_texpack.py; docs/engine/texture-pack-625-layout.tsv

<a id="f475"></a>
### #475 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0016f9fa — object pack pool; igMemoryDirEntry _memSize; NDMW.AFS 621

The object pack's pool residual (621: 141 bytes, 179: 58) is NOT a trailing normal buffer: the 32 bytes before pool+sum are identical to the 32 after it, so the pool's tail is a 32-byte-period record running straight across the cut and the tail block(s)' declared _memSize is simply 141 short. A shortfall at the very end leaves every earlier block's offset correct, which is consistent with 446 of 668 live model blocks being byte-identical in 621. Finding #464's normal-buffer guess is refuted.

**Notes:** THE OBJECT-PACK POOL RESIDUAL IS A SEAMLESS TAIL, NOT A TRAILING NORMAL BUFFER.

NDMW.AFS entry 621's pool is 3,554,920 bytes and the igMemoryDirEntry `_memSize` values sum to
3,554,779 -- 141 bytes short -- with the highest-offset block ending exactly at pool+sum, so the
unaccounted bytes are the last 141. Finding #464's addendum guessed they were a trailing
`_vdata[1]` normal buffer. They are not:

 bytes at [pool+sum-32, pool+sum) == bytes at [pool+sum, pool+sum+32) (identical)

The pool's tail is a 32-byte-period record that runs straight across the cut, so the residual is
the *continuation of the same data*, i.e. the tail block(s)' declared `_memSize` is collectively
141 too small -- there is no separate buffer, and no 12-byte `vec3f` normal array (141 is not a
multiple of 12 either). Entry 179's 58-byte residual was re-checked and does NOT have this property (see below).

Consequence for the offline route: the offset of a block is the prefix sum of the `_memSize`
values below its id, so a shortfall at the very END leaves every earlier block's offset correct.
That is consistent with the earlier measurement that 446 of the 668 live model blocks are
byte-identical in 621. What is still unmeasured is whether any *middle* block is also short --
that, not the tail, is what would move later offsets.
CHECKED: entry 179's 58-byte residual does NOT have the seamless-tail property --
its 32 bytes before and after the cut differ -- so the two packs are not the same case. The
trailing 58 bytes are the same kind of *non-float* data as the bytes just before the cut
(neither region reads as plausible floats), so it is still extra tail data the id table does not
account for rather than a distinct asset; it just does not sit inside one periodic record.


CORRECTION (finding #478): the interpretation here is WRONG. The 141 bytes are not a short tail on the
last block -- they are 1-3 byte *alignment* pads scattered along the pool, because every block is placed
on a 4-byte boundary (`sum(align4(_memSize))` is the pool size exactly). The observation itself stands:
the bytes either side of `pool+sum` are continuous, which is a stream crossing the padded end.
 Folded into #478, the current answer for Object-pack content pool layout (4-byte aligned); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** scripts/igb_pool.py

<a id="f476"></a>
### #476 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0016f9fa — NDMW.AFS entry 625 texture pack; igImage._pClut/igClut._pData; data/reflection.json

The texture->palette link is solved offline: an igImage record is a serialised igImage object (fields at the reflected offsets; the supposed 'descriptor entry' is its own tail, with _pClut at +68), and the pack carries the same {tableBytes}{idCount} id table shape as the object packs but holding each id's CLASS (pack-local numbering, e.g. 625: clut block 0 / igClut object 8). _pClut is an id in that space, not a block id; its rank among the ids of its class is its creation order, so it names the r-th igClut record in FILE order, whose _pData is the palette block. All 26 textures of NDMW.AFS 625 then decode pixel-for-pixel against the live dump, fully offline.

**Notes:** THE TEXTURE -> PALETTE LINK: `_pClut` IS AN ID IN THE PACK'S OWN ID SPACE, RESOLVED BY RANK.

Finding #474 left the texture->palette half open and #467/#469 misframed the records. Both are
resolved, and the whole offline texture path is now exact.

1. An igImage record is a *serialised igImage object*, not a 60-byte descriptor plus a separate
 "descriptor entry". Its fields sit at the reflected offsets (data/reflection.json), and the
 `[1, 0, C, bitsIdx, DIM, 0, 0, namelen, name]` run earlier work read as a second record is just
 this record's own tail: _localImage(+60)=1, _bitsInt(+64)=0, _pClut(+68)=C, _bitsIdx(+72)=8,
 _bytesPerRow(+76)=DIM=width, _compressed(+80)=0, _bitsDepth(+84)=0, _pNameString(+88)={len,name}.
 So `_pImage`(+52) is the grid block id and `_pClut`(+68) is the palette reference -- one record,
 W*H/`_imageSize`(+48), `_pfmt`(+44)=0x10000, `_order`(+24)=100. Every ignImage record carries a
 `{u32 class=10}{u32 bytes}` header; an igClut is class 11 with `_fmt`(+8)=7, `_numEntries`(+12),
 `_stride`(+16), `_pData`(+20)=block id, `_clutSize`(+24).

2. The pack has the **same id remap table shape as the object packs (#460)** -- `{u32 tableBytes}`
 `{u32 idCount}` then idCount u16, with `tableBytes == 8 + 2*idCount` -- but here the entries are
 the **class of each id**, not a record index. On NDMW.AFS 625 it is at +0x1099 with 190 ids.
 The class numbering is pack-local (625: clut block 0 / igClut object 8; 626: 4 / 1; 619: 0 / 18;
 632: 1 / 16). It validates itself: one class's id set is exactly the clut blocks' ids, another's
 exactly the igImages' `_pClut` values.

3. `_pClut` is an id in that space, so it is **not** a block id. The ids of a class appear in the
 loader's creation order = file order, so the rank of `_pClut` among the ids of its class names
 the r-th igClut record (file order), whose `_pData` is the palette block:

 palette_block = igClut_records_in_file_order[ rank(cls(_pClut) == cls(_pClut), _pClut) ]._pData

 The rank is monotone in the clut record index (0->3, 1->20,... 25->188 on 625), which is why
 the id is an id and not an offset.

VERIFIED, end to end and offline: all 26 textures of NDMW.AFS entry 625 decode pixel-for-pixel
against the live dump through
`pool offset (#472/#473) -> igImage._pImage grid -> _pClut -> rank -> igClut._pData -> palette`.
That is `testing/verify_texture_pack.py` (live, 26/26) and the committed fixture
`docs/engine/texture-pack-625-textures.tsv` diffed by `scripts/verify_texpack_layout.py`.
No load replay, no RAM dump, no loader decompilation is needed.

Also newly validated: the class table exists and pairs on NDMW.AFS 626 (190 ids), 618 and 619
(148 ids), 632 (78 ids) and 639 (113 ids); 646/676/713/720 do not validate (multi-asset packs).

Two traps this cost real time on, worth keeping: `find_records` returns records sorted by *id*, so
"the r-th igClut" must sort by **file offset**; and a pad row in the layout reuses the id of the
block it follows, so a dict over layout rows must skip pads or it silently picks the 4-byte pad.

**Evidence:** scripts/igb_texpack.py

<a id="f477"></a>
### #477 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0016f9fa — NDMW.AFS 621 (stag0102's model pack); igGeometryAttr1_5 -> igVertexArray1_1 -> pool block; igb_ids/igb_pool

Models offline: the geometry chain resolves through the id tables (an igGeometryAttr1_5's _vertexArray is an igObjectRef id whose igVertexArray1_1 gives _vdata -- a memory block listing the array's stream ids -- and _numVerts; slot k at offset 4*(k-1)), and _memSize IS the stream's content size exactly (12*numVerts for positions, 8*numVerts for UVs, verified over all 549 vertex arrays). The stream starts at prefix_sum(id) + p(id), where p is a monotone step function of the id (15/16, 20, 45, 59, 141) that ENDS at the pack's residual -- so the 141 bytes are the pool's total drift, accumulated in ~5 steps, not a trailing oddity. Remaining, bounded: which blocks contribute the drift. This blocks an offline extractor only: a port runs the engine's loader, which allocates the pool and never needs p.

**Notes:** THE MODEL PACK'S POOL LOOKUP: THE GEOMETRY CHAIN RESOLVES OFFLINE, BUT A BLOCK'S STREAM DOES NOT
START AT ITS BLOCK BOUNDARY.

The chain (verified -- this part is done):

 igGeometryAttr1_5 slot4 = _vertexArray (an igObjectRef id)
 -> id 13842 -> section2 record 10362 igVertexArray1_1
 slot3 = _vdata = id 13843 (a MEMORY block, 80 bytes = 20 u32)
 slot4 = _numVerts = 55
 _vertexFormat 65537, _blendWeights/_blendIndices/_pointSpriteSize -1
 igGeometryAttr1_5 slot5 = _indexArray = -1 (NULL, matching #455's 1005/1006)
 slot6 = _primType = 4 (triangle strip)
 slot7 = _numPrims = 14
 slot k sits at offset 4*(k-1): +0x0c _vertexArray, +0x10 _indexArray, +0x14 _primType,
 +0x18 _numPrims, +0x30 _stripLengths (= slot 13) -- all four confirmed by value.

So an offline reader CAN reach a geometry buffer: the refs resolve through the pack's id remap
table (#460) and a block id is a memory entry. What blocks it is where inside the block the
stream starts.

MEASURED against live RAM. `scripts/extract_live_models.py --from <dump>.bin
--pack NDMW.AFS:621` finds the live vertex bytes in the pack byte-identically for **446** of the
669 meshes of stag0102. Comparing each found offset with the block boundaries the `_memSize`
prefix-sum rule predicts (the packmap is `testing/model-pack-offsets-621.tsv`, the summary
`testing/model-pack-offsets-621.md`):

 +45 213 cases +59 171 +16 36 +141 12
 +20 4 +15 3 +9 2 +11/+65/+333/+1557/+1 1 each

**Zero of the 446 are at a block start.** The delta does not correlate with the block's size or
the vertex count (e.g. +45 occurs with 48-, 288- and 360-byte blocks and 4, 24 and 30 verts), and
it does NOT accumulate across the pack -- it stays within 141 bytes for all 446 even though the
pack has 12,362 blocks and 3.55 MB. So the `_memSize` prefix sum places every block's *region*
correctly (the regions are contiguous for 12,362 blocks and the total lands on the pool end), and
the stream inside a block simply begins at a small per-block offset.

Two instances, both byte-checked by hand:
 * the cylinder, live mesh 0x0111ca30 (55 verts, _primType 4, 14 prims): positions in memory
 block id **38756** (660 B = 55*12 = `_memSize` exactly) at **block+59**;
 * a 4-vertex quad, live mesh 0x123e7c0: positions at **block+45** into memory block **33885**
 (48 B = 4*12) -- so it starts 3 bytes before that block ends and runs into the next two
 blocks. The quad itself reads correctly there: (2,15,10) (-2,15,10) (2,-15,10) (-2,-15,10).

READING. A block's declared `_memSize` is its *stream's* size (55*12 = 660 exactly), while the
stream sits `p` bytes into the block's region and therefore overruns it by `p`. That is the same
shape as the texture pack's documented quirk ("an image's data is one byte longer than its block,
so its last byte lands on the next block's first byte", #472/#473) -- but with a *variable* `p`,
so the pool is best read as a byte stream of concatenated streams whose boundaries are stated, not
as a list of blocks whose data starts at their own start.

OPEN, and the decisive next test: what determines `p`. Candidates to test against the 446 rows:
(a) `p` == the previous stream's overrun, i.e. a chain of "each stream is written at the previous
one's end" (which would make the block statement a *size* list only); (b) `p` == the size of a
header the previous record left behind; (c) `p` is `_memSize`-relative bookkeeping the memory dir
entry states in a field not yet decoded (`_memTypeIndex` differs per stream kind). Test (a)
first: for a located row, take the *previous* block id, read its declared size, and compare with
the stream size implied by its own numVerts. If the difference equals `p`, the rule is (a).
CONFIRMED SINCE (same session):

* **The streams inside the pool are CONTIGUOUS.** Of the 24 pairs of adjacent located blocks, **23**
 have (offset of the next stream - offset of this one) == this block's `_memSize` == 12*numVerts
 exactly; the one exception is the stray +333 row. So a stream begins where the previous one ends,
 and `_memSize` is the stream's own size.
* **`p` (a stream's offset into its block's region) is constant across runs of consecutive blocks** --
 runs of 2 to 9 blocks share one `p` (15, 16, 20, 45, 59, 141 all observed as run values). That is
 what contiguity implies: a shift is carried along the run and only changes when some stream's true
 size differs from its declared `_memSize`.
* So `p` is a **drift**, not a per-block header: `p(k) = sum(true stream sizes below k) -
 sum(_memSize below k)`. It is 0 for most of the pack (23 of 24 adjacent pairs), which is why the
 `_memSize` prefix sum is right to within 141 bytes everywhere and exactly right for the run
 structure; the deformations come from the few blocks whose true size differs from the declared one.
* The pool opens with small id-list blocks (ids 7/8/13/14/19/20, sizes 4/12/4/56/12/80 -- the first
 bytes are the integers 34059, 25661, 11171, 11172, 12210 then a run of 3s and 4s), so `p` is not a
 fixed file offset: the lowest located live vertex stream is already at block+15 (block id 3088).

Next test, now sharp: walk the pool in id order and, wherever a block's *content-derived* size is
available (a positions stream's is 12*numVerts from its igVertexArray), compare it with `_memSize`.
The blocks where they differ ARE the drift; identifying their kind identifies the rule.

CONFIRMED (same session) -- the geometry IS locatable offline; only a small drift is left:

* **The offline chain, verified end to end.** `igGeometryAttr1_5` slot 4 (+0x0c) is `_vertexArray`
 (an igObjectRef id; slot k sits at offset 4*(k-1), so +0x10 `_indexArray` = -1, +0x14 `_primType` = 4,
 +0x18 `_numPrims` = 14, +0x30 `_stripLengths` = slot 13 all check out by value). id 13842 ->
 section-2 record 10362 `igVertexArray1_1`, whose slot 3 (+8) is `_vdata` = id 13843 -- a MEMORY block
 of 80 bytes = 20 u32 holding the ids of that array's streams (positions, UVs,...) -- and slot 4 (+12)
 is `_numVerts` = 55. So a reader reaches a geometry buffer with no replay at all.
* **`_memSize` IS the stream's content size, exactly.** Walking all **549** `igVertexArray1_1` records,
 resolving each `_vdata` block and its stream ids, and comparing each stream block's `_memSize` with
 `12*_numVerts` / `8*_numVerts`: `_memSize - 12n == 0` for positions and `_memSize - 8n == 0` for UVs,
 across hundreds of refs (e.g. nv=110 -> 1320 = 12n and 880 = 8n; nv=527 -> 6324 = 12n and 4216 = 8n;
 duplicates included, so shared geometry shows up as two ids with the same size). A vertex array's
 `_vdata` block itself is class 7 and always 80 bytes.
* **The stream starts at `prefix_sum(id) + p(id)`, and `p` is a monotone step function of the id.**
 Attributing the live-located buffers only where the block's `_memSize == 12*numVerts` (428 of the 446
 rows -- the other 18 are duplicate geometries matched to another copy) gives a clean curve:

 ids 3088.. 12684 p = 15 / 16
 ids 19423.. 22790 p = 20
 ids 27853.. 34638 p = 45
 ids 38740.. 39173 p = 59
 ids 45816.. 50495 p = 141

 **`p` ENDS AT 141, which is exactly the pack's residual** (`pool_size - sum(_memSize) = 141`). So the
 residual is not a trailing oddity at all: it is the pool's *total drift*, accumulated in ~5-6 steps of
 +1, +4, +25, +14, +82 (plus the initial 15). The mid-curve p=9/p=11 rows (ids 33882, 39165) are the
 duplicate-block mis-attributions, not real dips.
* **Consequence and what is left.** A stream is inside a <=141-byte window that starts at its block's
 region, and `_memSize` gives its length, so the geometry is one small search away -- but a bound-check
 validator is NOT discriminating enough on its own (scanning p = 0..159 on the 55-vertex cylinder, 40
 values decode to finite bounded floats, the true 59 among them, because the pool's filler is also
 small dyadic values). The remaining, bounded question is therefore: **which blocks contribute the
 drift** -- i.e. which of the 12,362 listed blocks has a true stream longer than its `_memSize`. A
 useful direction: the pool's own byte content is the unaccounted data (the p-transition windows
 locate each event to within a few thousand ids), and the steps +1/+4/+25/+14/+82 are the *sizes* of
 what is missing, so hunting for blocks with those `_memSize` values, or scanning the pool bytes for
 id-shaped values that no `igMemoryRef` names, are both concrete.
* Note on priority: this is an *offline extractor* blocker, not a port blocker. A port runs the
 engine's own loader over a host `igFile` (plan P2.3), which allocates the pool itself and therefore
 never needs `p`. The drift only has to be solved for a static/offline decoder.


CORRECTION (finding #478): the "drift" measured here IS the block alignment. `p(id)` was the
accumulated `align4` padding, which is why it ended at exactly the residual 141. The measurements in
this finding (446 located buffers, 428 attributable, the p curve, the contiguity of streams, `_memSize`
== `12*numVerts`) all stand and are what led to the rule; only the conclusion "the remaining question is
which blocks contribute the drift" is superseded -- nothing is unaccounted for, the offsets were simply
computed without `align4`.
 Folded into #478, the current answer for Object-pack content pool layout (4-byte aligned); this finding is kept for its evidence and history.

**Evidence:** scripts/igb_pool.py

<a id="f478"></a>
### #478 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0016f9fa — pack content pool; igMemoryDirEntry._memSize; dw4_igb_refs_open/igb_pool.load

The content pool lays every block out 4-byte aligned: offset(id) = pool_start + sum(align4(_memSize) of every id below it), so a _memSize that is not a multiple of 4 is followed by 1-3 padding bytes. sum(align4(_memSize)) is the pool size exactly -- 621: 3554920, 179: 1803056, 622: 7112 -- so the '~141-byte residual' of #464/#475 WAS this alignment (all 141 bytes come from types 3/8/10: 120+16+5), and #477's monotone drift p (15/16 -> 20 -> 45 -> 59 -> 141) was the accumulated padding, ending exactly at the residual. With it, 432 of the 446 live-located vertex buffers land exactly on their block start (the other 14 are duplicate-geometry mis-attributions), and a new offline extractor (scripts/igb_models.py) pulls 454 of 621's 549 geometry attributes with no replay, 376 of them byte-identical to a live extraction. Corrects #464 (missing align4), #475 (the 141 is scattered padding, not a short tail) and #477 (drift = alignment).

**Notes:** THE POOL LAYS EVERY BLOCK OUT 4-BYTE ALIGNED -- THAT WAS THE "RESIDUAL", AND IT MAKES THE
POOL EXACT ON THE MODEL PACKS TOO.

 offset(id) = pool_start + sum( align4(_memSize) of every id below it)

`_memSize` is a byte count that need not be a multiple of 4 (igClut/igImage/geometry sizes
usually are, but types 3, 8 and 10 are not: entry 621 has blocks of 123, 231, 302, 403, 465,
501, 681,... bytes, 45 of them odd), and the next block starts on the next 4-byte boundary.
So a block whose `_memSize % 4 != 0` is followed by 1-3 padding bytes.

EVIDENCE

* `sum(align4(_memSize))` is the pool size **exactly** on every pack checked, where the plain
 sum was short by what findings #464/#475 called the "residual":

 NDMW.AFS 621 (stag0102's model pack) 3554920 bytes (was "short by 141")
 NDMW.AFS 179 (a stage model pack) 1803056 bytes (was "short by 58")
 NDMW.AFS 622 (a generator pack) 7112 bytes (already exact -- all sizes %4 == 0)

* The 141 bytes on 621 are exactly the alignment padding, and all of it comes from three
 block kinds: type 3 contributes 120 (61 blocks with a size %4 != 0), type 10 contributes 16
 (8 blocks), type 8 contributes 5 (3 blocks). 120 + 16 + 5 = 141.

* Against live RAM: of the 446 vertex buffers that `scripts/extract_live_models.py` finds in
 621 by content, **432 now land *exactly* on the block start this predicts** (the other 14 are
 duplicate geometries matched to a different copy, thousands of bytes away, i.e. a wrong
 attribution rather than a wrong offset). Previously none did, and the shortfall looked like a
 per-id "drift" (#477): p = 15/16 -> 20 -> 45 -> 59 -> 141 was the accumulated padding, and its
 end value 141 *was* the residual, which is why the two numbers kept agreeing.

* End to end: `scripts/igb_models.py` (new) extracts model geometry from a pack with no game, no
 RAM dump and no replay -- `igGeometryAttr1_5._vertexArray` -> `igVertexArray1_1` (`_vdata` is a
 memory block listing the stream ids, `_numVerts` the count) -> the positions stream is the one
 whose `_memSize == 12*_numVerts` and the UVs the one with `8*_numVerts`; `_stripLengths` gives
 the primitive lengths, validated by `sum(lengths) == _numVerts`. It extracts **454** of the
 pack's 549 geometry attributes, and **376 of those 454 are byte-identical (to 5 decimals) to a
 live-extracted model** -- the rest belong to geometry the live session never loaded.

CORRECTIONS

* #464's rule was `offset = pool_start + sum(_memSize...)`: right in structure, missing `align4`.
* #475's "the last block's declared `_memSize` is short by 141" is **wrong**: the 141 bytes are
 scattered along the pool as 1-3 byte pads, not a short tail. (Its *observation* -- that the
 bytes either side of `pool+sum` are continuous -- was the tail of a stream crossing the padded
 end, which is consistent.)
* #477's "drift" is the same phenomenon; its measurements stand, its interpretation does not.

TOOLING

* `scripts/igb_pool.py`: `run += (ms + 3) // 4 * 4`, `--check` now reports the aligned accounting
 and prints "exact" for the model packs.
* `port/src/igb.c`: the same, in `dw4_igb_refs_open`; `make check` still agrees with the Python
 on entries 622/183/140/621.
* `scripts/igb_models.py`: the offline model extractor (`--list`, `--obj ID`, `--out-dir`).
* The texture packs declare only multiples of 4 (1024-byte palettes, `W*H` grids), so their
 layout is unaffected -- their own 8 unexplained bytes are a separate, smaller question. Topic head for Object-pack content pool layout (4-byte aligned): also read #464, #475, #477 (folded in, kept for their evidence).

**Evidence:** testing/model-pack-offsets-621.tsv

<a id="f479"></a>
### #479 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x20e33000 — igGeometryAttr1_5 / igVertexArray1_1 / igPrimLengthArray1_1; the model pack's content pool

A model pack's geometry resolves completely offline: with the 4-byte pool alignment (#478) and the field offsets taken from data/reflection.json (igGeometryAttr._vertexArray +0x0c / _primType +0x14 / _numPrims +0x18, igGeometryAttr1_5._stripLengths +0x30, igVertexArray._vdata +0x08 / _numVerts +0x0c, igPrimLengthArray._lengthData +0x08 / _numStrips +0x0c), all 549 of NDMW.AFS entry 621's igGeometryAttr1_5 records resolve -- no search, no replay, no RAM dump. 450 of the 549 are byte-identical (vertex for vertex) to a model extracted from a saved live EE RAM dump; 52 of the rest have a vertex count the live session never held and 47 are same-count models from another part of the stage. The earlier 'search for the fields' version resolved only 454, and the 95 it skipped were a candidate-filter artifact (a count of 1, or a mis-paired stream list), not a format gap. The port's C reader (dw4asset models, port/src/models.c) implements the same chain: it agrees with the Python on the stream table over entries 621/179/642 (1,526 models) and writes byte-identical OBJs -- in make check.

**Notes:** MODEL GEOMETRY NOW RESOLVES COMPLETELY OFFLINE -- ALL 549 OF ENTRY 621'S ATTRIBUTES, 450 OF THEM
BYTE-IDENTICAL TO THE LIVE GAME'S -- AND THE PORT READS THE SAME CHAIN IN C.

With #478's alignment the chain needs no search and no replay. The remaining wobble was the field
mapping: the container reports each value's *ordinal* (a per-class declaration index, offset by the
inherited `igObject` fields), which does not map straight to a byte offset, so the first extractor
*searched* for `_vdata`/`_numVerts`/`_lengthData` by trying candidate pairs and keeping the one whose
arithmetic worked. `data/reflection.json` settles it outright, because it gives each class the fields
it *declares*, at absolute offsets within the object:

 igGeometryAttr._vertexArray +0x0c _indexArray +0x10 _primType +0x14 _numPrims +0x18
 igGeometryAttr1_5._stripLengths +0x30
 igVertexArray._vdata +0x08 _numVerts +0x0c
 igPrimLengthArray._lengthData +0x08 _numStrips +0x0c

(the base classes hold them -- reflection.json records no base link, so the declarer has to be named).
Reading them directly, with the same self-validating arithmetic kept as a *check* rather than as a
search (the `_vdata` block must list a `12*_numVerts` stream; the lengths must sum to `_numVerts`),
resolves **all 549** of entry 621's geometry attributes where the search resolved 454.

Verification, three ways:

* **against the live game** (`testing/verify_models_offline.py`): 450 of the 549 are byte-identical
 (positions, line for line) to a model `scripts/extract_live_models.py` pulls out of a saved EE RAM
 dump. Of the 99 that are not, 52 have a vertex count the live session never held (the dump only has
 what was loaded) and 47 are same-count models from another part of the stage -- not mismatches, and
 none of the 549 failed to resolve.
* **against the port's C reader** (`port/tools/verify_models.py`): `dw4asset models` and
 `igb_models.py` agree on the stream table over three packs (621/179/642, 1,526 models) *and* write
 **byte-identical OBJ files**. That is in `make check`.
* **against a committed fixture** (`docs/engine/model-pack-621-attributes.tsv`):
 `scripts/verify_models_layout.py` re-derives all 549 rows from the pack (`make rules`).

Also closed: the 95 attributes the earlier version skipped were never a format gap -- they are
`_stripLengths` records whose count is 1 (excluded by a `1 < n` candidate filter) or whose stream list
the search mis-paired; the fixed offsets resolve every one.

What is left on this path is only naming: the offline extractor writes `model_\<vertexArrayId>.obj`
while the live side names 599 of 669 models through the `M_*Shape` object's `igAttrList` (+0x20) ->
attribute link, which the offline reader can follow too.

**Evidence:** scripts/verify_models_layout.py

<a id="f480"></a>
### #480 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0015a80a — igGeometry (M_*Shape) / igAttrList; scripts/igb_models.read_names; port/src/models.c name_from_shape

A model's name resolves offline through the shape -> attribute link, found by ordinal (not offset): an igGeometry (M_*Shape) record's ordinal-2 value is the DCC part name and its ordinal-8 value is an igAttrList whose _array (+0x10) is a pool block of attribute ids -- the ids that are igGeometryAttr1_5 records are that shape's geometry. This is finding #458's live M_*Shape walk reached from the pack. It must be the ordinal: an igGeometry record's byte offsets shift with the length of its name string (a 16-byte name puts the list at +0x28, a 1-byte one at +0x10), so reading +0x20 named only 43 of the 549 attributes and left 371 of the 584 shapes pointing at a block id or an igNodeList, while ordinal 8 named 444 and resolved for all 584. The list's _count (+0x08) equalling its array block's u32 count is the self-check. Coverage: 444 of 549 attributes named in NDMW.AFS entry 621; against the live namer, 383 of the 386 content-matched models get the identical name (the 3 that differ are content duplicates where either name is right for its own attribute). The C side exposed a decode bug: a type-4 value of length 0 pointed str at the following bytes, so an empty name became the next byte in the file.

**Notes:** A MODEL'S NAME RESOLVES OFFLINE THROUGH THE SHAPE -> ATTRIBUTE LINK -- FOUND BY ORDINAL, NOT OFFSET.

 igGeometry ("M_*Shape") ordinal 2 -> the DCC part name (a string)
 ordinal 8 -> an igAttrList id
 igAttrList +0x08 _count, +0x10 _array
 -> a pool block of u32 attribute ids
 -> the ids that are igGeometryAttr1_5 records are that shape's geometry

This is the live `M_*Shape` walk (finding #458: the shape's attr list holds its geometry attribute)
reached from the pack instead of from RAM. What matters is that the link is found by the container's
**ordinal**, never by a byte offset: an `igGeometry` record's offsets *shift* with the length of its
name string (a 16-byte name puts the attr list at +0x28, a 1-byte one at +0x10), while the slot
numbers do not. Reading "+0x20" -- which is what the live object's layout suggests -- resolves to a
block id or a node list for 371 of the 584 shapes; reading ordinal 8 resolves for all 584.

Two traps, both recorded in `scripts/igb_models.py`:

* the list's `_count` (+0x08) must equal its array block's u32 count -- the self-check, which is what
 makes the pairing safe to trust;
* a *variant* record whose name is not a string (the `slot2` value is an int in the neighbouring
 attribute classes) must not be read as one. On the C side this exposed a real bug in
 `dw4_igb_decode`: a type-4 value with length 0 set `str` to the bytes *following* the record, so an
 empty name became the next byte in the file. It is the empty string now.

COVERAGE AND VERIFICATION

* **444 of the 549** geometry attributes of `NDMW.AFS` entry 621 take a name this way (266 distinct DCC
 names; 48 names cover more than one attribute -- a reused primitive). The other 105 belong to shapes
 whose name is empty (105 of the 584). Nothing is guessed: an unnamed shape leaves its attributes
 unnamed rather than borrowing a neighbour's.
* Against the live namer (`scripts/name_live_models.py --from \<dump>`), matched by *content*:
 **383 of the 386** models that are named live get the identical name offline. The 3 that differ are
 content duplicates -- two shapes sharing one vertex list (`pCylinderShape1` vs `obj0Shape1`) -- where
 either name is right for its own attribute. Checked by `testing/verify_models_offline.py`, which now
 reports the name count as well as the geometry one.
* The three cylinder attributes of the pack (13842/13844/13846, the three copies of the same mesh) come
 out as `pCylinderShape3` / `pCylinderShape1` / `pCylinderShape2` -- which is exactly what the live
 namer says of the same addresses.

TOOLING

* `scripts/igb_models.py`: `read_names`, the name column in `--tsv`, and `model_\<name>.obj` (sanitised)
 from `--out-dir`; `scripts/verify_models_layout.py` and its fixture
 `docs/engine/model-pack-621-attributes.tsv` carry the column.
* `port/src/models.c`: `name_from_shape` / `add_names` and `dw4_models_obj_basename`, so
 `dw4asset models --out-dir` writes the same filenames; `port/src/igb.c` fixed the empty-string bug.
 `port/tools/verify_models.py` still compares the C and Python row for row and byte for byte (the OBJ
 *filenames* are now part of that diff), and `make check` is green.

**Evidence:** testing/verify_models_offline.py

<a id="f481"></a>
### #481 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x20e33000 — dw4_scene_load/dw4_scene_handle/dw4_scene_host; dw4_igfile_open_stage_ref; dw4asset scene

The port's scene loader (port/src/scene.c) is faithful: dw4_scene_load places every id's payload at a 32-bit arena handle in id order and reads geometry back through those handles, and dw4asset scene --check asserts (a) every id placed in order exactly once, (b) every id's payload byte-identical to the file's bytes for that id -- 50,497 ids, 0 differences for NDMW.AFS entry 621 -- and (c) every mesh handle resolves (549 meshes, 82,134 vertices, 0 broken). With testing/verify_models_offline.py (file geometry == live geometry, 450 of 549 byte-identical) the chain arena == file == live is closed link by link. The loader also removes the pool's padding from the port's path (it allocates, so it chooses the layout) and reaches the pack the way the game does: manifest -> stag0102.igb -> its own [0.621] [pt.entry] reference (#445). Deliberately deferred: rewriting the records' reference fields into handles, which needs a (class, ordinal) -> field-kind table because a pack's descriptor type ids are wire sizes, not kinds (type 0 serves object refs, name slots, floats and enums; type 4 serves strings and ints).

**Notes:** THE PORT'S LOADER: A PACK BECOMES AN ARENA PLUS 32-BIT HANDLES, AND THE ENGINE'S OWN ROUTE REACHES IT.

`dw4_scene_load` (port/src/scene.c) walks a pack's id table, places every id's payload at a
handle in one arena, and records the table:

 handle = arena offset (0 is NULL, the first payload starts at 0x1000)
 id = the pack's own id (finding #460)

then reads the geometry back *through the handles* -- the same walk the file reader does, but over
the loaded heap, which is what the engine's loader produces (finding #423: the loader converts the
pack's ids into pointers in a later pass).

The check that the loader is faithful, asserted by `dw4asset scene --check` on every run:

* every id is placed **in order, exactly once** (the arena tiles: entry[i+1].off ==
 entry[i].off + align(entry[i].size)), and
* every id's payload is **byte-for-byte the file's bytes for that id** -- 50,497 ids, 0 differences
 for NDMW.AFS entry 621 -- and
* every mesh handle resolves inside the arena (0 broken of 549 meshes / 82,134 vertices).

Together with `testing/verify_models_offline.py` (the file's geometry == the live game's, 450 of 549
byte-identical) that closes the chain **arena == file == live** for the model path, each link checked
separately.

THE ROUTE IS THE GAME'S, NOT AN ARCHIVE INDEX. `dw4asset scene --stage \<roms> stag0102.igb --ref 1`
opens the pack through the manifest -> the stage file -> its own `[0.621]` `[pt.entry]` reference
(finding #445), which is how the game reaches its model pack. The loader's input is therefore a name
and a stage, never a byte offset.

THE POOL'S PADDING IS GONE. In the file a block starts at a 4-byte boundary, so a `_memSize` that is
not a multiple of 4 is followed by 1-3 pad bytes (finding #478). The loader *allocates*, so it
decides: the arena packs ids densely at DW4_SCENE_ALIGN (16), and no code in the loader path depends
on any file offset. (The offline extractor still needs the alignment arithmetic; the port does not.)

DELIBERATELY NOT DONE YET: rewriting the object records' *reference fields* into handles. That is
what running decompiled engine code over the arena needs, and it needs a (class, ordinal) ->
field-kind table, because a pack's descriptor type ids are **wire sizes, not kinds** -- measured over
every class of entry 621, type 0 serves object references (148), inherited name slots (81), floats (2)
and enums (2), and type 4 serves strings and ints alike. So the classification has to be generated
from `data/reflection.json` rather than read from the pack. Until then a caller resolves an id with
`dw4_scene_handle` / `dw4_scene_host`.

Verified by `port/tools/verify_scene.py` (in `make check`): the loader's own check, the mesh count
against the file reader (549), and the OBJ bytes of all 371 distinct meshes identical across the
loader, the file reader (`dw4asset models`) and the repository's decoder (`igb_models.py`).

**Evidence:** port/tools/verify_scene.py

<a id="f482"></a>
### #482 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x20e33000 — igb_inst.TID2REF; DW4_IGB_TY_OBJECTREF/DW4_IGB_TY_MEMREF; dw4_scene_load's materialise

A pack's descriptor type id IS the field's kind, so the loader needs no (class, offset) table to do finding #423's id -> pointer pass: a type-0 value (igObjectRef) is always an object id or NULL and a type-7 value (igMemoryRef) always a pool-block id or NULL -- 0.0% 'neither' over 112,891 + 50,591 values of six NDMW.AFS entries -- while every other type has values outside the id space (floats 88%, matrices 100%, ints are small so they land in it). The apparent counter-evidence (type 0 against floats and enums) came from inherited slots (ordn <= K, not fields) and from the classes where igb_inst.find_K cannot map ordinals to fields. Implemented in port/src/scene.c: dw4_scene_load rewrites type-0/type-7 values from ids into arena handles (NULL stays NULL) and reads the geometry through them; the loader counts it and dw4asset scene --check reports '0 NEITHER' on the real pack every run (37,816 rewritten, 21,875 NULL), and the OBJ output stays byte-identical across the loader, the file reader and igb_models.py over 549 meshes -- the proof the classification is right. Re-measured by scripts/verify_field_kinds.py (make rules).

**Notes:** THE DESCRIPTOR'S TYPE ID IS THE FIELD'S KIND -- NO (class, offset) TABLE WAS NEEDED.

The loader has to do finding #423's id -> pointer pass, so it must know which of a record's values
are references. I set out to generate a (class, offset) -> kind table from `data/reflection.json`,
and the first measurement seemed to justify it: grouping values by type id and printing the reflected
field name they landed on showed type 0 against `igObjectRefMetaField` (148), inherited `_name` slots
(81), `igFloatMetaField` (2) and `igEnumMetaField` (2). That reading was wrong, twice over:

* the `_name` rows are inherited slots (`ordn <= K`) that are not fields at all, and
* the float/enum rows are the handful of classes where `igb_inst.find_K` cannot map ordinals to
 fields (`87 of the 93`).

The container already states the kind. `igb_inst.TID2REF` maps the descriptor's type id to the
reflection's type name -- 0 `igObjectRef`, 1 `igInt`, 2 `igBool`, 4 `igString`, 5 `igRawRef`,
7 `igMemoryRef`, 16 `igFloat`, 36 `igVec3f`,... -- and it is the same map `find_K` validates
against. The bytes agree, decisively (NDMW.AFS entries 621/179/642/622/183/140):

 type 0 igObjectRef 62.2% object id, 37.8% NULL, 0.0% neither (112,891 values)
 type 7 igMemoryRef 0.0% object id, 61.6% block id, 38.4% NULL, 0.0% neither (50,591)
 type 1 igInt 91.2% "object", 8.2% "block" -- small integers land in the id space
 type 16 igFloat 11.3% "object", 88.0% neither
 type 40 igMatrix44f 0.1%, 99.9% neither

**Every value of type 0 is an id or NULL, and every value of type 7 as well -- 0.0% "neither" over
163,482 values -- and no other type is like that** (the ones that look like it are small types whose
values are trivially in range, or doubles whose low word is zero). So the rule is simply: a type-0
value is an object reference, a type-7 value a memory (pool-block) reference, everything else a
plain value -- read from the value's own descriptor, with no table and no reflection.

IMPLEMENTED IN THE PORT (`port/src/scene.c`). `dw4_scene_load` now performs the pass: after
placing every payload it walks each object record's descriptor and rewrites type-0 and type-7 values
from ids into handles (NULL stays NULL), then reads the geometry back through them. The loader
counts what it did on the pack it loaded and `dw4asset scene --check` reports it:

 50,497 ids placed in order, 0 payloads differ from the file (blocks), 0 broken mesh handles,
 549 meshes / 82,134 vertices
 id->pointer: 37,816 reference(s) rewritten, 21,875 NULL, 0 NEITHER (must be 0)

`0 NEITHER` is the C-side check of this finding, on the real pack, every run. And because the mesh
walk now reads `_vertexArray`, `_vdata`, `_stripLengths`, `_lengthData` and the shape's attr list as
*handles*, the OBJ output is the strongest available proof the classification is right: 549 meshes,
**byte-identical across the loader, the file reader and `igb_models.py`**.

Tooling: `scripts/verify_field_kinds.py` re-measures the matrix (`make rules`), asserting 0.0%
"neither" for both reference types and flagging any other type that a range test could not tell from
a reference. Two earlier misreadings in the port are worth keeping: an id is *not* distinguishable
from a handle by range (ids reach 50,497 and the arena is 5 MB), so a reader must know which it
holds rather than guess; and the geometry chain's fields must be found by *ordinal*, not by offset,
whenever the class serialises a string before them (a long name shifts every later value).

Still not done, and deliberately: rewriting the *contents* of a memory block. A `_vdata` block holds
the ids of the streams it lists and the live engine sees pointers there, but a block can be shared and
the item kind is a property of the field that points at it, so that pass needs its own rule; readers
resolve those items through `dw4_scene_handle` today.

**Evidence:** port/tools/verify_scene.py

<a id="f483"></a>
### #483 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x20e33000 — dw4_scene_load's build_graph/graph_add/list_items; igSceneInfo/igGraphPathList/igGraphPath/igNodeList/igMatrix44f

A stage's scene graph walks offline through the loader's handles: igSceneInfo (name at ordinal 2) -> igGraphPathList -> igGraphPath -> its own igNodeList (an igObjectRef, NOT a memory ref on the path -- walking that found zero nodes) -> node chains, where a node carries a name (a string value), an igMatrix44f (type 40, row-major with the translation at words 12..14), an igNodeList of children and a reference to an igGeometry when it draws one. Every value is found by its descriptor type and every reference is a materialised handle; a list's items are ids in the block behind its igMemoryRef. On NDMW.AFS entry 621 it yields 18 scenes / 99 nodes with the game's own entity names (Ogremon, Goblimon, Sdc0102a, Slope) and plausible camera chains (animCamera03_300 -> cameraanim_GP1 -> cameraanim_group01 (30,130,24) ->...). So P4.2's scene data reads offline, no live capture needed. Open: these graphs are camera/scene-animation chains -- no node references an igGeometry, and igSceneInfo's own igNodeList (ordinal 11) is empty in all 18 records, so a stage's objects are placed elsewhere (the beModelCtrl* family is the candidate: 1,075 beModelCtrlNode2, 584 beModelCtrlData, 147 beModelCtrlInfo).

**Notes:** THE STAGE'S SCENE GRAPH WALKS OFFLINE, THROUGH THE LOADER'S HANDLES (P4.2's scene data).

 igSceneInfo name (ordinal 2) + igGraphPathList (a reference) -- 18 records in entry 621
 igGraphPathList -> igGraphPath ids (a list object: items in its
 igMemoryRef block)
 igGraphPath -> its own igNodeList (a reference, not a memory ref)
 igNodeList -> node ids
 node -> its name (a string value)
 an igMatrix44f (type 40), when it has one
 an igNodeList of children (recurse)
 a reference to an igGeometry, when it draws one

The whole walk is over the **arena's handles**, not file offsets: a node's children are a
materialised `igObjectRef`, and a list's items are ids in the block behind its `igMemoryRef`
(resolved through the handle table, because a block's contents are deliberately not rewritten).
Every value is found by its descriptor **type** -- the matrix is whichever value is an igMatrix44f,
the children whichever reference resolves to an igNodeList, the geometry whichever resolves to an
igGeometry -- so no class or offset is hardcoded.

What it yields on `NDMW.AFS` entry 621 (`dw4asset scene --stage \<roms> stag0102.igb --ref 1 --graph`):

 18 scene(s), 99 node(s)
 scene 0 Scene Graph 15 nodes scene 6 Ogremon 21 nodes
 scene 1 Slope 21 nodes scene 7 Sdc0102a 21 nodes... scene 8 Goblimon 21 nodes

and the node chains are recognisable and geometrically plausible: `BG01 -> igSwitch -> igTransform
(0, 1, 0) -> igTransform (0, -5, 0) -> igTransform (0, 0, 130) -> igCamera cameraShape00`, and
`animCamera03_300 -> cameraanim_GP1 -> cameraanim_group01 (30, 130, 24) -> cameraanim03 (0, 0, 90)
-> igCamera cameraanimShape03`. The matrix reads row-major with its translation at words 12..14
(`_m` is the igTransform value at its reflected +32), which is what a renderer needs.

Two things this settles and one it opens:

* the scene records carry the **entity names from the game's own data** -- `Ogremon`, `Goblimon`,
 `Sdc0102a`, `e_blosso`, `i_bgclear`, `Slope` -- so a stage's scene list reads offline (no live
 capture, contrary to what P4.2 assumed).
* a graph path reaches its roots through a *separate* `igNodeList` object (an `igObjectRef`), not
 through a memory ref on the path itself -- the first attempt walked `igGraphPath`'s own
 `igMemoryRef` and found zero nodes.
* **open:** these graphs are the camera/scene-animation chains. No node in them references an
 `igGeometry`, and the `igSceneInfo`'s own `igNodeList` (ordinal 11) is empty in all 18 records, so
 the *objects* of a stage are placed somewhere else -- the `beModelCtrl*` family (1,075
 `beModelCtrlNode2`, 584 `beModelCtrlData`, 147 `beModelCtrlInfo`) is the obvious candidate. That
 is the next probe, and the last piece a renderer needs.

**Evidence:** port/src/scene.c

<a id="f484"></a>
### #484 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x20e33000 — beModelCtrlNode2 / beModelCtrlInfoRamData / igGeometry / igTransform; port/src/render.c; dw4asset render

A stage's object placement is NOT in the pack -- it is a load-time assignment. beModelCtrlNode2 (1,075 records, the model node) carries a name, an igMatrix44f (type 40), an igNodeList of children and an igAttrList that is EMPTY in all 1,075; its other references are NULL. No file-structural record references an igGeometry (a reverse index over every type-0 value finds only runtime records: beNumberCtrlInfoRam 43, the *InfoRam family), the same for igTransform (beParticleCtrl2InfoRam 161), the *InfoRam* classes are referenced by nobody (all 1,075 beModelCtrlInfoRamData have no parent) and igSceneInfo's own igNodeList is empty in all 18 records. So the model->node pairing needs a live capture or the controller's load code. What the pack DOES give a renderer is each mesh: the loader's output is renderer-ready (positions/UVs/triangles/names), and the port's new software rasteriser (dw4asset render: camera fitted to the mesh bbox, perspective, z-buffered barycentric scan, Lambert shading, PNG out) renders it as recognisable shapes -- pCylinderShape3 is a barrel with lugs and a 45-cell sheet reads as rocks, crystals, a sphere, a fern, a cauldron. tools/verify_render.py checks three meshes are drawn, cover a sane fraction of the frame and are byte-identical across runs.

**Notes:** WHAT A RENDERER CAN AND CANNOT GET FROM A PACK -- MEASURED.

The scene graphs of finding #483 are camera/scene-animation chains, so the next question was where a
stage's *objects* are placed. They are not in the file:

* `beModelCtrlNode2` (1,075 records -- count for count the largest node class) is the model node: a
 name, an **igMatrix44f** (type 40, the same ordinal an igTransform carries), an **igNodeList** of
 children, and an **igAttrList** that is **empty in all 1,075 records**. Its other references are
 NULL: ordinal 3 (all 1,075), ordinal 11 (all 1,075).
* **No file-structural record references an `igGeometry` at all.** A reverse index over every type-0
 value in entry 621 finds `igGeometry` referenced only by runtime records (`beNumberCtrlInfoRam` 43
 times; `beCtrlInfoRam`-family), and the same for `igTransform` (`beParticleCtrl2InfoRam` 161).
* The `*InfoRam*` classes are referenced by nobody (all 1,075 `beModelCtrlInfoRamData` have no
 parent), which is the signature of runtime-constructed data.
* And `igSceneInfo`'s own `igNodeList` (ordinal 11) is empty in all 18 records.

So the model -> node assignment is made **at load time** by the model controller, not stored: the
pack has the models (549 geometry attributes, 584 named `igGeometry` shapes) and the nodes (1,075
matrices), and the loader pairs them. A stage's *layout* therefore needs either a live capture
(P4.2's original assumption, which the scene *names* turned out not to need but the placement does) or
the controller's own load code decompiled. What a renderer *can* do from the pack alone is draw each
mesh: the loader's output is already renderer-ready (positions, UVs, triangles, names -- #481/#482).

IMPLEMENTED (P4.3's first increment, `dw4asset render`): a software rasteriser -- camera fitted to
each mesh's bounding box, perspective projection, z-buffered barycentric scan, Lambert shading, a
per-mesh tint -- writing a PNG through the port's own encoder. `--name/--attr` draws one model,
`--limit/--cols` draws a sheet, `--wire` draws edges.

It is the end-to-end check of everything below it: the geometry comes out of a pack, through the
arena and the handles, and renders as recognisable shapes (`pCylinderShape3` is a barrel with lugs;
a 45-cell sheet of entry 621 reads as rocks, crystals, a sphere, a fern, a cauldron, a diamond).
`tools/verify_render.py` (in `make check`) renders three named meshes and asserts each image is not
the background, covers a sane fraction of the frame (26 %, 11 %, 31 %) and is byte-identical across
two runs.

Not the game's pipeline: the GS's register state, the VU1 microcode's transform/lighting and the
stage's object placement (above) are all still open. A live scene capture is what closes the last of
those.

**Evidence:** port/tools/verify_render.py

<a id="f485"></a>
### #485 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x20e33000 — igGeometryAttr1_5, igTextureBindAttr, igTextureAttr, beTextureCtrlAttr; port/src/render.c dw4_render_mesh_tex

The mesh -> texture binding is NOT in the pack, and igGeometryAttr1_5 has no material reference at all: its ordinals 5, 9, 11 and 12 are NULL in all 549 attributes (only 4 = igVertexArray1_1 and 13 = igPrimLengthArray1_1 are ever set). The texture side is file data (igTextureBindAttr -> igTextureAttr ordinal 12 = igImage; beTextureCtrlAttr ordinal 2 = the texture's own name like TA01GM07.png), but no record links a mesh to a binding and the texture names do not match the model DCC names, so the pairing is made when the controller is built -- the same shape as the placement negative (#484). The UV path is proven regardless: dw4asset render --texture \<name> --texentry \<n> decodes a named texture through the port's texpack reader (#476) and samples it per texel with barycentric UV interpolation (v flipped, the pack stores grids bottom-up), so pCylinderShape3 with BJGR01.png renders as a correctly textured barrel; tools/verify_render.py asserts the textured frame differs from the flat one and is non-blank.

**Notes:** THE MESH -> TEXTURE BINDING IS NOT IN THE PACK; THE UV PATH IS PROVEN ANYWAY.

Texturing the port's renderer needed to know which texture a mesh uses. What the pack does have:

 igTextureBindAttr (130) ordinal 4 -> an igTextureAttr, ordinal 5 -> an int (a unit/flags)
 igTextureAttr (130) ordinal 12 -> an igImage, ordinal 16 -> an igImageMipMapList
 beTextureCtrlAttr (96) ordinal 2 -> the texture's own name ("TA01GM07.png"), ordinal 3 ->
 an igAttrSet

so the chain texture -> image is file data, and the bindings exist as records. What it does NOT have
is a link from a mesh to a binding:

* `igGeometryAttr1_5`'s references are ordinal 4 (`igVertexArray1_1`), 5, 9, 11, 12 and 13
 (`igPrimLengthArray1_1`). Ordinals **5, 9, 11 and 12 are NULL in all 549 attributes** -- there is no
 material or texture reference on a geometry attribute at all;
* no record references an `igTextureBindAttr` from the geometry side, and the texture-side names
 (`TA01GM07.png`) do not match the model-side DCC names (`pCylinderShape3`), so name matching is not
 a route either.

This is the same shape as finding #484: the pairing is made when the *controller* is built. A mesh's
texture therefore needs the load-time assignment (a live capture, or the controller's load code), not
a pack walk.

THE UV PATH IS PROVEN ANYWAY. `dw4asset render --texture \<name> --texentry \<n>` decodes a named
texture from a texture pack through the port's own reader (#476) and samples it per texel with
barycentric UV interpolation, flipping `v` because the pack stores grids bottom-up. Rendering
`pCylinderShape3` with `BJGR01.png` (a 64x64 texture of entry 625) produces a correctly textured
barrel: the game's own palette, mapped through the mesh's own UVs. `tools/verify_render.py` asserts
the textured frame differs from the flat-shaded one and is still non-blank, so the sampler cannot
silently stop working.

So everything the renderer needs is now in place except (a) the mesh -> texture binding and (b) the
stage's object placement -- both load-time assignments, and both closed by the same live scene
capture.

**Evidence:** port/tools/verify_render.py

**Where:** 0x002e7640 — tools/typed_coverage.py; config/SLUS-20836/signatures.tsv; P1.1/P1.2/P1.3

P1's typing lever is NAMING, not more signatures -- measured by provenance over the 2,746 raw *(T *)((char *)x + N) casts in the matched C (scripts/typed_worklist.py): 2,097 (76.4%) apply the offset to the RECEIVER (arg0..), so what is missing is the function's class; 492 (17.9%) to a declared pointer local (a type); 112 (4.1%) to an undeclared local with no assignment before the cast (body inspection); 45 (1.6%) to a global (its class). The direct-call-signature route (P1.2) covers only 7 callees / <=21 casts (0.8%) -- spent. So P1.3 (naming) is the lever, exactly as P1.2's ceiling said, and the worklist makes the next session's target explicit.

**Notes:** Measured with scripts/typed_worklist.py (new): it reuses typed_coverage.py's parser so its counts agree with the gate (2,746 vs the gate's 2,823 offset casts) and buckets every raw cast by where its base comes from. The per-callee table (which signature would unlock which cast) now doubles as the evidence that the signature route is spent: 7 callees, <=21 casts, the top being FUN_0024ce50 (5), a function-pointer call through a vtable slot (5), and a C++ virtual call (3).

<a id="f487"></a>
### #487 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x005f9234 — igGeometry (0x528e70) / igTransform._m +32 / igAttrList; scripts/capture_scene.py; dw4asset render --scene

A stage's model placement can be captured from a SAVED EE RAM dump -- no emulator needed -- because the load-time assignments (#484/#485) exist in RAM: scanning for igGeometry objects (vtable 0x00528e70, 4-byte aligned) and reading +0x08 (the DCC name), +0x10 (_parentList -> the parent node chain) and +0x20 (the igAttrList whose entries with vtable 0x005238c0 are the geometry attrs) gives 826 objects in the pre-leomon EE RAM dump, 702 with a plausible world matrix (the chain's igTransform _m at +32 composed child*parent, validated as affine/orthonormal -- which is what proves the offsets) and 786 with a geometry attr. dw4asset render --scene \<tsv> then joins capture to pack by NAME and transforms every matching mesh by the row's matrix: 424 rows placed, 195,868 vertices / 130,194 triangles drawn at the stage's real coordinates (pCylinderShape3 at (0,5.13,-1.5), M_trisideShape at (235,0,0), unnamed nodes at (267,42,225)). Traps: the objects are 4-byte aligned (an 8-byte scan finds none) and +0x10 is a LIST to dereference, not the parent node. Still coarse: one tint for the whole scene, no culling, no GS/VU1, and the capture is per-session.

**Notes:** THE LIVE SCENE CAPTURE RUNS FROM A SAVED DUMP -- AND PUTS REAL GEOMETRY WHERE THE GAME DOES.

Findings #484/#485 both ended at the same wall: which geometry a node draws and which texture it uses
are **load-time assignments**, so they are not in the pack. They *are* in RAM -- and a saved RAM dump
is as live as a paused emulator, so `scripts/capture_scene.py` reads one and needs no emulator:

 igGeometry ("M_*Shape", vtable 0x00528e70) 826 of them in a loaded stage
 +0x08 the DCC name string
 +0x10 _parentList -> its array's first entry is the parent node
 +0x20 igAttrList {_count +0x08, _array +0x10}; entries whose vtable is 0x005238c0 are the
 geometry attributes this node draws
 igTransform _m at +32

A node's world matrix is its chain's transforms composed (child * parent), and only **plausible
affine** matrices are used -- last row (0,0,0,1), orthonormal rotation rows -- which is also what
proves the offsets: a wrong field reads as zeros, NaNs or garbage and is dropped instead of being
multiplied in. (The first attempt scanned 8-byte aligned words and found nothing: these objects are
4-byte aligned. The second read the parent *list* pointer as the parent node.)

Result on the pre-leomon EE RAM dump (32.5 MB of EE RAM, a paused stag0102):

 826 igGeometry objects, 786 with a geometry attr, 681 named
 702 with a plausible placement, 40 of 826 with no geometry attr, 145 with no name
 e.g. pCylinderShape3 (0.00, 5.13, -1.50), M_trisideShape (235, 0, 0), M_loadShape (0, -90, 0),
 unnamed (267.78, 42.47, 225.11) -- the stage's coordinates, not a model's

**And it renders.** `dw4asset render --scene \<tsv>` uses the captured *name* as the link back to the
pack (the runtime attr address is not the pack's id): every mesh whose DCC name matches a row is
transformed by that row's matrix and merged into one buffer, so one camera fits the stage and the
geometry is drawn where the game puts it -- 424 rows placed, **195,868 vertices / 130,194 triangles**.
`--span`/`--cam` frame a region (compacting the vertex list too, otherwise one far-flung node shrinks
everything to a dot).

So the renderer's two gaps are closed as far as *drawing a stage* is concerned: the layout comes from
a capture, the geometry from the pack, and the name is the join. What is still coarse is the *view*:
one tint for the whole scene (the merge loses per-mesh colour), no back-face culling, no GS state and
no VU1 microcode -- and the capture is per-session, so a different stage needs its own dump.

**Evidence:** port/src/dw4asset.c
