**Where:** 0x003bf650 — module_map.py / docs/engine/module-map.md; typed_worklist.py --by-region --game-only; beNDMWMdlPlayer2InfoWork_GetVTable (0x3bf5f0)

Of the 2,097 raw casts applied to a function's receiver (finding #486), only 289 (13.8%) are game+engine code -- 1,676 (85%) are third-party middleware that neither goal needs typed: Intrinsic Alchemy core (801 casts, 274 funcs), Alchemy core registry/ini/pools (246+149), Sony SDK+C runtime (140), CRIWARE ADX/Sofdec/MPS (112), Lua 4.0 (88), CRI audio SNDF/SUD (57), Alchemy insight/serialise (56), libpng (20), Alchemy p2 (8). The map that shows this is mechanical: contiguous runs of symbols (a run IS the original translation unit, MWCC emitting in source order) labelled by the strings their functions reference -- scripts/module_map.py -> docs/engine/module-map.md, 341 runs: 0x100000 SDK+C runtime, 0x141000 Alchemy core incl. the VU1 ucode symbol table, 0x1f0000 p2 (the PS2 renderer backend), 0x20d000 libpng, 0x226000 Alchemy insight/registry, 0x246000 Lua 4.0, 0x25d000 CRIWARE, 0x2b0000 CRI audio, 0x2d1000 Alchemy core, 0x310000 memory pools + IOP loader, 0x334000 GAME+engine, 0x460000 actor/scene managers. So P1.3's queue is 68 functions/289 casts, and the game remainder sits in class blocks (FUN_00388e60 between beHitLandModelOrder vfunc_22 and vfunc_21; FUN_003bf650 after beNDMWMdlPlayer2InfoWork_GetVTable and before _Register) -- the class is proven by the block, the role is what remains: _GetVTable builds a temporary on the stack (a0=sp+32), calls FUN_003bf650 (an _InitFields shape), stamps vtable 0x54d500, then calls FUN_00350410 on it -- called by exactly three _GetVTable's, all stamping 0x54d500, which is not an RTTI vtable in data/vtables.json. Naming it needs that framework type recovered, not a class guessed from the block.

**Notes:** 85% OF THE "NAMING" BACKLOG IS THIRD-PARTY MIDDLEWARE -- P1.3's QUEUE IS 289 GAME-CODE CASTS.

P1.3's plan was: name the 738 `FUN_*` functions that own the 2,097 receiver-based raw casts, and each
name makes that function's accesses typeable. Before doing it, ask *whose* code they are. Vendor
code is loud about itself, so the map is mechanical -- contiguous runs of symbols (a run is the
original translation unit; MWCC emits a unit's functions in source order), each labelled by the
strings its functions reference (`scripts/module_map.py` -> `docs/engine/module-map.md`, 341 runs):

 0x100000-0x141000 Sony SDK + C runtime libdma/libmpeg/libipu/libcdvd/libsd/libc
 0x141000-0x1f0000 Intrinsic Alchemy core IG_GFX_*, shaders, the VU1 ucode symbol table
 0x1f0000-0x20d000 Alchemy `p2` the PS2/EE renderer backend
 0x20d000-0x226000 libpng
 0x226000-0x246000 Alchemy insight/registry/serialise
 0x246000-0x259000 Lua 4.0
 0x25d000-0x2b0000 CRIWARE ADX / Sofdec / MPS / SUD / PS2RNA
 0x2b0000-0x2d1000 CRI audio SNDF / SUD / MWSFD
 0x2d1000-0x310000 Alchemy core registry, ini, memory pools
 0x310000-0x334000 Alchemy pools + IOP module loader
 0x334000-0x460000 GAME + engine be*/MODELCTRL/STAGECTL/menus
 0x460000-0x540000 engine scene/overlay/actor managers

Bucketing the receiver casts by region (`typed_worklist.py --by-region --game-only`):

 Intrinsic Alchemy core (graphics, math, shaders, GS/VU1) 274 funcs 801 casts
 GAME + engine 62 funcs 281
 Alchemy core (registry, ini, memory pools) 107 246
 Alchemy memory pools + IOP module loader 109 149
 Sony SDK + C runtime 52 140
 CRIWARE 42 112
 Lua 4.0 30 88
 CRI audio (SNDF/SUD/MWSFD) 22 57
 Alchemy insight/registry/serialise 23 56
 libpng 9 20
 Alchemy `p2` + actor managers 7 16
 ----------------------------------------------------------------
 game code total 68 289 (13.8%)

So P1.3's queue is **68 functions / 289 casts**, not 738/2,097. Naming Alchemy's or CRIWARE's
internals buys nothing for either goal -- a modder recompiles against the retail binaries and a port
replaces the middleware wholesale -- so the 1,676 middleware casts should not be typed at all. This
is the same shape as the finding that made `scripts/typed_worklist.py` worth writing: the lever is
real but the *target list* was 7x too big.

Where the game-code remainder sits: in **class blocks**. `FUN_00388e60` (36 casts) is between
`Gap__Bec__beHitLandModelOrder__vfunc_22` and `..._vfunc_21`; `FUN_003bf650` (29) is immediately
after `beNDMWMdlPlayer2InfoWork_GetVTable` and before `_Register`/`_GetMetaObject`; `FUN_003ba610`
(17) likewise for `beNDMWStatusSubSlot`. So the *class* is proven by the unit block; what is still
open is the **role**, and the block's shape is regular enough to attack it:

 beNDMWMdlPlayer2InfoWork_GetVTable 0x3bf5f0, 88 bytes
 a0 = sp+32; jal FUN_003bf650 <- build+init a temporary on the stack
 v1 = 0x54d500; sw v1,32(sp) <- then stamp its vtable
 a0 = sp+32; a1 = 0; jal FUN_00350410 <- and call a method on it

i.e. an `_InitFields`-shaped function whose caller then sets the vtable -- `FUN_003bf650` is called
by exactly three `_GetVTable`s (`beNDMWMdlItemInfoWork`, `beNDMWMdlPEBaseInfoWork`,
`beNDMWMdlPlayer2InfoWork`), all three stamping `0x54d500`, which is *not* an RTTI vtable in
`data/vtables.json` and so is a framework structure rather than a class's. Naming it correctly means
recovering that framework type, not guessing a class from the block -- which is exactly the
"recovered, never guessed" rule.

<a id="f489"></a>
### #489 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00491910 — p2 backend 0x20d790 (the symbol-table parser); scripts/vu1_microcode.py; docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md

The VU1 microcode symbol table at 0x491910 parses to the renderer's complete program map (P4.1, extending #383): 237 entry points / 236 end markers over a 109,184-byte ucode image (vu1_entry_UCODE_BEGIN;0.. vu1_end_UCODE_END;109184). By family: LIGHT 91 programs/~89,984 B, TRANS 34/19,392, CLIP 25/8,896, COMPRESS 11/8,112, MULTIPASS 19/7,184, PARTICLE 4/4,336, BLEND 8/3,632, VIEWPORT 8/3,168, CALC 10/3,088, FOG 3/1,840, TEXGEN 4/1,392, EXECUTE 10/1,360, DECOMPRESSION 2/1,120, CULL 4/528, SCALE 1/288, NORMALIZE 1/176, XGKICK 1/48. Lighting is 82% of the programs. 35 start offsets carry more than one name -- those are aliases (LIGHT_SHARED at 11152 is the same code as LIGHT_N_DIFFUSE_AMBIENT_DIRECTIONAL, the shared name being the outer envelope), and the naming is systematic: \<FAMILY>_SHARED_\<variant>, the variant giving light count, LOCAL/LOCAL_OBJ, linear attenuation, _NOSPEC and a _MC_A/_MC_E/_MC_D/_MC_AD/_MC_S multipass-colour suffix. The engine selects programs BY NAME, which is why the table is in the image: the p2 backend parses the string once at runtime (0x20d790 splits it on ';' in place, token base 0x57c2b0, count at 0x4964d0). scripts/vu1_microcode.py -> docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md, checked by make rules.

**Notes:** THE VU1 MICROCODE MAP IS COMPLETE AND USABLE (P4.1) -- 237 PROGRAMS, AND LIGHTING IS 82% OF THEM.

Finding #383 recorded that the VU1 assembler left its symbol table in rodata at 0x491910. Parsed
out, it is the renderer's *complete* program map, and `scripts/vu1_microcode.py` turns it into one
(`docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md`, `make rules` checks it is current):

 237 entry points / 236 end markers over a 109,184-byte ucode image
 the table runs vu1_entry_UCODE_BEGIN;0.. vu1_end_UCODE_END;109184

 family progs bytes reaches offset
 LIGHT 91 89984 62848
 TRANS 34 19392 90016
 CLIP 25 8896 80544
 COMPRESS 11 8112 105552
 MULTIPASS 19 7184 98416
 PARTICLE 4 4336 94352
 BLEND 8 3632 109184
 VIEWPORT 8 3168 89536
 CALC 10 3088 67440
 FOG 3 1840 79680
 TEXGEN 4 1392 68832
 EXECUTE 10 1360 69232
 DECOMPRESSION 2 1120 98944
 CULL 4 528 81072
 SCALE 1 288 69520
 NORMALIZE 1 176 5056
 XGKICK 1 48 624
 OTHER 1 0 0

(The byte column sums past 109,184 because ranges **nest**: 35 start offsets carry more than one name.
Those are aliases -- `LIGHT_SHARED` at 11152 is the same code as
`LIGHT_N_DIFFUSE_AMBIENT_DIRECTIONAL`, and the shared name is the outer envelope covering its
variants; the naming is systematic, `\<FAMILY>_SHARED_\<variant>` where the variant names the number of
lights, whether it is LOCAL or LOCAL_OBJ, linear attenuation, `_NOSPEC`, and a multipass/colour
suffix `_MC_A`/`_MC_E`/`_MC_D`/`_MC_AD`/`_MC_S`.)

**Lighting is the bulk of the pipeline**: 91 of 237 programs and ~90 KB, with 8- and 4-light
variants, spot and infinite lights, and per-material-colour multipass. Next: TRANS (34),
CLIP (25), COMPRESS (11 -- the VU1-to-GIF packet compression), MULTIPASS (19), PARTICLE (4),
BLEND (8, the W1-W4 skinning weights), VIEWPORT/CALC (projection), TEXGEN (linear/spherical/eye
texgen), FOG, CULL, XGKICK (the GIF transfer).

It is *used* by name, which is why it is in the image at all: the `p2` backend parses the string once
at runtime (`0x20d790` -- splits it on ';' in place, keeping the token base at 0x57c2b0, and the
parsed count at 0x4964d0) and the renderer then selects a program by name and jumps the VU1 to its
offset. So the names in this table are the engine's own vocabulary for the pipeline, and they are
what a port has to implement (or interpret) -- not a reconstruction.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md

<a id="f490"></a>
### #490 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00467178 — VU1 microcode blob 0x467178 (109,184 bytes); the p2 renderer's EE-side vfunc stubs and vtable 0x542a48; scripts/vu1_microcode.py; docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md

The VU1 microcode image of 109,184 bytes (the 237 programs mapped in #489) is at BLOB_VA 0x00467178, ending 0x00481BF8. Two independent methods agree on the start: the image is ZERO-FILLED from start+109184 (0x481BC0 holds real microcode, 0x481BF8 onward is 00000000), and a per-8-byte VU-plausibility scan over the surrounding rodata peaks exactly at 0x467178. The content confirms it without any VU disassembler: 1483 NOP pairs in (lower,upper) order and ZERO reversed (canonical pair: 8000033c lower-NOP then 000002ff upper-NOP -- which fixes word order); lower-word opcode profile over 13,648 instructions is 61% the 0x40+ upper-only marker, then LQ 12.1% (1649), ILW 8.8% (1198), IADDIU 4.8% (649), ISUBIU 2.2% (300), IBGTZ 231, JR 203, ISW 196, SQ 164, BAL 149, IBEQ 112, B 103, IBNE 98; and the even/odd opcode-asymmetry measure is 0.90 here vs 0.076 in known EE code at 0x200000. The blob sits immediately after a run of EE-side vfunc stubs: 0x542a48 holds pointers to the jr ra stubs 0x466d00..0x467110 whose last ends where the microcode begins. WHOSE table that is is NOT established -- it is not an RTTI vtable in data/vtables.json, no [rtti,0] header appears within 0x200 bytes before it, and the words just before the run are igAdxAudio vfunc values 16-19, so it may be a subclass vtable rather than the renderer's; treat the adjacency as a layout fact, not an attribution. NEGATIVES so they are not retried: no code references 0x467178 and no word holds that address or the constant 109184, so the upload does not build the address inline; and B/BAL targets do NOT land on the vu1_entry_* offsets (0-5% over 252 branches vs ~2.7% chance), so the 237 programs are entered by the EE setting the VU1 PC, not by branches. Ghidra provides only r5900:LE:32:default, no VU language. scripts/vu1_microcode.py --elf \<game.elf> --extract \<out> writes the image (game data, never committed) and re-measures; --check gates the generated doc in make rules.

**Notes:** THE VU1 MICROCODE IMAGE IS AT 0x467178 -- AND IT VERIFIES WITHOUT A VU DISASSEMBLER.

The map (#489) says 237 programs over 109,184 bytes; this is where those bytes are.

 BLOB_VA = 0x00467178 BLOB_SIZE = 109184 end = 0x00481BF8

Two independent methods agree on the start. (1) The image is **zero-filled from start + 109184**:
the bytes at 0x481BC0 are real microcode (`81edab7d 010021e3 81e88b7d 000002ff`... `10010801
000002ff`) and from 0x481BF8 onward they are 00000000, so the size can only be right if the start is
right. (2) A per-8-byte VU-plausibility scan over the surrounding rodata peaks at exactly 0x467178.

What makes it *microcode* and not a coincidence is the content, and none of it needs a disassembler:

 * **1483 NOP pairs in `(lower, upper)` order, zero reversed** -- the canonical pair is the 64-bit
 instruction `8000033c` (lower NOP) then `000002ff` (upper NOP). That fixes the word order, which
 is what a decoder has to know first, and it is a 1483-to-0 measurement rather than a convention.
 * the lower-word opcode holds a real VU profile over 13,648 instructions: **61 % carry the
 `0x40+` upper-only marker** (a vector unit's code is mostly upper instructions), then
 **LQ 12.1 % (1649), ILW 8.8 % (1198), IADDIU 4.8 % (649), ISUBIU 2.2 % (300), IBGTZ 231,
 JR 203, ISW 196, SQ 164, BAL 149, IBEQ 112, B 103, IBNE 98** -- loads, stores, integer
 immediates and a real loop/branch structure.
 * the metric is not flattering everything: the same even/odd opcode-asymmetry measure is **0.90 in
 this region against 0.076 in known EE code** (`0x200000`).

The blob sits **immediately after the p2 renderer's EE-side vfunc stubs**: `0x542a48` is a vtable
whose slots are `0x466d00`, `0x466e00`, `0x466e10`, `0x466f80`, `0x4670b0`, `0x4670c0`, `0x4670d0`,
`0x4670e0`, `0x4670f0`, `0x467100`, `0x467110` -- `jr ra` stubs whose last one ends where the
microcode begins. So the EE side of the renderer (its vfunc table) and its VU side are laid out
together in rodata, which is a useful landmark for finding the rest of the p2 layer.

Negative results, so they are not retried: **no code references 0x467178** (and no word in the image
holds that address or the constant 109184), so the upload does not build the address inline -- it
comes through a table or the VU1 PC is set by an index; and **B/BAL targets do not land on the
`vu1_entry_*` offsets** (0-5 % over 252 branches, against ~2.7 % chance), so the 237 programs are
*not* entered by branches from inside the microcode -- the EE sets the VU1's program counter to the
named offset, which is what the symbol table's existence already implied.

Tooling: `scripts/vu1_microcode.py --elf \<game.elf> --extract \<out>` writes the image (game data,
never committed), re-measures the facts above, and `--check` gates the generated doc in `make rules`.
Ghidra cannot help here: the extension provides only `r5900:LE:32:default`, no VU language.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md

<a id="f491"></a>
### #491 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001f0960 — FUN_001f0960 (VU1 uploader, 0x1f0960); FUN_00203c50 / FUN_00203580 (the two transfers); FUN_00169e30 (the dispatcher, 0x169e30); global descriptor *(0x568F04); FUN_001f8190 (GS accessor)

The EE-side p2 renderer's VU1 upload path is traced end to end. p2 is 0x1f0000-0x20d000 (169 functions, all FUN_*) and only 6 of the 169 touch a memory-mapped register (platform_map.py --code-lo 0x1f0000 --code-hi 0x20d000): FUN_001f0960 (924 B, dma_spr+dmac+dmac_ctrl+vif1+vu1_regs -- the uploader), FUN_001f00e0 (dmac), FUN_001f8190 and FUN_001f8050 (gs), FUN_0020cd10 (timer), FUN_001f8350 (dma_spr). The chain: FUN_00169e30 (Alchemy core) takes a program INDEX in a1, computes s0 = *(a0+868) -> +16 + a1*48, i.e. a table of 48-BYTE PROGRAM RECORDS with a type at +28 and a size at +40; type==2 calls FUN_00203c50 (1196 B, string 'Buffer not aligned' -- the code transfer) and anything else calls FUN_00203580 (the data transfer), passing the record's +40. Both set the single global transfer descriptor *(0x568F04) = {+48 cursor advancing by 16, +56 source, +60 size, +64 flags with bit 5} and call FUN_001f0960 with that descriptor as a0. FUN_001f0960 waits on the DMA pointer page (0x10003020 & 0x1F001FE6 == 6, with a 100,000-iteration timeout that calls 0x1eba30), writes 2 to the dmac_ctrl page (0x1000E020) and 0x00800000 to VIF1 (0x10003C00), touches the VU1 registers at 0x10005000, then reads the size from descriptor+60 and masks it with 0xFFF8 -- i.e. it transfers in 8-BYTE UNITS, one VU instruction pair, which is what microcode upload looks like -- and calls the GS accessor FUN_001f8190 with 68. Six functions in the region name themselves in their own strings, so the identification is recovered not guessed: 0x1f8050 'p2readGSPrivRegister(%d)' (and it does touch the gs block), 0x20c270 'Unsupported texture format in p2LoadTexture: %d', 0x1fb710 'p2lighting', 0x1f7490 'GS csr.id = %d, csr.rev = %d', 0x207210 'swapbuffersPredicate'/'swapbuffersDrawPredicate', 0x2082f0 'textureLoadedPredicate'/'textureDeferredPredicate' -- the layer's whole outside face (texture upload, GS readback, swap, lighting). What this gives a port: the unit of work is a 48-byte program record addressed by an index (so 'which VU program runs' is a table index into the 237 names of #489), state reaches the VU1 as a TRANSFER rather than as calls (so a reimplementing port replaces the uploader with a 'here is program N and its constants' entry point and never needs VIF1 or SPR DMA), and the outside face is small and named. OPEN: nothing references the microcode image at 0x467178 (#490) -- not as an lui/addiu pair and not as a stored word -- so the code transfer's source is filled at load time from somewhere this static trace does not reach, and whether the linked-in copy is what gets uploaded (or the runtime image comes from a file) is unresolved.

**Notes:** See docs/engine/p2-renderer.md (hand-written analysis) for the full tables, the hardware-block names as tools/platform_map.py defines them, and the reproduction commands.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/p2-renderer.md

<a id="f492"></a>
### #492 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0048dd60

The VU1 program list is two tables that line up, and the second one's flags field is a capability bitmask. The metadata table at 0x48dd60 (NAME;flags;;code;;;; 201 records, #489) covers the PROGRAMS AND NOT THEIR ALIASES: of the 237 entry names, 201 have a record and the other 36 are 35 names containing SHARED (CLIP_SHARED, LIGHT_SHARED_LOCAL_1_NOSPEC, MULTIPASS_SHARED2..SHARED8,...) plus UCODE_BEGIN, the image-start marker at offset 0 rather than a program -- so there is exactly one record per distinct program, confirming from the other table what the 35 duplicated start offsets showed. code>>16 is a per-family counter (LIGHT 0..21, CALC 0..10, BLEND 0..7, VIEWPORT 0..4, TEXGEN/PARTICLE/FOG 0..3) and code&0xFFFF is 1 in 171 of 201 (the rest all odd: 0x31, 0x11, 0x5, 0x131, 0x51, 0x41). flags is a BITMASK with one bit per pipeline capability -- 29 bits used, 46 distinct values, so programs carry combinations -- and it reads by measurement: for each bit, compare the programs that have it set with the programs whose names contain each pipeline token. Exact matches (f1 1.00) settle the bit's meaning: 0x1 COMPRESS (11), 0x2 PARTICLE (4), 0x4 BLEND (8), 0x40 NORMALIZE (1), 0x100 LOCAL_OBJ (20), 0x1000 TEXGEN (4), 0x8000 SPOT (16), 0x100000 SCALE (10). The rest come with match quality: 0x10 CONVERT prec 1.00/rec 0.57, 0x800 MV 0.96, 0x40000 MVP 0.94, 0x2000000 MVPS 0.88, 0x80000 SPRITE 0.90, 0x400000 TRIANGLE 0.80, 0x4000 LOCAL 0.95 prec, 0x10000 SAVE, 0x20000 MT, 0x10000000 FAN, 0x20000000 CALC, 0x40000000 EXECUTE, 0x80000000 OUTPUT/XGKICK, 0x1000000 FOG, 0x8000000 CLIP_FOG, 0x800000 CULL, 0x4000000 SPRITE-KICK, 0x8 SETCOLOR. One bit 0x2000 (LIGHT_N_DIFFUSE_AMBIENT_DIRECTIONAL, LIGHT_N_SPECULAR_DIRECTIONAL) matches no token at all and is recorded as unmatched rather than guessed. For the port this is the engine's own PER-PROGRAM SPECIFICATION: the pair (family, flags) says what a program is -- a LIGHT program with 0x100|0x80|0x4000 is a local object-space 4-infinite-light non-specular variant -- without reading any VU instruction. Generated into docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md by scripts/vu1_microcode.py, gated by make rules.

**Notes:** THE VU1 PROGRAM LIST IS TWO TABLES THAT LINE UP, AND `flags` IS A CAPABILITY BITMASK.

#489/#490 mapped the 237 programs from the assembler's symbol table and recorded a second VU table
at 0x48dd60 (`NAME;flags;;code;;;;`, 201 records) without reading its fields. Read together the two
tables are one list plus its attributes, and the fields decode by measurement:

**The metadata covers the programs and not their aliases.** Of the 237 entry names, 201 have a
record; the other 36 are **35 names containing `SHARED`** (e.g. `CLIP_SHARED`,
`LIGHT_SHARED_LOCAL_1_NOSPEC`, `MULTIPASS_SHARED2`..`SHARED8`) **plus `UCODE_BEGIN`**, which is the
image-start marker at offset 0 rather than a program. So there is exactly one record per distinct
program, and the `SHARED` names are alternate spellings -- which is the same fact the 35 duplicated
start offsets showed, now confirmed from the other table.

**`code >> 16` is a per-family counter** -- LIGHT runs 0..21, CALC 0..10, BLEND 0..7, VIEWPORT 0..4,
TEXGEN/PARTICLE/FOG 0..3 -- while `code & 0xFFFF` is 1 in 171 of the 201 records (the rest are 0x31,
0x11, 0x5, 0x131, 0x51, 0x41: all odd).

**`flags` is a bitmask with one bit per pipeline capability** -- 29 bits used, 46 distinct values, so
programs carry combinations. And it can be *read*: for each bit, compare the programs that have it
set with the programs whose names contain each pipeline token, and score the match. Where the match
is exact, the bit's meaning is settled:

 0x00000001 COMPRESS (11 programs, f1 1.00)
 0x00000002 PARTICLE ( 4, f1 1.00)
 0x00000004 BLEND ( 8, f1 1.00)
 0x00000040 NORMALIZE ( 1, f1 1.00)
 0x00000100 LOCAL_OBJ (20, f1 1.00)
 0x00001000 TEXGEN ( 4, f1 1.00)
 0x00008000 SPOT (16, f1 1.00)
 0x00100000 SCALE (10, f1 1.00)

and the rest come with their match quality attached: `0x10` CONVERT (prec 1.00, rec 0.57),
`0x800` MV (0.96), `0x40000` MVP (0.94), `0x2000000` MVPS (0.88), `0x80000` SPRITE (0.90),
`0x400000` TRIANGLE (0.80), `0x4000` LOCAL (0.95 prec), `0x10000` SAVE, `0x20000` MT,
`0x10000000` FAN, `0x20000000` CALC, `0x40000000` EXECUTE, `0x80000000` OUTPUT/XGKICK,
`0x1000000` FOG, `0x8000000` CLIP_FOG, `0x800000` CULL, `0x4000000` SPRITE-KICK, `0x8` SETCOLOR.
One bit, `0x2000` (2 programs: `LIGHT_N_DIFFUSE_AMBIENT_DIRECTIONAL`,
`LIGHT_N_SPECULAR_DIRECTIONAL`), matches no token at all -- recorded as unmatched rather than
guessed.

Why it matters for the port: this is the engine's own **per-program specification**. A reimplemented
renderer has to reproduce what each program computes, and the pair (family, flags) now says what a
program *is* -- e.g. a `LIGHT` program with `0x100|0x80|0x4000` is a local, object-space, 4-infinite-
light, non-specular variant -- without reading a single VU instruction. The generated table is in
docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md and `make rules` keeps it current; the correlation code is deliberately
reproducible so a wrong token shows up as a low f1 rather than in prose.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md

<a id="f493"></a>
### #493 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00467178 — VU1 microcode blob 0x467178; VU1 micro memory (0x11000000, 16 KiB); scripts/p2s_ram.py vu1MicroMem.bin; the symbol-table lookup at 0x20d790 and the uploader 0x001f0960 (finding #491)

The linked-in VU1 microcode at 0x467178 IS what runs: it is uploaded in place, in a per-program VU1 window, and never copied. Measured live on a running game (savestate vu1probe), because PINE cannot read VU memory (0x11000000 is outside EE main RAM -- it reads back zeros) but a PCSX2 savestate carries vu1MicroMem.bin (16,384 bytes; 4,051 of 4,096 words non-zero, 112 VU NOP pairs -- populated). Three measurements: (1) A word unique in the whole 109,184-byte blob (0x10af05ed, blob offset 16) occurs EXACTLY ONCE in 32 MiB of live EE RAM, at its linked address 0x467188 -- so no heap working copy of the microcode is ever made and an upload must read the image in place from rodata. (2) 1,635 of the 2,026 non-empty resident instruction pairs (80.7%) are present in the blob, and 5,787 of the blob's 13,648 pairs (42.4%) are resident -- so the resident code is this microcode, not a different build. (3) The mapping is NOT identity: VU1 offset 0 holds image offset 24 (a 24-byte delta), and a second resident window maps image offset ~0x11ac0 (72,384) to VU1 ~0x5a8 with delta 70,936 -- so the engine places programs at chosen VU1 addresses with a per-program delta, i.e. a program cache/overlay. That is what VU1's 16 KiB versus a 109,184-byte image forces, and it is why the symbol table exists at all: the engine looks up a program BY NAME, finds its image offset, and copies it to a VU1 address of its choosing. Consistent with the 24-byte shift at the start: 0x467178 is 8 mod 16, and the transfer needs 16-byte alignment (finding #491's "Buffer not aligned" path), so the first resident run begins at the 16-byte-aligned 0x467190 -- recorded as the reading of the delta, not as a proof of the alignment step. Consequence for the port: the microcode is real, and the (name -> image offset -> VU1 address) placement is the mechanism a native renderer replaces with "run program N".

**Notes:** Method: pi-state save vu1probe on a running game, then scripts/p2s_ram.py to read vu1MicroMem.bin. The same three checks can be re-run from any savestate. PINE's find/read cannot see VU memory; the savestate is the route.

**Evidence:** scripts/capture_scene.py

<a id="f494"></a>
### #494 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0048dd60 — the VU1 program metadata table at 0x48dd60 (finding #492); scripts/vu1_microcode.py cap_conditions; docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md

The VU1 program flags bitmask is decoded: 25 of its 29 bits are PROVEN, by set equality over all 201 records, to mean one thing. Method: read each bit's program set as a boolean condition over the words in the program names (words present, words absent, combined with OR), and accept a condition only if it reproduces the set EXACTLY. The search is exhaustive up to four terms and is cheap only because the terms are deduplicated by the set they select -- thousands of candidate terms collapse to a few dozen distinct sets, which took the generator from >30 minutes to 0.5 s. Proven meanings: 0x1 DECOMPRESSION OR COMPRESS; 0x2 PARTICLE; 0x4 BLEND; 0x8 SET OR SETCONVERT OR SETCOLOR; 0x10 CONVERT+EXECUTE OR CONVERT+SCALE OR SETCONVERT; 0x40 NORMALIZE; 0x100 OBJ (i.e. object-space local light); 0x1000 TEXGEN; 0x2000 DIRECTIONAL; 0x4000 POSITIONAL OR (LOCAL - OBJ); 0x8000 SPOTLIGHT OR SPOT; 0x10000 MULTIPASS+NORMAL; 0x20000 MT; 0x200000 SAVE - NORMAL; 0x100000 SCALE; 0x80000 MVSPRITE OR (SPRITE+TRANS); 0x1000000 FOG - CLIP; 0x4000000 END+SPRITE; 0x10000000 SETUP; 0x20000000 CALC; 0x40000000 GS OR (EXECUTE - COLOR); 0x80000000 MULTIPASS - SAVE; 0x40000 MP OR MVPN OR (MVP - CALC) OR (MVPS - CALC); 0x8000000 (CLIP+END) OR (CLIP+FOG) OR CULL; 0x800000 FLOAT OR STANDARD OR TRIVIAL. The remaining four bits have no condition up to four terms because they are combinations rather than one name feature, so the generated doc prints their EXACT program sets -- which is the answer a port needs anyway -- and the readings of those sets are kept in the generator as a separate, explicitly-labelled interpretation table: 0x80 = the four-infinite-directional-light programs (every INFINITE_4 program and none of the LIGHT_LOCAL_N_INFINITE_4_* ones, which are 0x4000); 0x800 = the model-view matrix is needed (every TRANS_MV* plus every COMPRESS_*, and the weaker of the two MV bits -- 24 of the 26 also carry 0x40000); 0x40000 = the model-view-projection matrix is needed (TRANS_MVP/MVPN/MVPS/MP and the COMPRESS_* programs); 0x2000000 = the projected MVPS matrix is needed (the TRANS_MVPS*/COMPRESS_* set plus all four VIEWPORT_KICK_END* programs, which project and then kick); 0x400000 = primitive-specific clipping (the CLIP_* programs parameterised by primitive kind -- LINE, LINESTRIP, POINT, SPRITE, TRIANGLE, TRIANGLEFAN, TRIANGLESTRIP and their _ARRAY/_CULLFACE forms -- as against the general clippers in 0x800000). Together with the family and the per-family counter in code>>16 this makes the pair (family, flags) a complete, engine-authored specification of what each of the 201 programs is, without reading a VU instruction. Generated into docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md by scripts/vu1_microcode.py; make rules gates it and runs in 0.5 s.

**Notes:** The condition grammar is (words present, words absent) ORed; proof is set equality over all 201 records. The four unproven bits' readings are in the generator as UNPROVEN_NOTES, deliberately separated from the proven rows so the difference between proof and interpretation stays visible in the generated doc.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/vu1-microcode.md

<a id="f495"></a>
### #495 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x00464d80 — the scene-graph walk (porting P4.2, #483) camera chain; dw4asset scene --cam in port/src/dw4asset.c; the live scene capture (#487)

The port extracts a stage's OWN camera, by composing a camera node's ancestor chain and inverting it: dw4asset scene --stage \<roms> \<stage.igb> --ref N --cam. The chain for stage pack 621's stage0102.igb is igSwitch (identity) -> igTransform (0,1,0) -> igTransform (0,-5,0) with a rotation about X (rows (1,0,0), (0,0.57358,-0.81915), (0,0.81915,0.57358)) -> igTransform (0,0,130) -> igCamera cameraShape00 (identity), and **the composition order is child x parent (leaf leftmost, row-vector convention): it gives (0.000, 102.490, 74.565)**. That order is the one that frames the geometry -- parent x child gives (0, -4.43, 129.18) and frames 3 of 702 while child x parent frames 389 of 702 -- and note the naive translation sum (0,-4,130) is a red herring, because a proper hierarchy DOES rotate the later (0,0,130) by the earlier rotation. Both igCamera nodes in the pack's graph (node 4 and node 8, in two different scenes) compose to the same matrix. **Correction to the first version of this finding:** it offered the observation that 95.3% of the 702 live placements (finding #487) lie on one side of this camera's plane as evidence that the offline camera and the live capture agree. That is weak -- a plane can have all geometry on one side trivially (a camera above the stage has everything below it) -- and the real evidence is in #496: with the stage's measured projection matrix, this camera puts 389 of the 702 placements ON SCREEN with the NDC range filling the frame. The first version also inferred a 90-100 degree field of view from the angular spread of the placements; that inference was WRONG (#496 measures 39.6 degrees vertical).

**Notes:** Tooling: port/src/dw4asset.c --cam (composes the ancestor chain, inverts it, prints world and view). The chain dump (--cam prints each ancestor's matrix too) is what settled the composition order. Cross-check script: transform the 702 live placements by the view matrix and the projection, and count what lands in NDC [-1,1]^2.

**Evidence:** port/src/dw4asset.c

<a id="f496"></a>
### #496 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x014c5180 — the stage projection matrix (per-visual-context copies in EE RAM); the pack camera chain (#495); the live placements (#487); the 640x448 framebuffer

The stage's projection matrix is in the live RAM dump, and together with the pack's camera it puts 55% of the live placements on screen -- which is what settles both. Search the 32.5 MiB dump for a 4x4 that is a perspective projection in row-major form with the translation at words 12..14: m[3]=m[7]=m[15]=0, m[1]=m[2]=m[4]=m[6]=m[8]=m[9]=m[12]=m[13]=0, m[11]=-1, m[0]>0, m[5]>0, m[14]<0. That matches **48 candidates over four distinct parameter sets**, of which the one that recurs is m[0]=1.9444, m[5]=2.7778, m[10]=-1.0690, m[14]=-20.6897 -- i.e. a **vertical field of view of 39.60 degrees** (2*atan(1/m[5])) and a **horizontal/vertical ratio of exactly 1.4286 = 448/640**, which is the PS2's standard 640x448 framebuffer, which is why this candidate is the game's and the others (0.6667, and one that is this matrix scaled by 1/6) are not. Note w = -m[11]*z = -v.z, so the camera looks down **-Z** and a point is in front when its view-space z is NEGATIVE -- getting that sign wrong is what made an earlier attempt conclude "only 1 of 702 placements is on screen". With the sign right: the pack's camera (finding #495, composed child x parent) plus this projection puts **389 of the 702 live placements (55.4%) on screen**, with NDC x in [-0.25, 0.94], y in [-0.93, 0.99] and view depth 97 to 355 -- the frame is filled, the median depth is plausible for a stage, and the 313 off-screen ones split into 33 behind the camera and the rest outside the field of view, which is what a stage with props beyond the visible area looks like. That is a genuine two-way corroboration: the pack's camera chain (#483), the live placements (#487), the composition order (child x parent), and this projection all agree at once, and an unrelated camera or projection would not fill the frame. Also recorded as a negative: the framing test alone does NOT identify a camera, because any sufficiently distant camera frames everything -- of 18,873 orthonormal affine matrices in the dump, many put more than 95% of the placements on screen when the field of view is narrow and the camera is far away. What discriminates is the *combination* with a projection whose aspect matches the framebuffer.

**Notes:** Candidates found by a numpy scan of the pre-leomon EE RAM dump for the perspective signature; the 1.4286 aspect (448/640) is the discriminator. The off-screen split (33 behind, the rest outside the FOV) is the sanity check.

**Evidence:** Offline scan of a saved EE RAM dump of a paused stag0102 for the perspective signature

<a id="f497"></a>
### #497 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x0055ebc0 — the 30 generic platform interfaces and the 32 igPsx2* classes over them (data/class-hierarchy.tsv, data/reflection.json); scripts/gen_platform_seam.py; DW4_Port include/dw4/platform.h + src/host.c

The platform seam is an inventory, not a claim: 32 igPsx2* classes, 31 of them under 30 generic interfaces carrying 153 fields, and igPsx2IopRpcConnection is the one that derives from igObject instead. Recovered from data rather than asserted (scripts/gen_platform_seam.py -> docs/engine/platform-seam.md, gated by make rules): data/class-hierarchy.tsv gives each class's base, so every igPsx2* class yields the generic class it sits under (igPsx2File -> igFile, igPsx2VisualContext -> igVisualContext, igPsx2Thread -> igThread,...), and data/reflection.json gives that base's fields with names, offsets and metafield types -- so each interface's state is recovered too (igFile is +12 _fileName igStringMetaField, +16 _readMode igEnumMetaField, +20 _openMode igEnumMetaField, +24 _fileHandle igRawRefMetaField, +28 _optimalReadChunkSize igIntMetaField,...). The 30 distinct interfaces are igAudioContext, igBlendEquationExt, igCallStackTracer, igClut, igController, igControllerManager, igDataList (carried by two igPsx2* classes), igDecalExt, igDisableExt, igEnvironmentMapShader2, igFile, igFolder, igImageConvert, igIndexArray, igLongTimer, igMedia, igMemoryImage, igMultiTextureExt, igParticleArray, igPointSpriteExt, igScissorExt, igSemaphore, igThread, igThreadManager, igUserUCodeExt, igVertexArray1_1, igVertexArray2, igVertexStream, igVisualContext and igWindow; the field counts range from 0 (igWindow, igMedia, igMemoryImage, igImageConvert, igCallStackTracer, igSemaphore, igScissorExt, igMultiTextureExt, igUserUCodeExt, igDisableExt) to 28 (igVisualContext) and 22 (igParticleArray). Nothing in the binary says "platform"; the seam is recovered by inverting the class hierarchy at the igPsx2 prefix, which is why it is exactly 31 of 32 and why the odd one out is the RPC class the porting plan already calls the seam itself. Port side (DW4_Port, P2.1 first increment): include/dw4/platform.h declares the inventory and the status type, src/host.c carries the 31-row table with each class's generic interface and field count plus the stubs, and `dw4asset platform [-v]` reports the seam (31 classes -> 30 interfaces, 1 implemented, 30 stub). The layering is real from the start: src/igfile.c already IS the host igFile (P2.3's first increment), which is what makes igPsx2File the one row marked implemented and gives the other 30 rows the shape to follow. tools/verify_platform.py pins the three totals in make check, so if the recovered seam changes the port's check fails instead of the port believing a stale seam.

**Notes:** The igPsx2 prefix is the only handle on the seam; the port deletes igPsx2IopRpcConnection because it derives from igObject rather than from a generic interface.

**Evidence:** port/src/host.c

<a id="f498"></a>
### #498 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x001fb710 — FUN_001fb710 / p2lighting (0x1fb710, 980 bytes) and the light state it assembles; the LIGHT program family (#489/#492/#494)

The engine's light structure: 16-byte records, assembled in p2lighting (0x1fb710, the p2 lighting function identified in #491 from its own log tag). Reading the function's body statically: a loop advances by 16 (addiu v0,v0,16) writing at record+12 a float read from state offset 64 (lwc1 $f0,64(s3); swc1 $f0,12(v0)) -- so the records are 16 bytes and that float is a per-light colour or intensity; and a 3-float vector is read from state offsets 112/116/120 (lwc1 $f0,112(s4) into a stack buffer) and passed as the payload of the "p2lighting" trace event (a0 = the string at 0x4e5150). So the light state is a 16-byte-record array reachable from a state structure, with a vector per light at state+112..120 and a scalar at state+64. NOT yet recovered: the full record layout, the light count, the ambient and material terms, or any light VALUES -- which is what an implementation needs. NEGATIVES so the shortcuts are not retried: (1) the stage pack has NO class whose name contains "Light" (dw4asset scan local/extracted Light -> 0 entries), so the stage's lights are not serialised in the pack and cannot be read offline; (2) VU1 data memory -- where #491 says the LIGHT programs' constants are uploaded, and which a savestate carries as vu1Memory.bin (16 KiB) -- is far too dense in float patterns to identify lights by shape: 696 of its 4096 floats look like unit vectors and 1685 look like rgb+0 quads, so pattern matching picks up ordinary geometry and matrices rather than lights. The right order is to finish reading p2lighting for the layout, THEN implement: the shading MODEL is already known from the program names (AMBIENT, DIRECTIONAL, SPOTLIGHT, POSITIONAL, LINEAR_ATT, _SPEC/_NOSPEC) and from the flags decode (#494), so only the parameters are missing.

**Notes:** Static reading only; no emulator. See docs/engine/p2-renderer.md for the p2 layer this sits in.

**Evidence:** DW4_Decomp/docs/engine/p2-renderer.md

<a id="f499"></a>
### #499 · refuted

**Where:** 0x001fb710 — p2lighting_0x1fb710

p2lighting (0x1fb710, the p2 lighting function of #491) is decoded end to end: it is the EE->VU1 light constant builder. It reads the runtime lighting state *(0x56AF60)[0] and the registered light descriptors ((void**)0x56AF40)[idx], and emits a stream of 16-byte records, then returns the advanced cursor. The 16-byte record order is: {state[48..59], lightCount=*(int*)0x56AF94}; state[0..15]; state[16..31]; the global block 0x56AEE0 (copied by FUN_001f8aa0); and per light {L[0x50..0x5b], state[64]}, then L[0x70..0x7f] (or homogeneous-transformed by the light matrix 0x56AFD0 when bit21 && !scale && count), then L[0x10..0x1f], then L[0x20..0x2f] with.w = L[0x54] when the flag is set. Before the per-light pass, when 0x56AFAE is set and (texgen==0 || mode51), it accumulates record0.xyz += L[0x10..0x1c]*state.xyz over the ambient-pass table 0x56AF30 (bound 0x56AF00). Callers: FUN_001fb0e0 (0x1fb0e0, the program selector) calls p2lighting for the lit path and FUN_001fb470 for the unlit path; FUN_001f9430 updates the state. The per-light descriptor is a 160-byte runtime struct (stride 0xa0 in FUN_001709d0), NOT the serialised igLightAttr (148 bytes, reflected fields at +0x10 _lightId/+0x14 _position, +0x20 _ambient, +0x30 _diffuse, +0x40 _specular, +0x50 _direction, +0x5c _falloff, +0x60 _cutoff, +0x64 _attenuation, +0x70 _shininess, +0x74 _cachedPosition) -- its fields carry measured roles (direction at +0x60 normalised by FUN_00170ed0; position at +0x70). MEASURED NEGATIVE: 0x56AF40 is all NULL in every one of the 17 saved states and 0x56AF94 (the light count) is 0, so the light list is populated only during the render pass and a savestate cannot supply the parameter VALUES; only a breakpoint mid-render or a statefile taken while rendering can. The shading MODEL is fully known from the LIGHT program names (#489/#492/#494: AMBIENT, DIRECTIONAL/INFINITE, POSITIONAL/LOCAL, SPOTLIGHT, LINEAR_ATT, _SPEC/_NOSPEC) and igLightAttr's fields.

**Notes:** PARTLY SUPERSEDED by #554: the record stream, the runtime state and the call chain here are correct, but the conclusion that the light PARAMETERS are not in the disc is wrong -- the source objects are igLightAttr records in the common packs (the 'no class containing Light' scan looked at class directories and missed them).
normalised refuted; superseded by #554 — the light parameters are on the disc (#554); the record stream and call chain stand.

**Evidence:** docs/engine/p2-lighting.md

<a id="f500"></a>
### #500 · confirmed

**Where:** 0x002fd7c0 — igMetaObject_CreateInstance

Runtime object model (P2.6) is bounded and constructible from a pack record. A loaded stage's records (NDMW.AFS entries 621+622) instantiate 39341 records over 203 distinct classes and use exactly 17 named MetaField types (igObjectRef 30242 values, igInt 17857, igVec3f 5817, igBool 4564, igFloat 4395, igLong 3807, igUnsignedInt, igVec4f, igMemoryRef, igEnum, igString, igMatrix44f, igUnsignedChar, igStruct, igRawRef, igUnsignedLong, igShort). Construction is the igMetaObject Construct/CreateInstance path; a record's descriptor is matched to its declared field by ordinal = declaredIndex + K + 1, with K recovered by the same type match igb_inst.py:find_K uses, then the value is copied to the reflected offset at the descriptor type's element width. Verified against the decompilation's independent decoder: 39341 records / 70077 field values, 0 disagreements over entries 621+622 (a slot whose descriptor type does not match the declared field is excluded: the ordinal map has non-conformers where either naming is a guess). The port implements it in src/object.c + include/dw4/object.h with the schema generated from data/reflection.json (tools/gen_reflection.py -> src/reflection_data.c, 1074 classes / 3680 fields) and a CLI dw4asset reflect; make check diffs it against igb_inst.py. Example: beGeneraterData round-trips _geneEnemyId 36, _startModeStr direct, _startData 150, _fillModeStr wipeout, _pos (-117,72,609) -- consistent with the live values #415 measured.

**Notes:** Offline, from the pack alone. The 203-class / 17-type census is the first bound of the object-model surface (the live instantiated set is 171 of 478 vtable classes, #415); the port's construction reproduces the Python decoder value-for-value on 39341 records. Not yet done: the igObject vtable interface block / MetaObject method slots (#359), i.e. constructing and reading works but running methods does not. Test: port/tools/verify_object.py, in make check.

**Evidence:** port/tools/verify_object.py
